# TGP — Toastty Graphics Protocol: Implementation Plan

> **Status:** complete. Dependency-ordered, test-first plan for implementing TGP across five phases (P1→P5).
> Every protocol surface (op/verb, header field, error code, capability, lifecycle event) is covered by ≥1 GPU-free unit test on one of three seams (ingress / interaction / lifecycle).

## Authoritative sources

This plan implements, and must be read alongside:

1. **Design of record:** [`2026-05-31-tgp-design-v2.md`](2026-05-31-tgp-design-v2.md) (§1–§19).
2. **Global invariants:** [`tgp-reconciliation.md`](tgp-reconciliation.md) (G1–G10).
3. **Ratified addendum (supersedes the above on conflict):** [`tgp-reconciliation-addendum.md`](tgp-reconciliation-addendum.md) — **G11** private per-token isolation model (`denied` reserved), **G12** error-code reachability (24 reachable / 2 reserved across the closed 26-code enum).

Per-phase source files live under [`tgp-impl/`](tgp-impl/); each phase also has a point-in-time verification review in `tgp-impl/P{n}.review.md` (audit trail — reflects the state at the time of that review).

## How to read a task

Each task carries a stable id (`P{n}-T{k}`), a **Deliverable** (crate/module/types), **Design refs** (design §§ + G-invariants), **Depends on** (task ids; cross-phase deps point only to earlier phases), and **Tests** (each: name / seam / concrete input bytes-or-events / concrete `&self`/`pty_replies` assertion).

# TGP implementation plan — overview & index

TGP (Toastty Graphics Protocol) is a clean-break, retained-mode 3D terminal-graphics
protocol for the toastty terminal (Rust workspace). This document indexes the five-phase
implementation plan, records the validated cross-phase dependency ordering, names the
three GPU-free test seams, and states the protocol-surface coverage guarantee.

**Authoritative sources.** The per-section design slices `sections/s05.md … s15.md`, the
reconciliation spine `../tgp-reconciliation.md` (global invariants **G1–G10**), and the
**authoritative addendum `../tgp-reconciliation-addendum.md` (G11–G12)** are the design
of record; where the spine disagrees with a slice, the spine wins, and **where the addendum
disagrees with the spine or a slice, the addendum wins** (it is explicitly authoritative). There
is **no RGP compatibility/adapter** (G8 clean break) — TGP lives in greenfield `tgp/*` trees in
`toastty-graphics`, `toastty-render`, and `toastty-term`; the legacy `rgp/*` path is untouched.

**Addendum G11/G12 (binding on all phases).** The v1 isolation model is a **private per-token id
map** (G11): each handshaked token owns a physically-disjoint `(id → object)` map, so cross-token
addressing is **unrepresentable by construction**. The consequences are applied uniformly across
P1–P5: a cross-token id "collision" is **coexistence** (two distinct `{tok,id}` entries, never an
error); a cross-token handle/patch ref is **`bad_ref`** with **no existence oracle** (byte-for-byte
identical to absent-everywhere); a cross-token viewport `camera`/`root` is **late-bind default**;
a token-requiring frame with no valid handshake is **`bad_token`** (a well-formed tokenless `tgp;p`
is a silent drop). Therefore **`denied` is RESERVED** — no reachable ingress trigger, carried only
by the closed-set encoder round-trip. G12 fixes one error-code reachability taxonomy across the
phases: **24 of 26 codes are REACHABLE, 2 are RESERVED** (`msg_too_large`, `denied`); `unknown_op`
is REACHABLE via the closed control-frame anim-verb locus (an `anim` verb ∉ {play,pause,seek,stop}),
and RESERVED for patch `do:` ops (which skip unknowns per §7.3). An over-`max_msg_mb` breach is
canonically **`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** (G12.1, `msg_too_large` reserved); and a node-level tint on an `Instanced` node is **`kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`**
(G12.4 — the base-spine `instanced_node_tint→bad_param` synonym is struck). P1-T16 owns the closed
26-variant enum + emitter; P1 and P5 agree on exactly this partition.

This overview is a coherence-validated index. The cross-phase critic findings (dependency
graph, surface partition) are summarized at the end; the per-phase files carry the full task
deliverables, tests, and review logs.

## The five phases (one-line goals)

| Phase | File | Goal |
|---|---|---|
| **P1 — Foundation** | `P1.md` (T1–T21) | Stand up the entire CPU-side spine end to end: APC binary/control framer + `apc_end -> ApcEnd` back-channel, CBOR patch decode, always-answered capability handshake (`tgp;r` before DA1, per-token namespace), retained per-token scene model (`u64` revisions + dirty flags), inline GLB assets, the PINNED default material, the injectable clock, the closed `tgp;x`/`tgp;a` enum + emitters, and ONE viewport at compositing parity. |
| **P2 — Differentiators** | `P2.md` (T1–T38) | The features that make TGP more than "GLB in a cell": multiple cell-anchored + pinned viewports with offscreen color+depth targets and depth-aware compositing, terminal-driven reflow, GPU instancing render, the full interactivity spine (CPU picking, `tgp;e` events, `sub` subscriptions, explore camera controller, F4 input routing), device-loss recovery, and RIS/DECSTR teardown for viewport/interaction state. |
| **P3 — Materials & light** | `P3.md` (T1–T19, incl. T4b) | Registered PBR (metal-roughness) materials, hardened PNG/GLB-embedded textures, punctual `Light` nodes with replace-not-augment per-viewport lighting, the per-viewport render stages (theme-tint, tone-map, MSAA clamp-down+ack, always-1× pick target), the sRGB-wire/linear-internal color pipeline, and VRAM accounting for MSAA+resolve+pick targets. |
| **P4 — Animation & accessibility** | `P4.md` (T0–T15, incl. T4a/T9a) | Terminal-side animation playback on the deterministic clock (`anim;play/pause/seek/stop`, `(clip,node)` handles, glTF clip import + clip-id ack), TRS-ownership/`node_busy`/`camera_busy` rules, per-node dirty render scheduling, `alt`-text accessibility surfacing via the CPU pick path, bind-pose skinning (no `feat=skin`), and anim/clip lifecycle teardown. |
| **P5 — Hardening & lifecycle** | `P5.md` (T1–T26) | Close the protocol against untrusted input and tie off every lifecycle event: enforce every allocation/framing/structural cap *before* allocation with the drain-then-error shape, the closed versioned error taxonomy end-to-end (consuming P1-T16's enum, with the G12 reachable/reserved partition), per-token isolation via the **private per-token id map** (the G11 confused-deputy fix; `denied` RESERVED), the single fuzz oracle at `Parser::advance(&mut Term)`, and G10 lifecycle (RIS/DECSTR/alt-screen/erase/eviction/device-loss/PTY-EOF). |

## Validated cross-phase dependency ordering

The phases form a strict DAG **P1 → P2 → P3 → P4 → P5** at the phase level: every cross-phase
`Depends on` points to an **earlier** phase, and no task depends on a later phase
(forward-dependency-free at phase granularity). The notable concrete cross-phase edges:

- **P2** builds only on **P1** (scene model, framer/CBOR decode, caps/handshake, world matrices,
  the deterministic clock, `Term::tgp_scene()`/`pty_replies` seams, and the `apc_end` back-channel
  P1 introduced). P2 internally re-orders for the shared `destroy_viewport` helper: **P2-T36 is
  built before its callers P2-T11/T12/T13 and P2-T1's `op=remove`** even though it is numbered
  later (numeric order ≠ topological order, documented in P2-T36).
- **P3** builds on **P1** (T6/T7/T8/T9/T11/T12/T13/T14/T15) and **P2** (T1 multi-viewport, T6
  offscreen targets, T20 picking, T21/T23/T24 sub/event/input, T37 device-loss). `asset.remove`
  (P3-T4b) is a **plan-defined op** pending §8.6 ratification (the design names "remove + add" but
  leaves the verb unnamed).
- **P4** uses cross-phase **aliases** mapped in its preamble to P1/P2: `TGP-FOUNDATION`,
  `SCENE-MODEL`, `ASSET-ADD`, `ACK-FRAME`, `ERROR-FRAME`, `APC-END-BACKCHANNEL`, `CAPS-REPLY`
  (P1); `CLOCK`, `PICK`, `EVENT-EMIT`, `EXPLORE` (P2). P4 extends the closed `ev=` enum with a
  node-scoped `anim_end` token (P4-T9a) and extends the `Viewport`/`tgp;vp` op with an `alt` key
  (P4-T1) — both flagged as design-reconciliation items.
- **P5** depends on P1–P4 (mostly via prose phase references: "P1 handshake/token plumbing",
  "P2 instancing", "P3 lights", "P4 anim") plus its own intra-phase chain rooted at P5-T1
  (the closed error enum/emitter) and P5-T2 (handshake+focus emission gate). The fuzz oracle
  (P5-T26) depends transitively on the cap/lifecycle tasks it asserts.

**Within-phase dependencies** are acyclic in every phase (P1's `T11→T15` install-seam edge was
made acyclic by removing the spurious `T15→T14` edge; P3's `T4→T4b` is forward-by-number but
backward-in-build with T4b delivering the op T4's test drives; P2's T36-before-callers is
documented).

### Residual dependency issue — RESOLVED

- **`LIFECYCLE` dangling alias — FIXED.** `P4-T15` (`Depends on: P4-T6, P4-T4, LIFECYCLE`) named an
  alias `LIFECYCLE` that was previously absent from P4's cross-phase alias legend. The P4 cleanup
  pass added it: the legend now maps **`LIFECYCLE` → P2-T38** (RIS/DECSTR teardown machinery) **+
  P2-T15** (alt-screen affinity / screen-buffer binding) — the established lifecycle/affinity
  machinery P4-T15 wires animation state into (G10). The alias now resolves to defined,
  earlier-phase tasks; no dangling alias remains.

A re-scan of every cross-phase `Depends on` line confirms each points to a concrete, defined task in
an earlier or the same phase. **No forward dependencies** (a task depending on a later phase) and **no
dangling task ids or aliases** were found.

## The three GPU-free test seams (G6)

Every protocol surface — each op/verb, header field, error code, capability, lifecycle event —
has ≥1 unit test on one of these three seams, all asserting CPU-side state with no GPU:

1. **INGRESS** (covers nearly all surfaces). Feed bytes via `Parser::advance(&mut Term, bytes)`;
   assert internal state via `&self` accessors: `Term::tgp_scene()` nodes (kind, local TRS,
   computed WORLD matrix), viewport pixel rects (cell→pixel CPU-side), decoded instance buffers
   (count/layout/values), asset/material/clip/light tables, `scene_revision`/`asset_revision` +
   per-node/per-viewport dirty flags, and queued PTY reply bytes in `pty_replies`
   (`tgp;r`/`tgp;x`/`tgp;a`). Tests state concrete input bytes and concrete accessor assertions.

2. **INTERACTION** (section-12 surfaces). Inject synthetic pointer/focus events via the `Term`
   input API (`input_pointer`/`input_focus`, P2-T24) — **never** via `Parser::advance`. Assert
   camera node TRS after explore, queued `tgp;e`/`x`/`a` reply bytes (`tok=`/`vp=`/`node=`/`inst=`),
   and hover/sub state. Picking is **CPU-resolvable** (ray-cast vs node world AABBs / instance
   transforms; P2-T20) so picks resolve with no GPU; any GPU color-ID pass is an accelerator that
   MUST agree. Time advances on the ONE injectable deterministic clock (P1-T5), stepped explicitly.

3. **LIFECYCLE** (G10). Drive RIS / DECSTR / alt-screen switch / CSI 2J / ED / resize /
   scrollback-eviction / device-loss / PTY-EOF as byte sequences or `Term` calls; assert
   teardown / affinity / recovery via accessors (`vram_used`, `viewport_alive`, `tgp_token_valid`,
   `pending_bytes`, etc.).

All geometry stays CPU-side and queryable (world matrices, pixel rects, instance decode, pick
resolution); the GPU only consumes it.

## Protocol-surface coverage (G6 testing contract)

**Every protocol surface introduced in v1 is covered by ≥1 unit test.** Cross-checked:

- **Frame types** (`q r p vp sub anim e x a`): all owned and tested — P1 (q/r/p/x/a), P2 (vp/sub/e),
  P4 (anim). P1-T3 `header_every_frame_type_recognized` asserts all nine map to a `FrameType`.
- **Ops** (`asset.add material.add node.upsert node.instances node.instance_set node.visible
  node.remove`): owned across P1/P2/P3, each decode+apply tested. `asset.remove` is the
  plan-defined removal op (P3-T4b) flagged for §8.6 ratification.
- **Anim verbs** (`play pause seek stop`): P4-T7, with verb/field error tests.
- **Closed `ev=` set** (`click dblclick down up hover enter leave drag wheel camera resize
  destroyed`): all owned/tested in P2; `anim_end` is the P4-T9a closed-enum extension.
- **Closed error enum (26 codes)**: **owned by P1-T16** (the 26-variant `TgpErrorCode` enum +
  `encode_error`/`wire_code`); **P5-T1 CONSUMES it** (adds only the `ErrorCorr` routing +
  `emit_error` formatter + handshake/focus gating, no second enum). The closed-set round-trip is
  co-owned (P1-T16 `closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26`, P5-T1 `error_code_wire_strings_closed_set`).
  Per the **G12.3 partition, P1 and P5 agree exactly: 24 REACHABLE, 2 RESERVED.** Each reachable
  code has ≥1 concrete ingress reject test across P1-T16 (`every_p1_error_code_emitted`,
  P1-reachable subset), P4-T7 (`unknown_op` via the closed anim-verb locus), and P5-T24 (the
  remaining semantic codes); the 2 RESERVED codes (`denied`, `msg_too_large`) are covered only by
  the closed-set round-trip (`denied` per G11(a) — cross-token addressing is unrepresentable;
  `msg_too_large` per G12.1 — over-msg-cap funnels through `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`).
  `unknown_op` is REACHABLE via the closed control-frame anim-verb locus (an `anim` verb ∉
  {play,pause,seek,stop}, P4-T7) but RESERVED for patch `do:` ops (which skip unknowns per §7.3).
  `instanced_node_tint` is `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (G12.4) in P1, P2,
  and P5 alike.
- **Capabilities / `feat=` (11 tokens) / `enc` / `color`**: P1-T6 advertises the frozen set;
  implication rules property-tested (P1-T6 `feat_set_satisfies_implications`, P3-T2
  `feat_pbr_implies_material_property`). Each feat token has an impl owner; use-time `unsupported`
  gates tested at the verb use-sites (P2-T16 instance, P2-T22 pick, P2-T28 explore).
- **Lifecycle events** (RIS, DECSTR, alt-screen, CSI 2J/ED, eviction, detach, device-loss,
  PTY-EOF, `ev=destroyed/resize`): owned in P2 (T36/T38), P3 (T18), P4 (T15), P5 (T17–T23).

### Residual coverage notes (not gaps)

- **Scene-level `alt`** is **out of scope for v1** by design — §13.1 mentions a scene caption but
  defines no wire op to set it (P4 reconciliation item 2). This is a deliberate documented gap,
  not a missing test.
- **`anim_speed_max`, `camera_report_hz`, `msaa`, `click_deadzone_px`** are advertised caps whose
  enforcement is behavioral (clamp/throttle/option), owned by their feature phase (P4 anim, P2/P4
  explore, P3/P2 render-input), explicitly scoped out of P5's allocation-cap tasks (P5 preamble
  "Cap-ownership scope"). Their tests live in the owning phase, so G7 advertised==enforced holds
  whole.

No protocol surface present in the design sections is assigned to **no** phase.

## Surface-partition findings (critic) — all three duplications RESOLVED

The three previously-flagged surfaces claimed by two phases are now each framed by the later phase
as an explicit **reuse/extension** of the earlier owner (verified in the cleanup passes). The
surfaces stay covered and are now singly-owned. Listed in original order:

1. **`node.instances` decode + instance-buffer hardened validation — P1-T12 owner, P2 reuses.**
   **RESOLVED.** **P1-T12 is the canonical owner** of the `node.instances` byte decode + hardened
   layout/cap/NaN validation + `InstanceBuffer` storage (it carries a "Canonical owner — later phases
   reuse this; do not re-implement" note). **P2-T16/T17 are recast as thin consumers + use-site
   policy**: T16 wires the `NodeKind::Instanced` scene-kind + the P2-only `feat=instance` use-time
   gate; T17 owns only the P2 scene-apply consequences (atomic last-committed-buffer retention on
   reject + the Instanced-kind tint invariant `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`); T18
   (`instance_set` re-tint) and T19 (GPU render path) are the genuinely P2-owned write/render paths.
   There is **no second `instance.rs` decoder** — P2's decode-value tests were renamed to
   surface-through-accessor tests (`instances_decode_surfaced_to_node`, etc.) asserting P2 surfaces
   the P1-decoded buffer unaltered. P2-T16/T17/T18/T19 all cite P1-T12 in `Depends on`.

2. **Closed error enum + `tgp;x` emitter — P1-T16 owner, P5-T1 consumes.** **RESOLVED.** P5-T1 is
   retitled "Error-correlator routing + handshake/focus gating — **EXTENDS P1-T16**" with a binding
   DEDUP note: it **reuses** P1-T16's `TgpErrorCode` enum + `wire_code` + `encode_error` (no
   redefinition, no second `error.rs`) and adds **only** the `ErrorCorr`/`TgpError` correlator types,
   the `emit_error` formatter, and the `should_emit` handshake/focus gating. `Depends on` cites
   `P1-T16 (consumed not redefined)`. The closed-set round-trip is co-owned (G12.3) and re-asserted in
   P5-T1 as a regression guard only.

3. **`tgp;a` ack emitter — P1-T17 owner, P5-T25 reuses.** **RESOLVED.** P5-T25 is retitled
   "`max_backchannel_bytes` enforcement on the ack/error/event queue — **EXTENDS P1-T17**" with a
   binding DEDUP note: it **reuses** P1-T17's `emit_ack` formatter + ack sites + correlator routing
   (no re-definition) and adds **only** the new `max_backchannel_bytes` enforcement on the shared
   reply queue + `Term::backchannel_pending(tok)`. `Depends on` cites `P1-T17 (reused not redefined)`.
   The retained ack tests are explicit regression guards.

Softer / partial overlaps (the later phase mostly adds new behavior, so noted not flagged):
**P5-T2** formalizes handshake-gated emission that P1-T10/T16 already arm, but adds genuinely-new
focus/fg-pgrp gating; **P5-T3** restates the per-`(token,id)` namespace keying P1-T7/T11 already
established, but consolidates `resolve_owned` for every reference site under the ratified G11 private
per-token map. RIS/device-loss teardown across P2-T38 / P3-T18 / P4-T15 / P5-T17 is **correct layered
ownership** (each phase tears down only the state it introduced), not duplication.

**Misplaced surfaces:** none. The scope split is honored — the default material is in P1 (P1-T20,
foundation), PBR materials/lights in P3 (P3-T3/T9), instancing render in P2, animation in P4,
caps-hardening/fuzz in P5. The `node.instances` decode is correctly a CPU surface in P1's scope
(P1-T12), consumed — not re-implemented — by P2.

# P1 — Foundation

This phase stands up the entire CPU-side spine of TGP end to end: the binary/control framer (including the cross-crate `apc_end -> ApcEnd::BinaryFollows{len,enc,trl}` back-channel that lets the dumb pre-scanner enter byte-counted binary-read mode, plus the opt-in `trl=1` integrity trailer), CBOR patch decode, the always-answered capability handshake (`tgp;r` before DA1, per-token namespace), the retained per-token scene model with `u64` revisions and per-node/per-viewport dirty flags, inline GLB assets with hardened bounds-checked accessors, the PINNED default material, the injectable deterministic clock, and a single viewport rendered at compositing parity. Every protocol surface introduced here — each op/verb, header field, error code, capability, and lifecycle event in scope — gets at least one GPU-free unit test at the ingress or lifecycle seam, asserting CPU-side state via `&self` accessors (`Term::tgp_scene()`, pixel rects, decoded buffers, revisions/dirty flags) or queued `pty_replies` bytes.

**P1 scope clarifications (round-1 review resolutions).** The following are explicitly **in P1** because they are pure-CPU protocol surfaces the testing contract requires: (a) `material.add` op decode + commit (default-material-only fields applied; PBR fields parsed-but-inert) — bumps `asset_revision`; (b) `node.instances` **decode + hardened layout/cap/NaN validation** storing the decoded `InstanceBuffer` (GPU upload + instanced draw defer to P2) — bumps `scene_revision` + `instance_dirty`, never `asset_revision`; (c) **node-id charset/length hardening** (≤64 bytes, no control/ESC → `bad_param;detail=charset|too_long`, §8.4/§15.5); (d) the **`trl=1` integrity trailer** parse + verify (advertised `bintrailer` feat is honored); (e) the three over-length binary-frame branches (ok / over-`max_msg_mb` drain-then-`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` / over-`BIN_HARD_CEIL` no-drain-`cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable`) — per **G12.1** the per-message decoded-size breach is `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` on BOTH the bin-drain and the decode-reject loci (`msg_too_large` is RESERVED, never emitted in P1); (f) an **empty-ops patch** is a valid commit that acks (bin-probe path). Per the **G11/G12 addendum (authoritative)**: a node-level tint on an `Instanced` node encodes as **`code=kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`** — **G12.4** rules this the single canonical code (a kind-invariant violation) and **strikes** the base-spine `instanced_node_tint→bad_param` synonym, so the addendum supersedes round-1's prior `bad_param` resolution (I9 is re-decided). `bad_param` remains reachable only on its own triggers (node-id charset/length, out-of-range op fields). Per **§7.3/G12.2** every unknown `do:` patch op string is **skipped** (not `unknown_op`), so `unknown_op` is in the closed enum and is **NOT P1-reachable** — but it is globally **REACHABLE** via the **P4 closed-anim-verb locus** (a verb outside the closed `anim` set {play,pause,seek,stop}, s14.3, is a malformed control frame that draws `unknown_op`, G12.3 row 8); it is therefore **NOT** a member of the reserved set, only *reserved for patch ops*. Per **G11(a)/G12.2** the two genuinely **RESERVED** codes (no reachable ingress trigger anywhere) are `denied` and `msg_too_large`: cross-token id collisions COEXIST as distinct `{tok,id}` entries under the private per-token id map (never `denied`), and the over-msg-cap breach funnels through `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (never `msg_too_large`). Per **§10.2/§10.3** an over-cap viewport is **rejected** (`cap_exceeded`), never silently clamped (the on-screen-intersection clamp is a separate, legitimate clamp).

**Isolation model (G11, authoritative).** P1 implements the ratified **private per-token id map**: each handshaked token owns a `T → (id → object)` map that no other token can index, so cross-token addressing is **unrepresentable by construction** (there is no shared id table, no cross-token lookup primitive). Two tokens that each create `"cam"` (or asset `7`, or viewport `9`) hold **two distinct, coexisting entries** `{A,"cam"}` and `{B,"cam"}` — this is **not** a collision and is **never** `denied`. Per **G11(b′)** the per-token maps MUST be **physically disjoint allocations** (token T's lookups never index/hash-into/contend any structure holding another token's entries) so the no-existence-oracle guarantee survives at the implementation level. `denied` is therefore **RESERVED** (closed-enum member, no reachable ingress trigger). Threat-model honesty (G11 scope): the token is a bearer credential with no unguessability requirement, echoed in cleartext; the private map defeats *accidental* collisions and *unguessing* adversaries but a stream-reading adversary who learns a token can write same-token frames that resolve and mutate the victim's objects without ever drawing `denied` — token confidentiality is load-bearing for isolation.

**RIS teardown (G10, in P1 — R3-2).** Because P1 is the phase that *creates* the per-token scene/session/reasm tables (T4/T7/T10/T14), it is also the canonical owner of their teardown hook: **RIS (`ESC c`) tears down the entire TGP scene across all tokens** (assets, materials, nodes, viewports, in-flight reassembly buffers) and invalidates all session tokens — the guaranteed VRAM-recovery path (§10.7 line 79, G10). **DECSTR (`ESC [ ! p`) leaves the TGP scene intact.** This lands in T4 (folded) so the create-without-teardown asymmetry is never shipped untested.

Out of scope and deferred to later phases: multi-viewport/reflow/instancing **render**/picking/events/explore/subscriptions (P2; `node.instance_set` re-tint, `sub` behavior); PBR pipeline/lights/MSAA/tone-map (P3); animation/accessibility (P4; `anim` behavior); and full lifecycle hardening beyond RIS/DECSTR — alt-screen/scrollback-eviction/`2J`/device-loss/PTY-EOF (P5). `sub`/`anim` control frames are **parsed and treated as safe no-ops** in P1 (recognized FrameType, never crash, no scene mutation); only default material + compositing parity of §11 lands here.

## P1-T1 — APC back-channel: `apc_end` returns a typed binary-follow signal (cross-crate change)

**Title:** Cross-crate `apc_end -> ApcEnd` parser/term back-channel (`Done | BinaryFollows{len,enc,trl}`).

**Deliverable:** Change the dumb APC pre-scanner so the Perform layer can tell it to enter byte-counted binary-read mode. In `crates/toastty-parser/src/perform.rs`: change `Perform::apc_end(&mut self)` to `fn apc_end(&mut self) -> ApcEnd`, and add the typed return `enum ApcEnd { Done, BinaryFollows { len: u64, enc: BinEnc, trl: bool } }` with `enum BinEnc { Bin }` (today it returns unit — this is the required signature change). The `trl: bool` carries whether the header set `trl=1` so the pre-scanner knows to read the 8-byte integrity trailer (§8.2) AFTER the `len` raw bytes — the trailer is NOT counted in `len` (P1-T2 handles the trailer bytes; the back-channel only signals its presence). In `crates/toastty-parser/src/parser.rs` (the `apc_end` call site, ~line 190) the parser inspects the returned `ApcEnd`: on `Done` it returns to Ground as today; on `BinaryFollows{len,enc,trl}` it transitions Ground → a new `BinaryRead { remaining: u64, enc: BinEnc, trl: bool }` parser state with escape-scanning disabled. Update the `BufferingApcHandler` forwarder and `VteAdapter` bridge to propagate the new return. The pre-scanner stays dumb: it holds NO `tgp`/CBOR/`len` knowledge; the Term/Perform impl (P1-T4) is what parses the header and decides the return value. Add a default `Perform::apc_end` body returning `ApcEnd::Done` so existing handlers (kitty/sixel/rgp) compile unchanged. **`BinEnc` vs `Enc` (J3):** `enum BinEnc { Bin }` lives in `toastty-parser` and is intentionally a **one-variant subset** — only `enc=bin` ever triggers the binary-read state, so the dumb pre-scanner only needs to know "this is a raw-byte frame." The full header enum `enum Enc { B64, Bin }` lives in `toastty-graphics` (P1-T3); the Term maps `Enc::Bin → BinEnc::Bin` when it returns `BinaryFollows`. `Enc::B64` never reaches the back-channel (b64 stays inside the APC, returns `Done`), which is why `BinEnc` has no `B64` variant.

**Design refs:** §8.2 (parser back-channel, normative); §5 (single ingress path). G-invariants: G6 (ingress seam), G8 (no RGP bridge — change is additive to the shared pre-scanner, not a fork). **Doc-consistency note (R3-3):** the s08 §8.2 inline code block spells the enum `BinaryFollows{len,enc}` (no `trl`), but that block predates the same section's "Integrity trailer (opt-in)" paragraph + the advertised `bintrailer` feat (§7.1) + I4, which JOINTLY require the back-channel to carry trailer presence (a Term cannot otherwise tell the dumb pre-scanner to read the 8 trailer bytes without re-parsing). The authoritative shape is therefore `BinaryFollows{len,enc,trl}` (the trailer paragraph + `bintrailer` feat win over the stale code block); `ApcEnd`/`BinEnc` are internal Rust API, not wire spellings, so G8/G1 are untouched. A doc-fix against s08 to add `trl` to the inline enum is filed separately.

**Depends on:** none.

**Tests:**
- `apc_end_default_is_done` / ingress / drive a non-`tgp` APC `ESC _ kitty;a=T ESC \` through a stub `Perform` that uses the default `apc_end` body / assert the parser stays in Ground (no `BinaryRead` state entered) and the call returns `ApcEnd::Done`.
- `apc_end_binary_follows_enters_read_state` / ingress / feed `ESC _ tgp;p;tok=A;txn=1;enc=bin;len=4 ESC \` then 4 raw bytes `01 02 03 04` to a stub `Perform` returning `ApcEnd::BinaryFollows{len:4, enc:Bin, trl:false}` / assert the parser captured exactly those 4 raw bytes verbatim (incl. if a byte were `0x1B`) and returned to Ground after the 4th byte; assert the stub never saw the raw bytes as APC chunks.
- `apc_end_binary_follows_disables_escape_scan` / ingress / same header with `len=2` then raw bytes `1B 5C` (`ESC \`) / assert both bytes are delivered to the binary buffer verbatim (the byte count, not escape scanning, ends the frame) and the parser is in Ground afterward.

## P1-T2 — Binary-read state machine: cross-`advance()` byte counting, optional-ST tolerance, hard ceiling

**Title:** Cap-bounded `BinaryRead` state that survives split reads and resyncs against length lies.

**Deliverable:** Implement the `BinaryRead` parser state in `crates/toastty-parser/src/parser.rs` (state added in P1-T1). Carry `remaining: u64`, the `enc`, the `trl` flag, the captured header `txn` (for truncation diagnostics), and a cap-bounded accumulation buffer. Each `advance()` appends `min(remaining, available)` bytes and decrements `remaining`; on `remaining == 0` the buffer is handed to the Term decoder hook (P1-T4 `Term::tgp_feed_binary`) and the parser returns to Ground. If the input slice empties first, the state is retained for the next call. After the Nth byte, tolerate exactly **one** trailing ST token (`ESC \`, `BEL`, or `0x9C`) consumed as a no-op sync marker; a second consecutive ST is ordinary input.

**Three over-length branches (the framer must distinguish all three; this resolves I11):**
1. `len > BIN_HARD_CEIL` (compile-time constant ≥ `max_reasm_mb`): return to Ground at the header boundary WITHOUT draining; surface a synthetic over-ceiling signal via `Term::tgp_feed_binary_overceil` (drives `cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable`). This is the no-drain branch.
2. `Caps::max_msg_bytes() < len ≤ BIN_HARD_CEIL` (the common over-cap case; `max_msg_bytes() = max_msg_mb * 1024 * 1024 = 67_108_864`, pinned in P1-T6 per J3): enter a **drain-only** consume-N mode — consume exactly `len` raw bytes WITHOUT buffering them (no allocation grows), return to Ground after the Nth byte, then surface an over-msg-cap signal via `Term::tgp_feed_binary_overmsg(txn)` (drives **`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** citing the header `txn` — per **G12.1**: the per-message decoded-size breach is canonically `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`, honoring §8.7's literal text; the base-spine `msg_too_large` is RESERVED and never emitted). This keeps the stream synced so the next `tgp;` header re-parses. The over-cap check happens at **header-parse time, before entering consume-N** (§15.2 "before entering consume-N mode").
3. `len ≤ Caps::max_msg_bytes()`: ordinary buffered read into the cap-bounded accumulation buffer.

The accumulation buffer never exceeds the per-frame cap regardless of split.

**Integrity trailer (`trl=1`, §8.2, opt-in `bintrailer`):** when the `BinaryRead` state carries `trl==true`, after the `len` payload bytes the parser reads 8 more bytes (NOT counted in `len`) and hands them to `Term::tgp_feed_binary` alongside the payload as the trailer; the Term verifies `TGPB` magic (`0x54 0x47 0x50 0x42`) + LE-u32 echo of `len`. A mismatched or absent trailer → `parse_error;detail=trailer_corrupt`, scene unchanged, parser back in Ground. The optional-ST tolerance applies after the trailer when `trl=1` (else after the payload). The 8 trailer bytes participate in cross-`advance()` straddling like the payload.

**Design refs:** §8.2 (cross-read lifecycle; optional-ST tolerance bounded to one token; integrity trailer `trl=1`, 8 bytes `TGPB`+LE-len echo not counted in `len`, mismatch→`parse_error;detail=trailer_corrupt`); §8.7 + §15.2 (`BIN_HARD_CEIL` no-drain→`cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable`; over-`max_msg_mb` drain-then-`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` per **G12.1**, which supersedes the §15.2 `msg_too_large` locus; over-cap check before consume-N). G-invariants: G6 (ingress seam), G2 (resync vs hostile sender), **G12.1** (over-msg-cap canonical code).

**Depends on:** P1-T1.

**Tests:**
- `binary_read_straddles_reads` / ingress / header `len=8`, then call `advance` with first 3 payload bytes, then 5 more in a second call / assert the decoder hook fires exactly once with all 8 bytes concatenated in order and the parser is Ground only after the 8th byte.
- `binary_read_consumes_one_trailing_st` / ingress / `len=4` payload `AA BB CC DD` followed by `ESC \` then text `Z` / assert payload is the 4 bytes, the single ST is swallowed, and the `Z` prints normally to the grid (parser back in Ground).
- `binary_read_second_st_is_ordinary` / ingress / `len=4` payload then `0x9C 0x9C` / assert first `0x9C` is swallowed as the no-op marker and the second `0x9C` is processed as ordinary input (not a second swallow).
- `binary_read_eof_with_remaining_truncated` / lifecycle / `len=10` then EOF after only 6 bytes / assert the partial buffer is discarded and a truncation signal carrying the header `txn` is surfaced to the decoder hook (drives `x;code=parse_error;detail=truncated` in P1-T9).
- `binary_read_hard_ceiling_no_drain` / ingress / header with `len` set to `BIN_HARD_CEIL + 1` / assert the parser returns to Ground at the header boundary, does NOT attempt to consume the fabricated count, and surfaces an unrecoverable-over-ceiling signal (`Term::tgp_feed_binary_overceil`) to the decoder (drives `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable`). (Branch 1.)
- `binary_read_over_msg_cap_drains_then_errors` / ingress / header `len = Caps::max_msg_bytes() + 1` (≤ `BIN_HARD_CEIL`; `max_msg_bytes()` = 67_108_864 per the J3-pinned conversion), feed that many junk bytes split across two `advance()` calls, then a valid `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=A` / assert (a) NO junk bytes leaked to the grid, (b) the framer consumed exactly `len` bytes WITHOUT growing the accumulation buffer (drain-only), (c) `Term::tgp_feed_binary_overmsg` fired with the header `txn` (drives **`x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** citing `txn` — per G12.1; the test branches on `code=cap_exceeded` and MAY additionally check `detail=max_msg` as documentation), (d) the following `tgp;q` parsed (stream re-synced) and produced a `tgp;r`. (Branch 2 — covers I11/R3-6; distinguishes ok / drain+`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` / no-drain+`cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable`. Note: a literal 67 MB junk feed is impractical for a unit test — use a test-only injectable `Caps` with a small `max_msg_mb` (e.g. so `max_msg_bytes()` is a few KB) so the boundary is exercised cheaply; the conversion formula, not the absolute value, is under test.)
- `bin_trailer_verified_ok` / ingress / header `trl=1;enc=bin;len=N`, the N payload bytes (a valid one-op `node.upsert` CBOR patch), then 8 trailer bytes `TGPB`+LE-u32(N) / assert the trailer is verified, the patch applies (node present in `tgp_scene()`), the trailer was NOT counted toward `len`, and the parser is back in Ground. (Covers I4 verify path.)
- `bin_trailer_corrupt_rejected` / ingress / header `trl=1;enc=bin;len=N`, N payload bytes, then 8 trailer bytes with a WRONG magic (`XXXX`+LE-u32(N)) / assert `parse_error;detail=trailer_corrupt` surfaced (eventual wire `x`), scene unchanged, parser back in Ground (stream synced for the next frame). Repeat with correct magic but a wrong LE-len echo (`N+1`) → same `trailer_corrupt`. (Covers I4 corrupt path.)
- `bin_trailer_straddles_reads` / ingress / `trl=1;len=4` with the 4 payload bytes in one `advance()` and the 8 trailer bytes split 5/3 across two further `advance()` calls / assert the trailer is reassembled and verified across reads (cross-`advance()` correctness for the trailer).

## P1-T3 — TGP text-header parser: `tgp;<type>;k=v` tokenization, field order, `enc`/`len`/`tok`/`txn`

**Title:** Parse the `ESC _ tgp;<type>;tok=…;k=v;… ESC \` control header and decide the `ApcEnd` return.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/frame.rs` with `fn parse_tgp_header(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<TgpHeader, HeaderError>`. `TgpHeader { ty: FrameType, tok: Option<Token>, txn: Option<u64>, v: Option<u8>, vmin: Option<u8>, enc: Enc, len: Option<u64>, trl: bool, extra: Vec<(Key,Val)> }`; `enum FrameType { Q, R, P, Vp, Sub, Anim, E, X, A }`; `enum Enc { B64, Bin }` (default `B64` when `enc=` absent). **`Enc` is the full header enum (J3); the parser-side `BinEnc` (P1-T1) is the one-variant subset (`Bin` only) — the Term maps `Enc::Bin → BinEnc::Bin` when deciding `BinaryFollows`, and `Enc::B64` never crosses the back-channel.** `v`/`vmin` are typed fields parsed from `v=`/`vmin=` on `tgp;q` (and echoed on `tgp;r`) so the negotiation helper (P1-T6) has a tested source for BOTH `app_max` (`v=`) and `app_min` (`vmin=`); they are `None` when absent (J5). `trl` parses from `trl=1` (default `false`); it is meaningful only for `enc=bin` (§8.2 — the integrity trailer). The header MUST reject `len=` appearing before any `enc=` → `HeaderError::FieldOrder` (handled control-frame style, no binary-read state entered, drives `x;code=parse_error;detail=field_order`). A `tgp` header whose body fails to parse at all → `HeaderError::Parse`. This module decides the `apc_end` return value (P1-T1): `ty==P && enc==Bin && len.is_some()` → `BinaryFollows{len, Bin, trl}` (carrying the parsed `trl` so P1-T2 knows to read the 8-byte trailer); everything else (incl. `enc=b64`, all control types) → `Done`. The Term wires this in P1-T4. **Pre-consume-N cap check (§15.2, I11):** the Term, after this header parse, checks `enc==Bin && len > max_msg_mb` **before** entering consume-N and selects the drain-only branch (P1-T2 branch 2); this module surfaces `len` and `enc` so the Term can make that decision at header-parse time.

**Design refs:** §8.2 (header field order normative; control vs binary frame shapes; `trl=1` trailer flag); §8.3 (`txn`/`enc`/`len` are text-header fields parsed before CBOR); §8.5 (message taxonomy / frame types); §8.4 (`tok=` in every header); §15.2 (pre-consume-N over-cap check). G-invariants: G4 (correlators in text header), G1 (token in text header).

**Depends on:** P1-T1.

**Tests:**
- `header_parses_control_query` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=2;vmin=1;tok=app-7` / assert `ty==Q`, `tok=="app-7"`, `v==Some(2)`, `vmin==Some(1)` (BOTH the inbound `v=` app_max and `vmin=` app_min are captured as typed fields so P1-T6 `negotiate(app_max, app_min)` has a tested source — J5), `enc==B64` (default), `len==None`, `apc_end` decision `Done`.
- `header_query_missing_v_captures_vmin` / ingress / `tgp;q;vmin=1;tok=A` (no `v=`) / assert `v==None`, `vmin==Some(1)`, `ty==Q` (the header still parses as a `tgp;q`; the `v=`-omission is handled at handshake dispatch in P1-T10, which still answers with the supported range and MAY also draw `parse_error` — J5). (Covers J5 inbound-`vmin` parse with `v` absent.)
- `header_parses_binary_patch` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=42;enc=bin;len=20512` / assert `ty==P`, `txn==42`, `enc==Bin`, `len==20512`, `trl==false`, `apc_end` decision `BinaryFollows{len:20512, Bin, trl:false}`.
- `header_parses_trl_flag` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=9;enc=bin;len=64;trl=1` / assert `trl==true` and `apc_end` decision is `BinaryFollows{len:64, Bin, trl:true}` (so the framer reads the 8-byte trailer per P1-T2). (Covers I4 header parse.)
- `header_b64_patch_stays_in_apc` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=7;enc=b64;len=12` / assert `enc==B64` and `apc_end` decision is `Done` (b64 never sets `BinaryFollows`).
- `header_enc_absent_defaults_b64` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=1` (no `enc=`) / assert `enc==B64`.
- `header_len_before_enc_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=1;len=8;enc=bin` / assert `Err(HeaderError::FieldOrder)` and that the parser does NOT enter binary-read state (the queued `x;code=parse_error;detail=field_order` for a handshaked token is asserted in T16 — I15 assertion seam).
- `header_every_frame_type_recognized` / ingress / one assertion per type string `q r p vp sub anim e x a` / assert each maps to the correct `FrameType` variant.

## P1-T4 — Term TGP demux: APC buffering, `apc_end` decision wiring, binary-buffer handoff

**Title:** Wire the parser back-channel into `Term`: buffer APC bytes, parse header, return `ApcEnd`, receive binary payloads.

**Deliverable:** In `crates/toastty-term/src/term.rs` add a `tgp: TgpState` field (the per-token scene container from P1-T7) and implement the `Perform` hooks for `tgp` frames. `Term::apc_start` clears the tgp APC buffer; `apc_chunk` appends; `Term::apc_end` (now returning `ApcEnd` per P1-T1) detects a `tgp;` prefix, calls `parse_tgp_header` (P1-T3), and either (a) for control/`b64` frames dispatches immediately and returns `ApcEnd::Done`, or (b) for `enc=bin` patches stashes the parsed header and returns `ApcEnd::BinaryFollows{len,enc,trl}` — UNLESS the pre-consume-N over-cap check fires: if `len > max_msg_mb` (≤ `BIN_HARD_CEIL`) the Term still returns `BinaryFollows` but flags the stashed header drain-only so the completing payload routes to `tgp_feed_binary_overmsg` instead of `tgp_feed_binary` (§15.2, I11). Add the decoder handoff hooks: `Term::tgp_feed_binary(&mut self, buf: &[u8], trailer: Option<[u8;8]>)` (called by the parser when the byte count + optional trailer complete per P1-T2; the Term verifies the trailer when present), `Term::tgp_feed_binary_truncated(&mut self, txn: Option<u64>)` (EOF), `Term::tgp_feed_binary_overceil(&mut self, txn: Option<u64>)` (over-`BIN_HARD_CEIL`, → `cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable`), and `Term::tgp_feed_binary_overmsg(&mut self, txn: Option<u64>)` (over-`max_msg_mb` drain branch, → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` per **G12.1**, NOT `msg_too_large`). Non-`tgp` APCs fall through to the existing kitty/sixel/rgp handlers and return `ApcEnd::Done`. **A handshaked `tgp;sub` or `tgp;anim` frame in P1 (behavior deferred to P2/P4) is a defined SAFE NO-OP: it parses, mutates no scene state, and never panics (G fuzz-oracle invariant §15.5); no `unsupported` is emitted in P1.** This is the single ingress path — no second framer. Add `Term::tgp_scene(&self) -> &TgpScene` mirroring the existing `Term::rgp_scene()` `&self` accessor pattern (legacy `rgp_scene` untouched).

**RIS / DECSTR teardown (G10, R3-2 — folded here because T4 is where the per-token tables come to live on `Term`).** Implement the reset hooks on `Term` for TGP state: **RIS (`ESC c`)** tears down ALL TGP state — `tgp.sessions` for every token (scenes: assets, materials, nodes, viewports), every in-flight reassembly buffer (`Scene::reasm`), and invalidates all session tokens — the guaranteed VRAM-recovery path (§10.7 line 79, G10); **DECSTR (`ESC [ ! p`)** leaves `tgp` state UNTOUCHED (soft reset). Wire these into the existing terminal RIS/DECSTR handling (additive; the RGP path is unaffected, G8). Without this hook the create-without-teardown asymmetry would leave per-token scenes + open chunk buffers dangling after any TGP traffic — a known unbounded-retention leak. Expose `Term::reasm_open_count() -> usize` (total in-flight reassembly buffers across all tokens) as the `&self` lifecycle-test accessor (also consumed by T13/T14).

**Design refs:** §5 (one ingress path, token demux); §8.2 (parser back-channel; over-cap drain branch); §8.5 (taxonomy; `sub`/`anim` types); §10.7 line 79 (RIS = full TGP teardown + token invalidation; DECSTR leaves scene intact); §15.5 (never-crash fuzz oracle). G-invariants: G6 (the `Term`/`&self` accessor seam), G8 (RGP path untouched), G10 (RIS VRAM-recovery teardown vs DECSTR-intact).

**Depends on:** P1-T1, P1-T2, P1-T3, P1-T7. (The RIS-teardown tests below additionally exercise an open chunk buffer from P1-T14 and a committed node from P1-T10/T11; they are written once those land — the RIS *hook* itself only needs the `tgp` field + `reasm` map. No DAG cycle: T14's deps are T11/T9/T5, none of which is T4.)

**Tests:**
- `term_routes_tgp_control_frame` / ingress / `Parser::advance(&mut term, b"\x1b_tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=A\x1b\\")` / assert a `tgp;r` appears in `term.drain_pty_replies()` (control frame dispatched, not buffered as binary).
- `term_non_tgp_apc_falls_through` / ingress / a kitty graphics APC `ESC _ Gf=24,... ESC \` / assert the kitty handler still runs (existing behavior unchanged) and no tgp state mutates.
- `term_binary_patch_roundtrip` / ingress / a `tgp;p;…;enc=bin;len=N` header + N raw CBOR bytes for a one-op `node.upsert` / assert `term.tgp_scene()` contains the new node after `tgp_feed_binary` completes.
- `term_tgp_scene_accessor_exists` / ingress / fresh `Term` / assert `term.tgp_scene()` returns an empty scene (0 nodes, `scene_revision==0`, `asset_revision==0`) and is distinct from `term.rgp_scene()`.
- `unimplemented_frame_type_is_safe_noop` / ingress / handshaked tok A, feed `ESC _ tgp;sub;tok=A;vp=v1 ESC \` and `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A ESC \` / assert NO panic, `tgp_scene()` for A is unchanged (no node/viewport mutation), and (P1 choice) no `x` is queued — a clean safe no-op for the deferred types. (Covers I7.)
- `ris_tears_down_all_token_scenes` / lifecycle / under tokens A and B each: handshake, commit a node, and open a `more=1` chunk (so `reasm_open_count()==2`); then `Parser::advance(&mut term, b"\x1bc")` (RIS) / assert per **G10/R3-2** the `tgp_scene()` for BOTH A and B is empty (0 nodes, 0 assets, 0 viewports), `reasm_open_count()==0`, and both session tokens are invalidated (`tgp_session("A")==None`, `tgp_session("B")==None`) — the guaranteed VRAM-recovery path leaves no dangling per-token state. (The RGP scene, if any, is untouched — G8.)
- `decstr_leaves_scene_intact` / lifecycle / handshake tok A and commit a node `n`, then `Parser::advance(&mut term, b"\x1b[!p")` (DECSTR) / assert per **G10** node `n` survives (`tgp_scene()` for A still holds `n`), the `Session` for A still exists, and `scene_revision` is unchanged — DECSTR is a soft reset that does NOT touch TGP state. (The RIS↔DECSTR distinction — teardown vs intact — is itself the protocol surface this pair covers.)

## P1-T5 — Injectable deterministic monotonic clock

**Title:** One injectable monotonic clock for all terminal-side time; tests advance it explicitly.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/clock.rs` with `trait TgpClock { fn now(&self) -> Nanos; }` (newtype `Nanos(u64)`), a production `SystemClock`, and a `ManualClock { now: Cell<Nanos> }` with `advance(&self, dt: Nanos)` for tests. `Term` holds a `clock: Box<dyn TgpClock>` (or generic) with a `Term::set_clock`/test constructor injecting `ManualClock`. In P1 the clock is consumed only by the partial-frame/inactivity-timeout path of reassembly (P1-T13) and by the capability `caps_gen` monotonicity; the explore/anim/throttle consumers land in later phases but MUST use this same clock. No wall-clock reads anywhere in tgp code.

**Design refs:** G-invariant G6 (deterministic injectable clock — the single source of terminal-side time). §8.7 (reassembly cap/timeout reclaim).

**Depends on:** none.

**Tests:**
- `manual_clock_advances_explicitly` / lifecycle / construct `ManualClock`, read `now()`, `advance(Nanos(1_000_000))`, read again / assert second read == first + 1_000_000 (never wall-clock).
- `term_uses_injected_clock` / lifecycle / construct `Term` with a `ManualClock`, query `term`'s clock-derived value (e.g. via the reassembly-timeout accessor from P1-T13) before and after `advance` / assert the value tracks the manual clock, not real time.

## P1-T6 — Capability reply (`tgp;r`): closed cap/feat set, encoder, version negotiation

**Title:** Build the always-answered `tgp;r` from one frozen closed cap/feat vocabulary.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/caps.rs` with `struct Caps { v_max, v_min, caps_gen: u64, feat: &'static [Feat], enc: &'static [Enc], color: Color, … all scope-suffixed numeric caps }` and `fn encode_reply(caps, negotiated_v, tok) -> Vec<u8>` producing `ESC _ tgp;r;v=…;vmin=…;tok=…;caps_gen=…;feat=…;enc=b64,bin;color=srgb;<caps> ESC \`. The v1 `feat=` set is frozen and closed: `geom,graph,instance,material,pbr,light,pick,event,explore,anim,bintrailer` (advertised whole even though most are implemented in later phases — the reply is the contract). Honor implication ordering when emitting. `enc=b64` listed first (safe default). Version negotiation helper `negotiate(app_max, app_min) -> v` = `min(app_max, term_max)`; when `app_max < term_min`, reply `v=term_min` (knowing-refuse path) with true `vmin=`. Caps are the **same closed set** as the enforced cap list (§15.2) — the encoder is the single source. Cap field values pinned to the §7.1 reference reply (`max_node_depth=256`, `max_id_bytes=64`, `max_msg_mb=64`, `max_reasm_mb=256`, `max_instances_per_buffer=1000000`, `max_nodes_session=1000000`, `max_viewports_session=64`, `max_inline_cols=256`, `max_inline_rows=256`, `max_viewport_px_w=8192`, `max_viewport_px_h=8192`, `max_verts_per_asset=4000000`, `max_indices_per_asset=8000000`, `msaa=1,4`, `click_deadzone_px=3`, `max_backchannel_bytes=4096`, etc.). **MB→bytes conversion pinned ONCE here (J3):** the advertised caps are megabyte counts, but the framer/decoder compare against byte/char quantities, so the caps module is the single source for the conversion — `Caps::max_msg_bytes() = max_msg_mb * 1024 * 1024` (= 67_108_864 for `max_msg_mb=64`, consistent with §7.1's `max_asset_bytes=67108864`) and `Caps::max_reasm_bytes() = max_reasm_mb * 1024 * 1024`. The MiB multiplier (1024·1024, NOT 1_000_000) is normative; every over-cap boundary test (T2, T9, T14) references `Caps::max_msg_bytes()`/`Caps::max_reasm_bytes()` rather than an ad-hoc constant, so the boundary cannot drift.

**Design refs:** §7.1 (query/reply, frozen feat vocabulary, enc/color, limits=enforced); §7.3 (forward-compat). G-invariants: G7 (one closed caps/feat set; enc default b64; color=srgb), G5 (handshake = error-reporting opt-in).

**Depends on:** none.

**Tests:**
- `caps_reply_carries_v_and_vmin` / ingress / `encode_reply` for negotiated v=1 / assert the bytes contain `v=1` and `vmin=1` (presence of any `tgp;r` ⇒ TGP exists).
- `caps_reply_feat_is_frozen_set` / ingress / decode the emitted `feat=` / assert it equals exactly the ordered set `geom,graph,instance,material,pbr,light,pick,event,explore,anim,bintrailer` (no `binframe` token present).
- `feat_set_satisfies_implications` / ingress / parse the emitted `feat=` into a set and assert, as a **property over the set** (not a literal match), each §7.1 implication holds: `pbr⇒material`, `pick⇒event`, `explore⇒event`, `instance⇒graph`, `anim⇒graph` — i.e. for each pair, presence of the implier ⇒ presence of the implied. (Future-proofs the invariant: dropping `event` while keeping `pick` would fail this even if the frozen-set literal were updated. Covers I10.)
- `caps_reply_present_caps_gen` / ingress / parse the emitted reply / assert a `caps_gen=` field is present and is a parseable u64 (the field exists on every `tgp;r`). (Monotonicity-across-replies is asserted in T10 `caps_gen_present_and_monotonic`. Covers I3 presence.)
- `caps_reply_enc_b64_first` / ingress / parse `enc=` / assert value is `b64,bin` (b64 first).
- `caps_reply_color_srgb` / ingress / parse `color=` / assert `srgb`.
- `caps_reply_pins_limits` / ingress / parse caps / assert `max_node_depth==256`, `max_id_bytes==64`, `max_msg_mb==64`, `max_reasm_mb==256`, `max_instances_per_buffer==1000000`, `msaa==1,4`.
- `caps_reply_advertises_full_cap_set` / ingress / parse the emitted `tgp;r` and collect every cap field NAME present / assert (a) **presence** of every field in the §7.1 reference reply (closed-set membership, G7 — the reply and the §15.2 enforced list are the SAME set): `max_verts_per_asset, max_indices_per_asset, max_texels_per_texture, max_tex_dim, max_instances_per_buffer, max_asset_bytes, max_nodes_session, max_node_depth, max_viewports_session, max_vram_mb_session, max_msg_mb, max_reasm_mb, max_pending_bytes_session, max_inline_cols, max_inline_rows, max_viewport_px_w, max_viewport_px_h, max_lights_per_viewport, anim_speed_max, camera_report_hz, max_id_bytes, msaa, click_deadzone_px, max_backchannel_bytes`; and (b) the **pinned value** of every cap P1 ENFORCES (so an advertise/enforce drift — the exact G7 hazard — is caught): `max_verts_per_asset==4000000`, `max_indices_per_asset==8000000` (enforced by T15 GLB caps), `max_inline_cols==256`, `max_inline_rows==256`, `max_viewport_px_w==8192`, `max_viewport_px_h==8192` (enforced by T19 vp rejects), `max_instances_per_buffer==1000000` (enforced by T12), `max_nodes_session==1000000`, `max_viewports_session==64`, plus the §7.1 reference values `max_texels_per_texture==67108864`, `max_tex_dim==8192`, `max_asset_bytes==67108864`, `max_vram_mb_session==512`, `max_pending_bytes_session==268435456`, `max_lights_per_viewport==16`, `anim_speed_max==16`, `camera_report_hz==30`, `click_deadzone_px==3`, `max_backchannel_bytes==4096`. (Closes R3-1: previously only ~6 of ~24 advertised fields had a presence/value assertion.) Each enforced cap's BOUNDARY test references the `Caps::<field>()` accessor rather than a literal so advertise==enforce is structurally guaranteed — see T15 `glb_over_vert_cap_rejected` (`Caps::max_verts_per_asset`) and T19 `vp_over_inline_cap_rejected` (`Caps::max_inline_cols`), mirroring J3's `max_msg_bytes()` pinning.
- `negotiate_min_of_ranges` / ingress / `negotiate(app_max=5, app_min=1)` against term_max=2 / assert negotiated `v==2`.
- `negotiate_app_too_old_knowing_refuse` / ingress / `negotiate(app_max=0, app_min=0)` against term_min=1 / assert reply carries `v=1` (term_min) and `vmin=1` so the app can knowingly refuse rather than hang.

## P1-T7 — Retained scene model data structures (per-token, revisions, dirty set)

**Title:** The CPU-side retained scene: graph, assets/materials/viewports tables, `u64` revisions, `DirtySet`.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` per §6.1: `struct TgpState { sessions: Map<Token, Session> }`; per-token `struct Scene { assets, materials, nodes, roots, viewports, clips, reasm, asset_revision: u64, scene_revision: u64, dirty: DirtySet }`. `struct Node { id: NodeId, parent: Option<NodeId>, trs: Trs, kind: NodeKind, visible: bool, alt: Option<String> }`; `enum NodeKind { Group, Mesh{asset,material,tint}, Instanced{…}, Light{…}, Camera{…} }` (Instanced/Light/Camera variants present but only Group/Mesh exercised in P1). `struct Trs { t:[f32;3], r:[f32;4], s:[f32;3] }`. `struct DirtySet` with per-node transform/visibility flags, per-node `instance_dirty`, and per-viewport flags; `bump_scene_revision`/`bump_asset_revision` use `saturating_add`. The default material is the implicit `MaterialId 0`. Expose `&self` accessors for tests: `Scene::node(id)`, `Scene::roots()`, `Scene::asset(id) -> Option<&MeshAsset>` (for the P1-T11/T15 inline-GLB install tests, J2), `Scene::material(id) -> Option<&Material>` (for the P1-T11 `material.add` commit tests), `Scene::scene_revision()`, `Scene::asset_revision()`, `Scene::is_node_dirty(id)`, `Scene::is_node_instance_dirty(id)`, `Scene::is_viewport_dirty(id)`, `Node::instance_buffer() -> Option<&InstanceBuffer>` (count/decoded xforms/tints, for the P1-T12 `node.instances` decode tests). Implement `Node::world_matrix` computation in P1-T8.

**Design refs:** §6.1 (data structures, per-field clear defaults); §6.5 (dirty-tracking, revision split). G-invariants: G1 (per-token namespace), G9 (u64 saturating revisions + dirty flags as the render gate).

**Depends on:** none.

**Tests:**
- `scene_starts_empty_zero_revisions` / ingress / fresh `Scene` / assert `nodes` empty, `roots` empty, `scene_revision()==0`, `asset_revision()==0`, default `MaterialId 0` resolvable.
- `revision_saturating_add` / ingress / set `scene_revision` to `u64::MAX` then `bump_scene_revision()` / assert it stays `u64::MAX` (saturating, wrap unreachable).
- `dirty_set_per_node_and_per_viewport_independent` / ingress / mark node A transform-dirty / assert `is_node_dirty(A)==true`, `is_node_dirty(B)==false`, and `is_node_instance_dirty(A)==false` (orthogonal flags).
- `per_token_namespaces_isolated` / ingress / two sessions tokens `A` and `B` each create a node id `"cam"` / assert `Scene` for A and `Scene` for B each hold a distinct `"cam"` node (no collision; one namespace per token).

## P1-T8 — World-matrix computation: lazy dirty-subtree walk, depth-bounded

**Title:** Compute world transforms as the product of the parent chain over the dirty subtree.

**Deliverable:** In `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` (or a sibling `transform.rs`) add `fn world_matrix(scene, id) -> Mat4` = product of local TRS over the root→node parent chain, plus `fn recompute_dirty_world_matrices(scene)` that walks only nodes flagged transform-dirty (and their descendants) iteratively (no recursion) and clears the flags. Carry a defense-in-depth `max_node_depth` (256) cap in the render-time walk even though commit-time validation (P1-T11) guarantees committed state never reaches it. Expose `Scene::world_matrix(id) -> Mat4` as a `&self` test accessor (computes on demand, CPU-side, queryable). Local TRS → matrix uses standard T·R·S composition with the quaternion rotation.

**Design refs:** §6.2 (world transform = product of parent chain; lazy dirty-subtree; depth cap as defense-in-depth). G-invariants: G6 (geometry CPU-side and queryable), G9 (dirty-flag-gated recompute).

**Depends on:** P1-T7.

**Tests:**
- `world_matrix_identity_for_default_trs` / ingress / a single root node with identity TRS / assert `world_matrix(id)` is the 4x4 identity.
- `world_matrix_is_parent_product` / ingress / parent translate `(10,0,0)`, child local translate `(0,5,0)` / assert child `world_matrix` translation column == `(10,5,0)`.
- `world_matrix_three_level_chain` / ingress / root translate `(1,0,0)`, mid translate `(0,1,0)`, leaf translate `(0,0,1)` / assert leaf world translation == `(1,1,1)`.
- `recompute_only_walks_dirty_subtree` / ingress / 3 sibling subtrees, mark one node transform-dirty, call `recompute_dirty_world_matrices` / assert the dirty node and its descendants had matrices recomputed (flags cleared) and the other two subtrees' dirty flags were never set (no whole-tree walk).
- `world_matrix_quaternion_rotation` / ingress / node with a 90° rotation quaternion about +Z and a child at local `(1,0,0)` / assert child world position ≈ `(0,1,0)` within f32 epsilon.

## P1-T9 — CBOR patch decode: `len`-vs-payload contract, b64 path, whole-buffer consumption

**Title:** Decode one CBOR patch from the framed buffer; enforce exact-buffer-consumption and both encodings.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/decode.rs` with `fn decode_patch(buf: &[u8]) -> Result<Patch, DecodeError>` where `struct Patch { txn: Option<u64>, ops: Vec<Op> }` and the op enum scoped to P1: `Op::AssetAdd`, `Op::MaterialAdd` (default-material-only fields in P1; PBR fields `model`/`base`/`metallic`/`rough`/`tex_base` parsed-but-deferred — accepted, stored inert, never rejected), `Op::NodeUpsert`, `Op::NodeInstances` (carries `id`, `mesh:<AssetId>` asset ref, `count:u32`, `xforms`/`tints` byte strings per §8.6/J1; decode + hardened layout/cap/NaN validation in P1; GPU upload + render deferred to P2 — see P1-T12 and I2), `Op::NodeVisible`, `Op::NodeRemove`. The decoder MUST consume the **entire** buffer: trailing bytes → `DecodeError::TrailingBytes`; truncated/needs-more → `DecodeError::Truncated`. For the b64 path, `Term` base64-decodes the in-APC payload first (`DecodeError::Base64` on failure) then feeds the **same** `decode_patch`. The decoded-size cap (`Caps::max_msg_bytes()`, the J3-pinned `max_msg_mb * 1024 * 1024` conversion) is enforced before decode on both encodings (b64 in-APC char length bounded at `ceil(Caps::max_msg_bytes()*4/3)` per §8.3/G7 — referencing the pinned byte count, not an ad-hoc `max*4/3`). For `enc=b64`, `len` is the base64 **character** count inside the APC, optional (ST delimits) but **validated against the actual char count when present** — mismatch → `DecodeError::B64LenMismatch` → `parse_error` (§8.3). `txn`/`enc`/`len` come from the text header (P1-T3), not the CBOR body, so the correlator is known even when the body fails to decode. Use a vetted CBOR crate (e.g. `ciborium`/`minicbor`) — pin in `Cargo.toml`. **Assertion seam (I15): the tests below assert the internal `Result<Patch, DecodeError>` value and "scene unchanged / stream synced" — the CPU-state seam available WITHOUT the `tgp;x` byte encoder (T16). The queued `x;code=…` bytes for each of these decode errors are asserted once in T16 `every_p1_error_code_emitted`; the `→ x;code=…` annotations here name the eventual wire mapping, not a same-task byte assertion.**

**Design refs:** §8.3 (CBOR; len-vs-payload three outcomes; two len semantics mode-gated by enc; b64 len char-count validation + char-cap; b64→same CBOR path; decode-error surfacing); §8.6 (`material.add` op). G-invariants: G4 (header correlators survive body failure), G6 (ingress seam).

**Depends on:** P1-T3, P1-T7.

**Tests:**
- `decode_one_complete_item_ok` / ingress / CBOR map `{txn:1, ops:[{do:"node.upsert", id:"a"}]}` consuming the whole buffer / assert `Ok(Patch{txn:Some(1), ops:[NodeUpsert{id:"a"}]})`.
- `decode_material_add_op` / ingress / CBOR map `{txn:1, ops:[{do:"material.add", id:3}]}` (default-material-only fields) / assert `Ok` with `ops==[MaterialAdd{id:3, ...}]` (the op decodes to the `Op::MaterialAdd` variant, not skipped, not `parse_error`). (Covers I1 at the decode seam.)
- `decode_material_add_pbr_fields_parsed_inert` / ingress / `{do:"material.add", id:4, model:"pbr", base:[10,20,30,255], metallic:0.9, rough:0.3, tex_base:8}` / assert `Ok` (PBR fields accepted, not rejected) and that the decoded `MaterialAdd` carries the fields as parsed-but-deferred (apply-time inertness asserted in T11). (Covers I1's PBR-deferred clause.)
- `decode_trailing_bytes_rejected` / ingress / a valid CBOR patch followed by one extra `0xFF` byte inside `len` / assert `Err(DecodeError::TrailingBytes)` (eventual wire: `x;code=parse_error;detail=trailing_bytes`), scene unchanged.
- `decode_truncated_rejected` / ingress / a CBOR map header claiming 3 entries but only 2 present within `len` / assert `Err(DecodeError::Truncated)` (eventual wire: `x;code=parse_error;detail=truncated`), scene unchanged.
- `decode_b64_path_same_contract` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=2;enc=b64;len=…` with base64 of the same CBOR patch / assert it decodes identically to the bin path (same `Patch`).
- `decode_b64_bad_base64` / ingress / `enc=b64` payload with a non-base64 char / assert `Err(DecodeError::Base64)` (eventual wire: `x;code=parse_error;detail=base64`).
- `b64_len_mismatch_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A;txn=1;enc=b64;len=N` where the actual base64 body is `N+3` chars / assert `Err(DecodeError::B64LenMismatch)` (eventual wire: `x;code=parse_error`), scene unchanged, stream synced. (Covers I12.)
- `b64_over_char_cap_rejected` / ingress / an `enc=b64` body whose char length exceeds `ceil(Caps::max_msg_bytes()*4/3)` (using a test-only small `max_msg_mb` so the boundary is cheap) / assert the over-cap is detected BEFORE CBOR decode (no decode attempted) → `Err(DecodeError::OverMsgCap)` (eventual wire: **`x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** per G12.1 — the SINGLE canonical over-msg-cap code, no longer the ambiguous `msg_too_large`/`cap_exceeded` pair), scene unchanged. (Covers I12 b64 char-cap + R3-6; references the J3-pinned `Caps::max_msg_bytes()`.)
- `decode_over_msg_cap_rejected` / ingress / a frame whose decoded size exceeds `Caps::max_msg_bytes()` (test-only small cap) / assert `Err(DecodeError::OverMsgCap)` (eventual wire: **`x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** per G12.1, NOT `msg_too_large`), scene unchanged, stream still synced. (R3-6: both over-msg-cap loci — this decode-reject and the T2 bin-drain — now emit the identical `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`.)

## P1-T10 — Handshake dispatch: `q`→`r`, token establish/echo/assign, `tgp;r` before DA1, error-arming, re-query

**Title:** Always-answer `tgp;q`, establish the per-token session, order `tgp;r` ahead of DA1, arm error reporting.

**Deliverable:** In `crates/toastty-term/src/term.rs`, dispatch a parsed `FrameType::Q` to: create/refresh a `Session` keyed by the proposed `tok=` (assign one when omitted; ≤16 bytes, printable, no control/ESC — else `bad_token`), run `negotiate` (P1-T6), `encode_reply` (P1-T6), and **enqueue the `tgp;r` strictly before any pending DA1** on `pty_replies`. A completed `q`/`r` handshake **arms structured error reporting** for that token (no separate `errors=` flag); tokenless/never-handshaked writers stay silent. Re-query is idempotent and always answered: same `v=` re-answers (caps_gen may advance); differing `v=` re-negotiates. A well-formed `tgp;q` with malformed/absent `v=` is still answered with the supported range and establishes **no negotiated session** (§7.1); per §7.1 the terminal **MAY** additionally draw a `parse_error` for the `v=` omission but is **NOT required** to (it is a permitted-not-required behavior, J5) — a test asserting the `parse_error` is present OR a test asserting only the answered-`tgp;r` (no `parse_error`) are both acceptable conformance choices; the plan picks "answer only, no `parse_error`" for v1 simplicity (the always-answer obligation is the hard requirement). `negotiate` consumes the typed `header.v` (app_max) and `header.vmin` (app_min) parsed in P1-T3 — both inbound fields drive the helper, not just `v=`. Add `Session { tok, negotiated_v: u8, caps_gen: u64, error_armed: bool, scene: Scene }` and `Term::tgp_session(tok) -> Option<&Session>` test accessor.

**Design refs:** §7.1 (always-answered normative; `tgp;r` before DA1; versioning; per-app token establish/echo/assign; ≤16-byte token cap; handshake = error opt-in); §7.2 (re-query idempotent/always-answered); **G11(d)** (a token-requiring frame with a malformed/unacceptable token → `bad_token`, NOT `denied`; a well-formed unhandshaked patch is dropped silently per G5). G-invariants: G5 (handshake = error opt-in), G7 (tgp;r before DA1), G1 (token established in text header — private per-token map per G11).

**Depends on:** P1-T3, P1-T4, P1-T6, P1-T7. (The `tgp;r` byte assertions below consume P1-T6's `encode_reply`; the two `tgp;x`-byte assertions — `bad_token`, error-arming — additionally consume P1-T16's `encode_error` and so are implemented after T16. Per I15: assert the internal Session state early, the `x` bytes once T16 lands.)

**Tests:**
- `query_always_answered` / ingress / `ESC _ tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=A ESC \` / assert `drain_pty_replies()` contains a `tgp;r` carrying `tok=A`.
- `tgp_r_enqueued_before_da1` / ingress / feed `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=A` immediately followed by `ESC [ c` (DA1) in one batch / assert in `drain_pty_replies()` the `tgp;r` bytes appear at a lower offset than the DA1 (`ESC [ ? … c`) bytes.
- `token_echoed_when_proposed` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=app-7` / assert reply contains `tok=app-7`.
- `token_assigned_when_omitted` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1` (no tok) / assert reply carries a non-empty assigned `tok=` and a `Session` exists for it.
- `bad_token_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=` followed by a token containing `0x1B` (control/ESC byte forbidden) / assert NO `Session` is created for the malformed token (`tgp_session(badtok)==None`); and (once T16 lands) the queued reply is `x;code=bad_token` rather than a `tgp;r`. (Internal-state seam first per I15; the `x` byte is also asserted in T16 `every_p1_error_code_emitted`'s `bad_token` repro. Per **G11(d)** this `bad_token` is about *validating/establishing* a token — distinct from the now-RESERVED `denied`.)
- `bad_token_too_long_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=<17-byte token>` (one over the §7.1/§8.4 ≤16-byte cap) / assert NO `Session` is created (`tgp_session==None`) and (once T16 lands) the queued reply is `x;code=bad_token`; then a 16-byte token at the boundary establishes a `Session` and draws a `tgp;r`. (Mirrors the node-id length-boundary pattern of T12 `node_id_too_long_rejected`; closes R3-7 — the `tok=` length cap had only a charset test before.)
- `unhandshaked_patch_silent_not_bad_token` / ingress / a well-formed `tgp;p;tok=Z;txn=1;enc=b64;len=…` patch from a token `Z` that NEVER sent a `tgp;q` / assert per **G5/G11(d)** the frame is processed safely (scene protected, caps enforced) but draws **NO** reply at all — neither `tgp;a` nor `tgp;x;code=bad_token` (a non-participant is dropped silently; `bad_token` is reserved for *malformed/unacceptable token establishment*, not for a well-formed-but-unhandshaked writer). Distinct from a no-handshake frame whose `tgp;` HEADER itself fails to parse from an already-handshaked token (that draws `parse_error`, §7.1).
- `query_missing_v_still_answered` / ingress / `tgp;q;vmin=1;tok=A` (no `v=`) / assert a `tgp;r` carrying the supported range is still queued (never silent drop), and — per the v1 MAY-path choice (J5: `parse_error` permitted-not-required, plan picks answer-only) — assert NO `tgp;x;code=parse_error` is queued for the omission (only the `tgp;r`). No negotiated session is established for the tokenless-on-`v` query (the supported range is informational). (If a future revision elects to also draw `parse_error`, this assertion flips; the always-answered `tgp;r` is the invariant either way.)
- `requery_same_v_pure_reanswer` / ingress / two identical `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=A` / assert both produce a `tgp;r` and the second does not change the negotiated version.
- `requery_differing_v_renegotiates` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1` then `tgp;q;v=2` for tok A against term_max=2 / assert second reply negotiates `v=2`.
- `caps_gen_present_and_monotonic` / ingress / two `tgp;q;v=1;vmin=1;tok=A` (re-query) / parse `caps_gen=` from both replies and assert (a) both are present, (b) the second's value ≥ the first's (monotonically non-decreasing across replies for the token). Drive any intervening `caps_gen` advance via the injected `ManualClock`/cap transition path (no wall-clock). (Covers I3 monotonicity.)
- `handshake_arms_error_reporting` / ingress / handshake tok A / assert `tgp_session("A").error_armed == true` (the completed handshake sets the arming flag — the internal G5 seam, asserted at T10); a never-handshaked tokenless writer has no `Session` so `error_armed` is unreachable (no arming). Once T16 lands, the wire consequence is asserted: a malformed patch for armed A queues an `x`, while the same malformed tokenless patch queues NO `x` (silent for non-participants) — this byte-level half lives in T16 `error_only_for_handshaked_token`.

## P1-T11 — Patch apply engine: staged commit, atomicity, order-independent ref resolution, cycle/depth validation

**Title:** Apply a decoded patch atomically against a staged scene copy, validate, then commit once.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/apply.rs` with `fn apply_patch(scene: &mut Scene, tok: &Token, patch: Patch, caps: &Caps) -> Result<Commit, TgpError>`. Stage a working copy; apply ops in receive order; resolve all references (`parent`, `mesh`/`asset`, `material`) against the txn's **final** staged state (order-independent — a child op may precede its parent, a `node.upsert{mesh:7}` may precede `asset.add{id:7}`); asset/material refs require a fully **committed** target (in-flight chunk buffer does not satisfy a ref → `bad_ref`). Unresolved ref → `bad_ref;detail=<kind>:<id>` citing first offending op index, whole txn rejected. **Inline GLB install seam (J2):** applying an `asset.add{id, fmt:"glb", data:<bytes>}` op calls `decode_glb(data, caps)` (P1-T15); on `Ok(MeshAsset)` the mesh is staged into the `assets` table keyed by `id` and the asset commits; a `decode_glb` error (`bad_index`/`bad_layout`/`parse_error`/`cap_exceeded`) **rejects the whole txn** atomically — so the GLB error codes become ingress-reachable THROUGH the apply path, not only via the direct unit calls in T15. (The chunked path, P1-T14, reassembles bytes then CBOR-decodes to a patch carrying this same `asset.add{fmt:glb, data}` op, which is then applied here through the identical `decode_glb` call site — one install seam for both inline and chunked GLB.) `material.add{id}` stages a material into the `materials` table (default-material-only fields applied; PBR fields stored inert per P1-T9). **Inert PBR fields are stored WITHOUT reference resolution (J4):** `tex_base`, `base`, `metallic`, `rough`, `model` on a `material.add` are parsed-but-deferred (P1-T9) and do NOT participate in the order-independent ref resolver — a `material.add{tex_base:8}` where texture asset 8 does not exist still commits (the field is inert; PNG texture decode is P3 per §11.2), it does NOT draw `bad_ref`. (§8.6 lists `tex` among resolved refs for the full protocol, but in P1 PBR is inert so its texture ref resolution is deferred with the rest of PBR.) A committed `material.add` bumps `asset_revision` (per §6.5 row 1). At commit, walk every node's parent chain iteratively with a visited set: self-parent/cycle → `cycle;detail=<id>`; chain over `max_node_depth` (256) → `depth_exceeded;detail=<id>`. On success bump `scene_revision` once and `asset_revision` once iff assets/**materials** changed; on any error the scene is unchanged. **An empty-ops patch (`ops:[]`) is a VALID commit (I14, §7.1 bin-probe): it bumps NEITHER revision, mutates nothing, and still returns `Ok(Commit)` so the ack path (P1-T17) can ack its `txn`; empty ops is never `parse_error`.** Enforce addressing isolation via the **private per-token id map (G11, authoritative)**: every reference resolves as `(bearing_token, id)` against the bearing token's OWN map only — there is no global id table and no cross-token lookup primitive, so cross-token addressing is **unrepresentable by construction**. A frame with `tok=B` naming id `X` resolves to `{B,X}` and NEVER to `{A,X}`, even when A previously created an `X`; the two are distinct, coexisting entries that never alias and neither can observe the other. **Consequence (G11(a)): a plain cross-token id "collision" is NOT an error — it COEXISTS; the apply engine NEVER emits `denied` (RESERVED, no reachable trigger).** A ref to an id absent from the bearing token's own map → `bad_ref;detail=<kind>:<id>` per the order-independent resolver above, with **NO existence oracle** (G11(b): `bad_ref` for "absent in my map" is byte-for-byte identical to "absent everywhere" — the resolver never consults another token's map, so it cannot leak whether the id exists elsewhere). **Implementation (G11(b′), normative): the per-token maps MUST be physically disjoint allocations** — token T's lookups touch only T's own storage and never index/hash-into/contend any structure holding another token's entries — so the spec-level indistinguishability survives implementation (a single global `(id→object)` table is non-conforming). Returns a `Commit` describing which revisions bumped / dirty flags set (so P1-T12 ops and the ack path consume it). **Assertion seam (I15): the tests here assert the internal `Result<Commit, TgpError>` value (e.g. `Err(TgpError::Cycle{detail})`) and the resulting CPU scene state (nodes present/absent, revisions) — NOT queued `tgp;x` bytes. The `x;code=…` wire form for these errors is asserted once in T16 `every_p1_error_code_emitted`; the `x;code=…` annotations below name the eventual wire mapping only.**

**Design refs:** §8.6 (atomic/transactional; reference resolution order-independent; acyclic depth-bounded; `material.add`; empty-ops commit); §6.2 (commit-time cycle/depth validation, iterative visited-set); §6.5 (material commit bumps `asset_revision`); §8.4 (addressing scoped per token — **as superseded by G11**: private per-token id map, collisions coexist, no `denied`); §11.2 (PBR/texture deferred → inert fields not ref-resolved in P1); **G11/G12 addendum (authoritative)** — G11(a) (`denied` RESERVED, collisions coexist), G11(b) (cross-token ref → `bad_ref`, no existence oracle), G11(b′) (physically disjoint per-token maps). G-invariants: G1 (per-token addressing isolation — **G11 supersedes its "→ denied" prose**: the model is now a private map where cross-token addressing is unrepresentable), G2 (bad_ref whole-txn rejection; in-flight ≠ committed), G9 (single scene_revision bump per commit, no tearing).

**Depends on:** P1-T7, P1-T9, P1-T15 (the inline/chunked GLB install seam calls `decode_glb`).

**Tests:**
- `patch_atomic_commit_bumps_scene_revision_once` / ingress / one patch with two `node.upsert` ops / assert `scene_revision` increased by exactly 1 (not 2) and both nodes exist.
- `patch_any_op_error_rejects_whole_txn` / ingress / patch `[node.upsert{id:"a"}, node.upsert{id:"b", parent:"a"}, <bad op>]` / assert the apply `Err` carries the offending op index, scene has neither `a` nor `b`, `scene_revision` unchanged.
- `ref_resolution_order_independent` / ingress / patch `[node.upsert{id:"w", parent:"car"}, node.upsert{id:"car"}]` (child before parent) / assert both commit and `w.parent == "car"`.
- `ref_to_inflight_chunk_is_bad_ref` / ingress / open a `more=1` chunk for asset 7, then a patch `node.upsert{id:"m", mesh:7}` / assert `Err(TgpError::BadRef{detail:"asset:7"})` (eventual wire `x;code=bad_ref;detail=asset:7`), scene unchanged.
- `cycle_rejected_whole_txn` / ingress / patch making `A.parent=B` and `B.parent=A` / assert `Err(TgpError::Cycle{detail})` names a node in the cycle (eventual wire `x;code=cycle`), scene unchanged.
- `self_parent_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"A", parent:"A"}` / assert `Err(TgpError::Cycle{detail:"A"})`.
- `depth_exceeded_rejected` / ingress / a chain of 257 nodes / assert `Err(TgpError::DepthExceeded{..})`, scene unchanged.
- `cross_token_ids_coexist_no_denied` / ingress / handshake tok A creating node `cam` with `trs` translate `(1,0,0)`; handshake tok B sends `node.upsert{id:"cam", trs:{t:[9,0,0]}}` / assert (per **G11(a)**, REWRITTEN from the old `addressing_isolation_denied`): B's apply returns `Ok(Commit)` (NOT `Err(Denied)` — `denied` is RESERVED and unreachable under the private map), the Scene for B now holds its OWN `{B,"cam"}` node with world translation x==9, A's `{A,"cam"}` node is UNCHANGED (world translation x==1), and NO `tgp;x;code=denied` is queued for either token. The two `"cam"` entries coexist as distinct map keys, independently mutable, neither observable from the other. (This is the confused-deputy fix made unrepresentable: there was never a shared key to collide on. Pairs with T7 `per_token_namespaces_isolated`.)
- `cross_token_mesh_ref_is_bad_ref_no_oracle` / ingress / handshake tok A committing asset `7` (a valid GLB); handshake tok B sends `node.upsert{id:"m", mesh:7}` (asset 7 exists ONLY under A, not under B) / assert (per **G11(b)**) B's apply is `Err(TgpError::BadRef{detail:"asset:7"})` — the SAME error byte-for-byte as if asset 7 existed nowhere; assert this is identical to the `bad_ref` produced by a control case where asset 7 is genuinely absent everywhere (no existence oracle: B's resolver never consults A's map), B's `m` node is not created, A's asset 7 is unchanged. (Eventual wire `x;code=bad_ref;detail=asset:7`; covers the G11(b) cross-token-ref-no-leak rule.)
- `asset_commit_bumps_asset_revision` / ingress / patch with `asset.add` (committed) + `node.upsert` / assert `asset_revision` +1 and `scene_revision` +1.
- `inline_asset_add_decodes_and_installs` / ingress / a single `tgp;p` with `{do:"asset.add", id:7, fmt:"glb", data:<minimal triangle GLB>}` / assert `apply_patch` calls `decode_glb` and installs the result: `Scene::asset(7)` is present with 3 positions and a finite AABB, `asset_revision` +1, `scene_revision` unchanged (no node op). This exercises the J2 inline install seam THROUGH ingress (not the direct `decode_glb` unit call). (Requires a `Scene::asset(id) -> Option<&MeshAsset>` test accessor — add to P1-T7's accessor list.)
