- `inline_asset_add_glb_error_rejects_txn` / ingress / a single `tgp;p` with `{do:"asset.add", id:7, fmt:"glb", data:<GLB with wrong magic>}` + a `node.upsert{id:"n"}` in the same txn / assert `Err(TgpError::ParseError{detail:"glb_magic"})` (the `decode_glb` error rejects the WHOLE txn), `Scene::asset(7)==None`, node `n` absent, both revisions unchanged. (J2: GLB error codes are ingress-reachable through the apply path, not only via T15's direct unit calls; eventual wire `x;code=parse_error;detail=glb_magic` asserted in T16.)
- `material_add_decodes_and_commits` / ingress / a `tgp;p` with `[{do:"material.add", id:3}, {do:"node.upsert", id:"m", mesh:7, material:3}]` (asset 7 committed in the same or prior txn) / assert the `materials` table contains id 3, `asset_revision` bumped exactly once, `scene_revision` bumped once, and the Mesh node `m` resolves `material:3`. (Covers I1 at the apply seam.)
- `material_add_pbr_fields_parsed_deferred` / ingress / `{do:"material.add", id:5, model:"pbr", metallic:0.9, rough:0.3, tex_base:8}` where texture asset 8 does NOT exist in the scene / assert the material commits (NOT rejected, and specifically NO `bad_ref` for the inert `tex_base:8` — J4: inert PBR fields are stored without reference resolution in P1), `asset_revision` +1, and the stored material's PBR fields (including `tex_base:8`) are present-but-inert (the default-material shading path, P1-T20, is what actually renders id 5 in P1 — PBR pipeline + PNG texture decode are P3). (Covers I1's PBR-deferred clause + J4 inert-tex_base edge.)
- `empty_patch_commits_neither_revision` / ingress / `{txn:9, ops:[]}` / assert `apply_patch` returns `Ok(Commit)`, both `scene_revision` and `asset_revision` unchanged, scene unchanged (NOT `parse_error`). (Covers I14; the resulting `tgp;a;txn=9` is asserted in T17 `empty_patch_commits_and_acks`.)

## P1-T12 — `node.upsert` + `node.instances`: sparse merge, kind immutability, node-id hardening, instance-buffer decode/validation

**Title:** Implement `node.upsert` (three-way sparse-merge + kind-selector immutability + id hardening) and `node.instances` (CPU-side instance-buffer decode + hardened layout/cap/NaN validation).

**Canonical owner — later phases reuse this; do not re-implement.** P1-T12 is the CANONICAL OWNER of the `node.instances` decode + hardened layout/cap/NaN validation + `InstanceBuffer` storage. P2 (GPU upload + instanced draw + `node.instance_set` per-instance re-tint) CONSUMES the already-decoded/validated `InstanceBuffer` produced here — it does NOT re-decode the `xforms`/`tints` byte strings, re-run the layout/cap/NaN checks, or define a second instance-buffer type. The CPU-side validated buffer is the single source the GPU consumes.

**Deliverable:** In `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/apply.rs`, implement `node.upsert` op handling. Three-way per-field rule driven by the CBOR value: **key absent → preserve**, **key present + CBOR null → clear to default**, **key present + value → set**. Clear defaults: `parent` null → `None` (node re-roots into `roots`, children + animation phase retained); `alt` null → `None`; `tint` (Mesh) null → opaque white `[255,255,255,255]`. `trs`, when present, replaced wholesale (sub-fields not individually nullable). CBOR null on a non-nullable field (`id`, kind selector) → `null_not_allowed;detail=<field>`, whole txn rejected. Kind: on first upsert zero selectors ⇒ `Group`; exactly one of `{mesh, instances, camera, light}` ⇒ that kind; two+ → `kind_conflict;detail=multiple_selectors`; a later upsert whose selector implies a different kind than existing → `kind_conflict`; a matching selector mutates kind-data in place (`mesh:9` swaps asset/material on an existing Mesh). Setting a node-level `tint` on an `Instanced` node → `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (parsed/enforced even though Instanced render is P2). **Per the G11/G12 addendum (G12.4, authoritative — supersedes round-1's I9 resolution): the canonical code is `kind_conflict` (a kind-invariant violation: an `Instanced` node may not carry the `Mesh`-style node-level tint), matching the live §8.6/§6.4 slice text; the base-spine G3 `instanced_node_tint→bad_param` synonym is STRUCK. The `code=` MUST be `kind_conflict`, `detail=` is the advisory `instanced_node_tint`.** Node-id hardening (§8.4/§15.5, security-critical because ids re-enter stdin in `tgp;e`): the `id` string MUST be valid UTF-8, ≤ `max_id_bytes` (64) bytes, and contain no C0 (`0x00`–`0x1F`), DEL (`0x7F`), C1 (`0x80`–`0x9F`), or ESC (`0x1B`) bytes — a violation → `bad_param;detail=charset|too_long`, op rejected, txn rejected atomically, scene unchanged, checked before any reply is queued. Each upsert sets the node's transform/visibility dirty flag.

**`node.instances` (decode + validate in P1; GPU upload/render is P2 — scope decision for I2):** §6.4 makes `node.instances` a **pure-CPU hardened-validation surface**, so its decode/validate lands in P1 (only the GPU upload + instanced draw defer to P2). **Op shape (§8.6 patch example, J1): the buffer + asset ride on the `node.instances` op, NOT on a `node.upsert{instances:…}` selector.** The §8.6 wire shape is `{do:"node.instances", id, mesh:<assetId>, count:u32, xforms:<bytes>, tints:<bytes>}`: `node.instances` BOTH names the asset (`mesh:` is the `AssetId` the Instanced node draws N times, required because §6.1 `NodeKind::Instanced{asset,material,instances}` cannot exist without an asset) AND supplies the instance buffer. The bare `instances` kind-selector on `node.upsert` only **establishes/keeps the Instanced kind** (it does not carry count/xforms/tints). The `mesh:` asset ref resolves order-independently against the txn's final committed state exactly like a Mesh `mesh:` ref (§8.6); an uncommitted/in-flight `mesh:` → `bad_ref;detail=asset:<id>`, whole txn rejected. Decode `count:u32` plus CBOR byte strings `xforms` (`count*64` bytes, 16 column-major LE f32 mat4 per record) and optional `tints` (`count*4` bytes RGBA8 sRGB). Hardened validation: `xforms.len() != count*64` or `tints.len() != count*4` → `bad_layout;detail=xforms_len|tints_len|count_mismatch`; `count > max_instances_per_buffer` (1000000) → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances`; any NaN/inf matrix element → `parse_error;detail=instance_nan` (reject, never clamp). On success store the decoded `InstanceBuffer` on the node as `NodeKind::Instanced{asset:<mesh>,material,instances}` (no GPU upload), set the node's `instance_dirty` flag, bump `scene_revision` (NEVER `asset_revision`, per G2/§6.5). Over-cap/malformed `node.instances` rejects the txn atomically and the node **retains its last committed instance buffer** (no flicker). Expose a `Node::instance_buffer() -> Option<&InstanceBuffer>` accessor returning count/decoded xforms/tints for tests. (Per-instance `node.instance_set` re-tint stays P2.)

**Design refs:** §6.1 (per-field clear defaults table); §8.6 (idempotent addressing sparse merge; kind immutable; null_not_allowed; `node.instances` layout/cap/NaN); §6.4 (instanced node-level tint forbidden; canonical instance layout; `instance_dirty` not `asset_revision`); §8.4 + §15.5 (node-id hardening: ≤64 bytes, no control/ESC, →`bad_param;detail=charset|too_long`). G-invariants: G1 (addressing/id hardening), G9 (dirty flag set, `instance_dirty` off `asset_revision`), G2 (kind/conflict rulings; instance change bumps scene_revision+instance-dirty not asset_revision), **G12.4** (`instanced_node_tint→kind_conflict`, authoritative — supersedes the base-spine `bad_param` synonym).

**Depends on:** P1-T11.

**Tests:**
- `upsert_creates_group_when_no_selector` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"g"}` / assert node `g` exists with `kind==Group`.
- `upsert_key_absent_preserves` / ingress / create `n` with `trs` translate `(1,2,3)`, then `node.upsert{id:"n", alt:"x"}` (no `trs` key) / assert `trs` translation still `(1,2,3)`.
- `upsert_null_clears_to_default` / ingress / set `tint` non-white, then `node.upsert{id:"n", tint:null}` / assert `tint == [255,255,255,255]`.
- `upsert_parent_null_re_roots` / ingress / child `c` parented to `p`, then `node.upsert{id:"c", parent:null}` / assert `c` is in `roots` and still has any of its own children.
- `upsert_value_sets` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"n", trs:{t:[5,0,0]}}` / assert world translation x == 5.
- `null_on_id_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:null}` / assert `Err(TgpError::NullNotAllowed{detail:"id"})` (eventual wire `x;code=null_not_allowed;detail=id`), txn rejected.
- `kind_two_selectors_conflict` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"n", mesh:1, camera:true}` / assert `Err(TgpError::KindConflict{detail:"multiple_selectors"})`.
- `kind_change_rejected` / ingress / create `n` as Mesh (`mesh:1`), then `node.upsert{id:"n", camera:true}` / assert `Err(TgpError::KindConflict{..})`, node still Mesh.
- `kind_matching_selector_mutates` / ingress / create `n` as `mesh:1`, then `node.upsert{id:"n", mesh:9}` / assert `n.kind == Mesh{asset:9}` (in-place swap, kind unchanged).
- `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected` / ingress / first commit `n` as an Instanced node via `[{do:"asset.add", id:5, fmt:"glb", data:<triangle GLB>}, {do:"node.instances", id:"n", mesh:5, count:1, xforms:<64B>}]`, then a follow-up `tgp;p` with `node.upsert{id:"n", tint:[1,2,3,4]}` / assert the internal apply result of the second txn is `Err(TgpError::KindConflict{detail:"instanced_node_tint"})` (per **G12.4**, NOT `bad_param` — the base-spine synonym is struck), node `n` retains no node-level tint and its committed instance buffer is unchanged, the second txn is rejected, `scene_revision` unchanged by the rejected txn. (The queued `x;code=kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` bytes are asserted in T16 `every_p1_error_code_emitted`'s `kind_conflict` repro, which reuses this exact case — re-decided per G12.4, superseding I9.)
- `node_id_control_byte_rejected` / ingress / handshaked tok A, `node.upsert{id:"a\x1bb"}` (id contains ESC `0x1B`) / assert internal apply result `Err(TgpError::BadParam{detail:"charset"})`, txn rejected, scene unchanged, no node created. Repeat-assert with `0x07` (BEL) and `0x9C` (C1 ST) embedded in the id → same `bad_param;detail=charset`.
- `node_id_too_long_rejected` / ingress / a `node.upsert` whose `id` is 65 bytes (one over `max_id_bytes`=64) / assert internal apply result `Err(TgpError::BadParam{detail:"too_long"})`, txn rejected, scene unchanged; a 64-byte id commits successfully (boundary).
- `upsert_sets_transform_dirty` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"n", trs:…}` then read dirty / assert `is_node_dirty("n")==true` before render consumption.
- `instances_decode_ok` / ingress / a `tgp;p` whose ops are `[{do:"asset.add", id:5, fmt:"glb", data:<minimal triangle GLB>}, {do:"node.instances", id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:2, xforms:<128B = two column-major identity mat4>, tints:<8B>}]` (asset 5 committed in the same txn; order-independent ref) / assert `Node::kind` for `atoms` is `Instanced{asset:5, …}` (the `mesh:` ref resolved to the registered asset, §6.1), `Node::instance_buffer()` reports `count==2`, the decoded xforms equal the two identity matrices (values readable), the tints decode to the two RGBA records, `is_node_instance_dirty("atoms")==true`, `scene_revision` +1, and `asset_revision` UNCHANGED for the `node.instances` op's contribution (the `asset.add` bumps `asset_revision` once; the instance op adds NO further asset bump — G2/§6.5). (Covers I2 decode + the instance-buffer accessor + J1 op shape/asset ref.)
- `instances_bad_ref_uncommitted_mesh` / ingress / a `tgp;p` with `{do:"node.instances", id:"atoms", mesh:99, count:1, xforms:<64B>}` where asset 99 is neither committed nor staged in the txn / assert `Err(TgpError::BadRef{detail:"asset:99"})` (eventual wire `x;code=bad_ref;detail=asset:99`), no `atoms` node created, `scene_revision` unchanged (parity with §8.6 order-independent ref resolution; covers J1's asset-ref clause).
- `instances_layout_mismatch_rejected` / ingress / `node.instances{id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:2, xforms:<120B (≠ 128)>}` with asset 5 committed / assert `Err(TgpError::BadLayout{detail:"xforms_len"})` (eventual wire `x;code=bad_layout;detail=xforms_len`), node retains its prior (or no) instance buffer, `scene_revision` unchanged. (Covers I2 `bad_layout` instance path.)
- `instances_over_cap_rejected` / ingress / `node.instances{id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:1000001}` (> `max_instances_per_buffer`) with asset 5 committed / assert `Err(TgpError::CapExceeded{detail:"max_instances"})`, no full buffer allocated, node retains last committed buffer. The cap is checked from the declared `count` BEFORE allocating the buffer (§15.5 declared-size-first). (Covers I2 `cap_exceeded` instance path.)
- `instances_nan_rejected` / ingress / `node.instances{id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:1, xforms:<64B with one element = NaN f32>}` with asset 5 committed / assert `Err(TgpError::ParseError{detail:"instance_nan"})` (rejected, NOT clamped), node retains last committed buffer. (Covers I2 `instance_nan`.)

## P1-T13 — `node.visible`, `node.remove` (cascade + reparent), reassembly timeout reclaim

**Title:** Visibility toggle, subtree-cascade removal with optional `reparent:"root"`, and clock-driven reasm reclaim.

**Deliverable:** In `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/apply.rs`: `node.visible{id, visible}` toggles `Node::visible` and sets the node's visibility dirty flag, bumping `scene_revision`. `node.remove{id}` deletes the node **and its whole descendant subtree** by default, erasing removed ids from `roots`, the `DirtySet`, and any subscription targets; optional `reparent:"root"` detaches the node's direct children to the scene root (local TRS retained) and removes only the named node. Removing a missing id is a no-op (no error, no revision bump). Add the reassembly reclaim path in `tgp/chunk.rs` (P1-T14) gated on the injected clock (P1-T5): an in-flight buffer left open past the inactivity timeout is reclaimed; expose `Scene::reasm_open_count()` / `Scene::reasm_age(id)` test accessors driven by `ManualClock`.

**Design refs:** §8.6 (`node.remove` cascade + `reparent:"root"`; removing missing id is no-op; visibility bumps scene_revision); §8.7 (in-flight buffer reclaimable under cap/timeout). G-invariants: G9, G6 (clock-driven reclaim, deterministic).

**Depends on:** P1-T11, P1-T5.

**Tests:**
- `visible_toggle_sets_dirty` / ingress / `node.visible{id:"n", visible:false}` / assert `n.visible==false`, visibility dirty flag set, `scene_revision` +1.
- `remove_cascades_subtree` / ingress / chain `a→b→c`, then `node.remove{id:"a"}` / assert `a`, `b`, `c` all gone and `roots` no longer contains `a`.
- `remove_reparent_root_keeps_children` / ingress / `a→b`, `a→c`, then `node.remove{id:"a", reparent:"root"}` / assert `a` gone, `b` and `c` now in `roots` with their local TRS retained.
- `remove_missing_id_is_noop` / ingress / `node.remove{id:"ghost"}` on empty scene / assert no `x`, `scene_revision` unchanged.
- `remove_clears_dirty_entries` / ingress / mark `n` dirty, then `node.remove{id:"n"}` / assert `is_node_dirty("n")==false` and no stale dirty entry remains.
- `reasm_timeout_reclaims_buffer` / lifecycle / open a `more=1` chunk, advance `ManualClock` past the inactivity timeout, trigger reclaim / assert `reasm_open_count()==0` and a later ref to that id yields `bad_ref` (no partial asset installed).

## P1-T14 — Chunked `asset.add`: per-(token,id) reassembly, reject-new-keep-prior, in-flight ≠ committed

**Title:** Multi-frame asset upload keyed by `(token, asset id)` with `more=1|0`, hardened conflict handling.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/chunk.rs` with `struct InFlightChunks { fmt, buf: Vec<u8>, last_touch: Nanos }` stored in `Scene::reasm` keyed by `(token, AssetId)`. A `more=1` frame is **pure pre-parse buffering** — bumps nothing, applies nothing, carries no txn effect; it appends to the per-id buffer. The final `more=0` frame completes reassembly: the assembled buffer is CBOR-decoded as one patch (P1-T9) and applied atomically through `apply_patch` (P1-T11), bumping `asset_revision` once. The reassembled patch carries an `asset.add{id, fmt:"glb", data:<assembled bytes>}` op whose `data` is then decoded by the SAME `decode_glb` install seam in P1-T11 (which calls P1-T15) — the chunked path does not have its own GLB decode call site; it reuses the inline-apply install seam (J2). A `decode_glb` error on the final frame discards the buffer and rejects the txn, installing no partial asset. Conflict (reject-new-keep-prior): a second open `more=1` for an id with an existing in-flight buffer, or an `fmt` change mid-stream → `chunk_conflict;detail=chunk_interleave|chunk_fmt_mismatch`, **prior buffer retained**, new stream dropped. A chunk targeting an already-**committed** id → `dup_id;detail=<id>`. In-flight ≠ committed: the dup-id check and `mesh:`/`asset:` ref resolution see only committed assets (first chunk does not self-error; a ref to a mid-upload id yields `bad_ref`). Accumulation bounded by `Caps::max_msg_bytes()` (per frame) and `Caps::max_reasm_bytes()` (total; the J3-pinned `*1024*1024` conversions) → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg|max_reasm`, buffer dropped. The txn correlator lives on the final frame's header.

**Design refs:** §8.7 (keying & isolation; interleaving reject-new-keep-prior; in-flight ≠ committed; atomicity; caps). G-invariants: G2 (reject-new-keep-prior; in-flight ≠ committed), G1 (token-scoped reassembly), G6.

**Depends on:** P1-T11, P1-T9, P1-T5.

**Assertion seam (I15):** the reject tests below assert the **internal** `TgpError`/return value AND the observable reassembly state (`reasm_open_count()`, which buffer is retained, whether the id committed) — both available without the T16 byte encoder. The `x;code=…` annotations name the eventual wire mapping; the queued `x` bytes (`asset=` correlated) are asserted in T16 `every_p1_error_code_emitted`.

**Tests:**
- `chunk_two_frames_commit` / ingress / `asset.add{id:7,fmt:glb,more:1, data:<half>}` then `asset.add{id:7, more:0, data:<rest>}` forming a valid GLB patch / assert asset 7 is committed and `asset_revision` bumped exactly once (the `more=1` frame bumped nothing).
- `chunk_more1_bumps_nothing` / ingress / a single `more=1` frame / assert `asset_revision` and `scene_revision` both unchanged and the id is NOT yet a committed asset (`reasm_open_count()==1`).
- `chunk_interleave_conflict_keeps_prior` / ingress / open `more=1` for id 7 (buffer X), then a second open `more=1` for id 7 (buffer Y) / assert `Err(TgpError::ChunkConflict{detail:"chunk_interleave"})` (eventual wire `x;code=chunk_conflict;detail=chunk_interleave`, correlated `asset=7`), the in-flight buffer is still X (prior retained — assert via the buffer-bytes accessor), Y dropped.
- `chunk_fmt_change_conflict` / ingress / `more=1 fmt=glb` then `more=1 fmt=png` for same id / assert `Err(TgpError::ChunkConflict{detail:"chunk_fmt_mismatch"})`, prior buffer retained.
- `chunk_on_committed_id_dup` / ingress / commit asset 7, then `asset.add{id:7, more:1}` / assert `Err(TgpError::DupId{detail:"7"})` (eventual wire `x;code=dup_id;detail=7` correlated `asset=7`).
- `chunk_independent_ids_interleave` / ingress / interleave `more=1` frames for ids 7 and 8, then close both / assert both commit successfully (`reasm_open_count()==0` after, both assets present).
- `chunk_over_reasm_cap_dropped` / ingress / chunks whose running total exceeds `Caps::max_reasm_bytes()` (test-only small `max_reasm_mb` so the boundary is cheap) / assert `Err(TgpError::CapExceeded{detail:"max_reasm"})`, buffer dropped (`reasm_open_count()==0` for that id), no partial asset committed.

## P1-T15 — Inline GLB asset decode: hardened full-mesh, bounds-checked accessors, caps

**Title:** Decode an inline `asset.add fmt=glb` blob into a mesh with every accessor bounds-checked.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/glb.rs` with `fn decode_glb(bytes: &[u8], caps: &Caps) -> Result<MeshAsset, TgpError>`. Parse the GLB container (12-byte header magic `glTF`, JSON chunk, BIN chunk), the glTF JSON, and extract positions/normals/UVs/indices for the full mesh set. **Every** accessor read is bounds-checked against the BIN chunk length and the declared `bufferView`/`accessor` `byteOffset`+`count`×stride: any out-of-range offset/length, mismatched component type, or overlong index → `bad_index` or `bad_layout` (never an OOB read or panic). Enforce caps before allocation: `verts > max_verts_per_asset` or `indices > max_indices_per_asset` → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_verts_per_asset|max_indices_per_asset`. Reject NaN/inf positions → `parse_error;detail=geom_nan`. A non-glTF magic or unsupported chunk → `parse_error;detail=glb_magic|glb_chunk`. `struct MeshAsset { positions, normals, uvs, indices, bounds: Aabb }` with the CPU-side AABB computed for later picking (P2). Embedded PNG textures are deferred to P3 (parse-skip in P1).

**Design refs:** §11.2 (GLB inline, one hardened decoder — texture decode deferred); §8.6/§8.7 (`asset.add fmt=glb`, atomic install); §6.4 referencing meshes. G-invariants: G6 (CPU-side geometry queryable), G8 (no RGP reuse — own decoder).

**Depends on:** P1-T6. (`decode_glb(bytes, caps)` is a standalone, side-effect-free decoder needing only `Caps`; both the inline-apply install seam in P1-T11 and the chunked reassembly in P1-T14 are CONSUMERS of this decoder — they depend on T15, not the reverse. The earlier `Depends on: P1-T14` was spurious and is removed to keep the DAG acyclic now that T11 depends on T15 for the install seam, J2.)

**Assertion seam (I15):** `decode_glb` returns `Result<MeshAsset, TgpError>` directly, so the reject tests assert the **internal** `Err(TgpError::…)` value and "no asset installed / no panic" — no T16 byte encoder needed. The `x;code=…` annotations name the eventual wire mapping; the queued `x` bytes are asserted in T16 `every_p1_error_code_emitted` (the `bad_index`/`bad_layout` GLB repros feed that test).

**Tests:**
- `glb_decode_minimal_triangle` / ingress / a minimal valid GLB with one 3-vertex triangle / assert `Ok(MeshAsset)` has 3 positions, 3 indices, and a finite AABB enclosing them.
- `glb_bad_magic_rejected` / ingress / 12 bytes with wrong magic / assert `Err(TgpError::ParseError{detail:"glb_magic"})` (eventual wire `x;code=parse_error;detail=glb_magic`), no asset installed.
- `glb_accessor_out_of_range_rejected` / ingress / a GLB whose position accessor `byteOffset+count*stride` exceeds the BIN chunk length / assert `Err(TgpError::BadIndex)` or `Err(TgpError::BadLayout)` (eventual wire `x;code=bad_index|bad_layout`), no panic, no asset installed.
- `glb_index_out_of_vertex_range_rejected` / ingress / a GLB whose index buffer references vertex N >= vertex count / assert `Err(TgpError::BadIndex)`, no asset installed.
- `glb_over_vert_cap_rejected` / ingress / a GLB declaring `verts > Caps::max_verts_per_asset()` (reference the accessor, NOT the literal `4000000`, so advertise==enforce is structural — R3-1; a test-only small `max_verts_per_asset` keeps the GLB cheap) / assert `Err(TgpError::CapExceeded{detail:"max_verts_per_asset"})`, no allocation of the full buffer (declared-size check fires before allocation, §15.5).
- `glb_nan_position_rejected` / ingress / a GLB with a NaN position float / assert `Err(TgpError::ParseError{detail:"geom_nan"})` (rejected, not clamped).
- `glb_computes_aabb` / ingress / a unit cube GLB / assert `bounds` min ≈ `(-0.5,-0.5,-0.5)` and max ≈ `(0.5,0.5,0.5)`.

## P1-T16 — `tgp;x` error encoder: closed versioned enum, correlation by txn/vp/asset, advisory detail

**Title:** Encode structured errors from the closed v1 enum with the right correlator and `tok=`.

**Canonical owner — later phases reuse this; do not re-implement.** P1-T16 is the CANONICAL OWNER of (a) the closed 26-variant `TgpErrorCode` enum and (b) the `encode_error`/`tgp;x` emitter. P5 (and every intervening phase that emits a structured error) REUSES this exact enum + emitter — it does not define a second error vocabulary or a parallel `tgp;x` encoder. The closed-set encoder round-trip test here (`closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26`) is the single closed-set conformance point; P5-T1's reachable-ingress suite consumes this same partition.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/error.rs` with `enum TgpErrorCode { ParseError, MsgTooLarge, Truncated, CapExceeded, VramExhausted, BadRef, DupId, UnknownOp, UnknownNode, UnknownClip, KindConflict, Cycle, DepthExceeded, BadIndex, BadLayout, BadParam, ChunkConflict, Unsupported, Denied, NodeBusy, CameraBusy, NotPlaying, NoTargets, InvalidSub, BadToken, NullNotAllowed }` (the exact closed G3 set, 26 variants — no synonyms on the wire). **The G3 synonym map is applied at the call sites so an internal name never reaches the wire as itself:** `forbidden→denied`, `dup_asset→dup_id`, `binary_decode→parse_error` (`detail=binary_decode`), `token_denied→bad_token`. **NOTE (G12.4, supersedes the base-spine synonym): `instanced_node_tint` is NOT a synonym for `bad_param` — it is `detail=instanced_node_tint` on `code=kind_conflict` (a kind-invariant violation); the base-spine `instanced_node_tint→bad_param` map entry is STRUCK.** Add `fn encode_error(tok, corr: Correlator, code, detail: Option<&str>, op: Option<u32>) -> Vec<u8>` producing `ESC _ tgp;x;tok=…;<corr>;code=…;[op=…];[detail=…] ESC \`. `enum Correlator { Txn(u64), Vp(VpId), Asset(AssetId), None }` per G4: patch→`txn` (+`op` index), vp→`vp` id (vp frames have **no** `txn`), chunked asset→`asset` id, no-correlator→code only. `detail=` is advisory/non-load-bearing. **Two RESERVED codes (G12.2 — closed-enum members with NO reachable ingress trigger anywhere, exercised ONLY by the encoder round-trip, never by an ingress repro): `denied`** (cross-token addressing is unrepresentable under the private per-token map per G11(a) — collisions coexist) and **`msg_too_large`** (the over-msg-cap breach funnels through `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` per G12.1). **`unknown_op` is NOT reserved**: it is **not P1-reachable** (every unknown `do:` patch op string is silently skipped per §7.3/P1-T18) but is globally **REACHABLE** via the **P4 closed-anim-verb locus** (G12.3 row 8) — so it has an ingress repro in P4, not P1, and is exercised in P1 only by the all-26 encoder round-trip. The partition is **24 REACHABLE + 2 RESERVED ({msg_too_large, denied})** (those reachable codes in P1 scope have an ingress repro in `every_p1_error_code_emitted`; the rest, including `unknown_op`, land in later phases). Emission gated on the token being handshaked (error-armed, P1-T10); routed only to the owning token. Wire `Term` to call this whenever a P1 op/decode/cap/lifecycle path produces a `TgpError`.

**Design refs:** §15.3 / §8.6 (offending op index); §10.3 (vp over-pixel-cap → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px` correlated by `vp`); **G11/G12 addendum (authoritative)** — G11(a) (`denied` RESERVED), G12.1 (over-msg-cap → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`, `msg_too_large` RESERVED), G12.2 (2-code reserved partition `{msg_too_large, denied}`; `unknown_op` REACHABLE via the P4 closed-anim-verb locus), G12.3 (full 26-code reachability table), G12.4 (`instanced_node_tint→kind_conflict`, base-spine `bad_param` synonym struck). G-invariants: G3 (closed versioned enum + synonym map, as amended by G12.4), G4 (correlation by txn|vp|asset + tok), G5 (gated on handshake).

**Depends on:** P1-T10, P1-T11, P1-T19 (for the `vp=` correlator repro).

**Tests:**
- `error_patch_cites_txn_and_op` / ingress / handshaked patch with a bad op at index 2 / assert queued `x` bytes contain `tok=A`, `txn=<n>`, `op=2`, `code=<...>`.
- `error_chunk_cites_asset_id` / ingress / a chunk conflict on asset 7 / assert `x` carries `asset=7` (not a txn) and `code=chunk_conflict`.
- `error_no_correlator_code_only` / ingress / a frame with no correlator that errors / assert `x` carries `code=` and `tok=` only (no `txn`/`vp`/`asset`).
- `error_only_for_handshaked_token` / ingress / a malformed frame from a never-handshaked tokenless writer / assert NO `x` queued; the same malformed frame from a handshaked token / assert an `x` is queued.
- `error_detail_is_advisory` / ingress / two paths producing the same `code` with different `detail` / assert the `code=` token is identical and `detail=` differs (detail non-load-bearing).
- `error_vp_cites_vp_id` / ingress / drive the over-pixel-cap vp reject from P1-T19 (`vp_over_pixel_cap_rejected`) for a handshaked token / assert the queued `x` carries `vp=<id>`, `code=cap_exceeded`, `detail=max_viewport_px`, and NO `txn`/`asset` token (G4: vp frames correlate by `vp` id only). (Covers I5.)
- `synonym_map_applied_at_call_site` / ingress / drive the instanced node-level-tint reject (T12 `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected`) for a handshaked token / assert the wire `code=` is the canonical **`kind_conflict`** (per **G12.4**, NOT `bad_param`) and that `instanced_node_tint` rides only as `detail=instanced_node_tint`, never as a `code=`. (Covers the synonym-map application gap. NOTE: `denied`/`forbidden` is NOT exercised here — under G11(a) `denied` has no reachable ingress trigger, so a synonym-at-call-site repro for it is impossible by construction; its encoder spelling is covered by the closed-set round-trip below.)
- `closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26` / unit (encoder, not ingress) / call `encode_error(tok, Correlator::Txn(1), c, None, None)` for **every** one of the 26 `TgpErrorCode` variants and decode each / assert the round-trip yields the exact `code=` string for all 26, INCLUDING the two RESERVED codes `denied`, `msg_too_large` that no ingress path anywhere can fire (G11(a)/G12.1) AND `unknown_op` (not P1-reachable — its sole ingress repro is the P4 closed-anim-verb locus, G12.3 row 8) — the closed-enum encoder can spell all of them even where no P1 ingress repro exists. The closed-set partition asserted here is **24 REACHABLE + 2 RESERVED ({msg_too_large, denied})**. (Resolves I6 + the G12.2 reserved-set partition: the closed set is stable/forward-compatible; the encoder is its single conformance point. Subsumes the prior `unknown_op_code_encoder_roundtrips`.)
- `every_p1_error_code_emitted` / ingress / one minimal ingress repro per **P1-reachable** code (`parse_error, truncated, cap_exceeded, bad_ref, dup_id, kind_conflict, cycle, depth_exceeded, bad_index, bad_layout, bad_param, chunk_conflict, bad_token, null_not_allowed`) / assert each emits its exact `code=` string. The `bad_param` repro is the node-id-charset case (`node_id_control_byte_rejected`, T12) routed through a handshaked token; the `kind_conflict` repro covers BOTH the multiple-selectors case (`kind_two_selectors_conflict`) AND the instanced-node-tint case (`instanced_node_level_tint_rejected`, `detail=instanced_node_tint`, per G12.4); the `cap_exceeded` repro set includes `detail=max_msg` (over-msg-cap, G12.1), `detail=unrecoverable` (over-`BIN_HARD_CEIL`), `detail=max_instances`, and `detail=max_viewport_px|max_inline_cols`. The two RESERVED codes `denied`, `msg_too_large` are deliberately ABSENT from the ingress list (no reachable ingress trigger anywhere — see deliverable, G12.2; covered by `closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26`); `unknown_op` is likewise ABSENT here because it is **not P1-reachable** (its sole ingress repro is the P4 closed-anim-verb locus, G12.3 row 8 — REACHABLE, just not in P1), also covered in P1 by the all-26 encoder round-trip; `vram_exhausted`, `unknown_node`, `unknown_clip`, `unsupported`, `node_busy`, `camera_busy`, `not_playing`, `no_targets`, `invalid_sub` are out of P1 scope and omitted.

## P1-T17 — `tgp;a` ack: txn-commit confirmation, available to any handshaked token

**Title:** Emit `tgp;a` on successful txn commit and for the `enc=bin` probe; available without a subscription.

**Canonical owner — later phases reuse this; do not re-implement.** P1-T17 is the CANONICAL OWNER of the `tgp;a` `encode_ack` emitter. Later phases that ack (e.g. P2 viewport acks `a;vp=<id>;msaa=<n>`, any future txn-commit ack) REUSE this `encode_ack` + `Correlator` plumbing — they do not define a second ack encoder. Only the correlator/field set carried in the call extends; the emitter is owned here.

**Deliverable:** Add `fn encode_ack(tok, corr: Correlator) -> Vec<u8>` to `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/error.rs` (or a sibling `ack.rs`) producing `ESC _ tgp;a;tok=…;txn=… ESC \`. On a successful patch commit, queue an `a` citing the patch `txn` (when the txn was present). Acks are available to **any handshaked token** — NOT gated on a subscription — used for txn-commit confirmation and the `enc=bin` transport probe. **The `enc=bin` probe is a minimal no-op patch = a CBOR patch with `ops:[]` (empty), which P1-T11 commits as valid (bumps no revision) and still acks its `txn` (I14).** Tokenless/never-handshaked writers get no ack.

**Design refs:** §8.6 (ordering & acks; `a` confirms a committed txn, available to any handshaked token; empty-ops commit acks); §7.1 (enc=bin probe→ack). G-invariants: G4 (ack correlation), G5 (handshake-gated).

**Depends on:** P1-T10, P1-T11.

**Tests:**
- `ack_on_successful_commit` / ingress / handshaked tok A sends a valid `tgp;p;txn=42` patch / assert `drain_pty_replies()` contains `tgp;a;tok=A;txn=42`.
- `empty_patch_commits_and_acks` / ingress / handshaked tok A sends `{txn:9, ops:[]}` (empty-ops, the canonical no-op) / assert `tgp;a;tok=A;txn=9` is queued and both `scene_revision`/`asset_revision` are unchanged (empty ops is a valid commit, NOT `parse_error` — covers I14; pairs with T11 `empty_patch_commits_neither_revision`).
- `ack_for_bin_probe` / ingress / handshaked tok A sends the empty-ops no-op patch `{txn:11, ops:[]}` as `enc=bin` / assert an `a;txn=11` is queued (the transport-probe path uses this exact empty-ops shape).
- `ack_not_for_unhandshaked` / ingress / a valid patch from a never-handshaked token / assert NO `a` queued.
- `no_ack_on_rejected_txn` / ingress / a patch that fails an op / assert an `x` is queued and NO `a` (commit did not happen).

## P1-T18 — Forward-compat within a version: skip unknown ops & unknown fields

**Title:** Unknown ops and unknown patch fields are skipped, not fatal, within a negotiated version.

**Deliverable:** In `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/decode.rs` / `apply.rs`, make unknown CBOR op map entries (unknown `do:` strings) and unknown fields inside a known op **skip** rather than reject — except where a hard rule applies (e.g. `null_not_allowed` on a known non-nullable field still rejects). A genuinely unknown op string is skipped silently within the txn (the rest of the patch commits) — distinct from a structurally malformed op which is `parse_error`. **Decision (resolves the T16↔T18 tension, I6): in v1 `unknown_op` is NOT P1-reachable** — every unknown `do:` string skips per §7.3, so no P1 ingress path ever emits `unknown_op`. The code stays in the closed G3 enum (it is the contract) and is exercised only by the T16 closed-set encoder round-trip (`closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26`); it is removed from T16's `every_p1_error_code_emitted` ingress list. Document the precise boundary in the module: **skip** = a well-formed op map with an unknown `do:` token, OR a known op carrying an unknown field key; **reject as `parse_error`** = a structurally malformed op (op entry that is not a CBOR map, or a map missing the `do:` key entirely), OR a hard-rule violation (e.g. `null_not_allowed`, `bad_param` id charset).

**Design refs:** §7.3 (forward-compat within a version: unknown ops/fields skipped, not fatal); §8.6. G-invariants: G6.

**Depends on:** P1-T9, P1-T11, P1-T16.

**Tests:**
- `unknown_field_in_known_op_skipped` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"n", trs:…, futurefield:123}` / assert node `n` commits with its `trs` and the unknown `futurefield` is ignored (no `x`).
- `unknown_op_skipped_rest_commits` / ingress / patch `[node.upsert{id:"a"}, {do:"future.op", x:1}, node.upsert{id:"b"}]` / assert `a` and `b` both commit and the unknown op is skipped.
- `structural_cbor_error_still_rejects` / ingress / a patch whose op is not a CBOR map / assert `x;code=parse_error` (malformed structure is not "unknown field").
- `op_missing_do_key_rejects` / ingress / a patch whose op IS a CBOR map but lacks the `do:` key entirely (`{id:"n"}`) / assert `x;code=parse_error` (a map with no `do:` is structurally malformed, NOT a skippable unknown op — this is the boundary that keeps `unknown_op` unreachable; see I6).

## P1-T19 — One viewport: `vp` create/update, cell→pixel rect, late-bound camera/root, implicit default camera

**Title:** A single inline viewport with CPU-computed pixel rect and an auto-framing default camera.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/viewport.rs` with `struct Viewport { id, anchor_cell, cols, rows, camera: Option<NodeId>, root: Option<NodeId>, settings: ViewportSettings, dirty }` and handle the `vp` frame (create/update; destroy is exercised but full multi-viewport/reflow is P2). Compute the **pixel rect** CPU-side from `(anchor_cell, cols, rows)` × the terminal's cell pixel size → `PixelRect { x, y, w, h }`. **Two distinct size rules, NOT one clamp (§10.2/§10.3 — the design forbids silent clamping of caps):** (1) **On-screen-intersection clamp** — allocation/pixel rect is always clamped to the intersection with the terminal surface (off-screen area never allocated); this is a legitimate clamp. (2) **Cap REJECT** — a `vp` whose `cols>max_inline_cols` or `rows>max_inline_rows` → `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_inline_cols|max_inline_rows`, vp NOT created/updated; a `vp` whose on-screen-clamped pixel size still exceeds `max_viewport_px_w/h` → `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px`, vp NOT created/updated. **The terminal NEVER silently clamps to a cap** (clamping reopens the §15.1 oversized-viewport VRAM vector). Both reject errors correlate by `vp` id (no `txn`), per G4. Camera/root are **late-bound** (not patch refs): a `vp` may be created before its camera node; a missing/removed camera → an **implicit auto-framing default camera** (never reject, never crash); a missing `root` defaults to the whole scene. Setting/clearing anchor/size/camera/settings sets the per-viewport dirty flag. Expose `Scene::viewport(id)`, `Viewport::pixel_rect()`, `Viewport::effective_camera()` (returns the implicit default when unbound), `Viewport::root() -> RootScope` (returns `WholeScene` when `root` is unbound/unresolved in the owning token's map, else the bound `NodeId` — the named seam for `vp_missing_root_is_whole_scene`, R3-5), and `Viewport::allocated_px() -> Option<PixelRect>` (`None` when the vp was rejected for a cap — no offscreen allocated; the clamped on-screen rect otherwise — the named seam for the cap-reject "no offscreen allocated" assertions, R3-5) as `&self` test accessors. Enforce `max_viewports_session` for P1 (single-viewport path, but the cap check exists).

**Design refs:** §10.2 (`max_inline_cols/rows` over-cap REJECT, never clamp); §10.3 (cell→pixel; on-screen-intersection clamp legitimate; `max_viewport_px` over-cap REJECT correlated by `vp`); §6.1 (viewports table); **G11(c)** (a vp camera/root naming an id absent from the OWNING token's map → late-bind implicit default per G2, never `denied`, never reject). G-invariants: G2 (late-bound camera/root → implicit default cam, never reject; missing root → whole scene), G4 (vp errors correlate by `vp` id), G6 (pixel rect CPU-computed & queryable), G9 (per-viewport dirty).

**Depends on:** P1-T7, P1-T8.

**Tests:**
- `vp_create_computes_pixel_rect` / ingress / `tgp;vp;tok=A;id=v1;col=2;row=3;cols=4;rows=2` with cell size 8×16 px / assert `pixel_rect() == {x:16, y:48, w:32, h:32}`.
- `vp_unbound_camera_uses_default` / ingress / create `v1` with no `camera=` / assert `effective_camera()` returns the implicit auto-framing default (not None, not an error).
- `vp_late_bound_camera_resolves` / ingress / create `v1` with `camera=cam` BEFORE node `cam` exists, then upsert a Camera node `cam` / assert `effective_camera()` resolves to `cam` after it is created (no `bad_ref` for the vp).
- `vp_removed_camera_falls_back` / ingress / bind `v1.camera=cam`, then `node.remove{id:"cam"}` / assert `v1` survives and `effective_camera()` is the implicit default again (vp not destroyed).
- `vp_cross_token_camera_late_binds_default` / ingress / handshake tok A creating a Camera node `cam`; handshake tok B creates `tgp;vp;tok=B;id=v1;camera=cam` (a `cam` exists ONLY under A) / assert (per **G11(c)/G2**) B's `v1` IS created (vp camera/root are NOT patch refs — never reject, never `bad_ref`, never `denied`), `effective_camera()` returns the implicit auto-framing default because `cam` is absent from B's OWN map, and B's vp silently adopts a real `{B,"cam"}` only if/when B later creates one. A's `cam` is never observed by B. (Covers the addendum's cross-token camera/root late-bind-default rule.)
- `vp_missing_root_is_whole_scene` / ingress / create `v1` with no `root=` / assert `Viewport::root()` resolves to the whole scene (all roots rendered).
- `vp_update_sets_dirty` / ingress / move `v1`'s anchor / assert `is_viewport_dirty("v1")==true` and the new pixel rect reflects the move.
- `vp_over_pixel_cap_rejected` / ingress / create a vp whose on-screen-clamped pixel size still exceeds `Caps::max_viewport_px_w()` (reference the accessor, NOT the literal `8192`, so advertise==enforce is structural — R3-1) / assert the internal handle is `Err(TgpError::CapExceeded{detail:"max_viewport_px", corr:Vp(id)})`, the vp is NOT created (`Scene::viewport(id)` is `None`), and `Viewport::allocated_px()==None` (no offscreen allocated — replaces the prose "no offscreen allocated", R3-5). (Fixes I13: REJECT, not clamp. The queued `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px` bytes correlated by `vp=<id>` with no `txn` are asserted in T16 `error_vp_cites_vp_id`, which reuses this exact repro — I15 seam, supplies the I5 `vp=` correlator coverage.)
- `vp_over_inline_cap_rejected` / ingress / a `vp` with `cols > Caps::max_inline_cols()` (reference the accessor, NOT the literal `256` — R3-1) / assert the internal handle is `Err(TgpError::CapExceeded{detail:"max_inline_cols", corr:Vp(id)})`, vp NOT created (§10.2 reject, not clamp), `Viewport::allocated_px()==None`. Eventual wire `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_inline_cols` correlated by `vp=<id>`.
- `vp_pixel_rect_clamped_to_onscreen_intersection` / ingress / create a vp that partially overhangs the terminal surface but whose full pixel size is under `max_viewport_px_w/h` / assert `pixel_rect()` is the on-screen intersection (off-screen area not allocated) and the vp IS created (this clamp is legitimate and distinct from the cap reject above — §10.3).

## P1-T20 — Default material: PINNED constants, sRGB→linear→sRGB color path, brightness clamp

**Title:** The implicit `MaterialId 0` matte-lit default material with its own pinned constants.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/default_material.wgsl` (and a CPU mirror in `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/material.rs` for the testable shading function `fn shade_default(base_srgb, tint_srgb, brightness, normal, view_up) -> Rgba_srgb`). PIN the §11.2 constants exactly: key light dir (view space) = `normalize((-0.3,-1.0,-0.4))`, key color `linear(1,1,1)`, key intensity `1.0`, ambient `linear(0.12,0.12,0.12)`, hemisphere sky `linear(0.30,0.34,0.40)`, ground `linear(0.16,0.14,0.12)`, hemi weight `0.25`, brightness clamped to `[0.0, 8.0]` (values outside clamp silently, never an error). Formula: `final_linear = clamp(base_linear × tint_linear × brightness, 0, large) × (ambient + hemi + key × max(N·L,0))`. Wire colors arrive sRGB → linearize on input → shade in linear → explicit sRGB-encode on output. Hemisphere term is **always** on (no gated mode). These are TGP's OWN constants — golden tests against TGP values, not byte-matched to `rgp.wgsl`.

**Design refs:** §11.2 (default material constants pinned; color space sRGB↔linear; brightness clamp); §11.1 (one linear-light offscreen, theme-tint pre-lighting stage — theme-tint flag parsed, default off). G-invariants: G8 (pinned own constants, no RGP match requirement), G7 (color=srgb path), G6 (CPU-mirror shading function testable without GPU).

**Depends on:** P1-T7.

**Tests:**
- `default_material_brightness_clamps_high` / ingress / `shade_default` with `brightness=100.0` / assert it behaves identically to `brightness=8.0` (clamped, no error).
- `default_material_brightness_clamps_low` / ingress / `brightness=-5.0` / assert behaves as `0.0` (output is the zero-surface result, no error).
- `default_material_white_base_lit` / ingress / base & tint white, brightness 1, normal facing the key light / assert the linear surface = `ambient + hemi + key·(N·L)` and the sRGB-encoded output round-trips (white input not blown to >1 before clamp).
- `default_material_tint_multiplies` / ingress / base white, tint `[128,0,0,255]` (sRGB) / assert the red channel of `final_linear` ≈ `linearize(128/255)` × lighting and green/blue ≈ 0 (tint multiplies, not emissive add, not base replace).
- `default_material_srgb_roundtrip` / ingress / base sRGB `[188,188,188,255]` neutral lit / assert linearize→shade→sRGB-encode yields a value within 1 LSB of the expected sRGB (color path well-defined).
- `default_material_hemisphere_always_on` / ingress / a fragment whose `N·L <= 0` (facing away from key) / assert it still receives `ambient + hemi` (nonzero) — the hemisphere term is never gated off.
- `theme_tint_off_by_default` / ingress / a viewport with `render.theme_tint` absent / assert the default-material diffuse base is unmodified by the terminal foreground color.

## P1-T21 — One viewport rendered at compositing parity: offscreen target, depth-test vs text plane, render gate

**Title:** Render a single viewport's subtree into a linear-light offscreen color+depth target and composite it depth-tested against the text plane.

**Deliverable:** Add `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/pipeline.rs`: a per-viewport forward pass that recomputes dirty world matrices (P1-T8), renders the viewport's subtree (default-material meshes) into its own offscreen `{color, depth}` target in linear light, sRGB-encodes into the color target, and composites the layer into the framebuffer **depth-tested against the text plane at z=0.5** (matching the existing text-plane convention). No MSAA/tone-map/PBR/lights here (P3); no second viewport/instancing (P2); the pick pass is P2. The render gate consumes the **per-node and per-viewport dirty flags** (cleared after consumption), NOT numeric `scene_revision` equality (G9) — a viewport renders on first-draw or when dirty. Expose GPU-free render-decision accessors: `Term::tgp_viewports_to_render() -> Vec<VpId>` (returns dirty/first-draw viewports), `Term::tgp_consume_dirty()` (clears consumed flags), and — for the compositing-parity surface (R3-4) — `Term::tgp_layer_depth(vp) -> f32` returning the CPU-side composite depth the viewport's layer would write (the value compared against the text plane at z=0.5), plus a derived `Term::tgp_composite_rel(vp) -> CompositeRel` where `enum CompositeRel { OccludesText, OccludedByText }` (`OccludesText` iff `tgp_layer_depth(vp) < 0.5`, matching the text-plane-at-0.5 convention, §11.1). The depth decision is computed on the CPU and queryable (G6), so the §11.1 compositing-parity surface does not rest on a GPU-only path. The actual draw is GPU-side; the gate logic, world-matrix inputs, and the composite depth decision are CPU-side and tested.

**Design refs:** §11.1 (per-viewport flow: one linear-light offscreen, sRGB-encode, depth-tested composite vs text plane — minus MSAA/tone-map/pick which are later); §6.5 (render gate consumes dirty flags). G-invariants: G9 (dirty/generation render gate, no per-frame scene_revision spam), G6 (gate decision CPU-testable), G7 (sRGB composite parity).

**Depends on:** P1-T8, P1-T19, P1-T20.

**Tests:**
- `render_gate_first_draw` / ingress / create a viewport with one mesh / assert `tgp_viewports_to_render()` lists that viewport on first draw.
- `render_gate_skips_clean_viewport` / lifecycle / render once (consume dirty), then call again with no scene change / assert `tgp_viewports_to_render()` is empty (no re-render of a clean viewport).
- `render_gate_redraws_on_node_dirty` / ingress / after a clean render, `node.upsert{id:"n", trs:…}` in the viewport's subtree / assert the viewport reappears in `tgp_viewports_to_render()`.
- `render_gate_not_driven_by_revision_equality` / lifecycle / advance `scene_revision` via a change in an UNRELATED token's scene / assert this token's clean viewport is NOT scheduled to render (gate is per-viewport dirty flags, not global revision).
- `render_consumes_world_matrices` / ingress / a parent-child subtree where the parent moved / assert the recomputed world matrix the renderer would consume matches `Scene::world_matrix(child)` (CPU geometry is the source the GPU consumes).
- `composite_depth_against_text_plane` / ingress / handshake tok A, then two concrete cases (R3-4 — concrete bytes + named seam): **(a)** `ESC _ tgp;vp;tok=A;id=vfront;col=0;row=0;cols=4;rows=2 ESC \` plus a Camera/root upsert that places the mesh node's resolved clip-space z at `0.3` (in front of the text plane); **(b)** `ESC _ tgp;vp;tok=A;id=vback;col=0;row=0;cols=4;rows=2 ESC \` with the camera/root placing clip-z at `0.7` (behind) / assert `Term::tgp_layer_depth("vfront") < 0.5` and `Term::tgp_composite_rel("vfront")==OccludesText`, and `Term::tgp_layer_depth("vback") > 0.5` with `Term::tgp_composite_rel("vback")==OccludedByText` — the CPU-queryable depth decision matches the text-plane-at-0.5 convention (§11.1 composite by z, depth-tested vs the text plane).

## Review resolution log (round 1)

Per review issue id: resolved (how) | deferred-with-reason | declined-with-justification. Verified each finding against the authoritative slices (§5–§8, §10, §11, §15) and the reconciliation spine (G1–G10, which wins).

- **I1 (high — `material.add` uncovered):** RESOLVED. Kept `Op::MaterialAdd` in scope (it is a first-class op in §8.6). Added decode tests `decode_material_add_op` + `decode_material_add_pbr_fields_parsed_inert` (T9) and apply/commit tests `material_add_decodes_and_commits` + `material_add_pbr_fields_parsed_deferred` (T11), asserting the materials table, the single `asset_revision` bump (§6.5 row 1), Mesh `material:` resolution, and PBR-fields-parsed-but-inert. T11 deliverable now stages `material.add` and bumps `asset_revision` on material change. Added to the P1 scope-clarifications paragraph.
- **I2 (medium — `node.instances` decode/validation scope):** RESOLVED. Made the boundary explicit: `node.instances` **decode + hardened validation** is IN P1 (pure-CPU surface, §6.4); only GPU upload/instanced draw + `node.instance_set` defer to P2. Added `Op::NodeInstances` to T9's enum; expanded T12 (renamed) with the decode/validate deliverable + `Node::instance_buffer()` accessor and tests `instances_decode_ok`, `instances_layout_mismatch_rejected` (`bad_layout;detail=xforms_len`), `instances_over_cap_rejected` (`cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances`), `instances_nan_rejected` (`parse_error;detail=instance_nan`). Asserts `scene_revision`+`instance_dirty`, never `asset_revision` (G2/§6.5). Scope paragraph updated.
- **I3 (medium — `caps_gen` monotonicity untested):** RESOLVED. Added T6 `caps_reply_present_caps_gen` (presence + parseable u64) and T10 `caps_gen_present_and_monotonic` (two re-queries, second `caps_gen ≥ first`, advanced via the injected `ManualClock`/cap path, no wall-clock).
- **I4 (medium — `bintrailer`/`trl=` advertised but no parse/verify task):** RESOLVED (chose keep+implement, per §8.2 normative + §7.1 "advertised=supported"). Threaded `trl` through the back-channel: `ApcEnd::BinaryFollows{len,enc,trl}` (T1), `BinaryRead{…,trl}` + 8-byte `TGPB`+LE-len trailer read/verify (T2) with tests `bin_trailer_verified_ok`, `bin_trailer_corrupt_rejected` (`parse_error;detail=trailer_corrupt`), `bin_trailer_straddles_reads`; header `trl=1` parse (T3) test `header_parses_trl_flag`; Term trailer verify in `tgp_feed_binary(buf, trailer)` (T4). Scope paragraph updated.
- **I5 (medium — `vp=` correlator never asserted):** RESOLVED. The over-pixel-cap vp reject (now a reject per I13) is the P1 vp-correlated error. Added T16 `error_vp_cites_vp_id` asserting the queued `x` carries `vp=<id>`, `code=cap_exceeded`, `detail=max_viewport_px`, and NO `txn`/`asset` (G4). T16 now `Depends on: P1-T19`.
- **I6 (high — `unknown_op` reachable in T16 vs skipped in T18):** RESOLVED. Decided v1: `unknown_op` is NOT P1-reachable (every unknown `do:` skips per §7.3). Removed it from T16's `every_p1_error_code_emitted` ingress list; added unit test `unknown_op_code_encoder_roundtrips` (later renamed/subsumed into `closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26`; encoder can spell the closed-set code without an ingress repro). Defined the skip/reject boundary in T18 + added `op_missing_do_key_rejects` (a map with no `do:` is `parse_error`, the boundary that keeps `unknown_op` unreachable).
- **I7 (low — `sub`/`anim` undefined handling):** RESOLVED. T4 deliverable now defines a handshaked `tgp;sub`/`tgp;anim` in P1 as a SAFE NO-OP (parses, no scene mutation, never panics per §15.5); added T4 `unimplemented_frame_type_is_safe_noop`.
- **I8 (medium — `bad_param` no repro; node-id charset hardening has no task):** RESOLVED. Added node-id hardening to T12 (≤64 bytes, no C0/DEL/C1/ESC → `bad_param;detail=charset|too_long`, §8.4/§15.5) with tests `node_id_control_byte_rejected` (0x1B/0x07/0x9C) and `node_id_too_long_rejected` (65-byte reject, 64-byte commit boundary). T16's `bad_param` repro now points at this concrete case. Scope paragraph updated.

Added explicit "Assertion seam (I15)" notes to those deliverables; T10's two `x`-byte tests note they consume T16; T19's vp rejects assert the internal handle and defer bytes to T16. ## Review resolution log (round 2)

Per round-2 review issue id: resolved (how) | deferred-with-reason | declined-with-justification. The round-2 review confirmed ZERO uncovered in-scope surfaces (coverage complete) and raised six NEW correctness/fidelity findings (J1–J6). Verified each against the authoritative slices (§5–§8, §11) and the reconciliation spine (G1–G10, which wins).

- **J1 (medium — `node.instances` op shape conflated with `node.upsert{instances:…}`; missing `mesh:` asset ref):** RESOLVED. The spine + §8.6/§6.1 win: the buffer + asset ride on the `node.instances` op (`{do:"node.instances", id, mesh:<AssetId>, count, xforms, tints}`), and an Instanced node REQUIRES an asset (§6.1 `NodeKind::Instanced{asset,material,instances}`). The bare `instances` selector on `node.upsert` only sets/keeps the Instanced kind. Edits: (a) T12 deliverable rewritten to state the op shape, the required `mesh:` asset ref (order-independent resolution, uncommitted → `bad_ref;detail=asset:<id>`), and that on success the node becomes `Instanced{asset:<mesh>,…}`; (b) `instances_decode_ok` now uses `[asset.add id:5, node.instances{id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:2, xforms:128B, tints:8B}]` and asserts `kind==Instanced{asset:5}` alongside the buffer accessor; (c) added `instances_bad_ref_uncommitted_mesh` (uncommitted `mesh:99` → `bad_ref;detail=asset:99`); (d) `instances_layout_mismatch_rejected`/`instances_over_cap_rejected`/`instances_nan_rejected` now carry `mesh:5` with asset 5 committed so the rejection is on layout/cap/nan, not `bad_ref`; (e) `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected` creates the node via `node.instances` then attempts the node-level `tint` via a follow-up `node.upsert`; (f) T9's `Op::NodeInstances` enum entry notes the `mesh:` field.
- **J2 (medium — inline `asset.add fmt=glb` → `decode_glb` → assets-table seam unwired):** RESOLVED. Edits: (a) T11 deliverable now states applying `asset.add{fmt:"glb", data}` calls `decode_glb(data, caps)` (P1-T15), installs the `MeshAsset` on commit, and a `decode_glb` error rejects the whole txn — so the GLB error codes (`bad_index`/`bad_layout`/`parse_error`/`cap_exceeded`) become ingress-reachable through apply; (b) `apply_patch` signature gains `caps: &Caps`; (c) added ingress tests `inline_asset_add_decodes_and_installs` (asset installed, `Scene::asset(7)` present, `asset_revision` +1) and `inline_asset_add_glb_error_rejects_txn` (wrong-magic GLB rejects the whole txn through apply); (d) added `Scene::asset(id)` and `Scene::material(id)` accessors to T7; (e) `T11 Depends on:` now includes `P1-T15`; (f) to keep the DAG acyclic (T11→T15 would have closed a T11→T15→T14→T11 cycle), the spurious `T15 Depends on: P1-T14` was removed — `decode_glb(bytes, caps)` is a standalone decoder needing only `Caps`; T11 and T14 are its CONSUMERS; (g) T14 deliverable now states the chunked path reuses the SAME T11 install seam (no separate GLB decode call site).
- **J3 (low — `BinEnc`/`Enc` mapping unstated; `max_msg_mb`→bytes conversion not pinned):** RESOLVED. (a) Added a sentence to T1 and T3 stating `BinEnc` is the parser-side one-variant subset (`Bin` only, the only enc that triggers binary-read), `Enc` is the full header enum, and the Term maps `Enc::Bin → BinEnc::Bin` (`Enc::B64` never crosses the back-channel). (b) Pinned the conversion ONCE in T6: `Caps::max_msg_bytes() = max_msg_mb * 1024 * 1024` (= 67_108_864, consistent with §7.1's `max_asset_bytes=67108864`) and `Caps::max_reasm_bytes()`; the MiB multiplier (1024·1024, not 1_000_000) is normative. T2/T9/T14 now reference `Caps::max_msg_bytes()`/`Caps::max_reasm_bytes()` instead of ad-hoc byte constants, and the over-cap tests note a test-only small `max_msg_mb`/`max_reasm_mb` so the boundary is exercised cheaply (the conversion formula, not the absolute 64 MB value, is under test).
- **J4 (low — inert PBR `tex_base` must not draw `bad_ref`):** RESOLVED. T11 deliverable now states inert PBR fields (`tex_base`, `base`, `metallic`, `rough`, `model`) are stored WITHOUT reference resolution in P1 (PBR + PNG texture decode are P3 per §11.2), so a `material.add{tex_base:8}` for a nonexistent texture asset still commits and does NOT draw `bad_ref`. (§8.6 lists `tex` among resolved refs for the FULL protocol; in P1 its resolution is deferred with the rest of PBR — a scope decision, not a contradiction.) `material_add_pbr_fields_parsed_deferred` now feeds `tex_base:8` with asset 8 absent and asserts the material commits with NO `bad_ref`.
- **J5 (low — inbound `vmin=` parse not asserted; `v=`-omission `parse_error` MAY-path thin):** RESOLVED. (a) Added typed `v: Option<u8>` and `vmin: Option<u8>` fields to `TgpHeader` (T3) so `negotiate(app_max, app_min)` (P1-T6) has a tested source for BOTH; (b) `header_parses_control_query` now asserts `v==Some(2)` AND `vmin==Some(1)`; (c) added `header_query_missing_v_captures_vmin` (`v==None`, `vmin==Some(1)`); (d) T10 deliverable + `query_missing_v_still_answered` now make the §7.1 MAY-path explicit: the plan picks "answer-only, no `parse_error`" for v1 (the always-answered `tgp;r` is the hard invariant; the optional `parse_error` is permitted-not-required), and the test asserts NO `parse_error` is queued for the omission.
- **J6 (low — `detail=` spellings; carried-over advisory I16):** DECLINED as not-actionable. The reviewer's own conclusion is "None required (advisory)." Re-verified: every `detail=` string in the plan (`field_order`, `unrecoverable`, `trailer_corrupt`, `multiple_selectors`, `token`, `max_msg|max_reasm`, `base64`, `trailing_bytes`, `truncated`, `instance_nan`, `xforms_len|tints_len|count_mismatch`, `max_instances`, `charset|too_long`, `max_viewport_px`, `max_inline_cols|max_inline_rows`, `geom_nan`, `glb_magic|glb_chunk`, `max_verts_per_asset`, `asset:<id>`) matches the design, `detail=` is non-load-bearing per G3, and no op/verb/frame-type/field wire spelling is invented outside the design. The new accessors introduced this round (`Scene::asset`, `Scene::material`, `Caps::max_msg_bytes`, `Caps::max_reasm_bytes`) are testing seams the contract invites. No edit required.

Per issue id + per duty: resolved (how) | declined (why). **Coverage invariant upheld:** every op/verb/header-field/error-code/cap/lifecycle-event in scope retains ≥1 concrete test; the two codes moved to RESERVED (`denied`, `msg_too_large`) keep a concrete closed-set encoder test (`closed_set_encoder_roundtrips_all_26`), and the surfaces they vacated (`kind_conflict` now also covers instanced-node-tint; `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` now covers over-msg-cap) gain ingress coverage — net coverage rises, none regresses.

# P2 — Differentiators

This phase builds the features that make TGP more than "GLB in a cell": multiple viewports anchored to authoritative inline placeholder cells (per F3) plus pinned regions, each with its own offscreen color+depth target and depth-aware compositing against the constant-depth text plane; terminal-driven reflow that follows the cells; the GPU instancing **render path** + the single-instance re-tint write op (the canonical column-major mat4 f32 LE + RGBA8 **decode/validation is owned by P1-T12**, which P2 **reuses** — P2 does not re-implement a second decoder; see the per-task DEDUP notes on P2-T16/T17); and the full interactivity spine — CPU-resolvable picking (G6), `tgp;e` event reports, per-viewport `sub` subscriptions, the explore camera controller, and F4 input routing that captures the viewport cell rect and suppresses SGR there. All geometry (world matrices, pixel rects, instance decode, pick resolution) stays CPU-side and queryable so every surface is GPU-free testable across the ingress, interaction, and lifecycle seams. It depends on the P1 foundation (framer/CBOR decode, caps/handshake, scene model, **P1-T12 instance decode/validation**, one viewport, the deterministic clock, and the `Term::tgp_scene()` / `pty_replies` seams) and never on any later phase.

> Cross-phase note: P1 establishes `tgp/*` greenfield trees in `toastty-graphics` (scene/decoder), `toastty-render` (one offscreen-less pass), and `toastty-term` (`Term::tgp_scene()` `&self` accessor + `queue_reply → pty_replies`). This phase **explicitly changes the cross-crate `apc_end` back-channel** so the parser returns decoded TGP frames to `Term` and so `Term` can surface decoded viewport/instance/pick state to tests; that change is called out where it occurs (see P2-T1).

> Feature-gating note (G7): the `feat=` token set and its implication rules (`instance⇒graph`, `pick⇒event`, `explore⇒event`) are **negotiated at handshake in P1**, and the closed-enum `unsupported` reply for a feature a terminal does not advertise is owned and tested by P1's handshake tasks. P2's instancing (P2-T16/T19), pick (P2-T20), and explore (P2-T28) tasks **assume the relevant feat was negotiated**. But the **use-sites** for these feats — the `node.instances` op, the `sub` verb, and the `vp;explore` field — are all **P2 ops**, so P2 owns the use-time `unsupported` gate where a token sends one of these without having negotiated the implied feat. Each is tested in P2 at its own use-site, mirroring the same pattern: P2-T16 gates `node.instances` on `feat=instance` (`instances_without_feat_unsupported`); P2-T22 gates `sub ev=<pointer>` on `feat=pick`/`event` (`sub_pick_without_feat_unsupported`); P2-T28/P2-T1 gate `vp;explore` on `feat=explore` (`explore_without_feat_unsupported`). Per G7's implication rules `pick⇒event` and `explore⇒event`, the implied base feat (`event`) is also required; the emitted `detail=` names the requested feat (`pick`/`explore`). The handshake **negotiation** itself remains P1's; P2 only exercises the use-time rejection at the verbs it introduces.

## P2-T1 — `vp` control-frame ingress + multi-viewport store (apc_end back-channel change)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/decoder.rs` add the `vp` verb decoder `decode_vp_frame(header, body) -> Result<VpFrame, TgpError>` where `VpFrame` is `enum { Upsert(VpOp), Destroy(ViewportId) }`. The **upsert** form (no `op=` key, or `op=upsert`) produces `VpOp { id: ViewportId, anchor: Anchor, cells: CellRect, camera: Option<NodeId>, root: Option<NodeId>, z: i32, clear: Option<Rgba>, render: RenderOpts, explore: Option<ExploreOpts>, screen_affinity: ScreenAffinity, clip_to_scroll_region: bool }` (sparse-merge over an existing viewport, same absent/null/value three-way as `node.upsert`, §6.1). The `explore` field is the first-class viewport controller field §10.1 (s10 line 17) defines on the `vp` frame; `Viewport.explore` carries the same type. `decode_vp_frame` parses the `explore` sub-map by **delegating to P2-T28's `ExploreOpts` decoder** (P2-T28 extends `apply_tgp_vp` to route the decoded sub-map through the `feat=explore` use-time gate before the controller is stored — see P2-T28); the field itself is defined here so the struct P2-T1 owns has a decode home for it. **Nullable vs non-nullable `vp` fields (three-way merge, §6.1).** Nullable (CBOR null clears to default): `camera` → `None` (implicit default camera, §10.5), `root` → `None` (whole scene), `clear` → `None` (transparent), `explore` → `None` (CBOR null clears/tears down the controller; absent → preserve the existing controller; value → set/replace, subject to the P2-T28 feat gate). Non-nullable (CBOR null is rejected with `tgp;x;code=null_not_allowed;detail=<field>`, viewport unchanged, no partial apply): the `vp` id itself, and — on an **initial create** (no prior viewport for that id) — `anchor` and `cells` (a viewport cannot exist without an anchor and a cell rect). `z`, `screen_affinity`, `clip_to_scroll_region`, and `render` are non-nullable scalars/sub-maps (absent → preserve; value → set; null → `null_not_allowed`). The **explicit-destroy** form is a `vp` frame carrying `op=remove` (text header) addressing an existing `vp=<id>`; it decodes to `VpFrame::Destroy(id)` and removes that viewport via the shared teardown path (P2-T36) with `DestroyReason::Explicit`. `op=remove` for an unknown `vp` id is a no-op (no `tgp;x` — idempotent teardown). This `op=remove` discriminant is the explicit-destroy wire form the design's §10.7 implicit-only list did not enumerate; it is introduced here so an app can deterministically tear down a pinned viewport (which is otherwise never cell-evicted). In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` extend `Scene.viewports: Map<ViewportId, Viewport>` (P1 created the single-viewport struct) to hold many, keyed in the **per-token** namespace (§8.4, G1). **`ViewportId` is a composite key `{ token: Tok, raw: u16 }`** (G1 id hardening / §8.4): the wire `vp=` carries only the raw `u16`, but the store key qualifies it with the creating token so the same raw `vp=1` may exist concurrently under AP1 and AP2 as two distinct map entries and namespace isolation holds. The test helper is token-aware: `vid(tok, n)` builds `{tok, n}`, and the single-token convenience `vid(n)` binds to the test's active handshake token (most tests use one token, so `vid(1) ⇒ {AP1, 1}`); multi-token tests use the qualified form. In `toastty-term/src/tgp/mod.rs` extend the apc_end back-channel: where P1's `apc_end` returned unit / a single scene op, it now returns a decoded `TgpFrame` enum that includes `TgpFrame::Vp(VpFrame)`, and `Term` applies it via `Term::apply_tgp_vp` (routing `Upsert`/`Destroy`), resolving the wire `vp=<raw>` against the frame's `tok=` to form the composite `ViewportId`. **This is the called-out cross-crate `apc_end` change**: the parser's `apc_end` signature gains a return value carrying the decoded frame so `Term` can route `vp`/`sub` frames (vp/sub are control frames, never binary-read — §7.2). Add `&self` accessor `Term::tgp_viewports() -> &Map<ViewportId, Viewport>` and `Term::tgp_viewport(id: &ViewportId) -> Option<&Viewport>`.

**Design refs.** §10.1, §10.5, §6.1 (three-way sparse merge + `null_not_allowed`); G1 + **G11** (private per-token id map: cross-token addressing is unrepresentable, raw-id collisions coexist as distinct composite keys, `denied` is RESERVED with no ingress trigger — supersedes the base-spine G1 "denied on cross-token" prose), G4 (vp frames correlate by `vp` id, no `txn`), G2 + **G11(c)** (late-bound camera/root never reject; a cross-token camera/root ref late-binds to default, never `denied`; no existence oracle per G11(b)), G3 (`null_not_allowed`).

**Depends on.** P1 (tgp scene model + single Viewport struct), P1 (framer/CBOR decode), P1 (`Term::tgp_scene()` accessor + `pty_replies`). Note: the `vp;op=remove` **decode** (producing `VpFrame::Destroy`) lands here; the **apply** routing that calls the shared `destroy_viewport` helper is completed when P2-T36 lands (no build cycle — the decoder is independent, and the `vp_op_remove_*` tests run once T36's helper is wired).

**Tests.**
- `vp_create_two_viewports_distinct_ids` / ingress / feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=10;rows=5 ESC\` then same with `vp=2;col=12` / assert `term.tgp_viewports().len() == 2` and `term.tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).unwrap().cells.col == 0`, `term.tgp_viewport(&vid(2)).unwrap().cells.col == 12`.
- `vp_sparse_merge_preserves_unset_fields` / ingress / create `vp=1` with `z=5;clear=#101010`, then feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;cols=20 ESC\` / assert `cells.cols == 20` AND `z == 5` AND `clear == Some(Rgba(0x10,0x10,0x10,0xff))` (absent keys preserved).
- `vp_clear_null_resets_to_transparent` / ingress / create `vp=1;clear=#101010`, then feed a `vp` frame whose CBOR `clear` key is null / assert `clear == None`.
- `vp_same_raw_id_two_tokens_coexist` / ingress / handshake `AP1` creates `vp=1;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=10;rows=5`; feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP2;vp=1;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=99;rows=5 ESC\` (a second token sending raw `vp=1`) / assert under the private per-token id map (G11) **both** entries coexist as distinct composite keys: `term.tgp_viewport(&vid("AP1",1)).unwrap().cells.cols == 10` (unchanged — AP2 cannot index AP1's map) AND `term.tgp_viewport(&vid("AP2",1)).unwrap().cells.cols == 99` (AP2 created its OWN `{AP2,1}`) AND **no** `tgp;x;code=denied` (and no `denied` of any kind) is queued — a raw-id "collision" between tokens is not an error, the two map keys simply coexist (G11(a): `denied` is RESERVED; cross-token addressing is unrepresentable under the private map; cf. P2-T8 z-order tiebreak where the two distinct entries are merely ordered). `apply_tgp_vp` resolves the wire `vp=<raw>` against the frame's `tok=` so `tok=AP2;vp=1` always lands on `{AP2,1}`, never on `{AP1,1}`.
- `vp_cross_token_camera_ref_late_binds_default` / ingress / handshake `AP1` creates a `Camera` node `cam` (in AP1's map) and a viewport; then handshake `AP2` and feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP2;vp=1;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=4;rows=2;camera=cam ESC\` (AP2 names a camera id that exists only under AP1) / assert AP2's `{AP2,1}` viewport is **created** (no reject) AND `term.tgp_viewport(&vid("AP2",1)).unwrap().effective_camera() == ResolvedCamera::Default(..)` (the `cam` ref does not resolve in AP2's own map → late-bind default per G11(c)/G2, NEVER `denied`, NEVER reject) AND AP1's `cam` node is unchanged AND **no** `tgp;x;code=denied` is queued (an optional diagnostic `tgp;x;tok=AP2;vp=1;code=bad_ref` MAY be queued on entering the missing state per G2/§10.5, but never `denied`, and the missing-state error must be byte-identical whether `cam` exists elsewhere or nowhere — no existence oracle, G11(b)).
- `vp_camera_null_clears_to_default` / ingress / create `vp=1;camera=cam` (cam exists as a Camera node), then feed a `vp` frame whose CBOR `camera` key is **null** / assert `term.tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).unwrap().camera == None` AND `effective_camera() == ResolvedCamera::Default(..)` (null-clear of camera falls back per P2-T14, distinct from the absent-key preserve case; G2 late-bind).
- `vp_root_null_clears_to_whole_scene` / ingress / two root nodes A,B; create `vp=1;root=A`, then feed a `vp` frame whose CBOR `root` key is null / assert `term.tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).unwrap().root == None` AND `effective_root()` contains both A and B (null-clear of root → whole scene).
- `vp_null_on_required_field_rejected` / ingress / create `vp=1;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=4;rows=2`, then feed a `vp` frame addressing `vp=1` whose CBOR `anchor` key is null on an existing viewport (or, for the create case, a first `vp=2` frame with a null `cells`) / assert `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=null_not_allowed;detail=anchor` AND the viewport is unchanged (`tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).unwrap().anchor` is still `Pinned`); for the create case assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(2)) == None`.
- `vp_apc_end_returns_decoded_frame` / ingress / drive `apc_end` with a `vp` body / assert the returned `TgpFrame` matches `TgpFrame::Vp(VpFrame::Upsert(op))` with the decoded `ViewportId` (locks the back-channel signature change).
- `vp_op_remove_destroys_viewport` / ingress / create pinned `vp=1`, then feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;op=remove ESC\` / assert the decoder yields `VpFrame::Destroy(vid(1))` AND `term.tgp_viewport(&vid(1)) == None` (explicit destroy; pinned removed via the shared teardown path).
- `vp_op_remove_unknown_id_noop` / ingress / feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=99;op=remove ESC\` with no `vp=99` created / assert no `tgp;x` queued (idempotent no-op) AND `term.tgp_viewports().is_empty()`.
- `vp_render_opts_decoded` / ingress / create `vp=1` whose CBOR `render` sub-map sets `tone_map=2` (e.g. ACES operator enum) and `theme_tint=true` (MSAA may be ignored/deferred to P3) / assert `term.tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).unwrap().render.tone_map == ToneMap::Aces` AND `.render.theme_tint == true` (RenderOpts color-path fields land on the viewport; consumed by the P2-T7 compositor, color chain per G7 `color=srgb`). MSAA field is parsed-and-stored but not exercised until P3.

## P2-T2 — Inline anchor: Unicode-placeholder-cell binding (cells authoritative, F3)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` (new) add the placeholder-cell binder: on `anchor=inline {line,col}` creation the terminal lays kitty-style Unicode placeholder glyphs into the grid for the requested `cols × rows` and records the **live placeholder-cell set** `Viewport.placeholder_cells: BitGrid` / `Vec<CellAddr>` on the viewport. **Concrete placeholder encoding:** each bound cell stores the Unicode placeholder codepoint `U+10EEEE` (the kitty Unicode-placeholder sentinel) as its glyph and carries the owning `ViewportId` in the cell's metadata field (reuse the P1 cell-metadata slot that today holds image-placement ids). A cell is a TGP placeholder iff its glyph is `U+10EEEE` AND its metadata holds a `ViewportId`; any other glyph written into that position is an overwrite (P2-T11). The stored `Anchor::Inline { line, col }` is an **initial hint only**; `Viewport::live_cell_rect(&self, grid) -> Option<CellRect>` is derived from the current landed placeholder cells, never re-reading the hint. Add `Term::tgp_viewport_live_rect(id) -> Option<CellRect>` and the per-cell accessor `Term::tgp_cell_placeholder(addr: CellAddr) -> Option<ViewportId>` (returns the owning viewport id iff the cell is a live TGP placeholder) so detach/re-emit/bind tests assert against a concrete seam. Wire the placeholder glyph emit through the existing `Perform` write path.

**Named grid-mutation test seam (resolves the "move helper" gap).** The cell-relocation precondition used by `inline_hint_not_reread` (P2-T2) and the reflow/scroll tests (P2-T9/T10) is driven through one concrete, deterministic seam, not an unnamed helper: `Term::test_scroll_grid(lines: i32)` (a `#[cfg(test)]` Term method that scrolls the grid — and the placeholder cells it carries — by `lines` rows exactly as a text scroll would, the same primitive the real scroll path uses) for plain relocation, and the real CSI/Perform byte sequences for the wrap/region cases: a resize→rewrap is driven by the existing terminal resize entry point `Term::set_grid_size(cols, rows)` (the SIGWINCH/resize path), and a DECSTBM scroll by feeding `ESC[<top>;<bot>r` (set region) followed by the scroll-inducing line feeds. P2-T9/T10 reference these by name so the "move/scroll/rewrap the cells" precondition is as concrete as the ingress byte sequences elsewhere.

**Design refs.** §10.1, §10.2 (placeholder cells authoritative); F3; G10 (inline lifetime).

**Depends on.** P2-T1, P1 (`Term` Perform/grid integration + cell-metadata slot).

**Tests.**
- `inline_create_binds_cells` / ingress / feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;anchor=inline;line=0;col=2;cols=4;rows=2 ESC\` / assert `term.tgp_viewport_live_rect(&vid(1)) == Some(CellRect{col:2,row:0,cols:4,rows:2})` AND each of the 8 cells at (col 2..6, row 0..2) returns `term.tgp_cell_placeholder(addr) == Some(vid(1))` AND its glyph is `U+10EEEE`.
- `inline_hint_not_reread` / ingress / create inline at `line=0;col=2`; relocate the placeholder cells to row 3 via the named seam `Term::test_scroll_grid(3)` (scrolls grid + placeholder cells by 3 rows) / assert `live_cell_rect().row == 3` while the stored `Anchor::Inline.line` is still `0` (hint never reconciled).
- `inline_reemit_same_glyph_not_detach` / interaction(grid write) / write the identical placeholder glyph (`U+10EEEE` carrying `vid(1)`) back into a bound cell via the Perform path / assert `term.tgp_cell_placeholder(addr) == Some(vid(1))` still (re-emit is not an overwrite).

## P2-T3 — Cell→pixel mapping, on-screen clamp, zero-size & off-screen

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/layout.rs` (new) add `pub fn cell_rect_to_px(rect: CellRect, metrics: CellMetrics, surface_px: SurfacePx) -> PxRect` computing pixel rects CPU-side from current cell metrics, **clamped to the on-screen intersection** with the terminal surface. Add `Viewport::pixel_rect(&self, grid, metrics, surface) -> Option<PxRect>` returning `None` for a fully off-screen rect and `Some(PxRect{w:0,h:0})`-flagged "legal-but-not-rendered" for a `cols==0 || rows==0` rect (no offscreen target allocated). Add `Term::tgp_viewport_pixel_rect(id) -> Option<PxRect>` and a `PxRect::is_rendered()` helper. Allocation of offscreen targets keys on `is_rendered()` (target allocation lands in P2-T6).

**Design refs.** §10.3 (cell→pixel, on-screen clamp, zero-size not-rendered); G6 (geometry CPU-side & queryable).

**Depends on.** P2-T1, P2-T2.

**Tests.**
- `px_mapping_basic` / ingress / metrics 8×16 px/cell, surface 800×400, create pinned `vp=1;col=2;row=1;cols=4;rows=2` / assert `tgp_viewport_pixel_rect(&vid(1)) == Some(PxRect{x:16,y:16,w:32,h:32})`.
- `px_clamp_to_screen` / ingress / surface 800×400 (cols=100), create pinned `vp=1;col=98;row=0;cols=10;rows=2` / assert clamped `PxRect.w == 16` (only 2 on-screen cols × 8px), not 80.
- `px_zero_size_not_rendered` / ingress / create `vp=1;cols=0;rows=3` / assert `tgp_viewport_pixel_rect(&vid(1)).unwrap().is_rendered() == false`.
- `px_fully_offscreen_none` / ingress / create pinned `vp=1;col=200;row=0;cols=4;rows=2` on a 100-col surface / assert `tgp_viewport_pixel_rect(&vid(1)) == None`.

## P2-T4 — Inline cell caps: max_inline_cols / max_inline_rows over-cap rejection

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` enforce the advertised `max_inline_cols` / `max_inline_rows` caps (from the P1 caps table) at inline-viewport creation/update: a `tgp;vp;anchor=inline` whose `CellRect` exceeds either cap is **rejected — not created, not partially bound** — and the terminal queues `tgp;x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_inline_cols` (or `max_inline_rows`) to the owning token. No silent clamp/truncate. `CellRect` stays `u16`.

**Design refs.** §10.2 (cell caps & over-cap rejection); G3 (`cap_exceeded`), G4 (correlate by `vp` + `tok`).

**Depends on.** P2-T2, P1 (caps/handshake providing cap values).

**Tests.**
