- `inline_over_cap_cols_rejected` / ingress / with `max_inline_cols=8`, feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;anchor=inline;line=0;col=0;cols=9;rows=1 ESC\` / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)) == None` (not created) AND `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_inline_cols`.
- `inline_over_cap_rows_rejected` / ingress / `max_inline_rows=4`, feed inline `rows=5` / assert not created AND reply `detail=max_inline_rows`.
- `inline_at_cap_accepted` / ingress / `max_inline_cols=8`, feed inline `cols=8;rows=1` / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).is_some()` and no `cap_exceeded` in `pty_replies`.
- `inline_update_over_cap_keeps_prior` / ingress / create inline `cols=4`, then update to `cols=99` (over cap) / assert live rect still `cols==4` AND a `cap_exceeded` reply was queued (update rejected, prior binding retained).

## P2-T5 — Per-viewport pixel cap: max_viewport_px_w / _h rejection

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/layout.rs` (validation entry called from `Term::apply_tgp_vp`) reject any viewport whose **on-screen clamped** pixel size still exceeds the per-viewport pixel cap with `tgp;x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px` to the owning token; the viewport is not created or updated. Applies to both inline and pinned. Together with the P2-T3 on-screen clamp this is the oversized-VRAM backstop (§15.1).

**Cap-key resolution (spine vs §10.3 drift).** The spine's G7 cap list names this `max_viewport_px` (singular family name); §10.3 uses the split `max_viewport_px_w` / `max_viewport_px_h`. A width/height cap genuinely needs two numbers, so **P1's caps table advertises the split pair `max_viewport_px_w` and `max_viewport_px_h`** (the two concrete keys P2-T5 reads), and the spine's singular `max_viewport_px` is the **scope-prefix/family name** under which that pair lives (and the `detail=max_viewport_px` advisory spelling, harmless per G3 since `detail=` is non-load-bearing). P2-T5 therefore reads `max_viewport_px_w`/`_h` from P1's table and emits `detail=max_viewport_px`. This pins the cross-doc key so P2-T5 never reads a key P1 does not expose.

**Design refs.** §10.3 (per-viewport pixel cap); G3 (`cap_exceeded`), G7 (cap list; `max_viewport_px` family name → split `_w`/`_h` keys).

**Depends on.** P2-T3, P1 (caps).

**Tests.**
- `viewport_px_over_cap_rejected` / ingress / `max_viewport_px_w=64`, metrics 8px/col, surface wide enough, create pinned `vp=1;cols=16` (=128px > 64) / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)) == None` AND reply `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px`.
- `viewport_px_clamped_under_cap_accepted` / ingress / `max_viewport_px_w=64`, create pinned `vp=1;col=98;cols=16` on a 100-col surface so the clamped on-screen width is 16px ≤ 64 / assert created, no error (cap tests the clamped size, not the logical size).

## P2-T6 — Per-viewport offscreen color+depth targets (allocation gated by is_rendered)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-render/src/tgp/targets.rs` (new) add `ViewportTargets { color: Texture (premultiplied RGBA, sRGB-encoded), depth: Texture (Depth32Float) }` and a `TargetPool` keyed by `ViewportId` that allocates/reallocates a target on the per-viewport dirty flag when `PxRect::is_rendered()`, sizes it to the on-screen clamped `PxRect`, and frees it when the viewport becomes not-rendered or destroyed. P1 had one shared depth pass with no offscreen targets; this adds per-viewport offscreen color+depth (no MSAA yet — that is P3). Keep a CPU-queryable mirror `Term::tgp_viewport_target_size(id) -> Option<(u32,u32)>` and `tgp_viewport_has_target(id) -> bool` so allocation is testable GPU-free (the GPU texture is created lazily/skipped in headless test mode; the size record is always computed).

**Design refs.** §10.4 (own offscreen color+depth), §10.3 (allocation gated/clamped); G9 (dirty-flag gate, not per-frame revision).

**Depends on.** P2-T3, P1 (render pipeline + depth pass).

**Tests.**
- `target_allocated_for_rendered_viewport` / lifecycle / create pinned `vp=1;cols=4;rows=2` (metrics 8×16) / assert `tgp_viewport_has_target(&vid(1)) == true` AND `tgp_viewport_target_size(&vid(1)) == Some((32,32))`.
- `target_not_allocated_for_zero_size` / lifecycle / create `vp=1;cols=0;rows=2` / assert `tgp_viewport_has_target(&vid(1)) == false`.
- `target_realloc_on_resize_dirty` / lifecycle / create `vp=1;cols=4`, then `vp` update `cols=6` / assert target size width grows `32 → 48` and the per-viewport dirty flag was consumed (cleared) after realloc.
- `target_freed_when_offscreen` / lifecycle / create rendered pinned `vp=1`, then `vp` update moving it fully off-screen / assert `tgp_viewport_has_target(&vid(1)) == false` while `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).is_some()` (state retained, target freed).

## P2-T7 — Depth-aware compositing against the constant-depth text plane

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-render/src/tgp/composite.rs` (new) add the compositor that blends each viewport's offscreen color+depth into the main framebuffer with a depth test against the **shared constant-depth text plane** (P1 text plane at z=0.5). Premultiplied-alpha "over" blend; offscreen depth clears to FAR, color alpha clears to 0. `clear=Some(rgba)` clears the color target to that opaque color (occlude cells); `clear=None` composites over underlying text with the depth test gating only drawn fragments (alpha-0 pixels contribute nothing → text shows through exactly; a drawn opaque fragment depth-tests vs the text plane). Expose a CPU model of the compositor decision per sample for tests: `composite_sample(viewport_depth: f32, viewport_alpha: f32, text_z: f32, clear: Option<Rgba>) -> CompositeDecision { Occlude | OverText | BehindText | Transparent }` so the depth/blend logic is unit-testable without a GPU. **Also expose the value-level color path (G7 `color=srgb`):** `composite_color(viewport_premul: Rgba /* sRGB-encoded premultiplied */, text: Rgba /* sRGB */, clear: Option<Rgba>) -> Rgba` that decodes both inputs sRGB→linear, performs the premultiplied-alpha "over" blend in **linear** space (or fills with `clear` when `Some`), then re-encodes the result linear→sRGB into the offscreen byte triple — exactly the wire→linear→offscreen chain G7/§11/MATE-9 mandate so the composite against the sRGB text plane matches. This makes the premultiplied-over math and the sRGB round-trip a concrete, GPU-free assertable value, not just a depth/blend gate.

**Design refs.** §10.4 (stickers with depth; opaque exact, translucent approximate; transparent fill); G7 (`color=srgb` wire→linear→sRGB-offscreen chain).

**Depends on.** P2-T6, P1 (text plane depth + main framebuffer pass).

**Tests.**
- `composite_opaque_in_front_covers_text` / interaction(CPU model) / `composite_sample(depth=0.2, alpha=1.0, text_z=0.5, clear=None)` / assert `== CompositeDecision::OverText` (near geometry covers text).
- `composite_opaque_behind_text_hidden` / interaction(CPU model) / `composite_sample(depth=0.8, alpha=1.0, text_z=0.5, clear=None)` / assert `== CompositeDecision::BehindText` (text wins).
- `composite_alpha0_shows_text` / interaction(CPU model) / `composite_sample(depth=FAR, alpha=0.0, text_z=0.5, clear=None)` / assert `== CompositeDecision::Transparent` (no hole punched over text).
- `composite_clear_some_occludes` / interaction(CPU model) / `composite_sample(depth=FAR, alpha=0.0, text_z=0.5, clear=Some(opaque))` / assert `== CompositeDecision::Occlude` (cells occluded regardless of geometry).
- `composite_premul_over_srgb_value` / interaction(CPU model) / blend a known **50%-alpha sRGB red** premultiplied viewport color (sRGB `(255,0,0)` at alpha `0.5` → premultiplied sRGB-stored bytes) over a known sRGB text color (e.g. white `(255,255,255)`), `clear=None`, via `composite_color(..)` / assert the returned **sRGB-encoded bytes** equal the value computed by hand (decode both to linear: red linear ≈ 1.0, white linear = 1.0; over-blend `out_linear = src_premul_linear + dst_linear*(1-α)`; re-encode linear→sRGB) within **1 LSB** per channel — locks the premultiplied-over math AND the sRGB round-trip (G7 `color=srgb`), not just the depth gate.
- `composite_clear_some_color_is_opaque_fill` / interaction(CPU model) / `composite_color(viewport_premul=any, text=any, clear=Some((0x10,0x10,0x10,0xff)))` / assert the returned bytes are exactly `(0x10,0x10,0x10,0xff)` (opaque clear fill occludes the text, no blend) — pins the `clear=Some` color path.

## P2-T8 — Z-order total ordering across viewports (cross-token tiebreak)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-render/src/tgp/composite.rs` add `fn composite_order(viewports: &Map<ViewportId, Viewport>, token_order: &TokenOrder) -> Vec<ViewportId>` producing a **total, deterministic** order: by `z` ascending (higher z on top), ties broken by `(z, owning-token order, ViewportId)`. Inline and pinned share one z-space. Order is recomputed every frame and never depends on map iteration. Expose `Term::tgp_composite_order() -> Vec<ViewportId>` for tests.

**Design refs.** §10.4 (Z-order, total & stable, shared inline/pinned z-space); G1 (token order).

**Depends on.** P2-T1, P2-T7, P1 (handshake token order — `composite_order` consumes the handshake `TokenOrder` artifact for the cross-token tiebreak; `zorder_tiebreak_by_token_then_id` reads it).

**Tests.**
- `zorder_by_z_ascending` / ingress / create `vp=1;z=5`, `vp=2;z=1`, `vp=3;z=3` (one token) / assert `tgp_composite_order() == [vid(2), vid(3), vid(1)]` (low z first / bottom).
- `zorder_tiebreak_by_token_then_id` / ingress / token AP1 creates `vp=1;z=0`; token AP2 creates `vp=1;z=0` (same raw id, same z, AP1 handshaked first) / assert `tgp_composite_order()` places `vid("AP1",1)` before `vid("AP2",1)` (token-order tiebreak on the composite `ViewportId`), then ViewportId — note the two are **distinct, coexisting** map entries under the private per-token id map (G11: a raw-id collision between tokens is never an error — the entries coexist and are merely ordered, exactly as P2-T1 `vp_same_raw_id_two_tokens_coexist` asserts), which is what makes this tiebreak observable.
- `zorder_inline_and_pinned_share_space` / ingress / inline `vp=1;z=2`, pinned `vp=2;z=1` / assert pinned `vid(2)` composites below inline `vid(1)` (pinned does not implicitly sit on top).

## P2-T9 — Terminal-driven reflow: inline rect recomputed from landed cells

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` hook the terminal's resize/rewrap path so that on `SIGWINCH`, font-size change, or rewrap that moves/splits the anchor line, each inline viewport's cell rect is recomputed from the **current landed placeholder cells** (cells authoritative; `{line,col}` hint not re-read). The app does not re-issue placements. Set **only** the per-viewport dirty flag (a re-composite, not a re-render, unless content/camera/size also changed — §6.5). If rewrap splits placeholder cells across a wrap boundary, the offscreen layer is rendered once at the logical `cols × rows` size and composited into the smallest axis-aligned grid rect bounding all live placeholder cells. Add `Viewport::bounding_grid_rect(grid) -> Option<CellRect>`.

**Design refs.** §10.3 (terminal-driven reflow, split handling); §6.5 (re-composite vs re-render); G9.

**Depends on.** P2-T2, P2-T6.

**Tests.**
- `reflow_follows_cells_on_resize` / lifecycle / create inline `vp=1;line=0;col=2;cols=4;rows=1`; trigger a resize-driven rewrap via the named seam `Term::set_grid_size(cols, rows)` (the SIGWINCH/resize entry point, P2-T2) narrowing the grid so the cells rewrap and land at row 2 col 0 / assert `live_cell_rect() == CellRect{col:0,row:2,cols:4,rows:1}`.
- `reflow_sets_only_viewport_dirty` / lifecycle / record `scene_revision`, then `Term::set_grid_size(..)` to reposition the cells (no content change) / assert per-viewport dirty flag set AND `scene_revision` unchanged (recomposite, not re-render; G9 no spam).
- `reflow_split_bounding_rect` / lifecycle / create inline `cols=6;rows=1` then `Term::set_grid_size(..)` to a width where 4 cells stay on one row and 2 wrap to the next / assert `bounding_grid_rect()` spans both rows and the offscreen target size still reflects logical `6×1`.

## P2-T10 — Scroll-region (DECSTBM) scroll + clip_to_scroll_region

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` make a DECSTBM-bounded scroll move an inline viewport's placeholder cells exactly as it moves text, recompute the viewport cell rect from the new positions, and set **only** the per-viewport dirty flag (cached layer re-composited at the new rect without re-render). Honor `clip_to_scroll_region` (default `true`): the cached layer is clipped to the intersection of the active DECSTBM region and terminal bounds; `false` clips only to terminal bounds. Inline viewports are permitted inside DECSTBM regions and are never silently downgraded to pinned. Add `Viewport::composite_clip_rect(grid, decstbm, surface) -> PxRect`.

**Design refs.** §10.3 (scroll-region scrolling, straddle/clip); §10.4 (clipping).

**Depends on.** P2-T9.

**Tests.**
- `decstbm_scroll_moves_inline_rect` / lifecycle / feed `ESC[1;10r` (set DECSTBM region rows 0..10), create inline `vp=1;line=5;cols=4;rows=2`, then drive a one-row region scroll-up via the named seam (a line feed at the region bottom, or `Term::test_scroll_grid(1)` constrained to the active region) / assert `live_cell_rect().row == 4` (moved with text) AND only per-viewport dirty flag set.
- `clip_to_scroll_region_true_clips_at_margin` / lifecycle / DECSTBM rows 2..8, inline straddling the bottom margin, `clip_to_scroll_region=true` / assert `composite_clip_rect` is clipped to the region's bottom margin (no bleed across).
- `clip_to_scroll_region_false_clips_to_bounds` / lifecycle / same setup with `clip_to_scroll_region=false` / assert `composite_clip_rect` extends past the DECSTBM margin to the terminal bounds.
- `inline_not_downgraded_in_region` / ingress / create inline inside a DECSTBM region / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).unwrap().anchor` is still `Anchor::Inline` (never silently pinned).

## P2-T11 — Detach-on-overwrite + auto-destroy when below required cell count

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` make writing a new, non-placeholder glyph into a cell that holds a viewport placeholder revert that cell to ordinary text and remove it from the viewport's live placeholder set (the app's text wins). Re-emitting the identical placeholder glyph is not an overwrite (consistent with P2-T2). If detaching drops the viewport below its required cell count, break the binding and auto-destroy the viewport by **calling the shared `destroy_viewport(vp, DestroyReason::Detached)` helper (P2-T36)** — this task does NOT re-implement the emit/teardown; it only detects the below-count condition and invokes the helper. The helper frees targets, drops subs, and (if the owner is subscribed) queues exactly one `tgp;e;…;ev=destroyed;reason=detached`; otherwise silent. No `tgp;x`.

**Design refs.** §10.2 (detach on overwrite, lifetime), §10.7; G10 (inline lifetime / teardown).

**Depends on.** P2-T2, P2-T36 (shared `destroy_viewport` helper, built first; brings P2-T6 targets + P2-T21 subs transitively). The silent teardown and the subscribed-event branch are both delivered by the helper, so this task is buildable as soon as T36 lands.

**Tests.**
- `overwrite_detaches_cell` / interaction(grid write) / create inline `cols=4;rows=1`, write a normal char (e.g. `'x'`) into one placeholder cell via the Perform path / assert `term.tgp_cell_placeholder(addr) == None` (reverted to plain text) AND `live_cell_rect().cols == 3`.
- `detach_below_count_destroys_silent` / lifecycle / create inline `cols=1;rows=1` (required count 1), overwrite the single cell, with no sub active / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)) == None` AND no `tgp;e` / no `tgp;x` queued (silent teardown via the helper).
- `detach_below_count_calls_helper_with_detached` / lifecycle / same but the owner holds a lifecycle sub on `vid(1)` / assert exactly one `tgp;e;tok=AP1;vp=1;ev=destroyed;reason=detached` in `pty_replies` (this trigger path invokes `destroy_viewport(vp, Detached)`; the canonical emit-behavior test is P2-T36 `destroyed_detached_reason`).
- `reemit_identical_glyph_no_detach` / interaction(grid write) / re-write the identical placeholder glyph (`U+10EEEE` carrying `vid(1)`) into a bound cell / assert no detach: `term.tgp_cell_placeholder(addr) == Some(vid(1))` and live rect unchanged.

## P2-T12 — Scrollback eviction destroys inline viewport (reason=evicted)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` key inline-viewport lifetime on placeholder-cell liveness in the bounded scrollback ring: when every placeholder cell has been evicted, destroy the viewport by **calling `destroy_viewport(vp, DestroyReason::Evicted)` (P2-T36)** — this task detects the all-cells-evicted condition and invokes the shared helper; it does NOT re-implement the emit/teardown. Destruction keys on cell liveness, never the stored `{line,col}`. The helper frees targets, drops subs, and (if the owner is subscribed) queues exactly one `tgp;e;…;ev=destroyed;reason=evicted`; otherwise silent. No `tgp;x` (normal teardown, not error). An inline viewport hidden by an alt-screen switch is NOT evicted while hidden (interacts with P2-T15).

**Design refs.** §10.2 (lifetime bounded by scrollback depth), §10.7; G10.

**Depends on.** P2-T2, P2-T36 (shared `destroy_viewport` helper, built first; brings P2-T6 targets + P2-T21 subs transitively).

**Tests.**
- `eviction_destroys_inline_silent` / lifecycle / scrollback cap small; create inline near top; push enough lines to evict all placeholder cells; no sub / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)) == None` AND target freed AND no reply queued (silent teardown via the helper).
- `eviction_calls_helper_with_evicted` / lifecycle / same with owner lifecycle sub / assert one `tgp;e;tok=AP1;vp=1;ev=destroyed;reason=evicted` (this trigger invokes `destroy_viewport(vp, Evicted)`; canonical emit test is P2-T36 `destroyed_evicted_routed_to_owner`).
- `partial_eviction_keeps_alive` / lifecycle / evict some-but-not-all placeholder cells / assert viewport alive AND `live_cell_rect()` recomputed over surviving cells.

## P2-T13 — ED/EL erase semantics for inline; pinned persists

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/inline.rs` make ED (CSI J) / EL (CSI K) erase underlying cells exactly like text for inline placeholder cells: erasing all placeholder cells of an inline viewport destroys it by **calling `destroy_viewport(vp, DestroyReason::Detached)` (P2-T36)** (erase is the same teardown path as overwrite/eviction; §10.7 maps erase-driven destruction onto `reason=detached`); a partial erase that leaves some placeholder cells leaves the viewport alive with a recomputed cell rect. `CSI 2J` erases on-screen placeholder cells → destroys those inline viewports via the helper. **Pinned** viewports are not cell-bound and persist across all ED/EL (incl. `2J`/`3J`/`2K`). `2J`/`3J` clear images per existing behavior but never free TGP scene nodes/assets and never remove pinned viewports.

**Design refs.** §10.7 (erase & reset).

**Depends on.** P2-T36 (shared `destroy_viewport` helper, built first), P2-T2 (placeholder cells).

**Tests.**
- `csi_2j_destroys_inline` / lifecycle / create inline `vp=1` on screen, feed `ESC[2J` / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)) == None` (placeholder cells erased → `destroy_viewport(vp, Detached)` invoked; if subscribed, a `reason=detached` `tgp;e` is queued — canonical emit test is P2-T36).
- `csi_2j_keeps_pinned` / lifecycle / create pinned `vp=2`, feed `ESC[2J` / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(2)).is_some()` (pinned persists).
- `partial_el_keeps_inline` / lifecycle / create inline `cols=4;rows=1`, feed `ESC[K` from a column that erases only the rightmost 2 placeholder cells / assert viewport alive AND `live_cell_rect().cols == 2`.
- `csi_2j_keeps_scene_nodes` / lifecycle / create a node + pinned vp, feed `ESC[2J` / assert `tgp_scene().nodes` still contains the node (2J never frees scene nodes/assets).

## P2-T14 — Late-bound camera/root + implicit auto-framing default camera

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` / `toastty-render/src/tgp/camera.rs` resolve a viewport's `camera`/`root` **late** (G2): `Viewport.camera = None` or naming a node that does not (yet) resolve to a `Camera` → render with an **implicit auto-framing default camera** that fits the root subtree's bounding sphere at a default FOV (mirrors implicit material id 0). A missing camera/root is never a viewport or txn error; rendering never stops. When the named `Camera` node later appears, the viewport silently adopts it. `node.remove` of a node used as a viewport camera/root succeeds and silently falls that viewport back to default. An optional diagnostic `tgp;x;code=bad_ref` (to the owning token) is queued on entering the missing state. Add `Viewport::effective_camera(scene) -> ResolvedCamera { Default(AutoFrame) | Bound(NodeId) }` and `Viewport::effective_root(scene) -> NodeSet` (whole scene when absent/unresolved).

**Design refs.** §10.5 (one scene many cameras; implicit default); G2 (late-bound vp camera/root never reject).

**Depends on.** P2-T1, P1 (scene model + node graph + bounding-sphere helper).

**Tests.**
- `vp_no_camera_uses_default` / ingress / create `vp=1` with no `camera` / assert `effective_camera() == ResolvedCamera::Default(..)` AND no `tgp;x` queued (not an error).
- `vp_camera_before_node_then_adopts` / ingress / create `vp=1;camera=cam` (cam does not exist yet), assert `Default`; then `node.upsert` cam as a Camera node / assert `effective_camera() == Bound(cam)` (silently adopted).
- `vp_camera_missing_optional_bad_ref` / ingress / create `vp=1;camera=ghost` / assert exactly one optional `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=bad_ref` queued AND rendering still resolves to `Default`.
- `node_remove_camera_falls_back` / ingress / create cam node + `vp=1;camera=cam`, then `node.remove cam` / assert remove succeeds AND `effective_camera() == Default`.
- `vp_no_root_uses_whole_scene` / ingress / two root nodes A,B, `vp=1` with no `root` / assert `effective_root()` contains both A and B.

## P2-T15 — Screen-buffer affinity (alt-screen gating, no teardown)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/mod.rs` add `ScreenAffinity { Primary, Alt, Both }` handling. Default a viewport's affinity to the buffer active at create time; inline viewports are implicitly bound to their create-time buffer. On alt-screen enter (DECSET 1049/1047/47) **hide** every viewport whose affinity excludes the now-active buffer and **render** those it includes; on exit restore the prior set exactly. Hiding is render-gating only — no GPU teardown, all state retained — so an inline viewport hidden by an alt-screen switch is NOT scrollback-evicted while hidden (interacts with P2-T12). Affinity gating is per owning token; scene nodes/assets are buffer-independent. Add `Term::tgp_viewport_visible(id) -> bool`.

**Design refs.** §10.6 (screen-buffer affinity); G10 (alt-screen).

**Depends on.** P2-T1, P2-T6, P2-T12.

**Tests.**
- `alt_screen_hides_primary_viewport` / lifecycle / create pinned `vp=1` on primary (default affinity Primary), feed `ESC[?1049h` / assert `tgp_viewport_visible(&vid(1)) == false` AND `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).is_some()` (state retained, no teardown).
- `alt_screen_both_persists` / lifecycle / create pinned `vp=2;screen_affinity=both`, enter alt screen / assert `tgp_viewport_visible(&vid(2)) == true`.
- `alt_screen_exit_restores` / lifecycle / hide primary vp on alt enter, then `ESC[?1049l` / assert `tgp_viewport_visible(&vid(1)) == true` again.
- `hidden_inline_not_evicted` / lifecycle / create inline on primary, enter alt screen (vp hidden), push lines that would evict its rows / assert `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).is_some()` (hidden inline not evicted while hidden).

## P2-T16 — Instanced node kind + `feat=instance` use-time gate (REUSES P1-T12 decode)

**DEDUP note (authoritative for this phase).** The canonical `node.instances` **byte decode + buffer validation** — `InstanceBuffer { count, xforms, tints }`, the `decode_instances(count, xforms, tints) -> Result<InstanceBuffer, TgpError>` decoder (`count*64`-byte column-major mat4 LE `xforms`, optional `count*4`-byte RGBA8 sRGB `tints`, zero re-pack), the `count=0` legal-empty rule, and all the length/NaN/cap hardening — is **owned by P1-T12** (P1's `node.instances` decoder/validator). **P2 does NOT introduce a second `instance.rs` decoder.** P2-T16/T17 are **thin consumers + use-site policy** over the P1-T12 buffer: T16 wires the Instanced **node kind** into the scene and adds the P2-only **`feat=instance` use-time gate**; T17 asserts the P1-T12 validation contract from the P2 apply path + the atomic last-committed-buffer retention; T18 (re-tint) and T19 (GPU render) are the genuinely P2-owned write/render paths. All decode/validate **tests below reference P1-T12 as the decode owner** and assert through the P2 `Term::tgp_node_instances` accessor (the P1-decoded buffer surfaced to the P2 scene), not a P2 re-implementation.

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` wire `node.instances` (decoded by **P1-T12** into the P1 `InstanceBuffer`) into `NodeKind::Instanced { asset, material, instances }` (P1 created the Mesh path; Instanced kind is added here). **Use-time feat gate (G7 — the one P2-owned policy on this op):** if the owning token did not negotiate the `instance` feat at handshake (P1), a `node.instances` / Instanced-kind upsert is rejected with `tgp;x;code=unsupported;detail=instance` to the owning token and no Instanced node/buffer is created (the P1-T12 decode is bypassed at the gate — nothing is materialized; this is the one place this phase materially gates a `node.*` op on a feat). **G4 correlation for node-op errors.** `node.instances`, `node.instance_set`, and Instanced `node.upsert` are **patch `p` ops** carrying a `txn` (text header) and an `op` index; per G4 every rejection reply for these ops echoes the full correlator `tgp;x;tok=<tok>;txn=<n>;op=<i>;code=…;detail=…` (so an app can correlate the error to the offending txn + op). This applies to the `unsupported` gate here and to every node-op error surfaced by P2-T17/T18. Add the P2 scene accessor `Term::tgp_node_instances(node) -> Option<&InstanceBuffer>` (surfaces the P1-decoded buffer committed to the node). The `count=0` legal-empty buffer is a **P1-T12 decode property** that P2 relies on for the render path (the renderer P2-T19 issues no degenerate draw, range `0..0`); the consuming assertion lives below, the decode rule itself is P1-T12's.

**Design refs.** §6.4 (canonical instance layout v1, mat4-only, column-major LE — **decoded by P1-T12**); G4 (patch `p` errors cite `txn` + `op` index + `tok`); G6 (instance decode CPU-side & queryable — owned by P1-T12, consumed here); G7 (`feat=instance⇒graph`, closed-enum `unsupported`).

**Depends on.** **P1-T12 (canonical `node.instances` decode + buffer validation + `InstanceBuffer` type — the decode owner; P2 reuses, does not re-implement)**, P1 (tgp scene model + node decoder + CBOR), P1 (asset/material tables, feat negotiation).

**Tests.** (The decode-value tests below exercise the **P1-T12 decoder** as surfaced through the P2 `tgp_node_instances` accessor + the P2 Instanced-kind wiring; the feat gate is the only P2-owned policy under test.)
- `instances_decode_surfaced_to_node` / ingress / feed a `node.instances` with `count=2`, `xforms` = two column-major identity mat4 LE (128 bytes) (decoded by P1-T12) / assert the P2 scene surfaces it: `tgp_node_instances(&n("pts")).unwrap().count == 2` AND `xforms[0]` ≈ identity AND `xforms[1]` ≈ identity (thin consumer of the P1-decoded buffer; the byte-level column-major/LE decode is P1-T12's `instances_decode_*` tests, not duplicated here).
- `instances_translation_value_surfaced` / ingress / one instance whose mat4 has translation (1.0, 2.0, 3.0) in column-major slots 12,13,14 / assert the surfaced `tgp_node_instances(&n("pts")).unwrap().xforms[0].w_axis == (1.0,2.0,3.0,1.0)` (column-major mapping is P1-T12's; this asserts P2 surfaces it unaltered).
- `instances_tints_surfaced` / ingress / `count=1`, `tints` = `[0xFF,0x00,0x00,0xFF]` / assert the surfaced buffer `tints == Some(vec![[255,0,0,255]])` (P1-T12 decode, P2 surface).
- `instances_tints_absent_surfaced_none` / ingress / `count=2`, no `tints` / assert surfaced `tints == None` (renderer defaults to opaque white per §6.4; P1-T12 decode rule, P2 surface).
- `instances_tint_srgb_to_linear` / ingress / `count=1`, `tints=[0xBA,0x00,0x00,0xFF]` (a mid sRGB red, ≈ 0.729 sRGB) / assert the **P2 render color-chain helper** `decode_tint_linear([u8;4]) -> [f32;4]` (added in `toastty-render` / the P2 render path, NOT a decoder — it consumes the P1-decoded RGBA8 record) yields `tint_linear` ≈ the sRGB-decode of `0xBA/255` (≈ 0.494 linear) within epsilon and that `final_linear = baseColor_linear * tint_linear` applies the multiply (not an emissive add / baseColor replace), per §6.4 color chain (G7 `color=srgb`). GPU-free seam. Alpha is multiplied straight through (no sRGB curve on alpha). (This is the render-consumption color chain, distinct from P1-T12's byte decode.)
- `instanced_node_kind_set` / ingress / `node.upsert` an Instanced node + `node.instances` / assert `tgp_scene().node(&n("pts")).kind` is `NodeKind::Instanced` (P2-owned scene-kind wiring).
- `instances_count_zero_empty_surfaced` / ingress / `node.instances` with `count=0`, zero-length `xforms`, no `tints` (the P1-T12 legal-empty decode) / assert accepted (no `tgp;x` queued) AND the surfaced `tgp_node_instances(&n("pts")).unwrap().count == 0` AND `xforms.is_empty()` AND `tints == None` (P2 surfaces the legal-empty buffer; the renderer's `tgp_instance_draw_range` is `Some(0..0)` per P2-T19 — see `instance_draw_range_empty_for_zero_count`). The legality is P1-T12's; this pins P2 surfaces it without error.
- `instances_without_feat_unsupported` / ingress / handshake a token that did NOT negotiate `feat=instance`, feed a patch `p;txn=7` carrying `node.instances`/Instanced upsert at op index 0 / assert `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;txn=7;op=0;code=unsupported;detail=instance` (full G4 correlator present) AND `tgp_node_instances(&n("pts")) == None` (no Instanced node created; the P1-T12 decode is bypassed at the gate; G7 use-time gate — the sole P2-owned policy under test here).

## P2-T17 — Instance-apply: atomic last-committed retention + Instanced-kind tint invariant (asserts P1-T12 validation)

**DEDUP note (authoritative for this phase).** The **buffer-validation logic itself** — `xforms.len() == count*64`, `tints.len() == count*4`, `count ≤ max_instances_per_buffer`, NaN/inf rejection, and the `bad_layout`/`cap_exceeded`/`parse_error` code emissions with their `detail=` discriminators — is **owned and unit-tested by P1-T12** (the canonical decoder/validator). **P2-T17 does NOT re-implement that validation or add a second validator.** P2-T17 owns only the **scene-apply consequences** that are P2-specific: (1) the **atomic last-committed-buffer retention** when the P1-T12 validator rejects a `node.instances` txn (the node keeps its prior committed buffer — no flicker, no partial upload), and (2) the **Instanced-kind tint invariant** (`instanced_node_tint`), which is a kind policy on the P2-wired Instanced kind. Its tests therefore **drive the P1-T12 validation through the P2 apply path** and assert (a) the correlated error reply and (b) the P2 retention/kind-invariant behavior — they cite P1-T12 as the validation owner.

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` (the `node.instances` apply path; **validation delegated to P1-T12's `decode_instances`**): when P1-T12 rejects a `node.instances` txn (`bad_layout;detail=xforms_len | tints_len | count_mismatch`, `cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances`, or `parse_error;detail=instance_nan`), the apply path rejects the txn **atomically** and the node **retains its last committed instance buffer** (no flicker, no partial upload) — this retention is the P2-owned scene-apply consequence; the codes/`detail=` originate from the P1-T12 validator. **P2-owned kind invariant:** setting a **node-level tint** on an Instanced node (via upsert or instances) → `tgp;x;code=kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (the Instanced kind is P2-wired in T16, so its no-node-level-tint invariant is enforced here). **Note (G12.4 — authoritative, supersedes the prior round's `bad_param` ruling):** an earlier revision emitted `bad_param` here, citing the base-spine G3 synonym map (`instanced_node_tint → bad_param`). The reconciliation **addendum G12.4** reverses that: it **strikes** the `instanced_node_tint → bad_param` synonym and rules the canonical code is `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (G12.3 row 11) — the trigger is a *kind-invariant* violation (an `Instanced` node may not carry a `Mesh`-style node-level tint), which is exactly what `kind_conflict` denotes, and the design slices (s06 §6.4 line 118, s08.6) are the live, more-specific normative text. The addendum supersedes the base spine on conflict, so the emitted closed-enum member is **`kind_conflict`** (NOT `bad_param`). `detail=instanced_node_tint` is the advisory/non-load-bearing discriminator per G3. `bad_param` no longer carries this trigger (it remains reachable on its own triggers — out-of-range op fields, node-id charset/length, G12.3 row 16 — owned by P1).

All node-op rejections in this task echo the full G4 correlator `tgp;x;tok=<tok>;txn=<n>;op=<i>;code=…;detail=…` (these are patch `p` ops; see P2-T16 deliverable).

**Design refs.** §6.4 (hardened validation §15.5 — **validation owned by P1-T12**; per-instance vs node-level tint; s06 line 118 `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`); G2 (over-cap retains last committed buffer — P2 apply consequence), G3 (`bad_layout`/`cap_exceeded`/`parse_error` from P1-T12; `kind_conflict` from the P2 kind invariant), **G12.4** (the `instanced_node_tint → bad_param` synonym is STRUCK; canonical code is `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`, G12.3 row 11 — addendum supersedes the base-spine synonym map), G4 (patch `p` errors cite `txn` + `op` index + `tok`).

**Depends on.** **P1-T12 (canonical instance decode + the `bad_layout`/`cap_exceeded`/`parse_error` validation this task asserts through the apply path — the validation owner; P2 does not re-implement)**, P2-T16 (Instanced kind wiring + `tgp_node_instances` accessor), P1 (caps providing `max_instances_per_buffer`).

**Tests.** (Each drives the **P1-T12 validator** through the P2 `node.instances` apply path and asserts the correlated error + the P2-owned atomic-retention / kind-invariant behavior; the byte-level reject decisions are P1-T12's own unit tests, not duplicated.)
- `instances_bad_layout_retains_prior_xforms_len` / ingress / first commit a valid `count=3` buffer, then feed `count=2` with `xforms` only 64 bytes in a patch `p;txn=11` at op index 0 (P1-T12 rejects `bad_layout;detail=xforms_len`) / assert `pty_replies` has `tgp;x;tok=AP1;txn=11;op=0;code=bad_layout;detail=xforms_len` (full G4 correlator: `tok`+`txn`+`op` present and correct) AND `tgp_node_instances().count == 3` (P2 atomic retention: node keeps its prior committed buffer).
- `instances_bad_layout_tints_len` / ingress / `count=2`, valid `xforms`, `tints` 4 bytes (need 8) (P1-T12 rejects) / assert `tgp;x;...;detail=tints_len` AND prior buffer retained.
- `instances_count_mismatch_detail` / ingress / `count=0` but non-empty `xforms` (P1-T12 rejects — distinct from the legal-empty zero-length case) / assert `detail=count_mismatch` AND prior buffer retained.
- `instances_over_cap_rejected_retains_prior` / ingress / first commit `count=3` (valid), then `count=max_instances_per_buffer+1` (P1-T12 rejects `cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances`) / assert `tgp;x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances` AND `tgp_node_instances().count == 3` (P2 atomic retention: last committed buffer retained — the §15.1 backstop).
- `instances_nan_rejected_retains_prior` / ingress / first commit a valid buffer, then one mat4 with a NaN element (P1-T12 rejects `parse_error;detail=instance_nan`) / assert `tgp;x;code=parse_error;detail=instance_nan` AND buffer unchanged (P2 atomic retention).
- `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert` Instanced node with a node-level `tint` field in a patch `p;txn=12` at op index 0 / assert `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;txn=12;op=0;code=kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (G12.4 authoritative: canonical code is `kind_conflict`, NOT `bad_param` — the `instanced_node_tint → bad_param` synonym is struck; full G4 correlator present) AND the node retains its prior committed kind/buffer.

## P2-T18 — instance_set single-instance re-tint + instance_dirty (NOT asset_revision)

**Deliverable.** This is a genuinely **P2-owned write op** (per the phase DEDUP duty: P2 owns the `node.instance_set` re-tint and the GPU render path; P1-T12 owns the bulk `node.instances` decode/validation). In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` (operating **on the P1-T12-decoded `InstanceBuffer`** committed to the node — no re-decode of the bulk buffer) add `node.instance_set { id, index: u32, tint:[r,g,b,a] }` writing exactly one `tints` record into that buffer (materializing a default-white `tints` vec if absent) and setting the node's dedicated `instance_dirty` flag in `DirtySet` (orthogonal to the transform/visibility flag). `index ≥ count` → `tgp;x;code=bad_index`, buffer unchanged. Any `node.instances` / `node.instance_set` change bumps `scene_revision` + sets `instance_dirty`, and **never** bumps `asset_revision` (G2/G9 — the per-frame point-cloud path must not re-upload meshes). The renderer (P2-T20) clears `instance_dirty` after upload. Add `Term::tgp_node_instance_dirty(node) -> bool` and `Term::tgp_asset_revision()` / `Term::tgp_scene_revision()` accessors (the latter two may exist from P1; reuse).

`node.instance_set` is a patch `p` op; its rejection (`bad_index`) echoes the full G4 correlator `tgp;x;tok=<tok>;txn=<n>;op=<i>;code=bad_index` (see P2-T16 deliverable).

**Design refs.** §6.4 (single-instance re-tint — the §12.6 click-highlight path, genuinely P2-owned); §6.5 (instance_dirty dedicated flag, never asset_revision); G2, G4 (patch `p` errors cite `txn` + `op` index + `tok`), G9.

**Depends on.** P1-T12 (the `InstanceBuffer` type + the committed bulk buffer this re-tint writes into; P2 does not re-decode it), P2-T16 (Instanced kind + accessor), P2-T17 (apply path).

**Tests.**
- `instance_set_writes_one_record` / ingress / `count=3` instances (default white), `node.instance_set index=1 tint=[0,255,0,255]` / assert `tints[1] == [0,255,0,255]` AND `tints[0] == [255,255,255,255]` (others untouched).
- `instance_set_sets_instance_dirty` / ingress / `node.instance_set` / assert `tgp_node_instance_dirty(&n("pts")) == true`.
- `instances_bump_scene_not_asset_revision` / ingress / record `asset_revision`; feed `node.instances` (new buffer) / assert `scene_revision` increased AND `asset_revision` unchanged.
- `instance_set_bad_index` / ingress / `count=2`, `node.instance_set index=2` sent in a patch `p;txn=20` at op index 0 / assert `tgp;x;tok=AP1;txn=20;op=0;code=bad_index` (full G4 correlator: `tok`+`txn`+`op` present and correct) AND `tints` unchanged.
- `instances_reupload_does_not_touch_asset_revision` / ingress / re-send `node.instances` twice (simulating per-frame point cloud) / assert `asset_revision` identical across both (no mesh re-upload).

## P2-T19 — GPU instancing render path (instance vertex buffer, draw 0..count)

**Deliverable.** This is the genuinely **P2-owned GPU instancing RENDER path** (per the phase DEDUP duty; the buffer it consumes is the P1-T12-decoded `InstanceBuffer`, NOT re-decoded here). In `toastty-render/src/tgp/pipeline.rs` add the instanced pipeline: upload the P1-T12-decoded `InstanceBuffer` to an instance **vertex buffer** with per-instance `@location` step-mode attributes (column-major mat4 columns + RGBA8 tint), matching the canonical layout with zero CPU re-pack, and issue `draw_indexed(indices, 0..count)`; the record index `0..count` is the `instance_index` used for picking (P2-T21). Upload is gated by `instance_dirty` (cleared after upload, G9); grow the buffer in place when `count` exceeds capacity (§6.5). A `count=0` buffer (P2-T16 legal-empty) yields draw range `0..0` and the renderer issues **no** `draw_indexed` for it (no degenerate draw). Keep a headless CPU mirror so tests can assert the upload decision and the per-instance attribute byte layout without a GPU: `Term::tgp_instance_upload_stride() -> usize`, a test hook `tgp_instance_upload_count(node) -> Option<u32>`, and `Term::tgp_instance_draw_range(node) -> Option<Range<u32>>` mirroring the issued `draw_indexed` instance range (`0..count`) so the draw-range assertion targets a concrete CPU seam, not the GPU.

**Design refs.** §6.4 (rendering: instance vertex buffer, draw 0..count), §6.5 (instance_dirty gate, grow in place); G9.

**Depends on.** P2-T18, P1-T12 (the decoded `InstanceBuffer` this render path uploads — P2 consumes, does not re-decode), P1 (render pipeline + vertex buffers).

**Tests.**
- `instance_upload_count_matches` / lifecycle / `node.instances count=5` / assert `tgp_instance_upload_count(&n("pts")) == Some(5)` AND `tgp_instance_draw_range(&n("pts")) == Some(0..5)` (draw range mirror).
- `instance_upload_clears_dirty` / lifecycle / set instances, run one render tick / assert `tgp_node_instance_dirty(&n("pts")) == false` after upload (G9 clear-after-consume).
- `instance_upload_stride_is_layout` / lifecycle / assert `tgp_instance_upload_stride() == 64 + 4` (mat4 + RGBA8 per-instance step) — locks zero-repack layout.
- `instance_buffer_grows_in_place` / lifecycle / `count=2` then `count=8` / assert upload count `8` and capacity grew (no asset re-upload — assert `asset_revision` unchanged).
- `instance_draw_range_empty_for_zero_count` / lifecycle / `node.instances count=0` (legal empty, P2-T16) / assert `tgp_instance_draw_range(&n("pts")) == Some(0..0)` AND no `draw_indexed` is issued for that node (degenerate draw suppressed).

## P2-T37 — GPU device-loss recovery: force re-upload of targets + instances ignoring dirty (G10)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-render/src/tgp/targets.rs` + `toastty-render/src/tgp/pipeline.rs` + `toastty-term/src/tgp/mod.rs` add the device-loss recovery path for the GPU state **this phase first allocates** — per-viewport offscreen color+depth targets (P2-T6) and instance vertex buffers (P2-T19). On a device-loss event (`Term::on_device_loss()` / the render backend's device-lost callback), rebuild the device and **force re-upload all targets and instance buffers ignoring the dirty gating** (`is_rendered` viewports get fresh targets even with the per-viewport dirty flag clear; instanced nodes get re-uploaded even with `instance_dirty == false`), without losing any CPU state (scene nodes, decoded `InstanceBuffer`s, viewport store, sub/explore state all retained — the CPU is the source of truth, G10). After rebuild the dirty flags return to their normal cleared state. Per G7, on device-loss/headless transition the terminal also pushes an **unsolicited `tgp;r`** (current caps/version range) to every handshaked token on the back-channel. Add `Term::tgp_device_generation() -> u64` (bumped on each rebuild) so tests can assert a rebuild happened.

**Design refs.** §10.4 (offscreen targets), §6.4/§6.5 (instance buffers); G10 (device-loss: rebuild + force re-upload ignoring dirty, never lose CPU state), G7 (unsolicited `tgp;r` push on device-loss).

**Depends on.** P2-T6 (targets), P2-T19 (instance upload), P1 (caps/handshake providing `tgp;r` + handshaked-token set).

**Tests.**
- `device_loss_realloc_targets_ignores_dirty` / lifecycle / create rendered pinned `vp=1` (target allocated), run a render tick so the per-viewport dirty flag is cleared, then call `Term::on_device_loss()` / assert `tgp_viewport_has_target(&vid(1)) == true` again AND `tgp_device_generation()` incremented (target re-allocated despite no dirty flag) AND `tgp_viewport(&vid(1)).is_some()` (CPU state retained).
- `device_loss_reuploads_instances_ignores_instance_dirty` / lifecycle / `node.instances count=4`, run a render tick so `tgp_node_instance_dirty(&n("pts")) == false`, then `Term::on_device_loss()` / assert `tgp_instance_upload_count(&n("pts")) == Some(4)` re-fired (re-upload happened) even though `instance_dirty` was clear pre-loss AND the decoded `InstanceBuffer` count is still 4 (CPU state retained).
- `device_loss_pushes_tgp_r` / lifecycle / one handshaked token AP1, call `Term::on_device_loss()` / assert `pty_replies` contains an unsolicited `tgp;r;tok=AP1;v=…;vmin=…` frame (G7 device-loss push to handshaked tokens).
- `device_loss_retains_cpu_scene_state` / lifecycle / create `vp=1` + instanced node + a `sub`, device-loss / assert `tgp_viewports().len()`, `tgp_node_instances(&n("pts"))`, and `tgp_sub(&vid(1))` all unchanged (only GPU state rebuilt).

## P2-T20 — CPU-resolvable picking core (ray-cast vs world AABBs + instance transforms)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/pick.rs` (new) add the **authoritative, always-present CPU pick path** (G6): `pick(viewport, scene, cursor_px) -> Option<PickHit { node: NodeId, inst: Option<u32>, world: Vec3, rev: u64 }>`. It builds a camera ray from the viewport's effective camera + cursor, ray-casts against each node's **world AABB** (computed from world matrices) and, for `Instanced` nodes, against per-instance transforms (the `instance_index` `0..count`), returns the nearest hit with the world-space hit point from the same CPU path, and stamps `rev` = the `scene_revision` it resolved against. Maintain a per-viewport **CPU last-pick buffer** refreshed on render/move; resolution is async against the most recent buffer (no GPU stall). The optional GPU color-ID pass (each pixel encoding node-id + instance-index) is an accelerator that MUST agree with the CPU result. **Parity-test seam (GPU-free, G6).** The §12.4 picking implementation names two CPU options — "ray-cast against node world AABBs / instance transforms, **or** a CPU-rasterized pick buffer". The parity test exploits both: `assert_pick_agrees(raycast, rasterized)` compares the **ray-cast** CPU pick against a **CPU-rasterized** pick buffer (node-id + instance-index per pixel, rendered on the CPU — the same encoding the optional GPU color-ID pass would produce, but produced without a GPU). Both inputs are CPU-side, so the parity test runs in the GPU-free unit harness and genuinely exercises the agreement contract; the always-present ray-cast CPU path is the authoritative one, independently covered by the other five P2-T20 tests. (True GPU-vs-CPU parity, if ever wanted, would be a separately-gated integration test requiring a headless adapter, explicitly exempt from the GPU-free unit contract — not this test.) Add `Term::tgp_pick_at(vp, x, y) -> Option<PickHit>`.

**Design refs.** §12.4 (picking implementation, CPU authoritative, "ray-cast … or a CPU-rasterized pick buffer", rev staleness); G6 (CPU-resolvable picking, no GPU — both parity inputs CPU-side).

**Depends on.** P2-T14 (effective camera/root), P2-T16 (instance transforms), P1 (world matrices).

**Tests.**
- `pick_hits_node_aabb` / interaction / one mesh node at origin, viewport camera looking down -Z, `tgp_pick_at(vp1, center)` / assert `Some(PickHit{ node: n("cube"), inst: None, .. })`.
- `pick_misses_empty` / interaction / pick at a corner with no geometry / assert `None`.
- `pick_instance_index` / interaction / Instanced node with 3 instances at distinct x positions; pick over the 2nd / assert `inst == Some(1)`.
- `pick_world_point` / interaction / pick a unit cube front face at z=0 / assert `world.z` ≈ the front-face z (from CPU depth), within epsilon.
- `pick_rev_stamp` / interaction / set `scene_revision` to 42 via a transform, refresh pick buffer, pick / assert `PickHit.rev == 42`.
- `pick_raycast_vs_cpu_rasterized_agree` / interaction / on the same scene/cursor, run the ray-cast CPU pick AND build a **CPU-rasterized** pick buffer (node-id + instance-index per pixel, no GPU), decode the cursor pixel / assert `assert_pick_agrees(raycast, rasterized)` holds (same `node` + `inst`) — exercises the §12.4 CPU↔CPU agreement contract entirely in the GPU-free unit harness (the GPU color-ID pass, when present, is held to the same contract but is not invoked here).

## P2-T21 — Subscriptions: `sub` verb decode, store, granularity, lifecycle

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/decoder.rs` add the `sub` verb decoder → `SubOp { vp: ViewportId, target: SubTarget { AllNodes | Ids(Vec<NodeId>) }, events: EvMask, raw: bool, both: bool }`. The `EvMask` decoder accepts the **whole closed `ev` set** (§12.5): `click|dblclick|down|up|hover|enter|leave|drag|wheel|camera|resize|destroyed`; an unrecognized `ev` token is **ignored** (forward-compat: apps MUST ignore unknown tokens per G7, and the terminal symmetrically ignores unknown `ev` tokens rather than erroring) — pin this so the decode is total. In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` (new) store subscriptions **owned by the namespace token, bound to a viewport id** (G1). Granularity: `nodes=*` is a viewport-wide filter matching any current/future node id; an explicit id list matches only those. A `sub` naming a non-existent id is accepted (resolves at event time). Lifecycle: viewport destroy drops all its subs (and frees pick target/buffer — interacts with P2-T20/T6); `node.remove` silently drops the removed id from explicit lists (no error/event); re-creating a removed id does NOT revive an explicit sub; `nodes=*` naturally covers re-created ids. All sub mutations and events route only to the owning token. Add `Term::tgp_sub(vp) -> Option<&Subscription>`.

**Design refs.** §12.5 (subscription granularity, closed `ev` set), §12.5.1 (subscription lifecycle); G1 + **G11** (private per-token id map: a `sub` under one token can never match another token's nodes — it is causally blind, not `denied`; `denied` RESERVED), G5 (handshake = opt-in), G7 (ignore-unknown tokens — symmetric for `ev` decode).

**Depends on.** P2-T1, P1 (handshake/token, node graph).

**Tests.**
- `sub_decode_and_store` / ingress / feed `ESC_ tgp;sub;tok=AP1;vp=1;nodes=*;ev=click,hover ESC\` / assert `tgp_sub(&vid(1)).unwrap().target == SubTarget::AllNodes` AND `events` contains click+hover.
- `sub_explicit_ids` / ingress / `sub vp=1;nodes=O1,O2;ev=click` / assert target is `Ids([O1,O2])`.
- `sub_nonexistent_id_accepted` / ingress / `sub vp=1;nodes=ghost;ev=click` (ghost absent) / assert stored, no `tgp;x`.
- `sub_ev_mask_full_decode` / ingress / feed `ESC_ tgp;sub;tok=AP1;vp=1;nodes=*;ev=click,dblclick,down,up,hover,enter,leave,drag,wheel,camera,resize,destroyed ESC\` / assert `tgp_sub(&vid(1)).unwrap().events` contains **every** member of the closed `ev` set (locks the `sub` decoder parses the whole `ev` enum into `EvMask`, not just click/hover). NOTE: a token may need `feat=pick`/`event` for the pointer-pick `ev` types (P2-T22 gate); this decode test assumes the feat was negotiated so the focus is the mask decode itself.
- `sub_ev_garbage_token_ignored` / ingress / `sub vp=1;nodes=*;ev=click,bogus_ev` / assert `events` contains `click` and the unknown `bogus_ev` token is silently ignored (no `invalid_sub`, the sub is stored) — pins the forward-compat ignore-unknown-`ev` rule (G7).
- `sub_node_remove_drops_from_list` / ingress / `sub vp=1;nodes=O1,O2`, then `node.remove O1` / assert sub list is now `[O2]`, no error/event.
- `sub_recreate_does_not_revive` / ingress / `sub vp=1;nodes=O1`, `node.remove O1`, `node.upsert O1` / assert O1 not in the explicit sub list (must re-subscribe).
- `sub_viewport_destroy_drops_subs` / lifecycle / `sub vp=1`, destroy vp1 (CSI 2J on its inline cells, which invokes the shared `destroy_viewport` helper, P2-T36) / assert `tgp_sub(&vid(1)) == None` AND its pick target freed (the sub-drop behavior is delivered by the P2-T36 helper reaching into this sub store).
- `sub_cross_token_matches_nothing` / ingress / AP1 creates vp1 + a node `O1` (in AP1's map) and subs `vp=1;nodes=O1;ev=click`; then handshake AP2 and feed `ESC_ tgp;sub;tok=AP2;vp=1;nodes=O1;ev=click ESC\` (AP2's raw `vp=1` resolves to `{AP2,1}` in AP2's OWN map; `O1` is an id absent from AP2's map) / assert under the private per-token id map (G11): AP2's sub is **accepted and stored** against `{AP2,1}` (a `sub` naming a non-existent viewport/node is accepted, resolves at event time — §12.5.1) AND it **matches nothing** (it can never resolve AP1's `O1` — AP2's resolver never consults AP1's map; no event ever fires for it) AND AP1's sub on `{AP1,1}` is unchanged AND **no** `tgp;x;code=denied` is queued (G11(a): cross-token addressing is unrepresentable; `denied` is RESERVED; a cross-token id ref is causally blind — it matches nothing, no existence oracle per G11(b), never an error). Pins that `sub` token isolation is coexistence-by-construction, not a `denied` reject.

## P2-T22 — Gesture arbitration & invalid_sub (raw mutual exclusion, wheel conflict)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` enforce capability arbitration at `sub` apply time: **raw is mutually exclusive with explore and events** — a `sub` requesting `raw` together with `explore` or `events` → `tgp;x;code=invalid_sub` **before any state change** (no subscription stored); raw on a viewport implicitly disables explore there. Enabling explore zoom AND subscribing `ev=wheel` on the same viewport → the **later** of the two is rejected with `tgp;x;code=invalid_sub` (mutually exclusive, §12.3.2). Store the advertised `click_deadzone_px` (default 3) for click-vs-drag. **Use-time feat gate (G7, `pick⇒event`).** A `sub` requesting any pointer-pick event (`ev` containing `click|dblclick|down|up|drag|hover|enter|leave|wheel`) from a token that did **not** negotiate the required feat at handshake (P1) is rejected with `tgp;x;code=unsupported;detail=pick` (the implied base feat is `event`; `detail=` names the requested `pick` feat) **before any state change** (nothing stored) — the `sub`-verb sibling of P2-T16's `node.instances` gate. Add `Term::tgp_sub_mode(vp) -> SubMode { Raw | ExploreEvents | None }`.

**Design refs.** §12.2.1 (gesture arbitration, raw mutual exclusion), §12.3.2 (wheel ownership conflict); G3 (`invalid_sub`, `unsupported`), G7 (`pick⇒event` implication; use-time `unsupported` at the `sub` use-site).

**Depends on.** P2-T21, P2-T28 (explore controller — the `sub_raw_plus_explore_rejected` and `wheel_plus_explore_zoom_conflict` arbitration tests can only enable explore on a viewport and then observe the `invalid_sub` arbitration once the explore controller exists; the explore-vs-events/wheel arbitration is only exercisable once P2-T28 lands. Acyclic: P2-T28 → {P2-T1, P2-T24, P2-T14}; P2-T28 does not depend on P2-T22, so adding P2-T22 → P2-T28 introduces no cycle).

**Tests.**
- `sub_raw_plus_events_rejected` / ingress / `sub vp=1;raw=1;ev=click` / assert `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=invalid_sub` AND `tgp_sub(&vid(1)) == None` (no state change).
- `sub_raw_plus_explore_rejected` / ingress / `sub vp=1;raw=1` on a viewport with explore enabled / assert `invalid_sub`, nothing stored.
- `sub_raw_alone_ok` / ingress / `sub vp=1;raw=1` / assert `tgp_sub_mode(&vid(1)) == SubMode::Raw`.
- `wheel_plus_explore_zoom_conflict` / ingress / enable explore zoom on vp1, then `sub vp=1;ev=wheel` / assert the later (`ev=wheel`) rejected with `invalid_sub`.
- `sub_pick_without_feat_unsupported` / ingress / handshake a token that did NOT negotiate `feat=pick`/`event`, feed `ESC_ tgp;sub;tok=AP1;vp=1;nodes=*;ev=click ESC\` / assert `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=unsupported;detail=pick` AND `tgp_sub(&vid(1)) == None` (no sub stored; G7 use-time gate at the `sub` use-site, mirroring P2-T16).

## P2-T23 — Event report wire format (`tgp;e`) emission + tagging

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` add the `tgp;e` report serializer emitting control frames on the back-channel (`queue_reply → pty_replies`), tagged with the owning `tok=` and `vp=` (G1, G4). `ev ∈ click | dblclick | down | up | hover | enter | leave | drag | wheel | camera | resize | destroyed`. Fields: `node=` (picked node id), `inst=` (instance index when applicable), `btn=` (button index, serialized for the pointer-button events `click | dblclick | down | up | drag`; omitted for `hover | enter | leave | wheel | camera | resize | destroyed`), `x,y` viewport-local normalized coords, `wx,wy,wz` world-space hit point (serialized for events that resolve a 3D hit point: `click | dblclick | down | up | hover | enter | drag`; `leave` cites only `node=`/`rev=` per §12.5; `wheel`/`camera`/`resize`/`destroyed` carry their own field sets), `rev=` the `scene_revision` the pick resolved against. The `wx/wy/wz` values are taken from the same `PickHit.world` the CPU pick (P2-T20) resolved, serialized verbatim. **Float serialization precision (pins "verbatim", interacts with P2-T31).** All `f32` fields in `tgp;e` frames — `x/y` normalized coords, `wx/wy/wz` world point, and (in P2-T31) the camera `t/r/s` — are serialized as the **shortest round-trippable decimal** (Rust `{}`/`core::fmt` for `f32`, i.e. the Ryū/Grisu shortest representation that parses back to the exact same `f32` bits). This guarantees `parse::<f32>(serialized) == original_f32` exactly (no precision loss), so "serialized verbatim" is a hard equality, not an approximation. Emission is **mode-gated** (G5): only for subscribed viewports/nodes, only while the owning token has a live sub AND the terminal is focused. Add `Term::tgp_last_reply()` / assert against `pty_replies` bytes.

**Design refs.** §12.5 (event report wire format); G4 (correlation `tok=`/`vp=`), G5, G1.

**Depends on.** P2-T20, P2-T21, P2-T24 (input injection seam for click).

**Tests.**
- `event_click_wire_format` / interaction / sub `vp=2;nodes=*;ev=click`, inject press+release with `btn=1` (no motion) over node O1 inst 5 / assert `pty_replies` contains `ESC_ tgp;e;tok=AP1;vp=2;ev=click;node=O1;inst=5;btn=1;x=…;y=…;wx=…;wy=…;wz=…;rev=42 ESC\` with `node=O1`, `inst=5`, `btn=1`, and `rev=42`.
- `event_world_point_in_frame` / interaction / sub `ev=click`, click a unit cube front face at world z≈0 in vp2 (same scene/cursor as P2-T20 `pick_world_point`) / parse the `wx=`/`wy=`/`wz=` floats back out of the emitted `tgp;e` bytes and assert they equal the `PickHit.world` P2-T20 resolves for that cursor **exactly** (bit-identical `f32` under the shortest-round-trippable format pinned in the deliverable — not within epsilon), locking that the serializer carries the world point verbatim with no precision loss.
- `event_btn_serialized` / interaction / sub `ev=click`, inject press+release with `btn=3` (right button) over O1 / assert the emitted frame carries `btn=3` (button index serialized for pointer-button events).
- `event_only_for_subscribed_ev` / interaction / sub `ev=hover` only, inject a click / assert NO `ev=click` frame queued (mode-gated to subscribed ev types).
- `event_tagged_with_token` / interaction / AP1 subs vp2; emit a click / assert the frame carries `tok=AP1`.
- `event_suppressed_when_unfocused` / interaction / sub vp2, set terminal unfocused, inject click / assert no `tgp;e` queued (focus gating, G5).
- `event_normalized_coords` / interaction / click at the exact center of vp2's rect / assert `x≈0.5;y≈0.5` in the frame.

## P2-T24 — Term input API: synthetic pointer/focus injection (interaction seam)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/input.rs` (new) add the **non-`Parser::advance`** input ingress (G6 second seam): `Term::input_pointer(PointerEvent { kind: Down|Up|Move|Wheel, btn: u8, px: (i32,i32), wheel: f32 })` and `Term::input_focus(focused: bool)`, routed to the interaction router. Pointer/focus events never enter the scene-mutation path. The router resolves entirely on the CPU (G6). This task delivers the injection API + plumbing into the router (hit-test in P2-T25, explore in P2-T26). Add `Term::tgp_hover(vp) -> Option<(NodeId, Option<u32>)>` accessor for assertions.

**Design refs.** §12.1 (input never via Parser::advance), §12 (interaction router); G6 (interaction seam, inject synthetic events).

**Depends on.** P2-T20, P2-T21.

**Tests.**
- `input_pointer_does_not_mutate_scene` / interaction / record `scene_revision`, call `input_pointer(Move ..)` over a node / assert `scene_revision` unchanged (input is not a scene mutation; only explore later may set dirty/TRS).
- `input_focus_toggle` / interaction / `input_focus(false)` then `input_focus(true)` / assert focus state accessor reflects each.
- `input_api_routes_to_router` / interaction / inject `Down` over a subscribed viewport / assert the router recorded a candidate hit-test for that viewport (probe accessor).

## P2-T25 — Cross-viewport input hit-testing (top-down by z, fall-through)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` add `fn hit_test(cursor_px) -> Option<(ViewportId, PickHit_or_empty)>`: walk candidate viewports **top-down by `z`** (highest first, using P2-T8 order), considering **only** viewports with an active `sub` (events or raw) whose **live placeholder-cell rect** (authoritative, §10.2) contains the cursor. For each candidate resolve the pick buffer at the cursor: a node hit → that viewport **owns** the event, walk stops; an empty pick on a **transparent** (`clear=None`) top candidate → fall through to the next lower subscribed viewport (click through a transparent layer lands on the object below); an empty pick on an **opaque** (`clear=Some`) candidate → it owns the event anyway (occludes the empty region). Non-subscribed viewports never participate (still composite). The owning viewport id is reported in `vp=`, tagged with that viewport's owning token. Add `Term::tgp_hit_test(x,y) -> Option<ViewportId>`.

**Design refs.** §12.1.1 (input hit-testing across viewports); G1 (token-tag the owning vp).

**Depends on.** P2-T8 (z order), P2-T20 (pick), P2-T21 (sub), P2-T24 (input).

**Tests.**
- `hittest_topmost_subscribed_owns` / interaction / two overlapping subscribed viewports vp1(z=1), vp2(z=2), both have geometry under cursor / assert `tgp_hit_test(cx,cy) == Some(vid(2))` (highest z owns).
- `hittest_falls_through_transparent_empty` / interaction / top vp2(z=2, clear=None) empty at cursor, lower vp1(z=1) has a node there / assert owner `== vid(1)` (fall-through to object below).
- `hittest_opaque_empty_consumes` / interaction / top vp2(z=2, clear=Some(opaque)) empty at cursor, lower vp1 has a node / assert owner `== vid(2)` (opaque occludes, consumes the empty region).
- `hittest_ignores_unsubscribed` / interaction / top vp2 unsubscribed with geometry, lower vp1 subscribed with geometry / assert owner `== vid(1)` (unsubscribed never participates).

## P2-T26 — Hover state machine (enter/leave/hover per viewport)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` hold per-viewport hover state as a single current target `(node, inst)` or `None`, recomputed **every render** from the refreshed pick buffer **and** on every pointer-move (whichever happens, so a stationary cursor tracks geometry moving under it via explore/animation/patch/reflow). On change: emit `leave` for the prior `(vp,node,inst)` **then** `enter` for the new; `hover` fires on pointer-move while a target is held. A held target that is removed, hidden (`visible=false`), or moves out from under the cursor for **any** reason (transform/animation/explore/reflow/scroll-driven rect motion/boundary crossing) synthesizes a `leave` citing the prior id (even if just removed); held target then becomes the new target or `None`. Hover is independent per viewport; crossing a boundary fires `leave` on the old vp then `enter` on the new. On `node.remove` of a hovered node, synthesize `leave` first (interacts with P2-T21).

**Design refs.** §12.5.2 (hover state machine); G2 (hover leave triggers — recomputed every render, leave on any reason).

**Depends on.** P2-T20, P2-T23, P2-T25.

**Tests.**
- `hover_enter_on_move_onto_node` / interaction / sub `ev=enter,hover,leave`, move pointer onto O1 / assert `enter` emitted with `node=O1` AND `tgp_hover(vp) == Some((O1, None))`.
- `hover_leave_then_enter_on_retarget` / interaction / hovering O1, move onto O2 / assert ordered `leave node=O1` THEN `enter node=O2` in `pty_replies`.
- `hover_leave_on_node_removed` / interaction / hovering O1, feed `node.remove O1` / assert synthesized `leave node=O1;rev=<new>` AND `tgp_hover(vp) == None`.
- `hover_leave_on_geometry_moves_away` / interaction / hovering O1 with a stationary cursor; apply a transform moving O1 out from under the cursor; trigger render recompute / assert `leave node=O1` emitted.
- `hover_per_viewport_boundary_crossing` / interaction / hovering O1 in vp1, move pointer into vp2 over O2 / assert `leave` against vp1/O1 then `enter` against vp2/O2.

## P2-T27 — Focus-out / pointer-loss gesture termination

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` on focus-out or pointer-leave while a gesture is active in a subscribed or exploring viewport: **end** the active gesture. Explore stops dragging immediately and lets damping decay to rest on the injectable clock (no infinite spin — interacts with P2-T28). For any viewport with an active sub, synthesize `ev=up` (for the in-flight button, if `down`/`up`/`drag` subscribed) **then** `ev=leave` for the held hover target, so app-side drag/highlight state can close. A subsequent focus-in/pointer-enter does **not** auto-resume the drag; it only re-derives hover from the current pointer (firing `enter` if over a target). Synthesized `up`/`leave` are token-routed.

**Design refs.** §12.5.3 (focus and pointer loss); G10 (in-flight gesture survives, damping decays; only device loss cancels).

**Depends on.** P2-T26, P2-T28 (explore damping).

**Tests.**
- `focus_out_synthesizes_up_then_leave` / interaction / sub `ev=down,up,leave`, press (down) over O1 (holding), then `input_focus(false)` / assert ordered `ev=up` THEN `ev=leave node=O1` in `pty_replies`.
- `focus_out_ends_explore_drag_damps` / interaction / start an explore orbit drag, `input_focus(false)`, advance the clock / assert angular velocity decays to ~0 (no infinite spin) — camera TRS converges.
- `focus_in_no_auto_resume` / interaction / after focus-out drag end, `input_focus(true)` without re-pressing / assert no drag resumes (camera TRS static) AND `enter` fires only if cursor is over a target.

## P2-T28 — Explore controller: orbit/zoom/pan mutate the camera node on the injectable clock

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/explore.rs` (new) add the opt-in explore controller. `ExploreOpts { orbit, zoom, pan, lock_axes: AxisMask, zoom_min, zoom_max, auto_spin: Option<{axis,speed}>, damping, initial: Option<CameraPose> }` decoded from the `vp` frame's `explore` sub-map (the `VpOp.explore: Option<ExploreOpts>` field P2-T1 defines, §10.1 s10 line 17). **P2-T28 extends P2-T1's `decode_vp_frame`/`apply_tgp_vp`:** `decode_vp_frame` delegates the `explore` sub-map to this module's `ExploreOpts` decoder, and `apply_tgp_vp` routes the decoded `explore` through the feat gate below **before** storing the controller (the rest of the `vp` upsert — anchor/cells/camera/etc. — applies via P2-T1's normal path regardless). **Use-time feat gate (G7, `explore⇒event`).** A `vp` (or `sub`) frame carrying an `explore` field from a token that did **not** negotiate `feat=explore` at handshake (P1) is rejected with `tgp;x;code=unsupported;detail=explore` **before any explore state is stored** (the viewport's non-explore fields still apply per the normal `vp` upsert, but no `ExploreOpts`/controller is created) — the `vp;explore` use-site sibling of P2-T16's `node.instances` gate. Explore **moves the camera node, never the model's nodes** (principle 1) — it mutates the viewport's resolved `Camera` node TRS, setting that node's per-node dirty flag (G9, not global scene_revision per frame). Drag→orbit/pan per `ExploreOpts`, wheel→zoom clamped to `[zoom_min, zoom_max]`, `lock_axes` constrains rotation. Damping, auto-spin, and report throttling advance on the **single injectable monotonic clock** (G6); tests step it explicitly. Damping decays angular velocity to rest deterministically (never unbounded). Add `Term::tgp_camera_trs(node) -> Trs` and `Term::tgp_explore_velocity(vp) -> f32`.

**Design refs.** §12.3 (explore controller, moves camera node, damping/clock); G6 (deterministic clock), G9 (per-node dirty, no per-frame scene_revision), G7 (`explore⇒event` implication; use-time `unsupported` at the `vp;explore` use-site).

**Depends on.** P2-T1 (the `vp` decode/apply path + the `VpOp.explore` field defined in P2-T1; P2-T28 extends `decode_vp_frame`/`apply_tgp_vp` to parse the `explore` sub-map and route it through the feat gate before storing the controller — acyclic: P2-T1 has no path back to P2-T28), P2-T24 (input), P2-T14 (resolved camera), P1 (deterministic clock + camera node TRS).

**Tests.**
- `explore_orbit_rotates_camera_node` / interaction / explore orbit enabled, inject drag (down, move +dx, up) over vp1 / assert camera node rotation quat changed AND the **model** node TRS unchanged (only the camera moved).
- `explore_zoom_clamped` / interaction / `zoom_min=2,zoom_max=10`, wheel-zoom past the limit / assert camera distance clamped to 10 (or 2).
- `explore_lock_axes_turntable` / interaction / `lock_axes=Y`, orbit drag with vertical motion / assert only Y-rotation changes (X/Z locked).
- `explore_pan_translates_camera_node` / interaction / `pan=true, orbit=false`, inject a drag (down, move +dx,+dy, up) over vp1 / assert the camera node **translation** changed AND its **rotation quat unchanged** (pan moves the camera node, distinct from orbit's rotation path) AND the model node TRS unchanged.
- `explore_damping_decays_to_rest` / interaction / flick an orbit (drag-release with velocity), advance the injectable clock in steps / assert `tgp_explore_velocity(vp1)` monotonically decreases to ~0 (deterministic, bounded).
- `explore_auto_spin` / interaction / `auto_spin={axis:Y,speed:1.0}`, advance clock by 1 unit / assert camera Y-rotation advanced by ~speed*dt.
- `explore_sets_node_dirty_not_scene_revision` / interaction / record `scene_revision`, run one explore tick / assert camera node per-node dirty flag set AND global `scene_revision` unchanged (G9).
- `explore_initial_pose_seeds_camera` / interaction / enable explore with `initial={t:(1,2,3), r:<quat>, s:(1,1,1)}` on a vp whose camera is materialized (interacts with P2-T29) / assert `tgp_camera_trs(owned_cam)` equals the provided `initial` pose **before** any gesture is injected (the decoded `ExploreOpts.initial` seeds the resolved/materialized camera).
- `explore_without_feat_unsupported` / ingress / handshake a token that did NOT negotiate `feat=explore`, feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=4;rows=2;explore=… ESC\` (an `explore` sub-map enabling orbit) / assert `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=1;code=unsupported;detail=explore` AND no explore controller stored (`tgp_explore_velocity(&vid(1))` reflects no controller / `tgp_camera_controller(..)` is not `Explore`); the viewport's non-explore fields still applied (G7 use-time gate at the `vp;explore` use-site, mirroring P2-T16).

## P2-T29 — Explore needs a concrete camera: materialize terminal-owned camera node

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/explore.rs` when `explore` is enabled on a viewport with **no resolvable camera**, materialize a **terminal-owned** `Camera` node (auto-framing initial pose, addressable only by the owning token for sync-back) bound to that viewport so the gesture has persistent state to write. Without explore, the default camera stays per-frame scratch (no node materialized). Add `Term::tgp_viewport_owned_camera(vp) -> Option<NodeId>`.

**Design refs.** §10.5 (explore needs a concrete camera), §12.3 (materialize terminal-owned camera).

**Depends on.** P2-T28, P2-T14.

**Tests.**
- `explore_materializes_camera_when_none` / interaction / create vp1 with no camera, enable explore / assert `tgp_viewport_owned_camera(&vid(1)).is_some()` AND it is a `Camera` node owned by AP1.
- `no_explore_no_materialized_camera` / ingress / create vp1 with no camera, no explore / assert `tgp_viewport_owned_camera(&vid(1)) == None` (default cam stays per-frame scratch).
- `explore_uses_existing_camera_no_materialize` / interaction / vp1 already bound to an app `Camera` node, enable explore / assert no new owned camera materialized (`tgp_viewport_owned_camera` is the app's node).

## P2-T30 — One controller per camera: camera_busy conflict (explore ↔ anim)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/explore.rs` enforce "one controller per camera": a viewport's camera is driven by at most one terminal-side controller. Enabling `explore` on a viewport **blocks terminal-side animation playback of that camera node** — a conflicting `anim;play` on it is rejected with `tgp;x;code=camera_busy`, and vice-versa (enabling explore while anim plays the camera is rejected). This phase implements the explore-side of the rule and the rejection emit; the anim subsystem (P4) consumes the same lock. Add `Term::tgp_camera_controller(node) -> CameraController { None | Explore(vp) | Anim(clip) }`.

**Design refs.** §12.3 (one controller per camera); G2 (camera owned by one controller, `camera_busy`).

**Depends on.** P2-T28.

**Tests.**
- `explore_blocks_anim_play_camera_busy` / interaction / enable explore on vp1's camera, then feed `ESC_ tgp;anim;play;node=cam ESC\` / assert `tgp;x;tok=AP1;code=camera_busy` AND `tgp_camera_controller(cam) == Explore(vp1)`.
- `anim_playing_camera_blocks_explore` / interaction / mark cam as Anim-controlled, then enable explore on vp1 / assert explore enable rejected with `camera_busy` AND controller stays `Anim`.

## P2-T31 — Camera sync-back (`ev=camera`) on settle + throttled, round-trip-identical

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/explore.rs` add camera sync-back: if the app subscribed `ev=camera` for a viewport, emit `tgp;e;...;ev=camera;node=<cam>;t=…;r=…;s=…` carrying the camera node's resolved **local TRS** in the exact schema the app would send in `node.upsert` (so the app can write it back round-trip-identically). The `t/r/s` floats use the **same shortest-round-trippable `f32` serialization pinned in P2-T23** (parses back to bit-identical `f32`), which is what makes the round-trip byte-identical rather than merely close. Cadence is **never per render frame**: emit on **gesture settle** (drag-end, or explore coming to rest after damping) and, while a continuous gesture is in progress, at a throttled steady-state cap (advertised `camera_report_hz`, default 30) measured against the **injectable clock**. Consecutive in-flight camera reports for one viewport coalesce to the latest (interacts with P2-T34). Emitted only if the app subscribed `ev=camera`.

**Design refs.** §12.3.1 (camera sync-back); G4, G6 (injectable clock throttle).

**Depends on.** P2-T28, P2-T23, P1 (caps: `camera_report_hz` — the throttle steady-state cap `camera_syncback_throttled_by_hz` reads from P1's caps table).

**Tests.**
- `camera_syncback_on_drag_end` / interaction / sub `ev=camera`, orbit-drag then release (settle) / assert exactly one `tgp;e;...;ev=camera;node=cam;t=...;r=...;s=...` emitted on settle, with `r=` equal to the camera node's current quat.
- `camera_syncback_roundtrip_identical` / interaction / capture the emitted `t/r/s`, feed them back as a `node.upsert cam` / assert the camera node TRS is byte-identical to pre-emit (round-trip).
- `camera_syncback_throttled_by_hz` / interaction / `camera_report_hz=30`, continuous drag with the clock advanced by < 1/30s between moves / assert at most one in-flight `ev=camera` queued in that window.
- `camera_syncback_not_subscribed_silent` / interaction / no `ev=camera` sub, orbit drag / assert no `ev=camera` frame queued.

## P2-T32 — Click-vs-drag arbitration + wheel ownership + raw/drag forwarding

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` implement gesture partitioning when explore and events coexist (§12.2.1): a **press→release with no intervening motion** within `click_deadzone_px` is a **click** (plus ordered `down` then `up` if those ev types are subscribed; `down` fires on press, `up` on release, `click` on the completed cycle); **any** motion between press and release is an **explore gesture** (and does NOT also emit a click); `hover`/`enter`/`leave` always fire regardless of explore; a drag is forwarded as `ev=drag` only when explore does not own it. **Double-click arbitration:** two click cycles on the same `(vp,node,inst)` whose second press lands within the double-click threshold of the first release — measured on the **single injectable monotonic clock** (G6) — emit one `ev=dblclick` (subscribers to `dblclick` get it; the constituent second `click` still emits unless the app subscribed only `dblclick`; v1 emits both `click` and `dblclick` for the second cycle so single-click subscribers are unaffected). The threshold is a fixed terminal-side constant (default 400 ms expressed in clock units; not separately advertised in v1 caps — §12.5 names `dblclick` but adds no cap) measured on the injectable clock; tests advance the clock explicitly. The second press must also fall within `click_deadzone_px` of the first to count as a double-click. Wheel ownership (§12.3.2) in fixed order: (1) explore zoom enabled → explore consumes wheel, no `ev=wheel`, surrounding text does not scroll; (2) else `sub ev=wheel` → emit `tgp;e;ev=wheel`, text does not scroll; (3) else wheel passes through to surrounding text/pager scroll. When **raw** is active, it consumes the entire pointer stream in the cell rect and suppresses both explore and events there (forwards raw pointer events to the app). Add `Term::tgp_wheel_owner(vp) -> WheelOwner { Explore | Event | PassThrough }`.

**Design refs.** §12.2.1 (click vs drag, drag forwarding), §12.3.2 (wheel ownership), §12.2 (raw forwarding), §12.5 (`dblclick`/`down`/`up` ev set); G6 (injectable clock for dblclick threshold).

**Depends on.** P2-T22, P2-T26, P2-T28, P1 (caps: `click_deadzone_px` — the click-vs-drag and double-click spatial-gate tests read the advertised `click_deadzone_px` default 3 from P1's caps table).

**Tests.**
- `click_no_motion_emits_click` / interaction / explore+events on vp1, press then release at the same px (within deadzone) over O1 / assert `ev=click node=O1` emitted AND camera TRS unchanged (no explore).
- `down_up_emitted_on_press_release` / interaction / sub `ev=down,up,click` on vp1, inject `input_pointer(Down,btn=1)` then `input_pointer(Up,btn=1)` within deadzone over O1 / assert ordered `ev=down;node=O1;btn=1` THEN `ev=up;node=O1;btn=1` THEN `ev=click;node=O1` in `pty_replies` (real press/release path, distinct from the synthetic focus-out path P2-T27).
- `dblclick_within_threshold_emits` / interaction / sub `ev=click,dblclick` on vp1, inject click cycle, advance the injectable clock by < threshold, inject second click cycle on the same O1 / assert exactly one `ev=dblclick;node=O1` queued for the second cycle.
- `two_clicks_past_threshold_no_dblclick` / interaction / sub `ev=click,dblclick`, two click cycles with the clock advanced **>** threshold between them / assert NO `ev=dblclick` queued (two independent `ev=click` only).
- `two_clicks_outside_deadzone_no_dblclick` / interaction / sub `ev=click,dblclick`, inject two click cycles **within** the time threshold (clock advanced < threshold) but with the second press **>** `click_deadzone_px` from the first press / assert NO `ev=dblclick` queued (only two independent `ev=click`) — locks the spatial gate (§12.5/§12.2.1), the axis the time-only tests do not cover.
