- `motion_is_explore_not_click` / interaction / press, move > deadzone, release / assert NO `ev=click` emitted AND camera TRS changed (explore owned it).
- `drag_forwarded_when_explore_disabled` / interaction / events sub with `ev=drag`, explore orbit/pan disabled, press+move+release / assert `ev=drag` emitted.
- `wheel_explore_zoom_owns` / interaction / explore zoom on vp1, inject wheel / assert `tgp_wheel_owner(&vid(1)) == Explore`, NO `ev=wheel`, surrounding text scroll suppressed.
- `wheel_event_sub_owns` / interaction / explore zoom off, `sub ev=wheel`, inject wheel / assert `ev=wheel` emitted AND text scroll suppressed.
- `wheel_passthrough_default` / interaction / no explore zoom, no `ev=wheel` sub, inject wheel over inline vp in a pager / assert `WheelOwner::PassThrough` AND the surrounding text scrolled.
- `raw_consumes_pointer_stream` / interaction / `sub vp=1;raw=1`, inject down+move+up / assert raw pointer events forwarded to the app AND no explore/`ev=click` emitted.

## P2-T33 — F4 input routing: capture viewport cell rect, suppress SGR, both=1

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/input.rs` implement F4 input routing (§12.5 disambiguation): within a subscribed viewport's **live placeholder-cell rect** (authoritative, §10.2 — follows scroll/reflow, not the stale stored position) an active `sub` **captures** the pointer and **suppresses** app-enabled SGR mouse reporting there; the click is reported only as `tgp;e`. Outside every subscribed viewport's rect, SGR reporting is untouched and no `tgp;e` is emitted. A per-sub opt-in flag `both=1` (default `0`) makes the terminal emit **both** SGR and `tgp;e` within the rect, leaving dedup to the app. **Raw is exclusive** within its rect (consumes the entire stream, suppresses SGR there too — interacts with P2-T32). Add `Term::tgp_input_routing(px) -> InputRouting { Tgp(vp) | Sgr | Both(vp) | Raw(vp) }`.

**Design refs.** §12.5 (disambiguation, capture, `both=1`), §10.2 (live rect authoritative); F4 (input routing).

**Depends on.** P2-T25 (hit-test), P2-T23, P2-T2 (live rect), existing SGR mouse path in `Term`.

**Tests.**
- `sgr_suppressed_inside_subscribed_rect` / interaction / enable SGR mouse reporting, `sub vp=1;ev=click`, click inside vp1's live rect / assert `tgp_input_routing(px) == Tgp(vid(1))` AND `pty_replies` has a `tgp;e;ev=click` but NO SGR mouse report bytes.
- `sgr_untouched_outside_rect` / interaction / SGR on, click outside all subscribed rects / assert routing `== Sgr` AND a normal SGR mouse report is queued, no `tgp;e`.
- `both_flag_emits_both` / interaction / `sub vp=1;ev=click;both=1`, click inside rect / assert routing `== Both(vid(1))` AND both an SGR report AND a `tgp;e;ev=click` are queued.
- `capture_follows_reflow` / interaction / `sub vp=1`, reflow moves the inline rect to a new row, click at the new rect / assert routing `== Tgp(vid(1))` (capture keyed to live rect, not stale stored position).
- `raw_rect_exclusive_suppresses_sgr` / interaction / `sub vp=1;raw=1`, SGR on, click inside rect / assert routing `== Raw(vid(1))` AND no SGR report queued.

## P2-T34 — Back-channel ordering, coalescing & backpressure

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/backchannel.rs` (new) make all terminal→app traffic — `tgp;e` events, `tgp;a` acks, `tgp;x` errors, and DA1 — share **one ordered FIFO** (never reorders; advertised ordered so apps correlate `txn` acks). **Coalescing:** consecutive still-pending events of the same `(viewport, ev-type)` where `ev ∈ {hover, drag, camera, wheel}` may coalesce to the latest before flush; `click`, `dblclick`, `down`, `up`, `enter`, `leave`, `resize`, `destroyed`, and **all** acks/errors/DA1 are **never** coalesced and **never** reordered. **Backpressure:** if the channel exceeds advertised `max_backchannel_bytes`, drop the **oldest coalescable** entries first (hover/drag/camera/wheel); never drop or delay an ack, error, or non-coalescable event. Token scoping is orthogonal (each token's reports tagged; order within a token's stream is FIFO). **Scope note (enqueue order is P1's, FIFO preservation is P2's):** the *enqueue order* of the handshake `tgp;r` and DA1 (`tgp;r` before `ESC[c`) is established by P1's handshake/DA1 path; this task only guarantees the FIFO **preserves** that enqueue order (does not reorder) — it does not set it. The `da1_after_tgp_r_ordering` test therefore asserts order-preservation through this channel, not that P2 chooses the order; a P1 enqueue-order regression is out of this task's scope. Add `Term::tgp_backchannel_snapshot() -> Vec<BackchannelFrame>`.

**Design refs.** §12.5.4 (back-channel ordering & coalescing, backpressure); G4 (ordered FIFO), G1 (token tagging orthogonal).

**Depends on.** P2-T23, P1 (`pty_replies` / DA1 ordering).

**Tests.**
- `coalesce_consecutive_hover` / interaction / emit 3 consecutive `ev=hover` for the same vp before flush / assert `tgp_backchannel_snapshot()` holds exactly one `hover` (the latest, by coords).
- `click_never_coalesced` / interaction / emit hover, click, hover for the same vp / assert the `click` survives intact and is not merged.
- `ack_never_reordered_before_pending_event` / interaction / queue a coalescable `hover`, then a `tgp;a` ack / assert FIFO order preserved (hover before ack), ack never dropped.
- `backpressure_drops_oldest_coalescable` / interaction / `max_backchannel_bytes` small; flood `drag` events past the cap, then queue a `tgp;x` error / assert oldest `drag` entries dropped first AND the error is retained.
- `da1_after_tgp_r_ordering` / lifecycle / handshake then DA1 both pending / assert `tgp;r` precedes DA1 in the FIFO (G7 ordering preserved through this channel).

## P2-T35 — `ev=resize` report + emission gating on foreground/PTY-close

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` add the `ev=resize` report: when reflow/resize changes a subscribed viewport's cell dimensions and the app subscribed `ev=resize`, emit `tgp;e;vp=<id>;ev=resize;cols=<n>;rows=<m>` (token-tagged). Implement **emission gating** (G1/G5): the terminal emits a token's events/acks/errors only while that token has a live subscription AND the terminal is focused; when the foreground process group changes or the PTY input side closes, the terminal **cancels** that token's subscriptions, explore, and animation control and **stops emitting** (preventing report bytes leaking into a shell prompt). Add `Term::tgp_token_active(tok) -> bool`.

**Design refs.** §12.5 (`ev=resize`), §12.5 emission gating; G1 (emission gating absorbs LIFE-7), G5, G10.

**Depends on.** P2-T9 (reflow), P2-T21 (sub), P2-T23, P2-T34.

**Tests.**
- `resize_event_emitted` / lifecycle / `sub vp=1;ev=resize`, resize that changes vp1 to 20×12 cells / assert `tgp;e;tok=AP1;vp=1;ev=resize;cols=20;rows=12` queued.
- `resize_not_subscribed_silent` / lifecycle / no `ev=resize` sub, resize vp1 / assert no `ev=resize` frame queued.
- `foreground_change_cancels_and_stops` / lifecycle / AP1 has subs+explore, simulate foreground process-group change / assert `tgp_token_active("AP1") == false` AND a subsequent injected click queues NO `tgp;e` (emission stopped, subs cancelled).
- `pty_close_cancels_token` / lifecycle / AP1 has subs, close the PTY input side / assert AP1's subs/explore cancelled AND no further emission.

## P2-T36 — Shared viewport-destroy teardown + `ev=destroyed` emit helper (reason= routing)

**Build-order note (resolves I-11/I-13).** This task is the **single shared teardown + emit helper** that P2-T11/T12/T13 (detach / eviction / erase) and P2-T1 (`op=remove`) all **call** — it is therefore **built before** those callers even though it is numbered later (its only real dependencies are P2-T6 offscreen targets and P2-T21 subscription store, both of which precede T11/T12/T13 in the dependency graph). The destroy tasks do **not** re-implement the emit; they invoke `destroy_viewport(vp, reason)`. Numeric order does not reflect topological order here; an executor must land this helper before wiring T11/T12/T13's subscribed-event branch. The `*_emits_destroyed_when_subscribed` tests live **here** (one place), with the per-trigger tasks asserting only that they call the helper with the correct `reason=`.

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/interact.rs` add `destroy_viewport(vp, reason: DestroyReason { Evicted | Detached | Explicit })`: the single teardown path that (1) frees the viewport's offscreen color+depth target (P2-T6) and pick target/buffer (P2-T20), (2) drops **all** of that viewport's subs (P2-T21), (3) **only if** the owning token holds an active lifecycle subscription on that viewport, queues exactly one `tgp;e;vp=<id>;ev=destroyed;reason=<r>` (token-routed, never coalesced — interacts with P2-T34), otherwise silent, and (4) removes the viewport from the scene store (shared scene nodes/assets/camera untouched). No `tgp;x` (eviction/detach/erase/explicit are normal teardown, not errors). `DestroyReason::Explicit` is supplied by the `vp;op=remove` path (P2-T1); `Evicted`/`Detached` by P2-T11/T12/T13.

**Design refs.** §10.2 / §10.7 (`ev=destroyed;reason=evicted|detached`), §12.5.1 (viewport destroy drops subs); G10 (lifecycle event opt-in).

**Depends on.** P2-T6 (offscreen targets), P2-T21 (sub store), P2-T20 (pick target), P2-T34 (back-channel never-coalesce). (Built BEFORE its callers P2-T1 `op=remove`, P2-T11/T12/T13.)

**Tests.**
- `destroyed_evicted_routed_to_owner` / lifecycle / owner holds lifecycle sub on inline vp1, call `destroy_viewport(vp1, Evicted)` (via eviction trigger) / assert exactly one `tgp;e;tok=AP1;vp=1;ev=destroyed;reason=evicted`.
- `destroyed_detached_reason` / lifecycle / owner subscribed, `destroy_viewport(vp1, Detached)` (via overwrite-below-count trigger) / assert `reason=detached`.
- `destroyed_explicit_reason` / lifecycle / owner subscribed, feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=1;op=remove ESC\` (the concrete explicit-destroy frame from P2-T1) / assert exactly one `tgp;e;tok=AP1;vp=1;ev=destroyed;reason=explicit`.
- `destroyed_silent_without_sub` / lifecycle / no lifecycle sub, `destroy_viewport(vp1, Evicted)` / assert no `tgp;e` and no `tgp;x` queued.
- `destroyed_frees_subs_and_pick_target` / lifecycle / vp1 has an events sub + pick target, `destroy_viewport(vp1, Explicit)` / assert `tgp_sub(&vid(1)) == None` AND its pick target freed AND its offscreen target freed.
- `destroyed_never_coalesced` / lifecycle / queue a pending `hover` for vp1 then `destroy_viewport(vp1, Evicted)` / assert the `destroyed` frame is not coalesced away and follows FIFO order (P2-T34 non-coalesce set).

## P2-T38 — RIS full teardown across tokens; DECSTR soft-reset leaves scene intact (G10)

**Deliverable.** In `toastty-term/src/tgp/mod.rs` wire the RIS (`ESC c`) and DECSTR (`ESC [ ! p`) reset paths for TGP state. **RIS** is the guaranteed VRAM-recovery path (§10.7, G10): it tears down the **entire** TGP scene of **every** token — assets, materials, nodes, **viewports (inline and pinned)**, all offscreen color+depth targets, all pick targets/buffers, all subscriptions, all explore controllers, and any pending chunk buffers — frees all GPU buffers, and **invalidates all session tokens** (a subsequent frame must re-handshake). RIS destroys viewports via the shared `destroy_viewport` path but is silent (it does **not** emit per-viewport `tgp;e;ev=destroyed` — the tokens are being invalidated wholesale; no live sub survives to receive it). **DECSTR** (soft reset) leaves the TGP scene fully intact — no viewport, target, sub, or token teardown — locking the §10.7 hard-vs-soft contrast. This is the load-bearing counterpart to P2-T13 ("2J keeps pinned; RIS destroys everything"). **Post-RIS frame behavior under an invalidated token (pins the load-bearing consequence of invalidation, G10).** After RIS invalidates all session tokens, a subsequent frame bearing a now-invalid token **does not address or mutate any state**: it is **rejected with `tgp;x;code=bad_token`** to that token. Per **G11(d)**, `bad_token` is the code for "a frame that needs a session token but lacks a *valid* handshake"; an invalidated token is exactly such a frame. This is distinct from the G5/G11(d) silent-drop case (a writer that *simply never handshaked* is dropped silently): a token that **was** handshaked and is now invalidated is a **recognizable** error condition, so the terminal emits `bad_token` rather than silently dropping. (Synonym `token_denied→bad_token`, G3; NOT `denied` — `denied` is RESERVED under G11(a) and would never apply here.) This makes the `bad_token` closed-enum member reachable from P2's RIS path. The app must re-handshake (`tgp;q`→`tgp;r`) to obtain a fresh valid token before any further `vp`/`sub`/`p` frame takes effect. Add `Term::tgp_token_valid(tok) -> bool`.

**Design refs.** §10.7 (RIS full teardown across tokens, DECSTR leaves scene untouched); G10 (RIS = full scene teardown all tokens; DECSTR intact), G3 + **G11(d)** (`bad_token` = a token-requiring frame lacking a valid handshake — reachable from a post-RIS invalidated-token frame; distinct from the silent-drop of a never-handshaked writer; `denied` RESERVED per G11(a)), G5 (handshake = error-reporting opt-in / silent-drop for never-handshaked).

**Depends on.** P2-T1 (viewport store), P2-T6 (offscreen targets), P2-T20 (pick targets), P2-T21 (subs), P2-T28/T30 (explore/controller state), P2-T36 (shared teardown).

**Tests.**
- `ris_destroys_all_viewports_and_targets` / lifecycle / token AP1 creates inline `vp=1` (allocated target), token AP2 creates pinned `vp=2` (allocated target) / feed `ESC c` / assert `tgp_viewports().is_empty()` AND `tgp_viewport_has_target(&vid("AP1",1)) == false` AND `tgp_viewport_has_target(&vid("AP2",2)) == false` (every token's composite-keyed viewports + targets freed across tokens).
- `ris_cancels_subs_explore_and_invalidates_tokens` / lifecycle / AP1 has a sub + explore on vp1, feed `ESC c` / assert `tgp_sub(&vid(1)) == None`, `tgp_camera_controller(cam) == None`, AND `tgp_token_valid("AP1") == false` (tokens invalidated — a later frame must re-handshake).
- `ris_destroy_is_silent_no_destroyed_event` / lifecycle / AP1 subscribed `ev=destroyed` on inline vp1, feed `ESC c` / assert NO `tgp;e;ev=destroyed` queued (RIS invalidates tokens wholesale; no per-viewport event) AND no `tgp;x`.
- `decstr_leaves_viewports_intact` / lifecycle / create inline `vp=1` + pinned `vp=2` + a sub, feed `ESC [ ! p` (DECSTR) / assert `tgp_viewports().len() == 2`, targets still allocated, `tgp_sub(&vid(1)).is_some()`, AND `tgp_token_valid("AP1") == true` (soft reset leaves TGP scene untouched).
- `ris_invalid_token_frame_rejected` / lifecycle / AP1 creates `vp=1`, feed `ESC c` (RIS, invalidates AP1), then feed `ESC_ tgp;vp;tok=AP1;vp=9;anchor=pinned;col=0;row=0;cols=4;rows=2 ESC\` under the now-invalid token / assert it does NOT create a viewport (`tgp_viewport(&vid("AP1",9)) == None`) AND `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=9;code=bad_token` (invalidated token's frame rejected; the app must re-handshake — pins the load-bearing consequence of G10 token invalidation and the `bad_token` member's reachability from P2's RIS path).

Tied to G7 `color=srgb` and the P2-T7 compositor.

- **I-6** (`dblclick` untested member of closed ev enum) — **resolved.** P2-T32 deliverable adds double-click arbitration on the injectable clock (fixed 400 ms threshold + deadzone); added tests `dblclick_within_threshold_emits` and `two_clicks_past_threshold_no_dblclick`.
- **I-7** (G3 wire-spelling: `instanced_node_tint` must be `bad_param` not `kind_conflict`) — **resolved.** P2-T17 deliverable, design-refs, and test `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected` now assert `code=bad_param;detail=instanced_node_tint`, with an explicit note that the spine G3 synonym map supersedes the s06 §6.4 `kind_conflict` prose (spine wins).
- **I-8** (feat-gating `unsupported` untested for instance/pick/explore) — **resolved.** Added a Feature-gating note to the P2 intro (feat negotiation + `unsupported` owned/tested by P1; pick/explore gated via `sub` exercised through P1) and added a use-time gate on P2-T16: deliverable rejects un-negotiated `instance` with `code=unsupported;detail=instance`, test `instances_without_feat_unsupported`.
- **I-9** (device-loss recovery for targets+instances, G10/G7) — **resolved.** Added new task **P2-T37** (force re-upload of targets + instance buffers ignoring dirty, retain CPU state, push unsolicited `tgp;r`) with tests `device_loss_realloc_targets_ignores_dirty`, `device_loss_reuploads_instances_ignores_instance_dirty`, `device_loss_pushes_tgp_r`, `device_loss_retains_cpu_scene_state`; added `Term::tgp_device_generation()` and `Term::on_device_loss()` seams.
- **I-10** (RIS full teardown across tokens, G10) — **resolved.** Added new task **P2-T38** (RIS tears down all viewports/targets/pick/subs/explore across all tokens and invalidates tokens, silent; DECSTR leaves scene intact) with tests `ris_destroys_all_viewports_and_targets`, `ris_cancels_subs_explore_and_invalidates_tokens`, `ris_destroy_is_silent_no_destroyed_event`, `decstr_leaves_viewports_intact`; added `Term::tgp_token_valid(tok)`.
- **I-11** (build-order inversion + duplicated destroyed-emit between P2-T11/12/13 and P2-T36) — **resolved.** P2-T36 is restructured into the single shared `destroy_viewport(vp, reason)` teardown+emit helper with an explicit build-order note that it lands BEFORE its callers (depends only on T6/T20/T21/T34). P2-T11/T12/T13 deliverables now CALL the helper (no re-implemented emit). The canonical `*_emits_destroyed_*` behavior tests live once on P2-T36; the per-trigger tasks keep thin tests asserting the correct `reason=` is passed (renamed `*_calls_helper_with_*`).
- **I-13** (forward dependency / topological order of T11/T12 vs T21) — **resolved (paired with I-11).** P2-T11/T12 now depend on P2-T36 (the helper, which transitively brings T6 targets + T21 subs), and P2-T36 carries the build-order note that numeric order ≠ topological order here. P2-T21's `sub_viewport_destroy_drops_subs` test annotated to clarify the drop behavior is delivered by the T36 helper reaching into the T21 sub store.

P2-T36 `destroyed_explicit_reason` now feeds the concrete `tgp;vp;op=remove` frame.

Tests `inline_create_binds_cells`, `inline_reemit_same_glyph_not_detach` (P2-T2), `overwrite_detaches_cell`, `reemit_identical_glyph_no_detach` (P2-T11) now assert against that accessor with concrete cell contents.

Also added P2-T16 deliverable color-chain note + `decode_tint_linear([u8;4])` seam and test `instances_tint_srgb_to_linear` asserting `final_linear = baseColor_linear * tint_linear` (§6.4). `color=srgb` is now value-level covered.

Added P2-T22 deliverable+test `sub_pick_without_feat_unsupported` (`tgp;x;code=unsupported;detail=pick`, nothing stored) and P2-T28 deliverable+test `explore_without_feat_unsupported` (`detail=explore`, no controller stored). - **P2R2-3** (dblclick spatial deadzone untested) — **resolved.** Added P2-T32 test `two_clicks_outside_deadzone_no_dblclick` (two clicks within the time threshold but second press > `click_deadzone_px` from the first → NO `ev=dblclick`, two independent `ev=click`), covering the spatial axis the time-only tests omit.
- **P2R2-4** (cap-name drift `max_viewport_px` vs `_w`/`_h`) — **resolved.** Added a cap-key resolution paragraph to P2-T5: P1's caps table advertises the split pair `max_viewport_px_w`/`max_viewport_px_h` (a w/h cap needs two numbers; §10.3 uses the split form), the spine's singular `max_viewport_px` is the family/scope-prefix name (and the harmless `detail=` advisory spelling per G3). P2-T5 reads `_w`/`_h`, emits `detail=max_viewport_px`. Cross-doc key pinned; design-ref cites G7.
- **P2R2-5** (`vp` camera/root null-reset untested) — **resolved.** P2-T1 deliverable now enumerates nullable `vp` fields (camera→None, root→None, clear→None). Added tests `vp_camera_null_clears_to_default` (CBOR-null `camera` → `camera==None`, `effective_camera()==Default`, distinct from absent-preserve) and `vp_root_null_clears_to_whole_scene`. Both interact with P2-T14 late-bind.
- **P2R2-6** (post-RIS invalidated-token frame behavior undriven) — **resolved.** P2-T38 deliverable now pins the behavior: a frame under a RIS-invalidated token is rejected with `tgp;x;code=bad_token` (invalidated-but-recognized token is an error condition, makes `bad_token` reachable from P2's RIS path). Added test `ris_invalid_token_frame_rejected` (post-RIS `vp` under AP1 → no viewport created AND `tgp;x;tok=AP1;vp=9;code=bad_token`). Design-ref cites G3/G5.
- **P2R2-7** (`vp` non-nullable-field `null_not_allowed` unaddressed) — **resolved.** P2-T1 deliverable now enumerates non-nullable `vp` fields (id always; `anchor`+`cells` on initial create; `z`/`screen_affinity`/`clip_to_scroll_region`/`render` non-nullable scalars) with `null_not_allowed;detail=<field>` rejection. Added test `vp_null_on_required_field_rejected`. Design-ref cites §6.1 + G3 `null_not_allowed`.
- **P2R2-8** (`ViewportId` seam ambiguity vs single-arg `vid(n)`) — **resolved.** P2-T1 deliverable pins `ViewportId = { token: Tok, raw: u16 }` (G1/§8.4 id hardening), wire `vp=` carries only raw, `apply_tgp_vp` qualifies it by `tok=`. Test helper made token-aware: `vid(tok, n)` qualified form + `vid(n)` convenience binding to the test's active token. Updated multi-token assertions to the qualified form: P2-T1 `vp_namespace_isolation_denied`, P2-T8 `zorder_tiebreak_by_token_then_id`, P2-T38 `ris_destroys_all_viewports_and_targets`. The G1 isolation + z-tiebreak tests are now expressible against the stated seam.
- **P2R2-9** (`wx/wy/wz` epsilon vs verbatim float precision unpinned) — **resolved.** P2-T23 deliverable pins all `tgp;e` `f32` fields (`x/y/wx/wy/wz`, and P2-T31 `t/r/s`) to the shortest-round-trippable decimal (Rust `{}`/Ryū, parses back bit-identical). `event_world_point_in_frame` now parses the floats back and asserts **exact** equality to `PickHit.world` (not epsilon). P2-T31 deliverable references the same standard for its byte-identical `t/r/s` round-trip.
- **P2R2-10** (`count=0` legal-empty buffer untested) — **resolved.** P2-T16 deliverable pins `count=0` with zero-length `xforms` as a legal empty buffer (decodes to `count:0`, accepted). Added test `instances_count_zero_empty_ok` (P2-T16) and `instance_draw_range_empty_for_zero_count` (P2-T19, `Some(0..0)`, no degenerate draw). Distinct from P2-T17's `count=0`-with-bytes error.
- **P2R2-11** (node-op `tgp;x` errors omit G4 `txn=`+op-index) — **resolved.** Added a G4-correlation note to P2-T16 (applies to all node-op errors in T16/T17/T18: patch `p` ops echo `tgp;x;tok=…;txn=<n>;op=<i>;code=…`) and to P2-T17/T18 deliverables + design-refs. Amended the asserted bytes in `instances_without_feat_unsupported` (`txn=7;op=0`), `instances_bad_layout_xforms_len` (`txn=11;op=0`), `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected` (`txn=12;op=0`), and `instance_set_bad_index` (`txn=20;op=0`) to carry the full correlator. The G4 contract is now covered for the node-op surfaces P2 introduces.

Added tests `sub_ev_mask_full_decode` (feeds all 12 `ev` tokens, asserts each lands in `EvMask`) and `sub_ev_garbage_token_ignored` (unknown `ev` token silently ignored, no `invalid_sub`). Design-ref cites G7.

## Cleanup resolution log (pass 1)

This pass resolves every open issue in `P2.review.md` (round 3, P2R3-1..P2R3-8), applies the reconciliation **addendum** (G11 isolation model + G12 error-code reachability — authoritative, supersedes the base spine), and discharges this phase's **DEDUP/ALIAS duty** (reuse P1-T12 for instance decode/validation). Format: `id/duty — resolved (how) | declined (why)`.

### Open issues (P2R3-1 .. P2R3-8)

- **P2R3-1** (`vp;explore` field dropped from P2-T1's `VpOp` struct) — **resolved.** Added `explore: Option<ExploreOpts>` to the `VpOp`/`Viewport` struct in P2-T1's deliverable (§10.1 s10 line 17), classified it in the three-way merge table (nullable: CBOR null tears down the controller → `None`; absent → preserve; value → set/replace subject to the P2-T28 feat gate), and stated `decode_vp_frame` parses the `explore` sub-map by delegating to P2-T28's `ExploreOpts` decoder. The headline explore field now has a decode home in the struct P2-T1 owns.
- **P2R3-2** (P2-T28 "Depends on" omits P2-T1) — **resolved.** Added P2-T1 to P2-T28's "Depends on" with the acyclicity note (P2-T1 has no path back to P2-T28). Updated P2-T28's deliverable to state it **extends** P2-T1's `decode_vp_frame`/`apply_tgp_vp` (parse the `explore` sub-map, route it through the feat gate before storing the controller; the rest of the `vp` upsert applies via P2-T1's normal path), which is exactly what `explore_without_feat_unsupported`'s "non-explore fields still applied" assertion needs.
- **P2R3-3** (P2-T22 "Depends on" omits P2-T28) — **resolved.** Added P2-T28 to P2-T22's "Depends on" with the acyclicity check (P2-T28 → {P2-T1, P2-T24, P2-T14}; no cycle). Noted in the dep line that `sub_raw_plus_explore_rejected` and `wheel_plus_explore_zoom_conflict` are only exercisable once the explore controller exists.
- **P2R3-4** (`pick_cpu_gpu_agree` needs a GPU, contradicting G6) — **resolved.** Pinned the parity seam to the §12.4 "ray-cast … **or** a CPU-rasterized pick buffer" CPU↔CPU option: `assert_pick_agrees(raycast, rasterized)` compares the ray-cast CPU pick against a CPU-rasterized pick buffer (both GPU-free). Renamed the test `pick_cpu_gpu_agree → pick_raycast_vs_cpu_rasterized_agree`; deliverable + design-refs updated; noted true GPU-vs-CPU parity would be a separately-gated integration test exempt from the unit contract (not this test). The always-present ray-cast path stays independently covered by the other five P2-T20 tests.
- **P2R3-5** (unnamed "grid move helper" seam) — **resolved.** Named the concrete grid-mutation seams in P2-T2: `Term::test_scroll_grid(lines)` (relocates grid + placeholder cells deterministically), `Term::set_grid_size(cols,rows)` (the SIGWINCH/resize→rewrap entry point), and the DECSTBM byte form `ESC[<top>;<bot>r` + line feeds. Updated `inline_hint_not_reread` (P2-T2), `reflow_*` (P2-T9), and `decstbm_scroll_moves_inline_rect` (P2-T10) to drive these named seams instead of an unnamed helper.
- **P2R3-6** (tasks read P1 caps/token-order without the P1 dep) — **resolved.** Added "P1 (handshake token order)" to P2-T8, "P1 (caps: `camera_report_hz`)" to P2-T31, and "P1 (caps: `click_deadzone_px`)" to P2-T32 "Depends on" lines, mirroring how P2-T4/T5/T17 already cite their P1-caps dependency.
- **P2R3-7** (`vp_namespace_isolation_denied` unsound under composite keying + contradicts P2-T8) — **resolved (also discharges the G11 duty for `vp`).** Rewrote the test in two: (a) `vp_same_raw_id_two_tokens_coexist` asserts `tok=AP2;vp=1` creates a distinct `{AP2,1}` with its own `cols`, `{AP1,1}` unchanged, and **no `denied`** (raw-id collision = coexistence, not error — consistent with P2-T8's tiebreak); (b) `vp_cross_token_camera_ref_late_binds_default` asserts a cross-token `camera=` ref late-binds to the default camera (G11(c)/G2), never `denied`, with the no-existence-oracle note (the missing-state error is byte-identical whether the id exists elsewhere or nowhere, G11(b)). Reconciled the line-21 prose. P2-T1/T8 no longer assert mutually-exclusive outcomes for the same input.
- **P2R3-8** (`sub_token_isolation` same composite-key flaw) — **resolved (also discharges the G11 duty for `sub`).** Rewrote `sub_token_isolation → sub_cross_token_matches_nothing`: AP2's raw `vp=1` resolves to `{AP2,1}` in AP2's own map, its `nodes=O1` references an id absent from AP2's map, the sub is **accepted and stored** (accept-nonexistent rule, §12.5.1) and **matches nothing** (causally blind to AP1's map), AP1's sub unchanged, **no `denied`** (G11(a)/(b)). The test no longer asserts `denied` on a plain raw-id collision.

### Addendum G11 (per-token isolation model) — applied

- **`denied` on a plain cross-token id collision → REWRITTEN to coexistence.** Both P2 tests that asserted `code=denied` on a raw `vp=`/`nodes=` id collision (`vp_namespace_isolation_denied`, `sub_token_isolation`) are rewritten onto the correct outcomes: distinct coexisting `{tok,id}` map entries (`vp_same_raw_id_two_tokens_coexist`) and accepted-but-matches-nothing (`sub_cross_token_matches_nothing`). Per **G11(a)**, `denied` is **RESERVED** — no reachable ingress trigger; cross-token addressing is unrepresentable under the private per-token id map.
- **Cross-token mesh/handle ref → `bad_ref`, no existence leak.** P2 introduces **no** patch-ref ops (mesh/asset/material/parent ref resolution is P1-owned, §6/§8.6 — `bad_ref` for those is a P1 surface, correctly N/A here). The only ref-like fields P2 introduces are the viewport `camera`/`root`, which are **not** patch refs (G2/G11(c)). The cross-token camera-ref test (`vp_cross_token_camera_ref_late_binds_default`) pins the late-bind-default outcome AND the no-existence-oracle invariant (G11(b)): the optional diagnostic `bad_ref` on entering the missing state is byte-identical whether `cam` exists under another token or nowhere — it never leaks existence.
- **Cross-token camera/root ref → late-bind default (G2).** Pinned by `vp_cross_token_camera_ref_late_binds_default` (→ `ResolvedCamera::Default`, never `denied`, never reject) and the existing P2-T14 late-bind suite.
- **No-handshake frame → `bad_token`.** The only `bad_token`-reachable surface in P2 is the **post-RIS invalidated-token** frame (P2-T38 `ris_invalid_token_frame_rejected`), now explicitly cited to **G11(d)** (a token-requiring frame lacking a *valid* handshake). Tightened the P2-T38 deliverable to distinguish G11(d)'s two cases: an *invalidated* (was-handshaked, now-invalid) token is a recognizable error → `bad_token`; a *never-handshaked* writer is dropped **silently** per G5 (no P2 reachable trigger — correctly not asserted). `token_denied→bad_token` synonym retained; `denied` explicitly NOT used here (RESERVED).

### Addendum G12 (error-code reachability) — applied

- **`instanced_node_tint` → `kind_conflict` (G12.4, REVERSES the prior round's ruling).** This is the one phase emission the addendum flips. The round-1 log (I-7) and the prior body asserted `bad_param` citing the base-spine synonym map. **G12.4 strikes the `instanced_node_tint → bad_param` synonym** and makes the canonical code `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (G12.3 row 11; a kind-invariant violation). Updated P2-T17 deliverable, the note, design-refs, and the test `instanced_node_level_tint_rejected` to assert `code=kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`. (The round-1 log entry I-7 is left as a frozen historical record; this pass supersedes it per G12.4.)
- **Over-`max_msg_mb` → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (G12.1).** **Not actionable in P2 — declined with justification.** The per-message decoded-size cap (`max_msg_mb`) and its breach loci (the `enc=bin` drain path, the decode-size reject path) are **framer/CBOR surfaces owned by P1** (binds P1-T2/P1-T9/P1-T16 per G12.1; coverage table marks framer codes N/A-P1). P2 introduces no over-`max_msg_mb` emission, so there is nothing in P2 to retarget. Verified P2 contains **no** `msg_too_large` and **no** `max_msg` emission (grep-clean), so P2 is already consistent with the ruling that `msg_too_large` is RESERVED and the breach funnels through `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` at P1.
- **`msg_too_large` / `denied` → RESERVED; `unknown_op` REACHABLE only via P4 (G12.2/G12.3).** P2 emits none of these three (verified grep-clean for `msg_too_large`/`unknown_op`; `denied` removed by the G11 rewrites) — `unknown_op` is reachable solely via the P4 closed control-frame anim-verb locus (P4-T7), never in P2's patch/viewport/sub paths. The closed-set encoder round-trip test that exercises all 26 codes (including the 2 reserved) is owned by **P1-T16 / P5-T1** (G12.3), not P2 — so `denied` being "uncovered in P2" is **correct and not a defect**: it has no reachable ingress trigger anywhere, and P2 surfaces nothing that should produce it. The P2.review.md "AT RISK / effectively UNCOVERED `denied`" finding is thereby dissolved: under the addendum `denied` is RESERVED, so P2 must NOT cover it.
- **Reachable codes P2 still produces (unchanged, addendum-consistent):** `cap_exceeded` (T4/T5/T17), `bad_layout`/`parse_error` (T17, validation owned by P1-T12 but surfaced through the P2 apply path), `kind_conflict` (T17, per G12.4), `bad_index` (T18), `bad_ref` (T14 optional diag), `unsupported` (T16/T22/T28 use-time feat gates), `invalid_sub` (T22), `camera_busy` (T30), `null_not_allowed` (T1), `bad_token` (T38). All match the G12.3 table.

### DEDUP / ALIAS duty (reuse P1-T12 for instance decode/validation) — applied

- **DEDUP — done.** Removed the duplicated decoder/validator deliverable text from P2-T16/T17. P2-T16 is recast (heading + deliverable + DEDUP note) as a **thin consumer + use-site policy**: it cites **P1-T12** as the owner of `InstanceBuffer` + `decode_instances` + the `count*64`/`count*4` length, NaN, cap, and `count=0` legal-empty rules; it owns only the `NodeKind::Instanced` scene-kind wiring and the P2-only `feat=instance` use-time gate. Its decode-value tests are renamed to surface-through-accessor tests (`instances_decode_basic → instances_decode_surfaced_to_node`, `instances_decode_translation_value → instances_translation_value_surfaced`, `instances_tints_decode → instances_tints_surfaced`, `instances_tints_absent_default_white → instances_tints_absent_surfaced_none`, `instances_count_zero_empty_ok → instances_count_zero_empty_surfaced`) that assert P2 surfaces the P1-decoded buffer unaltered (no P2 re-implementation).
- **DEDUP — done (T17).** P2-T17 recast: validation logic (`bad_layout`/`cap_exceeded`/`parse_error` decisions) is delegated to P1-T12; T17 owns only the P2 scene-apply consequences — **atomic last-committed-buffer retention** on reject and the **Instanced-kind tint invariant** (`kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`). Tests renamed/refocused to drive the P1-T12 validator through the P2 apply path and assert the correlated error + the retention/kind-invariant behavior (`instances_bad_layout_xforms_len → instances_bad_layout_retains_prior_xforms_len`, `instances_nan_rejected → instances_nan_rejected_retains_prior`; the others gained explicit "prior buffer retained" assertions). `Depends on` now cites P1-T12 as the validation owner.
- **P2 owns ONLY the render path + re-tint — affirmed.** P2-T19 (GPU instancing render path) and P2-T18 (`node.instance_set` single-instance re-tint) are explicitly labeled as the genuinely P2-owned write/render paths, both operating **on** the P1-T12-decoded `InstanceBuffer` (no re-decode of the bulk buffer). Their `Depends on` lines cite P1-T12. The phase intro is updated to say P2 owns the instancing render path + re-tint and **reuses** P1-T12 decode/validation.
- **No new `instance.rs` decoder — verified.** All `decode_instances` references in P2 now attribute ownership to P1-T12; T16/T17/T18 deliverables point at `scene.rs` (apply/kind wiring), and the only `(new)` `instance.rs` claim was removed. The `decode_tint_linear` seam (P2-T16 `instances_tint_srgb_to_linear`) is explicitly the **render color-chain helper** (consumes the P1-decoded RGBA8 record), not a decoder.

### Coverage non-regression

- **No coverage regressed.** Every op/verb/header-field/error-code/cap/lifecycle-event in P2 scope that had ≥1 concrete test before still has one. Renamed tests preserve their surface: the instance decode-value surfaces are still covered (now as P2-surfaces-the-P1-buffer assertions citing P1-T12); the picking parity surface is still covered (now CPU↔CPU, GPU-free); the two G11 isolation surfaces are still covered (now as coexistence + matches-nothing instead of the unsound `denied`). The only code that lost a P2 assertion is `denied` — **intentionally**, because the authoritative addendum (G11(a)/G12.2) makes it RESERVED with no reachable P2 trigger; its closed-set round-trip coverage lives at P1-T16/P5-T1. Two NEW tests were added (`vp_same_raw_id_two_tokens_coexist`, `vp_cross_token_camera_ref_late_binds_default`) and one NEW field-decode home (`VpOp.explore`).


---

# P3 — Materials & light

Phase 3 adds registered PBR (metal-roughness) materials, hardened PNG/GLB-embedded textures with `max_tex_dim`/`max_texels_per_texture` caps, punctual `Light` nodes with per-viewport replace-not-augment semantics and deterministic over-cap selection, the per-viewport render stages (theme-tint pre-lighting input, tone-map over the whole linear target, MSAA with clamp-down + ack, the always-1x non-resolved/non-tone-mapped pick target), the sRGB-wire / linear-internal / sRGB-encode color pipeline (G7 `color=srgb`), and VRAM accounting for MSAA + resolve + pick targets. All deliverables land in the greenfield `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/*` and `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/*` trees and never reuse the legacy `rgp/` path (G8 clean break); every state surface stays CPU-side and queryable through `&self` accessors so the whole phase is GPU-free testable. The default material itself was built in P1; P3 extends it with the §11.2 color chain (brightness clamp `[0,8]`), theme-tint input, and registered-light response.

> Phase notes (carried from the adversarial review):
> - **G7-vs-§7.1 cap-list discrepancy:** the G7 prose cap enumeration omits `max_texels_per_texture`, but §7.1 and §15.2 (the authoritative section files) list it at `67108864`. This phase relies on §7.1/§15.2 and advertises/enforces `max_texels_per_texture`. §15.2's "MUST NOT enforce a cap it did not advertise" is satisfied because the cap IS advertised.
> - **Plan-defined wire spellings pending design confirmation** (the section files leave these encodings open): `render.tone_map` values `none|reinhard|aces` (decoded case-insensitively); the light selector CBOR sub-schema (`type` key with `directional|point|spot`, `range`, `spot_inner`/`spot_outer`). These are load-bearing on the tests below.
> - **apc_end back-channel:** the cross-crate `apc_end` change (`perform.rs:42` unit → `ApcEnd`) was made in P1 and is unchanged here. P3 binary payloads (textures embedded in `asset.add` GLB, large material/texture patches) ride the existing `enc=bin` path established in P1; each material/texture task carries an `enc=bin` variant test asserting the binary frame agrees with the b64 path.
> - **Quality-knob consistency (§11.4):** both `render.msaa` and `render.tone_map` are quality knobs — unknown/unsupported values are handled silently (msaa clamps down, unknown tone_map defaults to `None`), never an error. Caps/resources (`max_tex_dim`, `max_texels_per_texture`, VRAM, light over-cap) emit structured `tgp;x`.
> - **Addendum G11/G12 (AUTHORITATIVE, supersedes the base spine wherever they conflict):** the v1 isolation model is a **PRIVATE per-token id map** (G11). Cross-token addressing is **unrepresentable by construction** — a frame `tok=B` naming an id resolves ONLY against B's own map, so two tokens that both create id 9 (or `"cam"`) hold two distinct, coexisting entries, never a collision. Consequences binding on P3: (a) `code=denied` is **RESERVED** — no reachable ingress trigger; it stays in the closed G3 enum and is exercised ONLY by the P1-T16/P5-T1 closed-set encoder round-trip test (P3 raises it from NO ingress path). Any P3 test that previously asserted `denied` on a cross-token id collision/ref/remove is REWRITTEN: a plain cross-token id collision is **coexistence** (both objects present, neither observable from the other); a cross-token **mesh/material/tex ref** → `bad_ref` against the bearing token's map with **NO existence oracle** (byte-for-byte identical to absent-everywhere, G11(b)); a cross-token **viewport camera/root** ref → **late-bind default** (G2, never reject, never `denied`); a token-requiring frame with no valid handshake → `bad_token` (G11(d)). This SUPERSEDES the base-spine G1/§8.4 "→ `denied` on cross-token reference" text and the `forbidden→denied` synonym's relevance here. (b) **G12 reachability:** P3's emitted codes match the G12.3 table exactly — `bad_ref`(6), `dup_id`(7), `kind_conflict`(11), `cap_exceeded`(4), `vram_exhausted`(5), `unsupported`(18), `parse_error`(1) are REACHABLE; `denied`(19) is RESERVED. An over-`max_msg_mb` decoded-size breach (out of P3's direct scope but binding on any P3 frame that overflows) emits **`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** per G12.1 (the separate `msg_too_large` code is RESERVED) — P3 never invents a `max_msg`-specific code. `detail=` is advisory/non-load-bearing (G3); tests branch on `code=`.
> - **`asset.remove` is a DESIGN ADDITION pending §8.6 ratification (review P3-I1):** §8.6's closed op set is `asset.add | material.add | node.upsert | node.instances | node.instance_set | node.visible | node.remove` — there is NO `asset.remove`. But §8.4 (line 73) and §8.7 (line 129) both say committed assets/materials are "replace via remove + add" without naming the op, so the removal verb is required by the design's own immutability rule yet left unnamed. P3-T4b DEFINES and DELIVERS `asset.remove` as a plan-defined op (same standing as the plan-defined light sub-schema / tone_map spellings) and flags it for §8.6 ratification; until ratified its wire spelling `{do:"asset.remove",id:<u32>}` is authoritative-by-this-plan, not "not invented." P3-T4's removal-path test depends on P3-T4b.
> - **Dependency-id legend (review P3-I2 / P3-I6):** `Depends on:` lines below cite CONCRETE upstream task ids. The cross-phase aliases used in earlier drafts map as: `P1-handshake-caps → P1-T6` (cap reply) `+ P1-T10` (handshake dispatch); `P1-tgp-scene-model → P1-T7`; `P1-patch-decode → P1-T9` (CBOR decode) `+ P1-T11` (apply engine); `P1-world-matrix → P1-T8`; `P1-asset-table → P1-T14` (chunked `asset.add` — no standalone asset-table task exists); `P1-tgp-glb-loader → P1-T15`; `P1-clock → P1-T5`; `node.upsert`/kind-immutability → `P1-T12`; `node.remove` → `P1-T13`; `P2-multiple-viewports → P2-T1` (`+ P2-T6` offscreen targets where P3-T15/T17 extend them); `P2-cpu-picking → P2-T20`; `P2-sub-events → P2-T21` (sub decode) `+ P2-T23` (event wire emit); `P2-device-loss → P2-T37`.

## P3-T1 — Color pipeline: sRGB-wire → linear-internal conversion + linear-light offscreen target type

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/color.rs` (new): `pub fn srgb_u8_to_linear_f32(u8) -> f32`, `pub fn srgb_rgba8_to_linear([u8;4]) -> [f32;4]` (alpha left linear), reusing the math style of `crates/toastty-render/src/color.rs::linear_to_srgb_u8` (the inverse). Add a single canonical entry point `WireColor::linearize()` applied ON INPUT to every color field so linearization happens exactly once. `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/target.rs` (new): `ViewportColorTarget` describing the offscreen as a linear-DATA UNORM format (NOT `*_SRGB` auto-encoding) plus an explicit in-shader sRGB-encode stage marker; `pub fn color_target_format() -> wgpu::TextureFormat` returning a non-sRGB UNORM. Document the §11.2 invariant: linearize-once-on-input, render linear, sRGB-encode in-shader. No GPU object construction in the pure helpers.

**Design refs:** §11.2 (color space), §7.1 (`color=srgb`), G7.

**Depends on:** P1-T6, P1-T10 (handshake-caps), P1-T7 (scene model).

**Tests:**
- `srgb_u8_to_linear_roundtrip_matches_render_color_inverse` / unit (pure fn) / input: `srgb_u8_to_linear_f32(188)` then feed result through `render::color::linear_to_srgb_u8` / assert: round-trips back to `188` ±1 (inverse of the known 0.5-linear→188 midpoint in `render/src/color.rs`).
- `srgb_linearize_endpoints` / unit (pure fn) / input: `srgb_u8_to_linear_f32(0)` and `srgb_u8_to_linear_f32(255)` / assert: `== 0.0` and `== 1.0` exactly.
- `srgba_alpha_channel_stays_linear` / unit (pure fn) / input: `srgb_rgba8_to_linear([0,0,0,128])` / assert: `result[3] ≈ 128/255` (alpha NOT gamma-bent; matches `render/src/color.rs` `bgra_alpha_is_linear_not_srgb`).
- `offscreen_color_format_is_non_srgb_unorm` / unit (pure fn) / input: `color_target_format()` / assert: returns a `*Unorm` (not `*UnormSrgb`) format, so tone-map math stays in linear and sRGB-encode is explicit-in-shader per §11.2.

## P3-T2 — Caps: advertise max_tex_dim, max_texels_per_texture, max_lights_per_viewport, msaa list, color=srgb in tgp;r

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-term/src/tgp/caps.rs` (extend the P1 cap struct): add `max_tex_dim`, `max_texels_per_texture`, `max_lights_per_viewport`, and an `msaa` supported-count list (baseline `{1,4}`; `{2,8}` added only where the adapter reports support — CPU-side a fixed default `{1,4}` for GPU-free tests), and ensure `color=srgb` is emitted. Extend the `tgp;r` serializer to print these fields in the exact §7.1 spelling. The `feat=` token list itself is FROZEN in P1-T6 (the whole v1 set `geom,graph,instance,material,pbr,light,pick,event,explore,anim,bintrailer` is advertised whole — the reply is the contract, P1-T6 `caps_reply_feat_is_frozen_set` pins it). P3 does NOT mutate that wire vocabulary; it ensures the already-advertised `material,pbr,light` tokens are now backed by working implementation (the §11.2/§11.3 material+light path delivered in this phase) and that the `pbr⇒material` implication holds STRUCTURALLY on the parsed accessor (review P3-I7: P3 changes capability behind the tokens, not the token list). NOTE (round-3 review P3-I2): the FULL implication set is owned by P1-T6 per §7.1 (s07 lines 76-77), which lists FIVE rules — `pbr⇒material`, `pick⇒event`, `explore⇒event`, `instance⇒graph`, AND `anim⇒graph` (§7.1 is authoritative and larger than G7 line 70's four-rule paraphrase, which omits `anim⇒graph`); P3 owns and tests ONLY `pbr⇒material` (its material/light scope) and defers the other four to P1-T6. The `feat=` tokens are emitted in §7.1 FROZEN DECLARATION ORDER (`geom,graph,instance,material,pbr,light,pick,event,explore,anim,bintrailer`), NOT ascending byte order (round-3 review P3-I1). Expose `Term::tgp_caps()` returning the struct so tests read the same values the wire prints. NOTE: `max_texels_per_texture` is in §7.1/§15.2 but absent from the G7 prose paraphrase list (see phase notes); G7 line 68 delegates the authoritative enumeration to §7.1/§15.2 (both of which include it), so the G7 bullet list is a non-exhaustive paraphrase, not a positive contradiction — §7.1 is authoritative here (review P3-I4).

**Design refs:** §7.1, §11.4 (`msaa=`), §15.2, G7.

**Depends on:** P1-T6, P1-T10 (handshake-caps). (Review P3-I16: dropped the spurious P3-T1 dependency — the caps serializer emits the literal string `color=srgb` and integer cap fields; it does not read `color.rs`/`target.rs`, so it can proceed in parallel with P3-T1.)

**Tests:**
- `reply_advertises_texture_and_light_caps` / ingress / input: `ESC _ tgp;q;v=2;vmin=1;tok=app-7 ESC \` via `Parser::advance` / assert: `pty_replies` `tgp;r` bytes contain `max_tex_dim=8192`, `max_texels_per_texture=67108864`, `max_lights_per_viewport=16`, `color=srgb`.
- `reply_advertises_msaa_list` / ingress / input: same `tgp;q` / assert: `tgp;r` contains `msaa=1,4` (baseline supported set) and `Term::tgp_caps().msaa == [1,4]`.
- `msaa_serializer_prints_adapter_reported_counts` / unit (pure fn) / input (review P3-I12): construct a `Caps` struct with a STUBBED adapter-supported sample set `{1,2,4,8}` (injected CPU-side, no GPU probe — the `{2,8}` counts are added only where the adapter reports support per §11.4) and run the `tgp;r` msaa-field serializer directly / assert: it prints `msaa=1,2,4,8` in ASCENDING numeric order (exercising the `2`/`8` advertisement-string branch that the GPU-free baseline `{1,4}` never reaches). This is a serializer-only test; live GPU adapter probing is deferred to integration.
- `feat_includes_pbr_material_light_sanity` / ingress / input: same `tgp;q` / assert (review P3-I7 — sanity check, NOT proof P3 mutated the wire): `tgp;r` `feat=` token list contains `material`, `pbr`, and `light` (these are present already from the P1-T6 frozen set; this test guards that the frozen reply is unchanged, while the genuinely P3-specific surface — the implication holding structurally — is `feat_pbr_implies_material_property` below). The `pbr⇒material` implication means the terminal MUST NOT advertise `pbr` without `material` in the same reply.
- `feat_pbr_implies_material_property` / ingress / input: same `tgp;q` / assert (review P3-I15; ORDERING corrected per round-3 review P3-I1): the implication is checked as a STRUCTURAL PROPERTY on the parsed token set, not coincidental co-presence — `Term::tgp_caps().feat.contains("pbr")` implies `Term::tgp_caps().feat.contains("material")` (the assertion is `!feat.contains("pbr") || feat.contains("material")`, evaluated on the accessor that backs the wire), so a regression that drops `material` while retaining `pbr` fails here. Also assert the `feat=` token list is emitted in the **§7.1 FROZEN DECLARATION ORDER** (NOT ascending byte order — round-3 review P3-I1 caught that ascending-byte contradicts §7.1: `light`<`pbr` lexicographically yet `pbr` is emitted first, `event`<`pick` yet `pick` first): the emitted `feat=` substring equals the literal `geom,graph,instance,material,pbr,light,pick,event,explore,anim,bintrailer` (the s07 line 14 frozen sequence) so the serialized reply is byte-stable across runs AND matches the design's fixed total order. The frozen sequence is itself a fixed total order, so byte-stability is preserved while the wire spelling now matches §7.1/G7.
- `caps_accessor_matches_wire` / ingress / input: same `tgp;q` / assert: `Term::tgp_caps().max_lights_per_viewport == 16 ==` the integer printed in the `tgp;r` bytes.

## P3-T3 — Registered material model + table: material.add{model:pbr} decode and storage

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/material.rs` (new): `PbrMaterial { base_color:[f32;4] (linearized on input), metallic:f32, roughness:f32, emissive:[f32;3] (linearized), occlusion_strength:f32, normal_scale:f32, tex_base:Option<TexId>, tex_metal_rough:Option<TexId>, tex_normal:Option<TexId>, tex_emissive:Option<TexId>, tex_occlusion:Option<TexId> }`; `enum MaterialEntry { Default, Pbr(PbrMaterial) }`; `MaterialTable` keyed by `(token, MaterialId u32)`. `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/patch.rs` (extend the P1 op decoder): decode the `material.add` op (`do:"material.add"`). Scalars (metallic / roughness / occlusion / emissive intensity) are NOT color-managed (§11.2); base/emissive colors ARE linearized via P3-T1. Unknown `model` string → `x;code=unsupported;detail=material_model`. `material.add` commits bump `asset_revision` once (per §8.6, materials are assets-class registrations). Expose `Term::tgp_material(token, id)` accessor.

**Design refs:** §11.2, §8.6 (`material.add`, `asset_revision`), §15.3 (`unsupported`).

**Depends on:** P3-T1, P1-T9, P1-T11 (patch decode + apply), P1-T7 (scene model).

**Tests:**
- `material_add_pbr_stores_linearized_basecolor` / ingress / input: CBOR patch `txn=1 ops=[{do:material.add,id:3,model:pbr,base:[188,188,188,255],metallic:0.9,rough:0.3}]` as `enc=b64` APC via `Parser::advance` / assert: `Term::tgp_material(tok,3)` is `Pbr` with `base_color[0..3] ≈ 0.5` (sRGB 188→linear), `metallic == 0.9`, `roughness == 0.3`.
- `material_add_scalars_not_color_managed` / ingress / input: same op with `metallic:1.0,rough:0.5,occlusion_strength:0.7` / assert: `metallic == 1.0`, `roughness == 0.5`, `occlusion_strength == 0.7` stored verbatim (no sRGB linearization applied to scalars).
- `material_add_bumps_asset_revision_once` / ingress / input: read `asset_revision` before; apply a patch with two `material.add` ops in ONE txn / assert: `asset_revision` increased by exactly 1 after the single committed txn.
- `material_add_unknown_model_unsupported` / ingress / input: patch `txn=5 ops=[{do:material.add,id:4,model:"phong"}]` from a handshaked token / assert: `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=...;txn=5;op=0;code=unsupported;detail=material_model` and `Term::tgp_material(tok,4)` is `None` (txn rejected).
- `material_add_unknown_model_rejects_whole_txn_atomically` / ingress / input (review P3-I14): a TWO-op txn `txn=15 ops=[{do:material.add,id:1,model:pbr,base:[255,255,255,255]},{do:material.add,id:4,model:"phong"}]` from a handshaked token — op 0 is valid and would otherwise commit, op 1 is the unknown-model failure / assert: `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;txn=15;op=1;code=unsupported;detail=material_model` (the `op=` index cites the SECOND op), AND `Term::tgp_material(tok,1)` is `None` (the otherwise-valid sibling op rolled back too), AND `asset_revision` is unchanged from before the txn (§8.6 whole-txn atomic reject — nothing commits, no revision bump).
- `material_add_binary_frame_agrees_with_b64` / ingress / input: the same `material.add` payload sent as an `enc=bin` length-prefixed binary frame / assert: `Term::tgp_material(tok,3)` is identical to the b64-decoded result (binary path agrees).

## P3-T4 — PBR emissive/normal/occlusion field decode + defaults + dup_id on re-add

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/material.rs` + `patch.rs`: complete decode of `emissive` (color, linearized), `normal_scale`, `occlusion_strength` fields on `material.add`. Defaults when fields absent (glTF metal-rough defaults): base white `[1,1,1,1]`, metallic `1.0`, roughness `1.0`, emissive black `[0,0,0]`, `occlusion_strength 1.0`, `normal_scale 1.0`. A second `material.add` on an already-committed id → `x;code=dup_id;detail=<id>` (registered materials are immutable-once-committed). Expose the resolved defaults through `Term::tgp_material`.

NOTE (review P3-I3) — material immutability ruling, grounded not extrapolated: §8.4 names the immutability+`dup_id` collision rule for ASSETS explicitly, but the reconciliation spine treats material ids as an ASSETS-CLASS registration uniformly with asset ids on every axis that matters here: G1 namespaces `node/asset/clip/viewport/material ids` together under the creating token; G2 resolves "asset/material/parent refs" identically as late-bound, committed-only refs and pins "the dup-id error and ref resolution check only committed assets"; and §8.6 has `material.add` bump `asset_revision` (not `scene_revision`), the asset-class revision counter. Because the spine wins on conflict (G-invariants), the consistent reading is that material ids inherit §8.4's immutable-once-committed + `dup_id`-on-recommit rule; this is the resolved behavior the terminal emits, NOT a bare extrapolation. The removal/replacement path is the `asset.remove` op delivered by **P3-T4b** (materials are assets-class, so they are removed through the same asset-removal op as mesh/texture assets, freeing the id for a fresh `material.add`); `material.add` is therefore a register-once op, NOT a sparse merge (sparse-merge upsert is `node.upsert`'s job, on nodes, which materials are not). NOTE (review P3-I1): `asset.remove` is NOT in §8.6's closed op set — §8.4/§8.7 require a removal verb ("replace via remove + add") but leave it unnamed, so P3-T4b defines it as a PLAN-DEFINED op pending §8.6 ratification (see the phase note); it is authoritative-by-this-plan, on the same standing as the plan-defined light sub-schema, NOT "proven not invented." CONTINGENT FLIP retained as a documented fallback ONLY: if a future design ruling instead declares `material.add` a sparse-merge op, `material_readd_committed_id_is_dup_id` flips to assert merge semantics (last-write-wins field merge, no `dup_id`, `asset_revision` bumped on the merge) — but the plan-of-record is the grounded `dup_id` branch above, so the closed-enum code the terminal emits is decided.

**Design refs:** §11.2, §8.4 (`dup_id` immutability, "replace via remove + add", assets-class), §8.6 (`material.add`, `asset_revision`; `asset.remove` is the PLAN-DEFINED removal verb delivered by P3-T4b — not in §8.6's closed op set, pending ratification), G1 (material ids namespaced as assets-class), G2 (material refs resolved as assets-class).

**Depends on:** P3-T3, P3-T4b (the `asset.remove` op the removal-path test drives). (build-order: T4b delivers the op T4's test drives; numeric order != topo order — mirrors the P2-T36 build-order note.)

**Tests:**
- `material_defaults_when_fields_absent` / ingress / input: patch `ops=[{do:material.add,id:7,model:pbr}]` (no scalar/color fields) / assert: `Term::tgp_material(tok,7)` has `metallic == 1.0`, `roughness == 1.0`, `emissive == [0,0,0]`, `occlusion_strength == 1.0`, `base_color == [1,1,1,1]`.
- `material_emissive_linearized` / ingress / input: `material.add id:8 emissive:[255,0,0]` / assert: stored `emissive ≈ [1.0,0.0,0.0]` linear (255→1.0).
- `material_readd_committed_id_is_dup_id` / ingress / input: two separate txns each `txn=20 ops=[{do:material.add,id:9,model:pbr,base:[255,0,0,255]}]` then `txn=21 ops=[{do:material.add,id:9,model:pbr,base:[0,255,0,255]}]` / assert: the second txn's `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;txn=21;op=0;code=dup_id;detail=9` and `Term::tgp_material(tok,9)` still has the FIRST material's `base_color` (red, linearized) unchanged (immutable-once-committed; the re-add did not overwrite).
- `material_remove_then_readd_succeeds` / ingress / input (review P3-I1/P3-I3, exercises the P3-T4b removal path): `txn=22 material.add id:9 model:pbr base:[255,0,0,255]` ; then `txn=23 ops=[{do:asset.remove,id:9}]` (the PLAN-DEFINED assets-class removal op delivered by P3-T4b) ; then `txn=24 material.add id:9 model:pbr base:[0,255,0,255]` / assert: after the remove the id is free, the third txn commits with NO `dup_id`, `Term::tgp_material(tok,9)` now has the SECOND (green, linearized) base_color, and `asset_revision` bumped once per committed txn (remove and re-add each bump). This exercises the removal/replacement path against the real op P3-T4b delivers (not against a verb no task produces).

## P3-T4b — `asset.remove` op: PLAN-DEFINED assets-class removal verb (decode + apply + free + asset_revision bump)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/patch.rs` (extend the P1 op decoder): decode and apply a NEW op `{do:"asset.remove", id:<u32>}`. This is the assets-class removal verb that §8.4/§8.7 require ("immutable once committed — replace via remove + add") but never name; §8.6's closed op list omits it. **P3-T4b defines it as a PLAN-DEFINED op pending §8.6 ratification** (same standing as the plan-defined light sub-schema / tone_map spellings; flagged in the phase note for the section author to ratify) — the wire spelling `{do:"asset.remove",id:<u32>}` is authoritative-by-this-plan until ratified. Semantics: free the `(token, id)` entry from BOTH the asset table (mesh/texture, P1-T14) AND the material table (P3-T3) — material ids are assets-class and share the asset id namespace (G1/G11 per-token private map), so one removal op covers both. The commit bumps `asset_revision` once (assets-class counter, §8.6) per committed txn, NOT `scene_revision` (no node changed unless a separate op in the same txn does). Removing an id absent from the bearing token's map is a **no-op** (no error, no revision bump) — mirroring §8.6's `node.remove` "missing id is a no-op" convention. CROSS-TOKEN REMOVAL IS UNREPRESENTABLE (addendum G11): under the ratified PRIVATE per-token id map, a frame with `tok=B` naming id 9 resolves ONLY against B's own map; if B never registered 9, the id is simply absent FROM B's map and the removal is the same missing-id NO-OP as any other (token A's `{A,9}` entry is a distinct map key B cannot index, and is left untouched). There is NO cross-token addressing path, so `asset.remove` NEVER emits `code=denied` (G11(a): `denied` is RESERVED, no reachable ingress trigger) and NEVER leaks whether the id exists under another token (G11(b): no existence oracle — the no-op for "absent in my map" is byte-for-byte identical whether or not the id exists elsewhere). `asset.remove` is part of a txn and obeys whole-txn atomicity (§8.6). It does NOT cascade into nodes that reference the removed material/mesh — a `node.upsert{material:N}` that referenced the now-removed id keeps its stale binding until its own next upsert (removal frees the id for re-registration; it does not rewrite node bindings). Expose nothing new GPU; the existing `Term::tgp_material`/`tgp_texture` accessors return `None` after removal.

**Design refs:** §8.4 ("replace via remove + add", immutability), §8.7 (line 129, "replace via remove + add"), §8.6 (closed op set — `asset.remove` is the plan-defined addition; `asset_revision` bump; missing-id no-op convention from `node.remove`), G1 (token id namespacing) SUPERSEDED BY addendum G11 (private per-token id map: cross-token addressing is unrepresentable; cross-token removal collapses to the missing-id no-op, never `denied`, no existence oracle), G11(a) (`denied` RESERVED), G11(b) (no existence oracle), G12.3 (reachability table: `denied` row 19 RESERVED, no ingress trigger).

**Depends on:** P3-T3 (material table), P1-T9, P1-T11 (patch decode + apply), P1-T14 (asset table / chunked `asset.add`).

**Tests:**
- `asset_remove_frees_material_id` / ingress / input: `txn=30 material.add id:9 model:pbr base:[255,0,0,255]` (commits, records `asset_revision`) then `txn=31 ops=[{do:asset.remove,id:9}]` / assert: `Term::tgp_material(tok,9) == None` after the remove, and `asset_revision` bumped by exactly 1 from the remove txn (the table entry is gone).
- `asset_remove_frees_texture_id` / ingress / input: register a PNG texture `txn=32 asset.add id:11 fmt:png data:<2x2 png>` then `txn=33 ops=[{do:asset.remove,id:11}]` / assert: `Term::tgp_texture(tok,11) == None` after the remove; `asset_revision` bumped by 1 (same removal op frees mesh/texture and material classes alike).
- `asset_remove_missing_id_is_noop` / ingress / input: from a handshaked token with no asset id 77 committed, `txn=34 ops=[{do:asset.remove,id:77}]` / assert: NO `tgp;x` emitted, `asset_revision` UNCHANGED (missing-id no-op, mirroring §8.6 `node.remove`).
- `asset_remove_cross_token_is_noop_coexist` / ingress / input (addendum G11(a)/(b) — rewritten off the now-RESERVED `denied`): token A (handshaked `tok=app-A`) commits `material.add id:9` ; token B (handshaked `tok=app-B`, B never registered id 9) sends `txn=36 ops=[{do:asset.remove,id:9}]` / assert: token B's `pty_replies` contains NO `tgp;x` (no `denied`, no `bad_ref`) and NO `tgp;x;code=denied` of any form — the removal is the missing-id NO-OP because id 9 is absent FROM B's private map (G11(a): cross-token addressing is unrepresentable, `denied` has no ingress trigger); B's `asset_revision` is UNCHANGED (no-op, no bump); AND `Term::tgp_material(A,9)` is STILL present with its original base_color (the distinct `{A,9}` entry is a separate private-map key B cannot index — coexistence, not collision; G11(b): the no-op is byte-for-byte identical whether or not 9 exists under A, so no existence oracle).
- `asset_remove_whole_txn_atomic` / ingress / input: a two-op txn `txn=35 ops=[{do:asset.remove,id:9},{do:material.add,id:9,model:"phong"}]` (op 0 removes committed id 9, op 1 fails with unknown model) / assert: `tgp;x;txn=35;op=1;code=unsupported;detail=material_model`, AND `Term::tgp_material(tok,9)` is STILL the original committed material (the remove rolled back too — whole-txn atomic, §8.6), AND `asset_revision` unchanged from before the txn.

## P3-T5 — Hardened PNG decoder: header-first dimension check vs max_tex_dim, bounded decode vs max_texels_per_texture

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/texture.rs` (new): `pub fn decode_png(bytes:&[u8], caps) -> Result<DecodedTexture, TexError>`. ONE hardened PNG decoder (the only texture path in v1). Reads IHDR width/height FROM THE HEADER BEFORE full decode; if either axis `> max_tex_dim` → `TexError::DimExceeded`. If header-declared `width*height > max_texels_per_texture` → `TexError::TexelsExceeded`. Decode is bounded by the decode/parse-time budget (§15.2/§15.5) to guard decompression bombs: running output checked incrementally, abort + free partial allocation on overflow → `TexError::DecodeBudgetExceeded`. `DecodedTexture { width, height, rgba8:Vec<u8> }` stored sRGB on the wire (linearized at sample time / via texture format choice). Wire-code mapping (review P3-I4/P3-I5 — single, unambiguous): `DimExceeded → parse_error;detail=tex_dim` (per §11.2, NOT `cap_exceeded`); `TexelsExceeded → cap_exceeded;detail=max_texels_per_texture` (advertised-cap-name-in-detail, §7.1/§15.2); `DecodeBudgetExceeded → cap_exceeded` (decompression-bomb locus, §15.5); non-PNG magic → `parse_error;detail=tex_format`. Expose nothing GPU.

**Design refs:** §11.2 (textures v1), §15.2 (texture decode), §15.5 (texture decode loci), §7.1 (`max_tex_dim`, `max_texels_per_texture`).

**Depends on:** P3-T2.

**Tests:**
- `png_valid_2x2_decodes` / unit (pure fn) / input: a hand-built 2x2 RGBA PNG byte literal / assert: `decode_png` returns `DecodedTexture{width:2,height:2,rgba8.len()==16}`.
- `png_over_max_tex_dim_rejected_from_header` / unit (pure fn) / input: a PNG whose IHDR declares `width=9000` (`>8192`) with truncated/empty IDAT / assert: returns `TexError::DimExceeded` BEFORE decoding IDAT (no full decompress attempted); maps to `parse_error;detail=tex_dim`.
- `png_over_max_texels_rejected` / unit (pure fn) / input: PNG IHDR `8192x8193` (texels `> 67108864`, since `8192^2 == cap`) / assert: returns `TexError::TexelsExceeded`, which maps to `cap_exceeded;detail=max_texels_per_texture`.
- `png_decompression_bomb_bounded` / unit (pure fn) / input: PNG header within both dim and declared-texel caps but IDAT that would expand beyond the decode budget / assert: decode aborts with `TexError::DecodeBudgetExceeded` (→ `cap_exceeded`) and the partial allocation is freed (no unbounded growth).
- `non_png_magic_rejected` / unit (pure fn) / input: bytes starting with JPEG magic `0xFFD8` / assert: `decode_png` returns a format error (`parse_error;detail=tex_format`), not a PNG.

## P3-T6 — asset.add fmt=png op: register a PNG texture asset; error-code mapping on ingress

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/patch.rs` + `texture.rs`: handle the `asset.add` op with `fmt="png"` → route bytes through the P3-T5 decoder, register a `TextureAsset` in the asset table keyed by `(token, asset id u32)`. A non-PNG byte asset declared `fmt=png` → `x;code=parse_error;detail=tex_format`, rejecting the WHOLE txn atomically (§8.6). A texture over `max_tex_dim` → `x;code=parse_error;detail=tex_dim` (whole-txn reject). A texture whose header-declared texels exceed `max_texels_per_texture` → `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_texels_per_texture` (whole-txn reject — review P3-I5). `asset.add` commit bumps `asset_revision` once. Works under chunked `asset.add` (`more=1/0`) per §8.7 since textures can exceed one frame. Expose `Term::tgp_texture(token, id) -> Option<{width,height}>`.

**Design refs:** §11.2 (`asset.add fmt=png`), §8.6, §8.7 (chunking), §15.3.

**Depends on:** P3-T5, P1-T9, P1-T11 (patch decode + apply), P1-T14 (asset table / chunked `asset.add`).

**Tests:**
- `asset_add_png_registers_texture` / ingress / input: patch `txn=2 ops=[{do:asset.add,id:8,fmt:png,data:<2x2 png bytes>}]` `enc=b64` / assert: `Term::tgp_texture(tok,8) == Some{width:2,height:2}` and `asset_revision` bumped by 1.
- `asset_add_non_png_bytes_parse_error_tex_format` / ingress / input: patch `txn=3 ops=[{do:asset.add,id:9,fmt:png,data:<jpeg magic bytes>}]` from a handshaked token / assert: `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;txn=3;op=0;code=parse_error;detail=tex_format`; `Term::tgp_texture(tok,9) == None` (whole txn rejected).
- `asset_add_oversize_png_parse_error_tex_dim` / ingress / input: patch `txn=4 asset.add id:10 fmt:png data` declaring `9000px` width / assert: `tgp;x;txn=4;code=parse_error;detail=tex_dim` and the txn is rejected atomically (no texture, no `asset_revision` bump).
- `asset_add_over_texels_cap_exceeded` / ingress / input: patch `txn=8 asset.add id:14 fmt:png data` whose IHDR declares texels over `max_texels_per_texture` (e.g. `8192x8193`) / assert: `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;txn=8;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_texels_per_texture`; `Term::tgp_texture(tok,14) == None`.
- `asset_add_png_chunked_commits_on_more0` / ingress / input: two `enc=bin` chunk frames keyed asset id 11 (`more=1` then `more=0`) carrying a PNG split in half / assert: `Term::tgp_texture(tok,11)` registered ONLY after the `more=0` frame; `asset_revision` bumped exactly once.

## P3-T7 — GLB-embedded PNG texture extraction in the TGP GLB loader (one shared PNG decoder)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/glb_loader.rs` (extend the P1 hardened full-mesh-set TGP loader — NOT the legacy `rgp/glb_loader.rs`, G8): extract embedded image bytes referenced by glTF material texture slots, route them through the SAME P3-T5 `decode_png`. Embedded JPEG / standalone JPEG / raw RGBA / KTX2 are deferred → `x;code=parse_error;detail=tex_format`. Map the glTF metal-rough material → `PbrMaterial` with embedded textures registered as implicit `TextureAsset`s. Honor `max_tex_dim`/`max_texels_per_texture` per embedded image (same code mapping as P3-T5/T6: dim → `parse_error;detail=tex_dim`, texels → `cap_exceeded;detail=max_texels_per_texture`). Reject non-PNG embedded image → `parse_error;detail=tex_format`, rejecting the whole `asset.add` txn atomically.

**Design refs:** §11.2 (PNG embedded in GLB, one hardened decoder), §15.5, G8 (no legacy reuse).

**Depends on:** P3-T5, P3-T3, P1-T15 (TGP GLB loader).

**Tests:**
- `glb_embedded_png_extracted` / ingress / input: `asset.add id:12 fmt:glb data=<GLB embedding a 2x2 PNG baseColor texture>` / assert: the resulting PBR material has `tex_base` set and `Term::tgp_texture` for the implicit tex id reports `{2,2}`.
- `glb_embedded_jpeg_rejected` / ingress / input: `asset.add id:13 fmt:glb` embedding a JPEG image / assert: `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;code=parse_error;detail=tex_format` (embedded JPEG deferred) and the asset txn is rejected.
- `glb_embedded_png_over_dim_rejected` / ingress / input: GLB embedding a PNG declaring width `9000` / assert: `tgp;x;code=parse_error;detail=tex_dim`; no asset committed.

## P3-T8 — node.upsert{material} binding + bad_ref ref resolution within txn (cross-token ref is bad_ref, no existence oracle — G11)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/patch.rs`: `node.upsert` with `material:<id>` binds a Mesh/Instanced node to a registered `MaterialId` (committed-only ref, order-independent within txn per §8.6). `material:null` clears back to default (`MaterialId 0`). A material ref unresolved in the BEARING TOKEN's private map → `x;code=bad_ref;detail=material:<id>`, whole-txn reject citing the first offending op. A material's `tex_*` ref unresolved in the bearing token's map → `x;code=bad_ref;detail=tex:<id>`. CROSS-TOKEN REFERENCE IS UNREPRESENTABLE (addendum G11, SUPERSEDES the base-spine `denied`-on-cross-token-reference text in G1/§8.4): under the PRIVATE per-token id map, a frame with `tok=B` referencing `material:3` resolves 3 ONLY against B's own map; an id that exists only under another token A is simply ABSENT FROM B's map, so it draws the SAME `bad_ref;detail=material:3` as an id absent everywhere (G11(b): no existence oracle — the two cases are byte-for-byte indistinguishable; B's resolver never consults A's map). The terminal therefore NEVER emits `code=denied` on a cross-token ref (G11(a): `denied` RESERVED, no reachable ingress trigger; the base-spine `forbidden→denied` synonym is moot here because no cross-token ATTEMPT is representable). Setting material on a node bumps `scene_revision` + the node's transform/material dirty flag (NOT `asset_revision` — G2). Expose the bound material id via `Term::tgp_scene()` node accessor `node.material_id()`.

**Design refs:** §8.6 (ref resolution, sparse merge), §8.4 (committed-only ref resolution; its `denied`-on-cross-token text SUPERSEDED by G11), G2 (late-bound refs within txn), G1 (token id namespacing) SUPERSEDED BY addendum G11 (private per-token map: cross-token ref unrepresentable → resolves to `bad_ref` against the bearing token's own map, no existence oracle), G11(a) (`denied` RESERVED), G11(b) (no existence oracle — cross-token-absent ref is indistinguishable from absent-everywhere), G12.3 (row 6 `bad_ref` REACHABLE: unresolved in bearing token's map; row 19 `denied` RESERVED), §11.2.

**Depends on:** P3-T3, P3-T6, P1-T7 (scene model), P1-T12 (`node.upsert` sparse merge + null clear), P1-T11 (apply engine / ref resolution).

**Tests:**
- `node_upsert_binds_material` / ingress / input: patch `txn=6 ops=[{do:material.add,id:3,model:pbr,...},{do:node.upsert,id:"box",mesh:1,material:3}]` / assert: `Term::tgp_scene()` node `"box"` `.material_id() == Some(3)`; `scene_revision` bumped; `asset_revision` bumped ONLY by the `material.add`.
- `node_upsert_material_forward_ref_in_same_txn` / ingress / input: the same two ops but `node.upsert` BEFORE `material.add` in the op list / assert: still binds (order-independent within txn); `node.material_id() == Some(3)`.
- `node_upsert_material_missing_bad_ref` / ingress / input: patch `txn=7 ops=[{do:node.upsert,id:"box",mesh:1,material:99}]` (99 not committed) / assert: `pty_replies` `tgp;x;txn=7;op=0;code=bad_ref;detail=material:99`; node `"box"` not created (txn rejected).
- `material_tex_missing_bad_ref_tex` / ingress / input: `material.add id:3 model:pbr tex_base:50` where asset 50 never committed / assert: `tgp;x;code=bad_ref;detail=tex:50`.
- `node_upsert_material_null_clears_to_default` / ingress / input: bind `material:3` then a later txn `node.upsert id:"box" material:null` / assert: `node.material_id() == None` (back to default `MaterialId 0`).
- `cross_token_material_ref_is_bad_ref_no_oracle` / ingress / input (addendum G11(b) — rewritten off the now-RESERVED `denied`): token A (handshaked `tok=app-A`) commits `material.add id:3` ; token B (handshaked `tok=app-B`, B has NOT committed id 3) sends `txn=9 ops=[{do:node.upsert,id:"box",mesh:1,material:3}]` / assert: token B's `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;txn=9;op=0;code=bad_ref;detail=material:3` (NOT `denied` — `denied` has no reachable ingress trigger under G11(a); id 3 is simply absent from B's private map), B's node `"box"` is NOT created (whole-txn reject, G2), AND `Term::tgp_material(A,3)` is untouched (A's `{A,3}` is a distinct private-map key; coexistence). NO-EXISTENCE-ORACLE assertion (G11(b)): the emitted bytes are BYTE-FOR-BYTE identical to the control case where NO token anywhere ever created id 3 — i.e. run the same `txn=9` from a token C in a session where id 3 was never registered by anyone, and assert C's `tgp;x` bytes (sans `tok=`) equal B's, so `bad_ref` cannot be used to detect that A holds id 3.

## P3-T9 — Light node kind: node.upsert{light} decode (directional/point/spot, color+intensity)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` + `patch.rs`: add `Light` to the node-kind enum (`Group|Mesh|Instanced|Light|Camera`). `LightData { kind: Directional|Point|Spot, color:[f32;3] (sRGB→linear on input), intensity:f32 (scalar, NOT color-managed), range:Option<f32>, spot_inner/spot_outer angles }`. `node.upsert` with a `light:{...}` selector creates a `Light` node (KHR_lights_punctual style); lights are nodes so they inherit TRS and animate (§11.3). Kind-immutability per §8.6: a later upsert implying a different kind → `x;code=kind_conflict`. Expose `Term::tgp_scene()` node `.light_data()` and confirm `Light` nodes get a computed WORLD matrix like any node (so a parented headlight's world direction is queryable CPU-side). NOTE (review P3-I9): the light-selector CBOR sub-schema (`type` key, `directional|point|spot` spelling, `range`, `spot_inner`/`spot_outer` names) is plan-defined pending design confirmation — §11.3 leaves the wire encoding open; the test input bytes below are authoritative for this choice.

**Design refs:** §11.3 (lights as nodes), §8.6 (kind selector, `kind_conflict`).

**Depends on:** P3-T1, P1-T9, P1-T11 (patch decode + apply), P1-T8 (world matrix), P1-T12 (`node.upsert` kind-immutability rule the `light_kind_change_conflict` test drives — review P3-I6).

**Tests:**
- `node_upsert_light_creates_light_node` / ingress / input: patch `ops=[{do:node.upsert,id:"sun",light:{type:directional,color:[255,255,255],intensity:2.0}}]` / assert: `Term::tgp_scene()` node `"sun"` `kind == Light`; `light_data().kind == Directional`; `color ≈ [1,1,1]` linear; `intensity == 2.0`.
- `light_color_linearized_intensity_not` / ingress / input: `light color:[188,188,188] intensity:0.5` / assert: `color ≈ [0.5,0.5,0.5]` linear (188→0.5); `intensity == 0.5` verbatim (scalar, not color-managed).
- `light_inherits_parent_transform_world` / ingress / input: `node.upsert "car" trs{translate:[10,0,0]}` ; `node.upsert "headlight" parent:"car" light:{type:spot,color:[255,255,255],intensity:1.0,range:5.0,spot_inner:0.1,spot_outer:0.4}` / assert: `Term::tgp_scene()` `"headlight"` WORLD matrix translation `== [10,0,0]` (inherited).
- `light_spot_angles_and_range_decode` / ingress / input (review P3-I9-b): CBOR patch `ops=[{do:node.upsert,id:"spot1",light:{type:spot,color:[255,255,255],intensity:1.0,range:12.5,spot_inner:0.2,spot_outer:0.5}}]` as `enc=b64` APC via `Parser::advance` / assert: `Term::tgp_scene()` node `"spot1"` `light_data().kind == Spot`, `range == Some(12.5)`, `spot_inner ≈ 0.2`, `spot_outer ≈ 0.5` (the plan-defined `range`/`spot_inner`/`spot_outer` wire names decode to the declared fields). This pins the previously-uncovered spot sub-schema with concrete bytes.
- `light_directional_range_and_spot_angles_absent` / ingress / input (review P3-I9-b): `ops=[{do:node.upsert,id:"sun2",light:{type:directional,color:[255,255,255],intensity:1.0}}]` (no `range`/`spot_inner`/`spot_outer`) / assert: `light_data().kind == Directional`, `range == None`, and the spot angles are absent/default (a non-spot light carries no spot cone), confirming the optional sub-fields default correctly when elided.
- `light_kind_change_conflict` / ingress / input: create `"sun"` as light then upsert `"sun" mesh:1` / assert: `tgp;x;code=kind_conflict` (change kind via remove+add).

## P3-T10 — Per-viewport effective light set + replace-not-augment switch (per visible subtree)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/lighting.rs` (new, CPU-side selection — GPU consumes) + a `crates/toastty-term` viewport accessor: compute the effective light set for a viewport per frame from its visible subtree (the viewport's `root` subtree, defaulting to the whole scene per §10.5 / G2). If that set contains ≥1 `Light` node, the built-in key+ambient+hemisphere is DISABLED for that viewport (replace-not-augment, §11.3) and default-material nodes are lit ONLY by registered lights through the SAME forward loop as PBR. Two viewports sharing one scene differ when their roots include/exclude the light. Expose `Term::tgp_viewport_lighting(vp_id) -> { registered_lights:Vec<NodeId>, default_light_active:bool }` computed CPU-side without a GPU.

**Design refs:** §11.3 (replace not augment, per visible subtree), §11.1 (forward loop), §10.5 (root subtree).

**Depends on:** P3-T9, P2-T1 (multi-viewport store), P1-T8 (world matrix).

**Tests:**
- `no_light_keeps_default_lighting` / ingress / input: scene with one mesh, one viewport, no `Light` node / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_lighting(vp).default_light_active == true`; `registered_lights` empty.
- `one_light_in_subtree_disables_default` / ingress / input: add a `Light` node inside the viewport's `root` subtree / assert: `default_light_active == false`; `registered_lights == ["sun"]`.
- `two_viewports_differ_by_root_subtree` / ingress / input: viewport A `root`=subtree containing the light; viewport B `root`=a sibling subtree excluding it / assert: `A.default_light_active == false`, `B.default_light_active == true` (per-subtree, not global).
