## P3-T11 — render.keep_default_light flag (additive fill) + deterministic over-cap light selection

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/lighting.rs` + viewport `RenderOpts`: `render.keep_default_light` (default false) keeps the built-in key+ambient+hemisphere on ADDITIVELY alongside registered lights. Over-cap selection: when the effective set exceeds `max_lights_per_viewport`, DETERMINISTICALLY select the N highest-intensity lights (by `light.intensity` scalar; ties broken by ASCENDING node-id lexicographic order) and ignore the rest for that frame — never crash, never nondeterministic (§11.3). The over-cap patch is NOT rejected (animated lights drift into view; rejecting would violate app-authoritative principle). Expose the selected/dropped split through `Term::tgp_viewport_lighting` so tests assert the deterministic choice.

**Design refs:** §11.3 (`keep_default_light`, deterministic selection, cap), G9.

**Depends on:** P3-T10, P3-T2.

**Tests:**
- `keep_default_light_additive` / ingress / input: vp update `render.keep_default_light=true` with one registered light in subtree / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_lighting(vp).default_light_active == true` AND `registered_lights == [the light]` (both on).
- `over_cap_selects_highest_intensity` / ingress / input: `max_lights_per_viewport` advertised small (e.g. `cap=2` in test caps); add 3 lights intensity `5,3,1` in subtree / assert: `tgp_viewport_lighting` selected set `==` the intensity-5 and intensity-3 lights; the intensity-1 light dropped; patch NOT rejected (all 3 nodes present in `tgp_scene()`).
- `over_cap_tie_broken_by_node_id_ascending` / ingress / input: `cap=1`; two lights id `"a"` and id `"b"` both intensity `4.0` / assert: selected `== ["a"]` (lexicographic ascending tie-break), deterministic across runs.

## P3-T12 — One-time-per-viewport cap_exceeded latch for light over-cap (lifecycle event)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-term/src/tgp/lighting_latch.rs` (new) + viewport state: going over `max_lights_per_viewport` queues a ONE-TIME-PER-VIEWPORT `x;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_lights_per_viewport`, routed only to the owning token and only if that token handshaked (§7, §15.3, G5). The latch clears (and may re-fire once) when the visible light count drops back under cap. The over-cap patch itself still commits (T11). Expose `Term::tgp_viewport_light_latch(vp) -> bool` for test assertion. Latch transitions are evaluated per frame against the deterministic injectable clock / render tick (G6).

**Design refs:** §11.3 (one-time-per-viewport `cap_exceeded`), §15.3 (gating), G5.

**Depends on:** P3-T11, P1-T5 (clock), P1-T6, P1-T10 (handshake-caps), P1-T13 (`node.remove` the `latch_clears_and_can_refire` test drives to drop a light under cap — review P3-I6).

**Tests:**
- `over_cap_fires_once` / ingress / input: `cap=1`; add 2 lights to subtree then advance a render tick twice / assert: `pty_replies` contains EXACTLY ONE `tgp;x;vp=<id>;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_lights_per_viewport` (not re-fired on the second tick); `latch == true`.
- `latch_clears_and_can_refire` / ingress / input: after firing, `node.remove` one light (back under cap) → tick → then add 2 lights again → tick / assert: `latch == false` after the drop; then a SECOND `cap_exceeded` is queued on re-exceeding (re-fire once).
- `tokenless_writer_gets_no_cap_event` / ingress / input: over-cap lights added via a frame whose token never handshaked / assert: NO `tgp;x` emitted (gated on handshake, G5); scene still safe (lights selected deterministically).

## P3-T13 — Theme-tint: render.theme_tint flag + pre-lighting default-material-input stage

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/theme_tint.rs` (new, CPU-resolvable resolver) + viewport `RenderOpts.theme_tint` (default false): when on, multiply the terminal's active DEFAULT-FOREGROUND color (read once per frame, linearized) into the default material's diffuse base, as a pre-lighting material-INPUT stage (§11.1 ordering, `strength` fixed `1.0` in v1). Applies ONLY to `MaterialId 0` (default-material) nodes whose per-node tint is the neutral default white `[255,255,255,255]`; a node with an explicit non-white tint is left untouched; never touches PBR nodes or registered material baseColor. `effective_diffuse_linear = base_linear × lerp(white, fg_linear, 1.0)`. The palette source is the terminal's active DEFAULT foreground (§11.2) — NOT an SGR-set fg (`CSI 3x m`) and not one of the 16 ANSI indices and not the background. Expose `Term::tgp_node_effective_diffuse(vp, node) -> [f32;3]` computed CPU-side so the stage is testable without a GPU. NOTE (review P3-I11): the default-fg test seam is an OSC 10 sequence (`ESC ] 10 ; <color> ESC \`) fed via `Parser::advance`, NOT an SGR set.

**Design refs:** §11.2 (theme-tint), §11.1 (ordering: pre-lighting, default-material only).

**Depends on:** P3-T1, P3-T8, P2-T1 (multi-viewport store).

**Tests:**
- `theme_tint_off_by_default` / ingress / input: default-material node, `render.theme_tint` not set, default fg = blue (via OSC 10) / assert: `Term::tgp_node_effective_diffuse == base` (no tint applied).
- `theme_tint_multiplies_fg_into_default_material` / ingress / input: `render.theme_tint=true`; terminal default fg set to a known color via OSC 10; a white-base default-material node with neutral tint / assert: `effective_diffuse == fg_linear` (white × `lerp(white,fg,1.0)`).
- `theme_tint_skips_explicit_non_white_tint` / ingress / input: `theme_tint=true`; default-material node with per-node tint `[255,0,0,255]` (red); fg=blue / assert: `effective_diffuse` stays red-derived (deliberate red sphere never recolored).
- `theme_tint_never_touches_pbr` / ingress / input: `theme_tint=true`; node bound to a PBR material with base `[0.2,0.2,0.2]` / assert: the PBR baseColor input is unchanged (`tgp_node_effective_diffuse` returns the registered baseColor, no fg multiply).
- `theme_tint_ignores_sgr_fg` / ingress / input: `theme_tint=true`; default fg set via OSC 10 to blue, then an SGR set-foreground (`CSI 31 m`, red) is applied; white-base neutral-tint default-material node / assert: `effective_diffuse == blue_linear` (the SGR fg does NOT feed theme-tint; only the default fg does, §11.2).

## P3-T14 — render.tone_map operator (none/Reinhard/ACES) over the whole linear target

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/tonemap.rs` (new): `enum ToneMap { None, Reinhard, Aces }` with pure CPU reference fns `tone_map(op, linear_rgb) -> linear_rgb` so the stage is GPU-free testable. Decode `render.tone_map` on the vp frame (default `None` = clamp-only passthrough). Applied UNIFORMLY to the WHOLE linear offscreen color target after lighting, before sRGB-encode (§11.1 ordering); never per-node. With `None`, default-material colors unchanged; Reinhard/ACES map ALL nodes identically (mixing PBR never silently alters default-material color unless an operator is chosen). Unknown operator string → SILENTLY default to `None` (review P3-I2: tone_map is a §11.4 viewport quality knob, parity with the msaa silent-clamp; NOT an error, NO `tgp;x` emitted). Wire value spelling `none|reinhard|aces` is decoded CASE-INSENSITIVELY (review P3-I8: lowercase is plan-chosen pending design confirmation). Expose `Term::tgp_viewport_render_opts(vp).tone_map`.

**Design refs:** §11.4 (tone-map none/Reinhard/ACES, whole target), §11.1 (ordering), §11.2 (sRGB-encode after).

**Depends on:** P3-T1, P2-T1 (multi-viewport store).

**Tests:**
- `tone_map_default_none` / ingress / input: vp create without `render.tone_map` / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_render_opts(vp).tone_map == ToneMap::None`.
- `tone_map_none_is_passthrough_clamp` / unit (pure fn) / input: `tone_map(None, [0.5,0.5,0.5])` / assert: `== [0.5,0.5,0.5]`; `tone_map(None,[2.0,..])` clamps high values only.
- `tone_map_reinhard_monotone_compress` / unit (pure fn) / input: `tone_map(Reinhard, [4.0,0,0])` / assert: `result[0] < 1.0` and `< input` (compresses HDR); deterministic value matches the Reinhard formula `x/(1+x)`.
- `tone_map_set_via_vp_frame` / ingress / input: `ESC _ tgp;vp;tok=A;vp=2;render.tone_map=aces;... ESC \` / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_render_opts(2).tone_map == ToneMap::Aces`.
- `tone_map_unknown_operator_silent_none` / ingress / input: vp frame `render.tone_map=filmic` (unknown) / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_render_opts(2).tone_map == ToneMap::None` AND NO `tgp;x` is emitted (quality-knob silent fallback, review P3-I2).
- `tone_map_value_case_insensitive` / ingress / input: vp frame `render.tone_map=ACES` (uppercase) / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_render_opts(2).tone_map == ToneMap::Aces` (decode is case-insensitive).

## P3-T15 — Per-viewport MSAA: render.msaa decode, clamp-down to supported set, ack-report effective count

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-term/src/tgp` viewport + `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/target.rs`: `render.msaa` per-viewport, default 4x. A requested count NOT in the supported set (P3-T2 `msaa` list) is silently clamped DOWN to the nearest supported count `≤` request (never up, never an error — quality knob). The effective clamped count is reported via the viewport ack `a;vp=<id>;msaa=<n>` to ANY HANDSHAKED token. CONTRADICTION RESOLVED (review P3-I5): §11.4 (s11 line 76) says the ack is reported "when the app subscribed to acks," implying an "ack subscription" concept — the spine G4 (line 48) states the opposite and WINS: acks are available to any handshaked token, never gated on `sub`. This task deliberately follows G4 and does NOT implement an ack-subscription gate; a future reader MUST NOT "fix" the test back toward §11.4's "subscribed to acks" phrasing. Expose `Term::tgp_viewport_render_opts(vp).effective_msaa`.

**Design refs:** §11.4 (MSAA per-viewport, clamp down, ack report — its "subscribed to acks" wording is overridden by G4), G4 (acks available to any handshaked token, not gated on `sub`).

**Depends on:** P3-T2, P2-T1 (multi-viewport store), P2-T6 (per-viewport offscreen color+depth targets this task extends with MSAA).

**Tests:**
- `msaa_default_4x` / ingress / input: vp create without `render.msaa` / assert: `effective_msaa == 4`.
- `msaa_requested_supported_kept` / ingress / input: vp `render.msaa=1` (supported) / assert: `effective_msaa == 1`.
- `msaa_unsupported_clamps_down` / ingress / input: supported `{1,4}`; vp `render.msaa=2` then a separate vp `render.msaa=8` / assert: `msaa=2 → effective 1` (nearest `≤2` in `{1,4}`); `msaa=8 → effective 4` (nearest `≤8`); neither errors.
- `msaa_effective_reported_in_ack` / ingress / input: handshaked token vp frame with `render.msaa=8` (clamps to 4) / assert: `pty_replies` contains `tgp;a;vp=<id>;msaa=4`.
- `msaa_ack_gated_on_handshake_not_sub` / ingress / input: a never-handshaked (tokenless) writer's vp frame with `render.msaa=8` (clamps to 4) / assert: NO `tgp;a` emitted (ack gated on handshake per G4, mirroring the tokenless negative test in T12); the clamp still applies (`effective_msaa == 4`).

## P3-T16 — Pick target ALWAYS 1x invariant: never MSAA-resolved, never tone-mapped (CPU-resolvable, agrees with GPU)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/target.rs` + `crates/toastty-graphics` pick path: the pick target is ALWAYS single-sample (1x) regardless of the viewport's `render.msaa`, and is NEVER MSAA-resolved and NEVER tone-mapped (§11.4). The CPU-resolvable pick buffer (P2, §12.4) is authoritative; the optional GPU color-ID pass must agree. Enforce that node-id + instance-index colors are written/read unmodified (nearest/point). Expose `Term::tgp_pick_target_samples(vp)` (must be `1` always) and ensure the existing P2 CPU pick result is unaffected by `render.msaa`/`render.tone_map`.

**Design refs:** §11.4 (pick always 1x, not resolved, not tone-mapped), §12.4 (CPU pick authoritative), G6.

**Depends on:** P3-T15, P3-T14, P2-T20 (CPU-resolvable picking), P2-T21 (sub decode), P2-T23 (event wire emit), P2-T24 (synthetic pointer injection — the interaction seam the `cpu_pick_*` tests drive), P1-T5 (clock).

**Tests:**
- `pick_target_always_1x_regardless_of_msaa` / ingress / input: vp with `render.msaa=4` and an active sub (pick enabled) / assert: `Term::tgp_pick_target_samples(vp) == 1` (never 4).
- `cpu_pick_unaffected_by_tone_map` / interaction / input: vp `render.tone_map=aces`; establish a live pick sub on the viewport first, advance the deterministic clock explicitly, then inject a synthetic pointer click over a known node via the `Term` input API / assert: the CPU pick resolves the same `(node,inst,world-point)` as with `tone_map=none` (pick not tone-mapped); the resulting `tgp;e;ev=click;node=...` is identical.
- `cpu_pick_unaffected_by_msaa` / interaction / input: same click flow (live sub + explicit clock advance) with `render.msaa=8` vs `1` / assert: identical `(node,inst)` reported in `tgp;e` — id colors never averaged.

## P3-T17 — VRAM accounting for MSAA color+depth + resolve (color only) + always-1x pick targets (two-phase, pre-upload)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-term/src/tgp/vram.rs` (extend the P1/P2 CPU-side two-phase accounting): a viewport's VRAM charge is `pixels × samples × (color_bytes + depth_bytes)` for the multisampled target, PLUS the 1x resolve target charged as `pixels × 1 × color_bytes` (COLOR ONLY — review P3-I1 / §11.1 line 19 "MSAA resolve (color target only)"; the resolve target carries NO depth), PLUS the always-1x pick target `pixels × 1 × (color_bytes + depth_bytes)` (when picking is active). Texture-asset bytes (P3-T5/T6) and PBR material textures count once (shared assets counted once). A material/texture/MSAA allocation whose net delta exceeds `max_vram_mb_session` rejects the WHOLE txn with `x;code=vram_exhausted`, committing nothing and not bumping revisions (§15.2). Expose `Term::tgp_vram_used()` and `Term::tgp_viewport_vram(vp)` computed CPU-side (GPU never queried).

**Design refs:** §11.4 (VRAM accounting: msaa + resolve + pick), §11.1 (resolve = color target only), §15.2 (two-phase CPU VRAM, `vram_exhausted`).

**Depends on:** P3-T15, P3-T16, P3-T6, P2-T1 (multi-viewport store), P2-T6 (offscreen targets whose VRAM this task accounts for).

**Tests:**
- `vram_charge_includes_msaa_multiplier_resolve_color_only_and_pick` / ingress / input: vp of known pixel size with `render.msaa=4` and an active pick sub / assert: `Term::tgp_viewport_vram(vp) == pixels×4×(color+depth)` [msaa] `+ pixels×1×color` [resolve, COLOR ONLY — excludes depth] `+ pixels×1×(color+depth)` [pick] (exact formula match; the resolve term MUST exclude the depth bytes).
- `shared_texture_counted_once` / ingress / input: two materials referencing the same texture asset id / assert: `Term::tgp_vram_used()` counts the texture bytes once, not twice.
- `texture_over_vram_rejects_txn_vram_exhausted` / ingress / input: test caps with a tiny `max_vram_mb_session`; `asset.add png` whose texels exceed remaining VRAM in a txn / assert: `pty_replies` `tgp;x;code=vram_exhausted`; `Term::tgp_texture == None`; `asset_revision` unchanged (whole-txn reject, nothing committed).

## P3-T18 — RIS / RGP-independence lifecycle: teardown of materials, textures, light state, and MSAA/pick targets across all tokens

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-term/src/tgp` lifecycle path (extend the P1 RIS teardown): RIS (`ESC c`) tears down the full TGP scene including the material table, texture assets, `Light` nodes, per-viewport MSAA/resolve/pick targets, and light-latch state across ALL tokens, returning `Term::tgp_vram_used()` to 0 and invalidating session tokens (§10.7 / G10). DECSTR leaves it intact. Confirm the TGP material/texture/light path is fully independent of the legacy `rgp/` path (G8) — no shared table mutation. Device-loss recovery (review P3-I13): on a synthetic device-loss event the CPU scene is the source of truth (G10) — the terminal force-recomputes every per-viewport MSAA/resolve/pick target from the CPU-side viewport state IGNORING dirty gating (so the new P3-T15/T16/T17 targets are rebuilt, not skipped) and pushes an UNSOLICITED `tgp;r` to each handshaked token (G7/G10); the recomputed CPU-side VRAM/target state matches its pre-loss formula exactly. Expose a synthetic device-loss injection hook (`Term::tgp_inject_device_loss()`, test-only) and confirm recovery via the existing `Term::tgp_viewport_vram`/`tgp_pick_target_samples` accessors (no GPU queried).

**Design refs:** §10.7 (RIS teardown), §11.4 (MSAA/resolve/pick targets), G10 (device-loss force re-upload ignoring dirty), G7 (unsolicited `tgp;r` to handshaked tokens on device-loss), G8 (RGP independence).

**Depends on:** P3-T8, P3-T12, P3-T15, P3-T16, P3-T17, P2-T37 (GPU device-loss recovery this task extends to the new MSAA/resolve/pick targets), P1-T5 (clock).

**Tests:**
- `ris_tears_down_materials_textures_lights` / lifecycle / input: build a scene with a PBR material, a png texture, a `Light` node, an MSAA viewport with pick; then feed `ESC c` (RIS) via `Parser::advance` / assert: `Term::tgp_material`/`tgp_texture` return `None`; no `Light` nodes in `tgp_scene()`; `Term::tgp_vram_used() == 0`; viewport targets freed.
- `decstr_leaves_tgp_intact` / lifecycle / input: same scene then `ESC [ ! p` (DECSTR) / assert: material/texture/light state and `vram_used` unchanged.
- `tgp_material_path_independent_of_rgp` / ingress / input: register a TGP PBR material id 3; inspect the legacy RGP scene state / assert: the legacy `rgp` material registry is untouched (TGP writes only the `tgp/` tables; G8 clean break).
- `device_loss_rebuilds_targets_and_pushes_tgp_r` / lifecycle / input (review P3-I13): build a handshaked-token scene with an MSAA viewport (`render.msaa=4`) of known pixel size with an active pick sub; record `Term::tgp_viewport_vram(vp)` and `Term::tgp_pick_target_samples(vp)`; then call `Term::tgp_inject_device_loss()` (synthetic, no GPU) / assert: after recovery `Term::tgp_viewport_vram(vp)` equals the SAME pre-loss formula value (msaa + resolve-color-only + pick targets recomputed from CPU state, ignoring dirty gating per G10), `Term::tgp_pick_target_samples(vp) == 1`, and `pty_replies` contains an UNSOLICITED `tgp;r` (with the handshaked `tok=`) pushed on device-loss per G7/G10; the CPU scene (materials/textures/lights) is unchanged (source of truth survives device-loss).

## P3-T19 — Default-material color chain: brightness clamp [0,8] + registered-light forward-loop shading (CPU reference)

**Deliverable:** `crates/toastty-render/src/tgp/default_material.rs` (extend the P1 default material with the §11.2 color chain as a CPU-resolvable reference): `pub fn default_material_final_linear(base_linear, tint_linear, brightness, lit_term_linear) -> [f32;3]` implementing `final_linear = clamp(base_linear × tint_linear × brightness, 0, large) × lit_term`, where `brightness` is a continuous gain clamped SILENTLY to `[0.0, 8.0]` (never an error, §11.2). Add a CPU forward-lighting reference `pub fn shade_default_material(node, lights, opts) -> [f32;3]` that, when the viewport has ≥1 registered light (P3-T10), shades the default material through the SAME forward loop as PBR using ONLY the registered lights (no built-in key/ambient/hemisphere unless `keep_default_light`), and otherwise uses the pinned built-in key+ambient+hemisphere constants (§11.2). Expose `Term::tgp_node_default_shade(vp, node) -> [f32;3]` and `Term::tgp_node_brightness(vp, node) -> f32` (post-clamp) computed CPU-side. This closes the §11.2 brightness-clamp and §11.3 "default material lit by registered lights" surfaces flagged as uncovered in the review (no flag-only test).

**Design refs:** §11.2 (default-material color chain, brightness clamp `[0,8]`), §11.3 (default-material lit by registered lights through the same forward loop), §11.1 (ordering).

**Depends on:** P3-T13, P3-T11, P3-T1, P2-T1 (multi-viewport store).

**Tests:**
- `brightness_clamps_high_silently` / ingress / input: default-material node with `brightness=9.0` (over cap) / assert: `Term::tgp_node_brightness(vp,node) == 8.0` (clamped to upper bound); NO `tgp;x` emitted.
- `brightness_clamps_low_silently` / ingress / input: default-material node with `brightness=-1.0` / assert: `Term::tgp_node_brightness(vp,node) == 0.0` (clamped to lower bound); NO `tgp;x`.
- `brightness_in_range_passthrough` / ingress / input: `brightness=2.5` / assert: `Term::tgp_node_brightness(vp,node) == 2.5` (in `[0,8]`, verbatim).
- `default_material_color_chain_pure` / unit (pure fn) / input: `default_material_final_linear([0.5,0.5,0.5],[1,1,1],2.0,[1,1,1])` / assert: `== [1.0,1.0,1.0]` (base×tint×brightness×lit, before any clamp on the `[0,8]` brightness).
- `default_material_lit_only_by_registered_light` / ingress / input: a default-material node in a viewport whose subtree contains ONE directional `Light` pointing along `+N` (so `N·L = 1`) and intensity `1.0`; no `keep_default_light` / assert: `Term::tgp_node_default_shade(vp,node)` equals the forward-loop result from ONLY that registered light (the built-in key/ambient/hemisphere is absent — value differs from the no-light default-lit result, confirming replace-not-augment shading, not just the flag).
- `default_material_built_in_when_no_light` / ingress / input: same node, no `Light` in subtree / assert: `Term::tgp_node_default_shade(vp,node)` equals the pinned built-in key+ambient+hemisphere shading (§11.2 constants).

## Review resolution log (round 1)

Per-issue disposition of the round-1 review (`P3.review.md`). The review re-verified all 11 prior-run seed findings; the eight seed issues it already marked RESOLVED in the current plan (P3-I1, P3-I2, P3-I4, P3-I5, P3-I6, P3-I7, P3-I8, P3-I10, P3-I11) are not re-listed here as they required no further edits this round. Only the 7 OPEN issues carried into round 1 are dispositioned below.

- **P3-I3 (material immutability / `dup_id` is a design extrapolation) — RESOLVED.** Re-grounded in P3-T4 rather than left as a bare "pending extrapolation." The reconciliation spine (which wins on conflict) treats material ids as ASSETS-CLASS uniformly: G1 namespaces `node/asset/clip/viewport/material` ids together; G2 resolves "asset/material/parent refs" identically as late-bound committed-only refs and pins the dup-id + ref-resolution rule to committed assets; §8.6 has `material.add` bump `asset_revision` (the asset-class counter). The consistent reading is therefore that material ids inherit §8.4's immutable-once-committed + `dup_id`-on-recommit rule — this is the decided plan-of-record, not an extrapolation. Named the concrete removal/replacement path (`asset.remove`, since materials are assets-class) — eliminating the reviewer's "the remove+add path does not exist" objection. Strengthened `material_readd_committed_id_is_dup_id` to concrete `txn=20/21` bytes asserting the first material is RETAINED (red base unchanged) with `txn=21;op=0;code=dup_id;detail=9`, and added `material_remove_then_readd_succeeds` exercising `asset.remove` → re-`material.add` (no `dup_id`, second base committed, `asset_revision` bumps per committed txn). The sparse-merge alternative is retained ONLY as a documented contingent fallback; the closed-enum code the terminal emits is now decided.
- **P3-I9-b (light `range`/`spot_inner`/`spot_outer` sub-fields declared but never decode-tested — UNCOVERED) — RESOLVED.** Added two concrete P3-T9 ingress tests: `light_spot_angles_and_range_decode` (CBOR `light:{type:spot,...,range:12.5,spot_inner:0.2,spot_outer:0.5}` asserting `kind==Spot`, `range==Some(12.5)`, `spot_inner≈0.2`, `spot_outer≈0.5`) and `light_directional_range_and_spot_angles_absent` (directional light asserting `range==None`, spot angles absent/default). Also filled in the previously-elided spot angle fields in `light_inherits_parent_transform_world`'s input bytes. All three plan-defined sub-fields now have ≥1 concrete-bytes test.
- **P3-I12 (`msaa={2,8}` adapter-reported advertisement branch uncovered) — RESOLVED.** Added `msaa_serializer_prints_adapter_reported_counts` to P3-T2: a serializer-only unit test that injects a stubbed adapter-supported set `{1,2,4,8}` CPU-side (no GPU probe) and asserts the `tgp;r` msaa serializer prints `msaa=1,2,4,8` in ascending order, exercising the `2`/`8` advertisement-string branch the GPU-free baseline `{1,4}` never reaches. Live adapter probing is explicitly scoped to integration.
- **P3-I13 (G10 device-loss re-upload of MSAA/resolve/pick targets not tested) — RESOLVED.** Extended the P3-T18 deliverable to cover device-loss recovery (force-recompute all per-viewport MSAA/resolve/pick targets from CPU state ignoring dirty gating per G10; push unsolicited `tgp;r` to handshaked tokens per G7/G10), added the test-only `Term::tgp_inject_device_loss()` hook, added dependencies on P3-T15/T16/T17/P1-clock, and added `device_loss_rebuilds_targets_and_pushes_tgp_r` (asserts post-loss `tgp_viewport_vram` matches the pre-loss formula, `tgp_pick_target_samples==1`, an unsolicited `tgp;r` is queued, and CPU scene survives). Now an in-scope, GPU-free-tested G10 surface.
- **P3-I14 (unknown-model reject atomicity asserted only at node level, not whole-txn) — RESOLVED.** Added `material_add_unknown_model_rejects_whole_txn_atomically` to P3-T3: a TWO-op txn (`txn=15`) where op 0 (`material.add id:1` valid) would otherwise commit and op 1 (`model:phong`) fails; asserts `x;txn=15;op=1;code=unsupported` (correct `op=` index), `tgp_material(tok,1)==None` (the valid sibling rolled back), and `asset_revision` unchanged — proving §8.6 whole-txn atomicity, not just per-op rejection.
- **P3-I15 (`feat=` implication asserted positively only; no property check / ordering) — RESOLVED.** Added `feat_pbr_implies_material_property` to P3-T2: asserts the implication as a STRUCTURAL property on `Term::tgp_caps().feat` (`!feat.contains("pbr") || feat.contains("material")`) so a regression dropping `material` while keeping `pbr` fails, and asserts deterministic ascending-byte-order token emission for byte-stable replies. The positive presence test is preserved.
