# P4 — Animation & accessibility

Phase P4 adds terminal-side animation playback and accessibility surfacing to TGP, layered on the scene model (S06), asset/GLB import + acks (S08), the wire/error framing + the `apc_end` outcome back-channel, and the deterministic injectable clock + CPU picking/event emission from the interactivity phase (S12). It delivers: `alt`-text in the scene with bounded validation and CPU-resolved terminal surfacing (S13); glTF clip import + auto-registration with the glTF-index→ClipId mapping returned in the asset `a` ack (S14.1); the opt-in `anim;play/pause/seek/stop` verbs on the deterministic clock with `node=` subtree-root channel resolution (S14.2–14.3); stop/pause/end-of-clip pose + TRS-ownership semantics and the `node_busy`/`camera_busy` conflicts (S14.4–14.5); per-node dirty + animation render-scheduling that never spams `scene_revision` (S14.7 / G9); bind-pose skinning that does NOT advertise `feat=skin` (S14.8); and full RIS/DECSTR/alt-screen lifecycle teardown of playbacks and clips (G10).

Every protocol surface in scope — each verb, header field, error code, cap, feat token, and lifecycle event — gets at least one concrete unit test on the INGRESS (`Parser::advance` → `&self` accessors / `pty_replies`), INTERACTION (synthetic events + explicit clock advance → camera/node TRS or `tgp;e` bytes), or LIFECYCLE (RIS / alt-screen / `node.remove` → teardown via accessors) seam. The `anim` verbs, the clip-id ack, and all anim errors/events route out through the cross-crate `apc_end` outcome back-channel (the foundation-phase change where `toastty-parser::Perform::apc_end` becomes outcome-bearing, returning a parsed-frame result that `Term` turns into queued `tgp;a`/`tgp;x`/`tgp;e` bytes); P4 does NOT re-do that plumbing and the relevant tasks (P4-T4, P4-T7, P4-T9) depend on it explicitly as `APC-END-BACKCHANNEL`.

**Cross-phase dependency aliases** (all reference EARLIER phases P1–P3): `SCENE-MODEL`, `ASSET-ADD`, `ACK-FRAME`, `TGP-FOUNDATION`, `ERROR-FRAME`, `APC-END-BACKCHANNEL` (P1 Foundation); `CLOCK`, `PICK`, `EVENT-EMIT`, `EXPLORE` (P2 Differentiators); `CAPS-REPLY` (P1 handshake/`tgp;r`); `LIFECYCLE` → **P2-T38** (RIS/DECSTR teardown machinery) + **P2-T15** (alt-screen affinity / screen-buffer binding) — the established lifecycle/affinity machinery P4-T15 wires animation state into (G10). Where the interactivity phase already landed the shared clock, P4-T0 re-exports it rather than duplicating.

**Design-reconciliation items surfaced by this phase** (resolved minimally here; flagged for the design owners): (1) S12.5's closed `ev=` enum does NOT contain `anim_end`, but S14.4 emits it — P4-T9a extends the EVENT-EMIT closed `ev=` vocabulary with a node-scoped `anim_end` token (fields `tok=`/`vp=`/`node=`/`clip=`/`reason=`), versioned under `v=`. (2) S13.1 says the scene "may carry an `alt` caption" but defines NO wire op to set it — scene-level `alt` is therefore **out of scope for v1** in P4. (3) S13.1 also says "each viewport may carry an `alt` caption", and S13.2 makes the viewport `alt` the headline cell-fallback caption that AT readers announce in the text flow — but the authoritative `Viewport` struct in S10.1 has NO `alt` field and the S10 `tgp;vp` op schema defines no `alt=` field. P4-T1 therefore carries an explicit **design-reconciliation item that EXTENDS S10.1**: add `alt: Option<String>` to the `Viewport` struct and define a concrete `alt` key on the `tgp;vp` create/update op payload (CBOR `alt` text-string key, mirroring the `node.upsert` `alt` key), governed by the SAME bounded `validate_alt` hardening (UTF-8, ≤64 bytes, no C0/DEL/C1/ESC) and the SAME `bad_param` whole-frame reject as node `alt`. This wire definition is added by P4-T1; viewport `alt` is in scope only because P4-T1 lands that S10 extension, and the a11y surfacing in P4-T2 (`viewport_accessible_name`, the cell-fallback caption) depends on it.

**Concrete-clip-id convention (G6 — every anim test feeds real wire bytes):** clip ids are numeric `u32` handles read from the asset `a` ack (S14.1), never the literal letter `C`. To keep every anim test concrete, the phase pins a fixture rule: for a single-animation GLB fixture, glTF-animation-index 0 maps to **`ClipId 0`** in the creating token's namespace, so a test that `asset.add`s a one-clip GLB writes `clip=0` in its anim frames. A test that needs to be explicit drains the `a` ack and binds its clip id from the `clip0=` mapping field, then writes that literal `u32` in the frame. Every P4-T6/T7/T9/T10/T11/T12/T13/T15 anim test below states the numeric `clip=` value it feeds (e.g. `clip=0`), never the placeholder `C`.

**Design-reconciliation item — the `clipN=` ack-mapping field spelling (round-3 review P4-R3-6):** S14.1 specifies that the commit `a` ack "returns the glTF-animation-index → `ClipId` mapping," and S08.5 fixes that clip ids are `u32` handles, but neither slice pins a concrete *wire spelling* for that mapping field. P4-T4 therefore LANDS the concrete spelling **`clipN=<u32>`** — one key per glTF animation index N (`clip0=<id0>`, `clip1=<id1>`, …) — and this spelling is **flagged for the design owners** at the same standard as the other two flagged inventions in this phase (the viewport `alt` = S10.1 extension in P4-T1; the `ev=anim_end`/`reason=` = S12.5 extension in P4-T9a). The P4-T4 test's literal `clip0=`/`clip1=` field names are authoritative for this phase, not incidental.

## P4-T0 — Inject the deterministic monotonic clock into the TGP subsystem

**Title:** Inject the deterministic monotonic clock into the TGP subsystem

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/clock.rs` (new): a `Clock` seam — `trait MonotonicClock { fn now(&self) -> Instant }` plus `ManualClock` (test) and `WallClock` (prod), or a thin `TgpClock(Instant)` newtype with `advance(Duration)` for tests. This is an **independent TGP module** under `tgp/` that may copy the *shape* of the existing RGP `tick_animations(now: Instant)` pattern but **shares no types or functions with `rgp/*`** (G8 clean break — it is a new code path, not a bridge). Wire a single clock handle into the TGP scene state owned by `Term` (`toastty-term/src/term.rs`). Add `Term::tgp_now()` / `Term::tgp_advance_clock(Duration)` test hooks and a `Term::tick_tgp(now: Instant)` driver mirroring (independently of) `tick_rgp_animations`. If the interactivity phase (`CLOCK`) already added a shared injectable clock, this task **re-exports/reuses** it instead of duplicating. All terminal-side TGP time (playback, seek, anim_end timing) reads from this one clock.

**Design refs:** S14.7; G6, G8

**Depends on:** TGP-FOUNDATION, SCENE-MODEL, CLOCK

**Tests:**
- `manual_clock_advances_only_on_explicit_step` / INGRESS / Construct `Term::new(24,80,0)`; read `t0 = Term::tgp_now()`; call `Term::tgp_advance_clock(Duration::from_millis(500))`; read `t1 = Term::tgp_now()`. / `t1 - t0 == Duration::from_millis(500)`; a second `tgp_now()` with no advance returns `t1` unchanged (no wall-clock drift).
- `tick_tgp_with_no_playbacks_is_noop_on_revisions` / INGRESS / Fresh `Term`; capture `scene_revision` + `asset_revision`; call `Term::tick_tgp(Term::tgp_now() + Duration::from_secs(1))`. / `Term::tgp_scene().scene_revision()` and `asset_revision()` are unchanged; no per-node dirty flags set (`DirtySet` empty).

## P4-T1 — Add `alt` to the scene model (nodes, viewports) with bounded validation

**Title:** Add `alt` to the scene model (nodes, viewports) with bounded validation

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs`: add `alt: Option<String>` to `Node` (already in the S06.1 struct). Add `validate_alt(&[u8]) -> Result<String, TgpError>` enforcing: valid UTF-8 (non-UTF-8 → `bad_param`); ≤ `max_id_bytes` (64 bytes, counted in **bytes** not chars, per S13.1 / S07.1); and rejection of any C0 control byte (`0x00`–`0x1F`), DEL (`0x7F`), C1 range (`0x80`–`0x9F`), or ESC (`0x1B`) — the exact id-hardening set of S08.4 that S13.1 says `alt` inherits because these strings can re-enter the host input path via AT tooling. Wire `alt` into the `node.upsert` sparse merge (key absent = preserve; CBOR null = clear to `None`; value = set — S06.1 table). Add accessor `Node::alt() -> Option<&str>`. **Alt-cap design decision (round-2 review P4-R2-4):** S13.1 says `alt` is "bounded like every other string field (UTF-8, ≤ 64 bytes...)" and S07.1 advertises `max_id_bytes=64` but no separate `max_alt_bytes`; this task pins the decision that the `alt` cap IS the advertised `max_id_bytes` (no new cap token), and the over-cap tests below read the bound from the advertised `max_id_bytes` (NOT a hard-coded literal 64) so the G7 advertised==enforced invariant holds for `alt` and the tests do not silently drift if a future caps change splits alt off. (A future design may name `max_alt_bytes`; until then `alt` shares `max_id_bytes` by this explicit decision — flagged for the design owners.)

**S10 viewport-`alt` wire extension (design-reconciliation item 3 above):** the authoritative `Viewport` struct in S10.1 has NO `alt` field and the S10 `tgp;vp` op defines no `alt=` key — but S13.1/S13.2 make viewport `alt` the headline cell-fallback caption. This task therefore EXTENDS S10.1: add `alt: Option<String>` to the `Viewport` struct (`toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs`) and define a concrete `alt` CBOR text-string key on the `tgp;vp` create/update op payload (the same spelling/shape as the `node.upsert` `alt` key), routed through the SAME `validate_alt` hardening and the SAME `bad_param` whole-frame reject. A `tgp;vp` frame carrying an over-cap or malformed `alt` is rejected whole (viewport not created/updated, correlator = `vp` id per G4). Add accessor `Viewport::alt() -> Option<&str>`. **Scene-level `alt` is out of scope** (no wire op exists, S13.1 reconciliation item 2 above).

**Design refs:** S13.1, S06.1, S08.4, S10.1; G3

**Depends on:** SCENE-MODEL, ERROR-FRAME

**Tests:**
- `node_upsert_sets_alt` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A` (`ESC _ tgp;q;v=2;tok=A ESC \` → `tgp;r`). `Parser::advance` a `node.upsert` patch creating `Mesh` node id=`mol` with CBOR field `alt="caffeine molecule, C8H10N4O2"`. / `Term::tgp_scene().node("A","mol").alt() == Some("caffeine molecule, C8H10N4O2")`; a `tgp;a` ack for the txn is queued in `pty_replies`.
- `alt_only_upsert_bumps_no_revision_no_dirty` / INGRESS / Create node id=`mol` (any kind). Capture `scene_revision`/`asset_revision` and the node's transform/visibility dirty flags. Send a second `node.upsert` touching only `alt="x"`. / `scene_revision` AND `asset_revision` both unchanged after the alt-only upsert; node's per-node dirty flags remain clear (alt never affects rendering/dirty/revisions, S13.1).
- `alt_clear_via_cbor_null` / INGRESS / Create node with `alt="label"`. Send `node.upsert` with CBOR null for the `alt` key. / `Term::tgp_scene().node("A","mol").alt() == None`.
- `alt_over_cap_rejects_whole_txn_bad_param` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`; drain the `tgp;r` and read the advertised `max_id_bytes` (= 64) — the over-cap length is `max_id_bytes + 1` bytes, derived from the advertised cap NOT a hard-coded literal. `node.upsert` creating node id=`n1` AND id=`n2` in one txn, where `n1.alt` is an `(max_id_bytes+1)`-byte (65-byte) ASCII string. / A `tgp;x;code=bad_param` is queued (detail names `alt`); NEITHER `n1` nor `n2` exists in `tgp_scene()` — whole txn rejected, scene unchanged (S13.1); the rejected length equals advertised `max_id_bytes + 1`, so the enforced alt cap == the advertised `max_id_bytes` (G7 advertised==enforced for the alt surface).
- `alt_multibyte_over_cap_counts_bytes` / INGRESS / `node.upsert` with an `alt` of 33 two-byte UTF-8 chars (66 bytes, 33 chars). / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` queued; node not created — the 64-byte cap is counted in BYTES, not chars (S13.1 / S07.1).
- `alt_with_esc_byte_rejects_bad_param` / INGRESS / `node.upsert` with `alt` containing a `0x1B` (ESC) byte. / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` queued; node not created (re-enters host input path, S13.1).
- `alt_with_c1_byte_rejects_bad_param` / INGRESS / `node.upsert` with `alt` containing a C1 codepoint (UTF-8 bytes `0xC2 0x80`). / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` queued; node not created (C1 range `0x80`–`0x9F` rejected, matching S08.4).
- `alt_invalid_utf8_rejects` / INGRESS / `node.upsert` with an `alt` CBOR byte string containing invalid UTF-8 (e.g. a lone `0xFF`). / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` (or `parse_error` per the CBOR text-string rule) queued; node not created (UTF-8 required, S13.1).
- `viewport_alt_set_and_read` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`. `Parser::advance` a `tgp;vp` create frame for `vp=2` whose CBOR payload carries the new `alt` text-string key = `"interactive 3D inspector"` (the S10.1 wire extension this task lands), e.g. `ESC _ tgp;vp;tok=A;vp=2;anchor=inline;<cells>;enc=b64;<cbor: {alt:"interactive 3D inspector"}> ESC \`. / `Term::tgp_scene().viewport("A",2).alt() == Some("interactive 3D inspector")`.
- `viewport_alt_over_cap_rejects_bad_param` / INGRESS / `tgp;vp` create for `vp=2` whose `alt` key is a 65-byte ASCII string (over the 64-byte cap). / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` queued (correlator = `vp=2` per G4); the viewport is NOT created (whole `vp` frame rejected, same `validate_alt` hardening as node alt, S10.1 extension).

## P4-T2 — Surface alt-text: accessible name + cell-fallback caption via the CPU pick path

**Title:** Surface alt-text: accessible name + cell-fallback caption via the CPU pick path

**Deliverable:** `toastty-term/src/term.rs`: add accessibility surfacing accessors. `Term::viewport_accessible_name(tok, vp) -> Option<&str>` (the viewport `alt`). `Term::accessible_name_at_cell(line, col) -> Option<&str>` that, for a cell inside a viewport's live placeholder rect, resolves the node under that cell via the **on-demand CPU ray-cast** pick primitive (ray vs node world AABBs / instance transforms — the authoritative-and-always-present CPU pick of S12.4, line "The CPU path ... is authoritative and always present"), NOT the sub-gated cached pick buffer. S12.4 sub-gates only the *cached pick buffer + its GPU pass* ("exists only for viewports with an active sub"); the a11y query carries NO requirement that a `sub` be active (S13.2: AT surfacing is the complement to events, available even on a viewport the app never subscribed to), so it MUST drive the on-demand ray-cast directly. The resolver returns that node's `alt`, falling back to the viewport `alt`, then `None` (terminal never fabricates). `Term::inline_viewport_caption(line, col) -> Option<&str>` returns the placeholder cell's accessible caption (viewport `alt`) so line-based readers announce the region in flow (S13.2). No GPU: all resolution is the CPU ray-cast pick + cell→pixel rect math.

**Design refs:** S13.2, S13.3, S12.4

**Depends on:** P4-T1, PICK, TGP-FOUNDATION

**Tests:**
- `cell_caption_is_viewport_alt_at_placeholder` / INGRESS / Create inline viewport `vp=2` with `alt="inspector"` anchored at a known cell rect (no node under cursor cell yet). Query `Term::inline_viewport_caption(line,col)` for a cell inside the rect. / Returns `Some("inspector")` — the placeholder announces the viewport alt as its accessible name (S13.2).
- `node_alt_resolved_under_at_cursor_cell` / INGRESS / Viewport `vp=2` with a `Mesh` node `O1` `alt="Oxygen"` framed so its world AABB projects under cell (line=3,col=5). NO synthetic pointer is injected AND NO `sub` is active on `vp=2` (so per S12.4 there is no cached pick buffer at all). This is an `&self` accessor query that drives the on-demand CPU ray-cast pick primitive directly. Query `Term::accessible_name_at_cell(3,5)`. / Returns `Some("Oxygen")` (node alt resolved by the on-demand ray-cast at that cell, S13.2); MUST agree with the result of that SAME ray-cast primitive (ray vs node world AABBs) at that cell — NOT the sub-gated cached buffer, which is absent here (S12.4).
- `node_alt_falls_back_to_viewport_alt` / INTERACTION / Viewport `vp=2` `alt="inspector"`; a node under the cell with NO `alt`. Query `accessible_name_at_cell`. / Returns `Some("inspector")` — falls back to the viewport alt when the picked node has none (S13.2).
- `unlabeled_graphic_returns_none` / INGRESS / Viewport `vp=2` with NO `alt` and a node with NO `alt` under the queried cell. / `Term::accessible_name_at_cell(...)` returns `None` — terminal never fabricates a description (S13.2).
- `headless_text_only_uses_cell_fallback_caption` / LIFECYCLE / Mark the session text-only (headless flag on `Term`, no GPU target); viewport `vp=2` `alt="inspector"`. Query `inline_viewport_caption` inside the rect. / Caption `Some("inspector")` is available with no GPU target present — the cell-level fallback caption is purely CPU-resolved (S13.2).

## P4-T3 — Parse glTF animation channels into AnimationClip on GLB import

**Title:** Parse glTF animation channels into AnimationClip on GLB import

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/clip.rs` (new): `AnimationClip { duration: f32, channels: Vec<Channel> }`, `Channel { target_rel: NodeRelPath, path: TrsPath /* T|R|S */, sampler: Sampler }`, `Sampler { interp: Interp /* Step|Linear|CubicSpline */, times: Vec<f32>, values: Vec<f32> }`. Add a glTF-animation parser in `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/glb_loader.rs` — an **independent TGP module** under `tgp/` that may copy the *shape* of `rgp/glb_loader.rs` but **shares no types or functions with `rgp/*`** (G8 clean break, NOT a bridge). It reads `animations[]` with their channels/samplers and resolves each channel target to a **relative node path within the imported hierarchy** (NOT a global node id — S14.2). Bounds-check all keyframe arrays against caps. CUBICSPLINE stores in/out tangents (3 values per keyframe). Pure CPU; no GPU.

**Design refs:** S14.1, S14.2; G8

**Depends on:** ASSET-ADD

**Tests:**
- `glb_with_one_clip_parses_channels` / INGRESS / `asset.add` a minimal GLB (b64) containing one animation: a single LINEAR rotation channel on the root joint, duration 1.0s. `Parser::advance` the `asset.add` with `more=0`. / After commit, `Term::tgp_scene().clip_table("A")` has exactly one `AnimationClip`; its `duration == 1.0`; `channels[0].path == R`, `interp == Linear`, `target_rel` resolves to the root joint.
- `step_and_cubicspline_interp_preserved` / INGRESS / `asset.add` a GLB whose clip has one STEP translation channel and one CUBICSPLINE scale channel. / `clip.channels` contains a `Step` T channel and a `CubicSpline` S channel; the CubicSpline sampler stores 3 values per keyframe (in-tangent, value, out-tangent).
- `glb_with_no_animations_yields_empty_clip_table` / INGRESS / `asset.add` a static GLB (no `animations[]`). / `Term::tgp_scene().clip_table("A")` is empty; the asset commits normally (`asset_revision` bumped once).
- `oversized_keyframe_array_rejected_cap_exceeded` / INGRESS / `asset.add` a GLB whose animation sampler exceeds a keyframe/cap bound (an advertised allocation-point cap, NOT the per-message size cap). / `tgp;x;code=cap_exceeded` queued citing the asset id (G12.3 row 4 — an advertised cap breached at the allocation point is canonically `cap_exceeded`; the keyframe-cap breach is distinct from the per-message `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` of G12.1, which P1 owns); no clip registered, asset not committed. The test branches on `code=cap_exceeded` only (`detail=` is advisory/non-load-bearing per G3/G12). Bounded, no crash — G8 fuzz oracle.

## P4-T4 — Auto-register clips on asset.add and return glTF-index→ClipId mapping in the `a` ack

**Title:** Auto-register clips on asset.add and return glTF-index→ClipId mapping in the `a` ack

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs`: on `asset.add` commit, allocate one `ClipId (u32)` per parsed `AnimationClip` in the creating token's id namespace (alongside asset/material ids), store in `Scene.clips` keyed by `(token, ClipId)`. Build a glTF-animation-index → `ClipId` mapping. `toastty-term/src/term.rs` (or the wire/ack module): include that mapping in the asset's commit `a` ack (correlator = asset id, tagged `tok=`) using the **`clipN=<u32>` field spelling** (one key per glTF animation index N: `clip0=<id0>;clip1=<id1>;…`) that P4-T4 LANDS as a design-reconciliation invention flagged for the design owners (see the preamble "Design-reconciliation item — the `clipN=` ack-mapping field spelling," P4-R3-6); this literal spelling is authoritative for the phase. There is NO separate `clip.add` op. **CROSS-CRATE `apc_end` back-channel:** the ack is emitted through the `apc_end` outcome back-channel (`APC-END-BACKCHANNEL`) — `apc_end` forwards the committed asset outcome (including the clip mapping) to `Term`, which formats the `tgp;a` bytes into `pty_replies`. Add accessor `Scene::clip_table(tok) -> &[ClipId]` (or equivalent) for tests.

**Design refs:** S14.1, S08.5; G4

**Depends on:** P4-T3, ASSET-ADD, ACK-FRAME, APC-END-BACKCHANNEL

**Tests:**
- `asset_add_returns_clip_id_mapping_in_ack` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`. `asset.add` (`more=0`) a GLB carrying two animations. Drain `pty_replies`. / A `tgp;a` frame is queued with `tok=A` and the asset correlator, carrying clip ids for glTF anim indices 0 and 1 (e.g. `clip0=<id0>;clip1=<id1>`); `Term::tgp_scene().clip_table("A")` contains exactly those two `ClipId`s.
- `clip_ids_coexist_per_token_no_denied` / INGRESS / Two handshaked tokens A and B each `asset.add` a GLB with one animation (each gets its own `ClipId 0` in its OWN private map, G11; A ALSO `asset.add`s a one-clip GLB whose hierarchy creates node `base`, so A holds both `{A,clip 0}` and `{A,"base"}`). B — which has its own `{B,clip 0}` but NO node `base` — sends `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=B;play;clip=0;node=base ESC \`. / This is NOT a collision and NOT `denied` (G11(a): `denied` is RESERVED — cross-token addressing is unrepresentable under the private per-token map). B's `clip=0` resolves to B's OWN `{B,clip 0}` (coexisting with, never aliasing, `{A,clip 0}`); the frame then fails on `node=base` because `base` is absent from B's map → `tgp;x;code=unknown_node;tok=B` (G11(b) / G12.3 row 9 — byte-for-byte identical whether or not `base` exists under A; no existence oracle). NO `tgp;e`/state mutation reaches A; A's own ack and B's own ack each cite only their own `clip0=` id; both `{A,clip 0}` and `{B,clip 0}` remain independently present and usable. (Cross-referenced by P4-T7 `play_foreign_id_resolves_in_own_map_not_denied`, the anim-parser reject-site owner.)
- `ack_available_without_subscription` / INGRESS / Handshaked `tok=A` with NO event sub. `asset.add` a GLB with one animation. / The `tgp;a` clip-mapping ack is still queued — acks are available to any handshaked token, not gated on `sub` (G4).

## P4-T4a — Advertise feat=anim and the anim caps in `tgp;r`

**Title:** Advertise feat=anim and the anim caps in the capability reply

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp` capability reply (S07.1): ensure the `tgp;r` `feat=` token list CONTAINS `anim` (and, by the implication rule, `graph` — `anim ⇒ graph`), and that the reply carries `anim_speed_max=<N>` (v1: 16) and `camera_report_hz=<N>` (v1: 30). This is the headline P4 feature-flag advertisement; the cap value `N=anim_speed_max` MUST be the SAME value enforced in P4-T7's speed-over-cap test (advertised = enforced, S15.2). No new types beyond the existing caps reply; this task asserts the surface is present. **Build-order note:** this is a pure capability-string change in the caps reply and consumes nothing produced by the glTF-animation parser (P4-T3); the `anim_speed_max=16` advertised==enforced coupling to P4-T7 is a test-coupling note in prose, NOT a build dependency — so P4-T4a does NOT depend on P4-T3 (per round-2 review P4-R2-3).

**Design refs:** S07.1, S15.2; G7

**Depends on:** CAPS-REPLY

**Tests:**
- `feat_anim_advertised_implies_graph` / INGRESS / `Parser::advance` `ESC _ tgp;q;v=2;tok=A ESC \`; drain `pty_replies` and parse the `tgp;r` `feat=` list. / The `feat=` token list CONTAINS `anim` AND `graph` (anim⇒graph holds, S07.1).
- `anim_caps_present_in_reply` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`; drain `pty_replies`; parse `tgp;r`. / The reply carries `anim_speed_max=16` and `camera_report_hz=30` (anim caps advertised, S07.1 / S15.2).

## P4-T5 — node= subtree-root relative channel resolution

**Title:** node= subtree-root relative channel resolution

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/anim.rs` (new): `resolve_targets(clip, subtree_root_node_id, scene) -> ResolvedTargets`. For each clip channel, resolve its relative target path against the subtree rooted at `node=` (by relative id/index within the imported hierarchy, S14.2). A channel whose relative target resolves to no node under `node=` is SKIPPED (animates nothing). If NO channel resolves to any existing target, return an error sentinel the caller turns into `no_targets`. Pure CPU; queryable resolved-target list. Add accessor `Scene::playback_resolved_targets(tok, clip, node) -> Option<&ResolvedTargets>` (exposed once a playback exists) and keep `resolve_targets` a pure fn callable directly in tests so the resolved list is asserted by name.

**Design refs:** S14.2

**Depends on:** P4-T3, SCENE-MODEL

**Tests:**
- `channels_resolve_relative_to_subtree_root` / INGRESS / Instantiate two copies of a rig under nodes `base_a` and `base_b`. Call `resolve_targets(clip, "base_a", scene)`. / `ResolvedTargets` map to `base_a`'s shoulder/elbow/wrist descendants (not `base_b`'s); the same clip resolved against `"base_b"` maps to `base_b`'s descendants — same clip, two subtrees (S14.2).
- `missing_descendant_channel_is_skipped` / INGRESS / Resolve a clip with 4 channels against a subtree where one channel's relative target does not exist. / `ResolvedTargets` contains 3 entries (the missing one skipped, not an error); resolution succeeds.
- `no_resolvable_targets_flags_no_targets` / INGRESS / Resolve a clip against a `node=` whose subtree contains none of the clip's relative targets. / `resolve_targets` returns the no-targets sentinel (the caller raises `x code=no_targets`, S14.2).

## P4-T6 — Playback state machine + (clip,node) handle store on the deterministic clock

**Title:** Playback state machine + (clip,node) handle store on the deterministic clock

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/anim.rs`: `Playback { handle: (ClipId, NodeId), state: Stopped|Playing|Paused, t: f32, started_at: Instant, loop_: bool, speed: f32, resolved: ResolvedTargets }`; `PlaybackTable` keyed by `(token, ClipId, NodeId)`. **Clip ids are numeric `u32` (S14.1); per the phase's concrete-clip-id convention, single-animation GLB fixtures pin glTF-anim-index 0 → `ClipId 0`, so the tests below feed `clip=0` (or the literal `u32` bound from the `clip0=` ack field), never the placeholder `C`.** State transitions: `play(Stopped) → Playing` from `t=0`; `play(Playing|Paused) → restart` from `t=0`; `pause(Playing) → Paused` holding pose; `play(Paused) → restart` from `t=0`; `seek(t) → set absolute t`, re-evaluate pose, keep Playing/Paused; `stop → Stopped`. `evaluate_pose(clip, t)` interpolates per `Interp` (Step holds previous key; Linear lerps T/S and slerps R; CubicSpline does Hermite from stored in/out tangents) and writes the resolved nodes' local TRS. Accessor: `Scene::playback(tok,clip,node) -> Option<&Playback>` exposing `state` + `t`. All time via the P4-T0 clock.

**TRS-ownership ACQUISITION site (round-3 review P4-R3-4 — this task OWNS acquire):** the ownership/`node_busy` machinery has three sites — acquire, release, enforce — and this task pins acquire. On `play` (and on `pause`, which retains ownership), mark EVERY resolved target node TRS-owned by that playback in the ownership map — i.e. set the per-node busy flag keyed to `(token, ClipId, NodeId)`. (`stop`/auto-stop CLEARS it — owned by P4-T10; the enforce-on-app-TRS-upsert reject is owned by P4-T11.) Because `play` populates the ownership map, a merely-Playing (never-paused) node is already busy — which is exactly what P4-T11 `trs_patch_over_playing_node_rejected_node_busy` and P4-T10 `pause_retains_ownership` require. The `Scene::node_trs_owner` accessor that reads this map is added by P4-T10.

**`PlaybackTable` entry-lifecycle rule (round-3 review P4-R3-5 — stated once here):** explicit `stop` and non-loop end LEAVE the `(clip,node)` entry in the table with `state=Stopped`, so `Scene::playback(tok,clip,node)` returns `Some(&Playback{state:Stopped})` (the held-pose + last-`t` remain queryable). `node.remove` of the bound node (subtree root or any driven descendant) EVICTS the entry entirely, so `Scene::playback` returns `None` (owned by P4-T13). `live` ≡ `Playing|Paused`; `pause`/`seek`/`stop` on a non-live (Stopped) or absent entry → `not_playing` (P4-T7); `play` on a retained-Stopped entry RESTARTS it from `t=0` (the `play(Stopped)→Playing` transition above operates on the retained entry).

**Design refs:** S14.3, S14.7; G6

**Depends on:** P4-T0, P4-T5

**Tests:**
- `play_starts_at_t_zero_playing` / INGRESS / Create rig node `base`; `asset.add` a one-clip GLB (clip → `ClipId 0`). `Parser::advance` `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0;speed=1 ESC \`. / `Scene::playback(A,0,"base").state == Playing` and `.t == 0.0`; AND `Scene::node_trs_owner(A,"base") == Some((0,"base"))` IMMEDIATELY after `play` (before any pause/stop) — pinning the TRS-ownership ACQUIRE site at `play`, P4-R3-4. (The `node_trs_owner` accessor is the P4-T10 one; this assertion exercises the acquire that THIS task lands.)
- `tick_advances_clip_time_and_pose` / INGRESS / `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0;speed=1 ESC \` on `base` (1.0s LINEAR rotation clip). `Term::tgp_advance_clock(500ms)`; `Term::tick_tgp(now)`. / `Scene::playback(A,0,"base").t == 0.5`; the driven node's local rotation == LINEAR-interpolated value at t=0.5; its WORLD matrix (`Term::tgp_scene().node(...).world()`) reflects the rotated pose.
- `cubicspline_eval_is_hermite_not_linear` / INGRESS / `asset.add` a clip (→ `ClipId 0`) with a single CUBICSPLINE translation channel with known keyframes/tangents (keys at t=0 and t=1, nonzero tangents). `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; advance to a mid-key t (e.g. 400ms) + tick. / The driven node's local `translate` equals the deterministic Hermite value computed CPU-side, and is DISTINCT from the LINEAR result at the same t (CubicSpline branch of `evaluate_pose`, S14.1/S14.3).
- `play_on_playing_restarts_from_zero` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; advance 700ms + tick (t=0.7); send `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` again. / `playback.t == 0.0` and `state == Playing` (restart semantics, S14.3).
- `pause_holds_pose_then_play_restarts` / INGRESS / `play;clip=0;node=base`; advance 300ms+tick (t=0.3); `tgp;anim;tok=A;pause;clip=0;node=base`; advance 1s + tick. / After pause: `state==Paused`, `t==0.3`, node pose frozen at t=0.3 even after the 1s tick (no advance while paused). Then `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` → `t==0.0` (resume = restart, S14.3).
- `seek_while_paused_sets_absolute_t` / INGRESS / `pause;clip=0;node=base` at t=0.3; `tgp;anim;tok=A;seek;clip=0;node=base;t=0.8`. / `state` stays `Paused`; `t==0.8`; node pose == pose at t=0.8 (seek scrubs while paused, S14.3).
- `seek_while_playing_keeps_playing_and_resumes_from_t` / INGRESS / `play;clip=0;node=base` (Playing); advance 200ms+tick (t=0.2); `tgp;anim;tok=A;seek;clip=0;node=base;t=0.8`; then advance 100ms+tick. / Immediately after seek: `state` still `Playing`, `t==0.8`, pose == pose(0.8). After the further tick: `t==0.9` (the clock resumes from the seeked t, S14.3/S14.7).
- `speed_scales_time` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0;speed=2`; advance 250ms + tick. / `playback.t == 0.5` (clip time advances at 2x wall time, S14.4).
- `step_interp_holds_value_between_keys` / INGRESS / Clip (→ `ClipId 0`) with STEP translation keys at t=0 (x=0) and t=1 (x=10). `play;clip=0;node=base`; advance 400ms + tick. / node local `translate.x == 0` (STEP holds the previous key, not interpolated).

## P4-T7 — anim control-frame ingress: parse play/pause/seek/stop + verb/field errors

**Title:** anim control-frame ingress: parse play/pause/seek/stop + verb/field errors

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/operation.rs` (or `tgp/anim_frame.rs`): parse `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=<…>;<verb>;clip=<u32>;node=<id>;loop=0|1;speed=<f32>;t=<sec> ESC \` where `<verb> ∈ {play,pause,seek,stop}`. Dispatch into the P4-T6 state machine. Errors (closed enum G3): unrecognized verb → `unknown_op` (this is the canonical, sole trigger for `unknown_op` per **G12.3 row 8 — REACHABLE, the closed control-frame-verb locus**: the `anim` verb set {play,pause,seek,stop} is CLOSED (S14.3) and the control frame is always fully parsed (S07.2), so an out-of-set verb is a malformed control frame and fatal — DISTINCT from a patch `do:` op, where an unknown op is SKIPPED not fatal (s07.3) and never emits `unknown_op`); clip id absent from **the bearing token's own private map** → `unknown_clip`; `node=` not a committed node **in the bearing token's own map** at process time → `unknown_node` (NOT queued for a future node, S14.6); `pause`/`seek`/`stop` on a `(clip,node)` that is not a live playback → `not_playing`; over-cap or non-positive `speed` → `bad_param`; no resolvable targets → `no_targets`. **G11 isolation — NO `denied` for "foreign" clip/node:** under the ratified private per-token id map (addendum G11(a)), `denied` is RESERVED and has NO reachable ingress trigger — cross-token addressing is *unrepresentable*, not rejected. An `anim` frame bearing token B can only ever name ids in B's OWN map; a `clip=`/`node=` value that happens to also exist under another token is simply resolved (or not) against B's map with the SAME causally-blind code it would emit for an id absent everywhere: `unknown_clip` (clip absent from B's map, G12.3 row 10) or `unknown_node` (node absent from B's map, G12.3 row 9), byte-for-byte indistinguishable from the absent-everywhere case (G11(b) — no existence oracle). The anim parser never consults another token's map and never emits `denied`. **CROSS-CRATE `apc_end` back-channel:** `anim` frames are control frames (always parseable, never enter binary-read mode, S07.2); errors/acks for anim verbs route out through the `apc_end` outcome back-channel (`APC-END-BACKCHANNEL`) into `pty_replies` tagged `tok=`. **Positive parse+dispatch of pause/seek/stop (round-3 review P4-R3-1):** the happy-path frame parse → correct state transition for `pause`/`seek`/`stop` is asserted through this SAME ingress seam by P4-T6 `pause_holds_pose_then_play_restarts` / `seek_while_paused_sets_absolute_t` / `seek_while_playing_keeps_playing_and_resumes_from_t` and P4-T10 `stop_holds_last_pose_and_releases_ownership` (each feeds concrete `ESC _ tgp;anim;…` bytes), so the four-verb parser-ownership coverage is complete; only the verb-token *negative* paths are owned directly here.

**Design refs:** S14.3, S14.6, S07.2; G3, G4

**Depends on:** P4-T6, ERROR-FRAME, APC-END-BACKCHANNEL

**Tests:**
- `unknown_verb_errors_unknown_op` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`; node `base` + clip `ClipId 0` exist; `Parser::advance` `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;frobnicate;clip=0;node=base ESC \`. / `tgp;x;code=unknown_op` (tok=A) queued; no playback created, scene unchanged. This is the canonical trigger for `unknown_op` per **G12.3 row 8 (REACHABLE — closed control-frame-verb locus)**: the closed verb set {play,pause,seek,stop} (S14.3) is always fully parsed (S07.2), so an out-of-set verb is a malformed control frame and fatal — DISTINCT from a patch `do:` op, where an unknown op is SKIPPED not fatal (s07.3) and never emits `unknown_op`.
- `play_unknown_clip_errors` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`; node `base` exists; `Parser::advance` `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=999;node=base ESC \` (no clip 999 registered). / `tgp;x;code=unknown_clip` queued (tok=A); no playback created (never a silent no-op, S14.1).
- `play_unknown_node_errors_not_queued` / INGRESS / Valid clip `ClipId 0`; `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=ghost ESC \` where `ghost` is not a committed node. / `tgp;x;code=unknown_node` queued; the play is NOT deferred — a later upsert of `ghost` does not start it (S14.6).
- `play_foreign_id_resolves_in_own_map_not_denied` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A` and `tok=B`. A `asset.add` a one-clip GLB (→ `{A,clip 0}`, `{A,"base"}`). B has handshaked but created NOTHING. B sends `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=B;play;clip=0;node=base ESC \`. / `tgp;x;code=unknown_clip;tok=B` queued (B's OWN map has no clip 0; G11(a) `denied` is RESERVED, G12.3 row 10) — NOT `denied`; the code is byte-for-byte identical whether or not `{A,clip 0}` exists (no existence oracle, G11(b)). No playback created; `{A,clip 0}`/`{A,"base"}` untouched and still usable by A. The anim parser never consults A's map and emits no `denied`. (Companion: if B DID hold its own `{B,clip 0}` but lacked `node=base`, the same frame would draw `unknown_node;tok=B` — see P4-T4 `clip_ids_coexist_per_token_no_denied`, the id-namespacing framing of the same G11 ruling. This is the direct anim-parser ingress test that the reject SITE the anim parser owns never produces `denied`.)
- `pause_seek_stop_on_dead_playback_not_playing` / INGRESS / Clip `ClipId 0`, node `base` exist but no live playback. Send `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;pause;clip=0;node=base ESC \` (and separately `seek;clip=0;node=base;t=0.1`, `stop;clip=0;node=base`). / Each draws `tgp;x;code=not_playing` (S14.3).
- `speed_over_cap_bad_param` / INGRESS / `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0;speed=99 ESC \` with `anim_speed_max=16` (same value advertised in P4-T4a). / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` queued; no playback created (speed clamped to `(0, anim_speed_max]`, S14.4).
- `speed_nonpositive_bad_param` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;speed=0` (and separately `speed=-1`). / `tgp;x;code=bad_param` for each (non-positive speed rejected, S14.4).
- `no_targets_error` / INGRESS / Clip `ClipId 0` whose channels resolve to nothing under the `node=` picked. `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=empty_group ESC \`. / `tgp;x;code=no_targets` queued; no playback (S14.2).
- `upsert_then_play_same_write_succeeds` / INGRESS / ONE `Parser::advance` byte buffer containing a `node.upsert` creating `base` FOLLOWED BY `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`. / Playback exists and is Playing — the upsert committed before the play observes the scene (strict receive order, S14.6).
- `play_then_upsert_same_write_errors_unknown_node` / INGRESS / ONE `Parser::advance` buffer with `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` FIRST, then a `node.upsert` creating `base` SECOND. / `tgp;x;code=unknown_node` for the play (processed before `base` exists, NOT deferred); `base` IS subsequently created by the upsert; the play does not retroactively start (S14.6).
- `distinct_clip_node_pairs_run_concurrently` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base_a`; `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base_b` (same clip, two subtree roots). / Two independent `Playback`s both Playing; advancing the clock ticks both (handle = (clip,node), S14.3).

## P4-T8 — Playback drives per-node dirty + render scheduling (no global scene_revision spam)

**Title:** Playback drives per-node dirty + render scheduling (no global scene_revision spam)

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/scene.rs` + `anim.rs`: each `tick_tgp`, for every Playing playback, evaluate the clip, write the affected nodes' local TRS, set those nodes' per-node transform dirty flags (`DirtySet`), and mark the viewport(s) containing those nodes per-viewport dirty. Recompute world matrices for ONLY the dirty subtree (reuse the S06.5 lazy walk). Do NOT bump global `scene_revision` per frame; NEVER touch `asset_revision`. Add accessors so tests can read `DirtySet` membership + the set of dirty viewport ids after a tick (e.g. `Scene::dirty_nodes()`, `Scene::dirty_viewports()`).

**Design refs:** S14.7, S06.5; G9

**Depends on:** P4-T6, SCENE-MODEL

**Tests:**
- `tick_sets_only_driven_node_dirty` / INGRESS / Scene with driven node `base` (in subtree) and an unrelated node `other`. `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; advance 100ms + tick. / `DirtySet` contains `base` (transform dirty) and NOT `other`; only `base`'s subtree world matrices recomputed.
- `tick_does_not_bump_scene_or_asset_revision` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; capture `scene_revision` + `asset_revision`; advance 100ms + tick (repeat 5 ticks). / `scene_revision` and `asset_revision` are BOTH unchanged across all 5 ticks (playback never bumps global revisions, S14.7 / G9).
- `tick_marks_only_affected_viewport_dirty` / INGRESS / Two viewports `vp=1` (root contains `base`) and `vp=2` (different root). `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; tick. / Per-viewport dirty set == `{1}`; `vp=2` not marked dirty (re-render only affected viewports, S14.7).
- `tick_does_not_reemit_text_cells` / INGRESS / Print text into the grid; `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; tick. / The text-cell damage set is unchanged by the tick (playback does not re-emit unrelated cells, S14.7).

## P4-T9a — Extend the closed `ev=` enum with a node-scoped `anim_end` event token

**Title:** Extend the closed `ev=` enum with a node-scoped `anim_end` event token

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp` event vocabulary (owned by S12.5 / EVENT-EMIT): add a new node-scoped `anim_end` token to the closed `ev=` enum `{click,dblclick,down,up,hover,enter,leave,drag,wheel,camera,resize,destroyed}`, versioned under `v=`. Define its emitted fields: `tok=`, `vp=`, `node=`, `clip=`, and `reason=` (`completed|removed|explore_takeover` — distinguishing a one-shot finish from an auto-stop). Concrete wire form (`clip=` is the numeric `u32` id, e.g. `clip=0`): `ESC _ tgp;e;tok=A;vp=2;ev=anim_end;node=base;clip=0;reason=completed ESC \`. **Viewport-selection rule for a viewport-independent playback (resolves the "which `vp=`?" gap):** a `Playback` is keyed by `(token, clip, node)` and is viewport-independent (the P4-T15 ruling — the `PlaybackTable` is not viewport-bound), but the `anim_end` event is gated and addressed by the per-`(viewport, node, ev-type)` subscription model of S12.5. The rule: **`anim_end` is emitted once per viewport whose `root` subtree contains the playback's `node=` AND that has a live `anim_end` sub for that node** (token-scoped, focus-gated per G1). A playback whose driven `node=` is in NO subscribed viewport emits nothing (no fabricated default `vp=`). A node visible in two subscribed viewports yields TWO `anim_end` frames (one per subscribed viewport, each carrying its own `vp=`). This makes P4-T9's single `vp=2` emission, P4-T12's `vp=2`, and P4-T15's "no `anim_end` for `vp=2` after the viewport is destroyed" all concrete and mutually consistent: each P4 anim fixture has exactly one hosting+subscribed viewport, so exactly one `anim_end` fires, and destroying that viewport (P4-T15) removes the only route. **Design-reconciliation item:** S12.5's closed `ev=` vocabulary never gained `anim_end` while S14.4 emits it — this task resolves that contradiction. This event is the single emission token reused by P4-T9 (non-loop end), P4-T12 (explore takeover), and P4-T13 (remove auto-stop). It is emitted through the EVENT-EMIT path (token-tagged, focus/sub-gated, into `pty_replies`); `destroyed` (viewport-scoped, `reason=`) is NOT overloaded for node anim notifications. **`sub` ingress ev-filter widened to ACCEPT `ev=anim_end` (round-3 review P4-R3-3):** the closed `ev=` enum is ONE vocabulary shared by both the emitter (`tgp;e;ev=…`) and the `sub` ingress ev-filter accept-list (S12.5: `sub … ev=click,hover`). Adding `anim_end` to the enum therefore has TWO coupled surfaces, and BOTH are landed here: the emit token (above) AND the `sub` parser's ev-filter accept-list, which this task WIDENS to include `anim_end` so a `sub … ev=anim_end` frame is ACCEPTED (stored as a live sub) rather than rejected `invalid_sub` or silently dropped as an unknown ev token. Without this widening, every "inject an `anim_end` subscription" precondition in P4-T9/T12/T13/T15 (and the two-viewport fan-out test below) would be unreachable and those emission tests would vacuously pass. The `sub`-acceptance path for `anim_end` is the new ingress surface this task owns and tests (see `sub_accepts_ev_anim_end_filter` below).

**Design refs:** S14.4, S12.5; G1

**Depends on:** EVENT-EMIT

**Tests:**
- `anim_end_is_in_closed_ev_enum` / INGRESS / Parse/round-trip an `ev=anim_end` event descriptor through the event formatter (unit-level: construct the event value and serialize). / The formatter accepts `anim_end` as a valid `ev=` token and emits `ev=anim_end;node=…;clip=…;reason=…`; an unrecognized `ev=` token is still rejected (enum stays closed, G3-adjacent).
- `anim_end_reason_field_round_trips` / INGRESS / Emit an `anim_end` with `reason=completed`, `reason=removed`, `reason=explore_takeover`. / Each serializes the exact `reason=` token; the three reasons are distinguishable by the reader.
- `sub_accepts_ev_anim_end_filter` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`; create viewport `vp=2` and node `base`. `Parser::advance` a concrete subscription frame whose ev-filter names the new token: `ESC _ tgp;sub;tok=A;vp=2;nodes=base;ev=anim_end ESC \`. / The sub is STORED LIVE — queryable via the sub-state accessor (e.g. `Term::sub_state(A,2)` lists an `anim_end` filter for `base`) — and NO `tgp;x;code=invalid_sub` (and no silent drop) is queued; `anim_end` is in the `sub` ingress ev-filter accept-list (P4-R3-3). This is the precondition the five subscribed-emission tests (P4-T9 `nonloop_end_emits_anim_end_when_subscribed`, P4-T12 `explore_takeover_emits_anim_end_when_subscribed`, P4-T13 `autostop_emits_anim_end_removed_when_subscribed`, P4-T15 `csi_2j_…`, and `anim_end_emits_once_per_subscribed_hosting_viewport` below) rest on — they "inject an `anim_end` subscription" through this accepted ingress path.
- `sub_rejects_unknown_ev_token_invalid_sub` / INGRESS / Handshake `tok=A`; create `vp=2`, node `base`. `Parser::advance` `ESC _ tgp;sub;tok=A;vp=2;nodes=base;ev=frobnicate ESC \` naming an ev token NOT in the closed enum. / `tgp;x;code=invalid_sub;tok=A` queued (the ev-filter accept-list stays CLOSED — only widened to add `anim_end`, not opened; G3-adjacent); no sub stored. (Pins that the widening in `sub_accepts_ev_anim_end_filter` is a single-token addition, not a relaxation of the closed-enum check.)
- `anim_end_emits_once_per_subscribed_hosting_viewport` / INTERACTION / Handshake `tok=A`; create node `base` framed in TWO viewports `vp=2` and `vp=3` whose `root` subtrees both contain `base`. Inject an `anim_end` sub for `base` on BOTH `vp=2` and `vp=3` via the Term input/sub API (the same accepted `ev=anim_end` filter exercised by `sub_accepts_ev_anim_end_filter`). `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0` (1.0s clip); advance 1.0s + tick. / TWO `tgp;e;…;ev=anim_end;node=base;clip=0;reason=completed` frames are queued — one carrying `vp=2`, one carrying `vp=3` (one per subscribed hosting viewport, the viewport-selection rule). A subsequent identical playback after subscribing only `vp=2` yields exactly ONE `anim_end` (`vp=2`); a playback whose `node=` is in no subscribed viewport yields none.

## P4-T9 — Non-loop end holds last frame + emits anim_end; loop wraps and never emits

**Title:** Non-loop end holds last frame + emits anim_end; loop wraps and never emits

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/anim.rs`: in the tick, when a non-looping playback's `t` reaches/exceeds `clip.duration`, clamp pose to the final frame, transition `Playing → Stopped`, RELEASE TRS ownership of its driven nodes (leave them at the final pose — no snap-to-bind), and emit an optional `anim_end` event (`reason=completed`) to the owning token if subscribed (S14.4). A looping playback wraps `t` modulo duration and NEVER emits `anim_end`. **CROSS-CRATE:** `anim_end` is a `tgp;e` event (the P4-T9a token) emitted through the EVENT-EMIT path (token-tagged, focus/sub-gated, into `pty_replies`).

**Design refs:** S14.4; G2

**Depends on:** P4-T6, P4-T8, P4-T9a, EVENT-EMIT

**Tests:**
- `nonloop_end_holds_last_frame_and_stops` / INGRESS / 1.0s clip (→ `ClipId 0`), `loop=0`. `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0`; advance 1.2s + tick. / `Scene::playback(A,0,"base")` returns `Some(&Playback{state:Stopped})` — non-loop end RETAINS the entry as `Stopped` (NOT `None`; the P4-T6 entry-lifecycle rule, P4-R3-5, same retention as explicit `stop`); driven node pose == final-frame pose (NOT bind/identity); TRS ownership released so a subsequent `node.upsert` of a TRS field on that node SUCCEEDS (no `node_busy`).
- `nonloop_end_emits_anim_end_when_subscribed` / INTERACTION / Handshake `tok=A`; the fixture has `base` hosted in exactly ONE viewport `vp=2`; inject an `anim_end` subscription for `base` on `vp=2` via the Term input/sub API. `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0` (1.0s clip); advance 1.0s + tick. / A `tgp;e;tok=A;vp=2;ev=anim_end;node=base;clip=0;reason=completed` is queued in `pty_replies` EXACTLY ONCE — one frame for the single subscribed hosting viewport, per the P4-T9a viewport-selection rule (one `anim_end` per subscribed viewport whose root contains `node=`).
- `nonloop_end_no_event_when_not_subscribed` / INGRESS / Handshaked `tok=A` with NO event sub. `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0` (1.0s clip); advance 1.0s + tick. / No `tgp;e` queued; playback still Stopped with last frame held (event is optional, S14.4).
- `looping_clip_wraps_no_anim_end` / INGRESS / 1.0s clip (→ `ClipId 0`), `loop=1`. `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=1`; advance 1.3s + tick. / `playback.state == Playing`; `t == 0.3` (wrapped modulo 1.0); NO `anim_end` ever queued across multiple wraps (S14.4).
- `anim_end_gated_off_on_focus_loss` / INTERACTION / Handshake `tok=A`, inject an event subscription via the Term input/sub API; `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base;loop=0` (1.0s clip); inject a focus-out event through the Term input API (the INTERACTION-seam driver per G6 — focus does NOT arrive via `Parser::advance`) BEFORE t reaches duration; advance the clock past duration + tick. / The playback's emission is cancelled on focus loss and NO `ev=anim_end` leaked into `pty_replies` (emission gating, G1 / S07). (If the intended driver is a foreground-process-group change instead, state that explicit LIFECYCLE driver in the test body and retag accordingly.)

## P4-T10 — stop releases ownership + holds last pose; pause holds + retains ownership

**Title:** stop releases ownership + holds last pose; pause holds + retains ownership

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/anim.rs`: `stop` transitions to `Stopped`, leaves driven nodes at their last-evaluated pose, releases TRS ownership (clears the busy-flag on those nodes), and emits an optional ack/event. `pause` holds the current pose AND retains ownership (nodes stay busy). Add accessor `Scene::node_trs_owner(tok,node) -> Option<(ClipId,NodeId)>` exposing which playback owns a node's TRS (`None` == app-owned).

**Design refs:** S14.3, S14.5; G2

**Depends on:** P4-T6

**Tests:**
- `stop_holds_last_pose_and_releases_ownership` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; advance 400ms+tick (t=0.4, some pose P); `tgp;anim;tok=A;stop;clip=0;node=base`. / `Scene::playback(A,0,"base")` returns `Some(&Playback{state:Stopped})` — the entry is RETAINED in the table after explicit `stop` (NOT `None`; the entry-lifecycle rule pinned in P4-T6, P4-R3-5), with `.t == 0.4`; node local TRS == pose P (held, not reset); `Scene::node_trs_owner(A,"base") == None` (released).
- `pause_retains_ownership` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; advance 200ms+tick; `tgp;anim;tok=A;pause;clip=0;node=base`. / `state == Paused`; `Scene::node_trs_owner(A,"base") == Some((0,"base"))` (pause holds AND retains, S14.3).
- `stop_then_app_patch_succeeds` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; `tgp;anim;tok=A;stop;clip=0;node=base`; then `node.upsert` setting `base.trs` to a new translate. / The `node.upsert` commits (no `node_busy`); `base.trs == the app-set value` (app regained control after stop, S14.5).

## P4-T11 — Ownership conflict: node_busy on app TRS patch over an active playback

**Title:** Ownership conflict: node_busy on app TRS patch over an active playback

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/operation.rs` (patch apply): when a `node.upsert` writes a TRS field of a node whose TRS is owned by an active (Playing|Paused) playback, reject the WHOLE txn with `x code=node_busy`, scene unchanged. Upserts of NON-TRS fields (`visible`, `tint`, material ref, `alt`) on a played node are unaffected and commit. Enforce against the P4-T10 ownership map.

**Design refs:** S14.5; G2

**Depends on:** P4-T10, ERROR-FRAME

**Tests:**
- `trs_patch_over_playing_node_rejected_node_busy` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` (Playing). `node.upsert` setting `base.trs` (new rotation). / `tgp;x;code=node_busy` queued; `base.trs` unchanged from its current playback pose; whole txn rejected (S14.5).
- `trs_patch_over_paused_node_also_node_busy` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; `tgp;anim;tok=A;pause;clip=0;node=base`; `node.upsert` setting `base.trs`. / `tgp;x;code=node_busy` (Paused retains ownership, S14.5).
- `non_trs_patch_on_played_node_succeeds` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` (Mesh node). `node.upsert` setting `base.visible=false` AND `base.tint` AND `base.alt` (no TRS field). / Upsert commits; `base.visible==false`, tint/alt updated; playback still Playing, still owns TRS (S14.5).
- `node_busy_txn_is_atomic` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; then ONE txn: `node.upsert other` (new free node) AND `node.upsert base.trs` (`base` is played). / `tgp;x;code=node_busy`; NEITHER op applied — `other` not created, `base` unchanged (whole txn rejected, S14.5).

## P4-T12 — Camera ownership: camera_busy vs explore (both directions)

**Title:** Camera ownership: camera_busy vs explore (both directions)

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/anim.rs` + the explore controller (`EXPLORE`): a viewport's camera node has at most one terminal-side controller. (a) `anim;play` targeting a node that is the camera of a viewport with explore enabled → reject `x code=camera_busy`. (b) Enabling `explore` on a viewport STOPS any playback already driving that viewport's camera node (releases TRS, holds pose, emits the `anim_end` event with `reason=explore_takeover` if subscribed **through the same auto-stop→emit helper P4-T9 lands** — the P4-R3-2 rule: both auto-stop-and-emit siblings (this task and P4-T13) reuse the P4-T9 helper, so both depend on P4-T9, not just P4-T9a). The explore-takeover auto-stop is a release-not-evict transition (the camera playback becomes a retained `Stopped` entry, like explicit `stop`; the P4-T6 entry-lifecycle rule, P4-R3-5). Ordinary (non-camera) model nodes are never contended. Add accessor `Term::viewport_camera_controller(tok,vp) -> Explore|Playback|None`.

**Design refs:** S14.5, S12.3; G2

**Depends on:** P4-T6, P4-T9, P4-T9a, EXPLORE

**Tests:**
- `play_on_explore_camera_rejected_camera_busy` / INTERACTION / Viewport `vp=2` with camera node `cam` and explore enabled (enabled via the explore-enable path). `Parser::advance` `ESC _ tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=cam ESC \`. / `tgp;x;code=camera_busy` queued; no playback on `cam`; controller stays Explore (S14.5).
- `enabling_explore_stops_camera_playback` / INTERACTION / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=cam` (Playing) with explore OFF; advance 200ms+tick; then enable explore on `vp=2`. / The `cam` playback transitions to Stopped (released, pose held); `Term::viewport_camera_controller(A,2) == Explore` (S14.5).
- `explore_takeover_emits_anim_end_when_subscribed` / INTERACTION / Handshake `tok=A`, inject an event subscription on `vp=2` via the Term input/sub API; `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=cam` (Playing); enable explore on `vp=2`. / A `tgp;e;ev=anim_end;node=cam;clip=0;reason=explore_takeover` (tok=A) is queued for the owning token (S14.5 / P4-T9a).
- `play_on_ordinary_node_never_camera_busy` / INTERACTION / Viewport `vp=2` explore enabled on camera `cam`. `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` where `base` is an ordinary model node (not the camera). / Playback on `base` is Playing; no `camera_busy` (only the camera is contended, S14.5).

## P4-T13 — node.remove auto-stops bound playback; no auto-rebind on same-id upsert

**Title:** node.remove auto-stops bound playback; no auto-rebind on same-id upsert

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/operation.rs` (node.remove apply): removing a node bound to an active playback (its subtree root OR any driven descendant) SUCCEEDS and auto-stops that playback — release ownership and EVICT the `(clip,node)` entry from the `PlaybackTable` ENTIRELY (so `Scene::playback` returns `None`, NOT a retained `Stopped` entry — the entry-lifecycle rule pinned in P4-T6, P4-R3-5; this is the one path that evicts rather than retaining, contrasting explicit `stop`/non-loop end which retain a `Stopped` entry), then emit the optional `anim_end` event (`reason=removed`) to the owning token if subscribed **through the same auto-stop→emit helper P4-T9 lands** (the P4-R3-2 rule: auto-stop emission reuses the P4-T9 helper, so this task depends on P4-T9). A later `node.upsert` reusing the same id starts FRESH (no auto-rebind — the new node is a new animation identity, S14.5).

**Design refs:** S14.5; G2

**Depends on:** P4-T6, P4-T9, P4-T9a

**Tests:**
- `remove_played_node_autostops` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` (Playing); `node.remove base`. / `node.remove` succeeds (`base` gone from `tgp_scene`); the playback is auto-stopped AND its table entry is EVICTED — `Scene::playback(A,0,"base") == None` (NOT `Some(Stopped)`; node.remove evicts, contrasting explicit-stop/non-loop-end which retain a `Stopped` entry — the P4-T6 entry-lifecycle rule, P4-R3-5); no error queued.
- `remove_driven_descendant_autostops` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` where the clip drives descendant `elbow`; `node.remove elbow`. / Removal succeeds and the `(0,base)` playback is auto-stopped (a driven descendant removal stops the playback, S14.5).
- `autostop_emits_anim_end_removed_when_subscribed` / INTERACTION / Handshake `tok=A`, inject an event subscription on `base`'s viewport/node via the Term input/sub API; `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; `node.remove base`. / A `tgp;e;ev=anim_end;node=base;clip=0;reason=removed` (tok=A) is queued for the owning token (S14.5 / P4-T9a).
- `same_id_upsert_after_remove_does_not_rebind` / INGRESS / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; `node.remove base` (auto-stop); `node.upsert` re-creating `base` (same id). / The new `base` node is Stopped/app-owned — no playback auto-restarts; `Scene::node_trs_owner(A,"base") == None` (no auto-rebind, S14.5).

## P4-T14 — Skinning: accept skinned GLB, render bind pose, do NOT advertise feat=skin

**Title:** Skinning: accept skinned GLB, render bind pose, do NOT advertise feat=skin

**Deliverable:** `toastty-graphics/src/tgp/glb_loader.rs`: parse glTF skins (joints, inverse-bind matrices) into the scene model with the same bounds checks as any asset (S14.8). The renderer (`toastty-render`) renders the BIND POSE (ignores joint weights) — never crashes, never garbage. `toastty-graphics/src/tgp` capability reply (S07): do NOT include `skin` in `feat=`. Add accessor `Scene::asset_has_skin(tok,asset) -> bool` so tests confirm the skin parsed and bind pose is the render path. **G8 clean-break guardrail (round-3 review P4-R3-7 — restated because this is the ONE P4 task that writes into `toastty-render`, the crate that today holds the legacy rgp pipelines + the shared Depth32Float pass):** the bind-pose render path uses the GREENFIELD TGP render path built in earlier phases and **shares no types or functions with `rgp/*`** — it does NOT reuse or bridge rgp's skinning or pipeline types (rendering a bind pose is ordinary static-mesh rendering on the TGP path). The legacy `rgp/*` render code is UNTOUCHED. This is the same clean-break constraint P4-T0 and P4-T3 carry; it is mandatory here, not advisory.

**Design refs:** S14.8, S07.1; G7, G8

**Depends on:** P4-T3, ASSET-ADD

**Tests:**
- `skinned_glb_accepted_parses_skin` / INGRESS / `asset.add` (`more=0`) a GLB containing a skin (joints + inverse-bind matrices) and skinned mesh vertices. / Asset commits (`asset_revision` bumped once); `Scene::asset_has_skin(A,asset) == true`; no `tgp;x` error (accepted, S14.8).
- `feat_skin_not_advertised` / INGRESS / Handshake: `Parser::advance` `tgp;q;v=2;tok=A`; drain `pty_replies` and parse the `tgp;r` `feat=` list. / The `feat=` token list does NOT contain `skin` (v1 contract, S14.8 / S07.1). (Complements P4-T4a, which asserts `anim`/`graph` ARE present.)
- `skinned_node_world_matrix_is_bind_pose` / INGRESS / `asset.add` the skinned GLB; instantiate its node hierarchy. / The skinned mesh node's WORLD matrix == its bind-pose transform chain (joint weights ignored, deterministic CPU value, S14.8).
- `malformed_skin_bounded_error_no_crash` / INGRESS / `asset.add` a GLB whose skin references an out-of-range joint index / oversized inverse-bind array. / `tgp;x` with a bounded code (e.g. `parse_error` / `cap_exceeded`) citing the asset; no panic, asset not committed (G8 fuzz oracle: bounded scene or structured error, never crash).

## P4-T15 — Lifecycle teardown of playbacks & clips: RIS tears down, DECSTR/alt-screen preserve

**Title:** Lifecycle teardown of playbacks & clips: RIS tears down, DECSTR/alt-screen preserve

**Deliverable:** `toastty-term/src/term.rs` (lifecycle handlers): wire animation state into the existing lifecycle paths (G10). **RIS** (`ESC c`) = full scene teardown across ALL tokens — clear the `PlaybackTable`, drop all clip tables, release all TRS ownership (the VRAM-recovery path). **DECSTR** (`ESC [ ! p`) leaves the scene intact — playbacks keep running and clips persist. **Alt-screen switch** hides inline viewports bound to the previous screen buffer but the playback state SURVIVES in resolution-independent terms (clock phase) and resumes on return (G10). **`CSI 2J`/ED erase-driven viewport destruction (S10.7):** erasing all of an inline viewport's authoritative placeholder cells destroys that viewport (`tgp;e;vp=<id>;ev=destroyed;reason=detached` to the owning token if subscribed, S10.2/S10.7) and drops that viewport's event subscriptions (S12.5.1). The RULING this task pins: the scene-level playback (clip + TRS, keyed by `(token,clip,node)` in the `PlaybackTable` — viewport-independent) **SURVIVES** the viewport destruction (it is not viewport-bound), but the destroyed viewport's per-viewport dirty bit is cleaned up and its torn-down subscription means a later `anim_end` for that playback is NOT emitted against the now-destroyed `vp` (no route to a dead viewport's subscribers). No new types; this task ensures the P4 animation state participates in the established teardown/affinity machinery.

**Design refs:** S14.7, S10.7, S12.5; G10

**Depends on:** P4-T6, P4-T4, LIFECYCLE

**Tests:**
- `ris_tears_down_playbacks_and_clips` / LIFECYCLE / Handshake `tok=A`; `asset.add` a clip (→ `ClipId 0`); `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` (Playing); feed RIS (`ESC c`) bytes via `Parser::advance`. / `Term::tgp_scene()` `PlaybackTable` is empty, `clip_table("A")` is empty, `Scene::node_trs_owner(A,"base") == None`; no panic (full teardown across all tokens, G10).
- `decstr_leaves_playbacks_and_clips_intact` / LIFECYCLE / `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base`; feed DECSTR (`ESC [ ! p`). / Playback still Playing (`Scene::playback(A,0,"base").state == Playing`); `clip_table("A")` still populated (DECSTR leaves the scene, G10).
- `altscreen_switch_preserves_playback_state` / LIFECYCLE / Inline-viewport-bound `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` (Playing); advance 300ms+tick (t=0.3); switch to alt-screen (`ESC [ ? 1049 h`); advance 200ms+tick; switch back (`ESC [ ? 1049 l`). / Playback survives the switch (state==Playing, resolution-independent clock phase, G10); on return the viewport's placeholder is restored and the playback's `t` reflects the elapsed clock (resumes, not reset).
- `csi_2j_destroys_anim_viewport_playback_survives_no_leaked_anim_end` / LIFECYCLE / Handshake `tok=A`; create an INLINE viewport `vp=2` whose placeholder cells host `tgp;anim;tok=A;play;clip=0;node=base` (Playing, 1.0s `loop=0`); inject an `anim_end` event subscription on `vp=2` via the Term input/sub API. Feed `CSI 2J` (`ESC [ 2 J`) bytes via `Parser::advance` that erase the on-screen placeholder cells (S10.7). Then advance the clock past 1.0s + tick. / (a) `vp=2` is destroyed — a `tgp;e;tok=A;vp=2;ev=destroyed;reason=detached` is queued (the erase-driven teardown path, S10.2/S10.7) because the sub was live at erase time; the viewport is gone from `tgp_scene()`. (b) The scene-level playback SURVIVES: `Scene::playback(A,0,"base")` still resolves (clip + TRS are viewport-independent, the pinned ruling above). (c) After the post-erase tick takes `t` past duration, NO `tgp;e;…;ev=anim_end;…;vp=2` is queued — the destroyed viewport's sub was torn down (S12.5.1) so the `anim_end` route to `vp=2` no longer exists; `pty_replies` contains no `anim_end` for `vp=2`.
- `ris_with_no_animation_state_is_safe` / LIFECYCLE / Fresh handshaked `Term` with no playbacks; feed RIS. / No panic; `PlaybackTable` and clip tables empty (idempotent teardown, G10).

## Review resolution log (round 1)

- **P4-1** (high — invented viewport `alt` wire field; S10.1 `Viewport` struct + `tgp;vp` op define no `alt=`): **resolved** via the review's preferred option (b). Added preamble reconciliation item (3) and an explicit "S10 viewport-`alt` wire extension" block in P4-T1's deliverable that EXTENDS S10.1: adds `alt: Option<String>` to the `Viewport` struct AND defines a concrete `alt` CBOR text-string key on the `tgp;vp` create/update op (mirroring the `node.upsert` `alt` key), routed through the same `validate_alt` hardening and the same `bad_param` whole-frame reject (correlator = `vp` id per G4). Corrected the verbatim false claim in old note (2) that viewport `alt` "already has a wire mechanism." Rewrote `viewport_alt_set_and_read` to cite the concrete `tgp;vp` frame bytes carrying the `alt` CBOR key, and added `viewport_alt_over_cap_rejects_bad_param` to test the new wire reject path. P4-T1 design refs gained S10.1.
- **P4-2** (medium — no CSI 2J/ED test for an anim-hosting inline viewport; a LIFECYCLE seam the contract names): **resolved**. Pinned the explicit ruling in P4-T15's deliverable (scene-level playback SURVIVES erase-driven viewport destruction because the `PlaybackTable` is viewport-independent; the destroyed viewport's per-viewport dirty bit is cleaned up and its subs torn down per S12.5.1 so no later `anim_end` routes to the dead `vp`). Added test `csi_2j_destroys_anim_viewport_playback_survives_no_leaked_anim_end` asserting (a) `ev=destroyed;reason=detached` on the erased viewport, (b) `Scene::playback(A,0,"base")` still resolves, (c) no `anim_end` for `vp=2` after the post-erase tick passes duration. P4-T15 design refs gained S10.7, S12.5.
- **P4-3** (low — `denied` foreign-token reject SITE owned by P4-T7 has no test there; only indirect via P4-T4): **resolved**. Added `play_foreign_token_clip_errors_denied` to P4-T7 as the direct anim-parser ingress test (B sends `tgp;anim;tok=B;play;clip=0;node=base` against A's clip/node → `tgp;x;code=denied;tok=B`, no playback). Cross-referenced it from P4-T4 `clip_ids_are_token_namespaced` and vice versa so the reject-site attribution is unambiguous.
- **P4-4** (medium — `clip=C` shorthand is not concrete wire bytes against a `u32` clip id, violating G6): **resolved**. Added a phase-level "Concrete-clip-id convention" (single-animation GLB fixture pins glTF-anim-index 0 → `ClipId 0`; tests feed literal `clip=0` or bind from the `clip0=` ack field). Stated the convention in the P4-T6 deliverable and rewrote EVERY anim test in P4-T6, P4-T7, P4-T8, P4-T9, P4-T10, P4-T11, P4-T12, P4-T13, P4-T15 to feed concrete numeric `clip=0` wire bytes (and concretized P4-T4 `clip_ids_are_token_namespaced` and the P4-T9a event wire form). The pre-existing concrete `clip=999` (P4-T7 `play_unknown_clip_errors`) was preserved.
- **P4-5** (low — two seam mislabels): **resolved**. (a) Retagged P4-T9 `anim_end_gated_off_on_focus_loss` from LIFECYCLE → INTERACTION (it injects a focus-out event through the Term input API, the INTERACTION seam per G6; noted that a foreground-process-group-change driver would instead stay LIFECYCLE if chosen). (b) Retagged P4-T2 `node_alt_resolved_under_at_cursor_cell` from INTERACTION → INGRESS (no synthetic pointer is injected — it is an `&self` accessor query reusing the CPU pick path; made the no-pointer-injected nature explicit in the test body).

All five review issues resolved; no findings deferred or declined. Coverage preserved: every pre-existing task/test retained, two new tests added (P4-T1 `viewport_alt_over_cap_rejects_bad_param`, P4-T7 `play_foreign_token_clip_errors_denied`) plus one new LIFECYCLE test (P4-T15 `csi_2j_destroys_anim_viewport_playback_survives_no_leaked_anim_end`).

## Review resolution log (round 2)

The round-2 review reported coverage clean (zero UNCOVERED surfaces) and 4 open issues (1 medium, 3 low). All four are actionable and resolved below; none declined or deferred.

- **P4-R2-1** (medium — a11y at-cell pick reuses the §12.4 pick path, but that *buffer* is sub-gated while the a11y query injects no `sub`): **resolved**. Verified against S12.4: the cached pick buffer + its GPU pass "exists only for viewports with an active sub", but the CPU ray-cast primitive (ray vs node world AABBs / instance transforms) "is authoritative and always present". Rewrote P4-T2's deliverable so `accessible_name_at_cell` explicitly drives the **on-demand CPU ray-cast** primitive (NOT the sub-gated cached buffer), citing S13.2 (AT surfacing is available even on a never-subscribed viewport). Rewrote `node_alt_resolved_under_at_cursor_cell` to state NO `sub` is active (so per S12.4 there is no cached buffer at all) and that the "agree with CPU pick" clause means "agree with that same on-demand ray-cast primitive". Removes the S12.4 contradiction; keeps the G6 CPU-resolvable-pick invariant.
- **P4-R2-2** (low — `anim_end` event lacks a rule for choosing the `vp=` of a viewport-independent playback): **resolved**. Added an explicit **viewport-selection rule** to P4-T9a's deliverable: `anim_end` is emitted once per viewport whose `root` subtree contains the playback's `node=` AND that has a live `anim_end` sub for that node (token-scoped, focus-gated per G1); a driven node in no subscribed viewport emits nothing (no fabricated default `vp=`); a node in two subscribed viewports yields two frames. This makes P4-T9's single `vp=2`, P4-T12's `vp=2`, and P4-T15's "no `anim_end` for `vp=2` after destroy" mutually consistent (each fixture has exactly one hosting+subscribed viewport). Tied the "exactly once" assertion in P4-T9 `nonloop_end_emits_anim_end_when_subscribed` to the per-viewport rule and added P4-T9a test `anim_end_emits_once_per_subscribed_hosting_viewport` (two-viewport fan-out: two frames; single-viewport: one; no subscribed viewport: none).
- **P4-R2-3** (low — spurious P4-T4a→P4-T3 build edge; advertising `feat=anim` is independent of GLB parsing): **resolved**. Dropped `P4-T3` from P4-T4a's `Depends on:` (now only `CAPS-REPLY`). Added a build-order note in P4-T4a's deliverable clarifying that the caps-string change consumes nothing from the glTF-animation parser and that the `anim_speed_max=16` advertised==enforced coupling to P4-T7 is a prose test-coupling note, not a build dependency.
- **P4-R2-4** (low — `validate_alt` borrows `max_id_bytes` as the alt cap without an advertised==enforced (G7) assertion for alt): **resolved** via the review's option (a). Added a one-line alt-cap design decision to P4-T1's deliverable (alt cap IS the advertised `max_id_bytes`; no new `max_alt_bytes` token; flagged for design owners as a future possibility) and reworked `alt_over_cap_rejects_whole_txn_bad_param` to read the bound from the drained `tgp;r` advertised `max_id_bytes` (length = `max_id_bytes + 1`, NOT a hard-coded 65) and to assert the enforced alt cap == the advertised `max_id_bytes`, so the G7 invariant is now tested for the alt surface and the test cannot silently drift.

All four round-2 issues resolved; none deferred or declined. Coverage preserved: every pre-existing task/test retained; one new test added (P4-T9a `anim_end_emits_once_per_subscribed_hosting_viewport`); two existing tests strengthened (P4-T2 `node_alt_resolved_under_at_cursor_cell` on-demand-ray-cast wording, P4-T1 `alt_over_cap_rejects_whole_txn_bad_param` advertised==enforced assertion); one dependency edge corrected (P4-T4a no longer depends on P4-T3). Task count unchanged at 18.

Concretized the two fates with assertions: P4-T10 `stop_holds_last_pose_and_releases_ownership` now asserts `Some(Stopped)` (retained), P4-T9 `nonloop_end_holds_last_frame_and_stops` now asserts `Some(Stopped)` (retained), P4-T13 `remove_played_node_autostops` now asserts `None` (evicted). P4-T12's explore-takeover note marks its auto-stop as release-not-evict (retained `Stopped`).

### Addendum duties (G11/G12 — authoritative)

- **G11 — `denied` is RESERVED; cross-token id collisions coexist; cross-token handle ref → `bad_ref`/`unknown_*` (no existence leak)**: **resolved**. The two phase tests that previously asserted `code=denied` on cross-token clip/node addressing were rewritten:
  - P4-T4 `clip_ids_are_token_namespaced` → renamed `clip_ids_coexist_per_token_no_denied`: now asserts COEXISTENCE under the private per-token map (both `{A,clip 0}` and `{B,clip 0}` present, independently usable, neither observable from the other — G11(a)) and, for B's frame naming A's-also-existing `node=base` that is absent from B's map, resolves to `unknown_node;tok=B` (G11(b) handle-ref outcome, byte-for-byte identical to absent-everywhere — no existence oracle), NOT `denied`.
  - P4-T7 `play_foreign_token_clip_errors_denied` → renamed `play_foreign_id_resolves_in_own_map_not_denied`: now asserts B's `clip=0` (B holds none) resolves to `unknown_clip;tok=B` (G11(b)/G12.3 row 10), NOT `denied`. P4-T7's deliverable error list dropped the "foreign-token clip/node → `denied`" entry and added a "G11 isolation — NO `denied`" paragraph stating the anim parser never consults another token's map, never emits `denied` (RESERVED), and resolves only against the bearing token's own private map with causally-blind `unknown_clip`/`unknown_node`. The clip/node refs are handle refs (G11(b)), so they take the `unknown_clip`/`unknown_node` resolver codes (which G11(b) explicitly lists as causally-blind), not the patch-ref `bad_ref` code; this is the addendum's "cross-token mesh/handle ref → no existence leak" outcome applied to the anim handle surface. Cross-token camera/root late-bind (G11(c)) and no-handshake → `bad_token` (G11(d)): no P4 test asserted the contrary, so nothing to rewrite; the anim camera contention test (P4-T12) uses same-token `camera_busy` (reachable, G12.3 row 21), unaffected.
- **G12 — error-code emissions match the reachability table; over-max_msg_mb → `cap_exceeded`**: **resolved**. Audited every error code P4 emits against G12.3: `unknown_op` is **REACHABLE per G12.3 row 8 — its sole trigger is the closed control-frame-verb locus, which P4-T7 owns** (an UNRECOGNIZED anim verb; see the now-RESOLVED note below); `unknown_clip`/`unknown_node`/`not_playing`/`no_targets`/`bad_param`/`node_busy`/`camera_busy`/`cap_exceeded`/`invalid_sub`/`bad_token` all REACHABLE per the table and emitted on their canonical triggers; `denied`/`msg_too_large` RESERVED (the only two RESERVED codes) and never emitted by any P4 test (G11/G12.2). Strengthened P4-T3 `oversized_keyframe_array_rejected_cap_exceeded` to state it is an allocation-point cap breach (G12.3 row 4), explicitly DISTINCT from the per-message `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` of G12.1 (which P1 owns), and to branch on `code=cap_exceeded` only (`detail=` advisory). No P4 test emits the now-superseded `msg_too_large`; the over-msg-cap ruling is honored vacuously here (P4 raises no per-message-size breach) and the keyframe/skin caps correctly use `cap_exceeded`.

### Dedup/alias duty

- **ALIAS FIX — P4-T15 `Depends on: LIFECYCLE` was a dangling alias absent from the legend**: **resolved**. Added `LIFECYCLE` to the preamble cross-phase alias legend, mapping it to **P2-T38** (RIS/DECSTR teardown machinery) + **P2-T15** (alt-screen affinity / screen-buffer binding) — the established lifecycle/affinity machinery P4-T15 wires animation state into (G10). The P4-T15 dependency now resolves.

### Decline / justification

- **P4-R3-1 "no new test required"**: the review itself rated this OPTIONAL ("No new test required; coverage is intact") because the verb happy-paths ARE exercised through the P4-T7 ingress seam by P4-T6/P4-T10 tests. Applied the prose cross-reference (the review's stated concrete fix) rather than adding a redundant duplicate test; this is the review's own preferred resolution, not a decline of coverage.
- **G12 `unknown_op` on an unrecognized anim VERB (P4-T7 `unknown_verb_errors_unknown_op`)**: **RESOLVED** — fully consistent with the authoritative addendum, no tension. The addendum makes `unknown_op` **REACHABLE via the closed control-frame-verb locus** (G12.3 row 8), and P4-T7 IS that locus: anim verbs are a CLOSED set `{play,pause,seek,stop}` (S14.3) carried in a control frame that is always fully parsed (S07.2), so an out-of-set verb is a malformed control frame and fatal → `unknown_op`. This is DISTINCT from an unknown OP *inside a patch* (which is SKIPPED, not fatal, per s07.3 forward-compat field/op skipping — patches never emit `unknown_op`). The P4-T7 mapping (closed anim verb violated → `unknown_op`) is the design's own S14.3 intent and matches the updated G12.3 row 8 exactly; the test note and deliverable now cite that row.

Coverage preserved: every pre-existing task/test retained; no op/verb/header-field/error-code/cap/lifecycle-event in scope lost a concrete test. Net test delta: +2 new INGRESS tests on P4-T9a (`sub_accepts_ev_anim_end_filter`, `sub_rejects_unknown_ev_token_invalid_sub`); 2 tests renamed+rewritten for G11 (P4-T4 `clip_ids_coexist_per_token_no_denied`, P4-T7 `play_foreign_id_resolves_in_own_map_not_denied`); several tests strengthened with new assertions (P4-T6 `play_starts_at_t_zero_playing` acquire-on-play; P4-T9/T10/T13 entry-lifecycle fates; P4-T3 keyframe-cap code clarification). One dependency edge added (P4-T12 → P4-T9); one dangling alias resolved (LIFECYCLE → P2-T38 + P2-T15). Task count unchanged at 18.


---

# P5 — Hardening & lifecycle

This phase closes the protocol against untrusted input and ties off every lifecycle event. It enforces every advertised **allocation/framing/structural cap** *before* allocation with the recurring drain-then-error shape, wires the closed versioned structured-error taxonomy (G3) end-to-end (gated on handshake per G5, tagged/routed per G1/G4, with the **reachable/reserved partition fixed by G12.2/G12.3**), isolates objects per-token via **private per-token id maps as the confused-deputy fix (G11, authoritative — supersedes G1's ownership-tagged single-global-namespace `denied`-on-cross-token model)**, stands up the single fuzz oracle at the `Parser::advance(&mut Term)` ingress seam (G6/G8), and implements G10 lifecycle: RIS teardown, DECSTR no-op, alt-screen affinity, CSI 2J/ED inline-placeholder erase, scrollback eviction, GPU device-loss force-reupload, and PTY-EOF mid-binary cleanup.
