For sections 7/10/8 it covers only their caps-enforcement, framing-robustness, and lifecycle surfaces (core surfaces shipped in P1/P2).

**Addendum binding (G11/G12, authoritative — supersedes the base spine and the s07/s08/s10/s15 slices on conflict).** Three taxonomy rulings reshape this phase's error emissions: (1) **G11** — cross-token addressing is *unrepresentable* under the private per-token map, so `denied` is **RESERVED** (no reachable ingress trigger; carried only by the closed-set round-trip); a cross-token id "collision" is **coexistence**, a cross-token handle ref is **`bad_ref`** (no existence oracle), a cross-token viewport `camera`/`root` is **late-bind default**, and a no-handshake token-requiring frame is **`bad_token`** (a well-formed tokenless `tgp;p` is a silent drop). (2) **G12.1** — an over-`max_msg_mb` breach is canonically **`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`**; `msg_too_large` is **RESERVED**. (3) **G12.2/G12.3** — the two reserved codes are `msg_too_large` and `denied` (**24 reachable, 2 reserved**); `unknown_op` is **REACHABLE** via the P4 closed-anim-verb locus (G12.3 row 8) — *not* reserved, just not emitted by any P5 ingress path; **G12.4** — `instanced_node_tint` is **`kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint`** (the `bad_param` synonym is struck). Every P5 error-emission test below conforms to this partition.

**DEDUP framing (binding on P5-T1 / P5-T25).** Two P5 deliverables are reframed as **extensions** of P1 tasks rather than re-implementations: **P5-T1 CONSUMES P1-T16** (reuses the `TgpErrorCode` enum + `wire_code` + `encode_error`; adds only the `ErrorCorr` correlator routing + the `emit_error` formatter + the handshake/focus `should_emit` gating P5 introduces — it does **not** redefine the enum or re-create `error.rs`); **P5-T25 REUSES P1-T17's `encode_ack`** (and its ack sites) and adds **only** the new `max_backchannel_bytes` enforcement on the shared reply queue. See each task's DEDUP note.

**Cap-ownership scope (G7 "advertised == enforced").** Of the closed advertised cap set (s07.1 / s15.2), P5 owns every *allocation, framing, structural, and sizing* cap — the per-asset/per-buffer, per-message, per-session reassembly/VRAM/node/depth/viewport/light/pending caps, the inline/pixel sizing caps, `max_id_bytes`, and `max_backchannel_bytes` (each gets a P5 reject test below, with its exact `detail=` field name). Three advertised members are **enforced in their owning phase, OOS for P5's caps tasks** because they clamp/throttle a *behavioral* surface rather than gate an allocation: **`anim_speed_max`** is enforced by P4 anim playback (clamp/reject of `anim;play;speed>cap`), **`camera_report_hz`** is enforced by P2/P4 explore as the camera-report throttle (driven off the G6 injectable clock), and the negotiation-hint members **`msaa`** and **`click_deadzone_px`** are render/interaction options owned by P3/P2 with no reject path. Their enforcement/clamp tests live in the owning phase; P5's "enforces every advertised cap before allocation" claim is scoped to the allocation/framing/structural/sizing caps it owns and does **not** silently absorb these four. This keeps G7's advertised-set == enforced-set whole across the plan without P5 double-owning behavioral clamps.

The cross-crate change called out throughout: the `toastty-parser` APC back-channel (`apc_end(&mut self) -> ApcEnd`, where `ApcEnd = Done | BinaryFollows { len, enc }`) and its drain/abort states are co-owned by parser and term; tasks that touch binary-frame draining, EOF cleanup, or inactivity abort name it explicitly.

## P5-T1

**Title:** Error-correlator routing + handshake/focus gating — EXTENDS P1-T16

**DEDUP note (binding).** This task **does not redefine** the error enum or re-create `error.rs`. P1-T16 already ships `pub enum TgpErrorCode` (the 26 v1 variants), `fn wire_code(&self)`, and the base `tgp/error.rs` + `encode_error`. **P5-T1 CONSUMES P1-T16** and adds ONLY the P5-introduced layer: the `ErrorCorr` correlator type, the `TgpError { code, detail, corr }` wrapper, and the `emit_error` routing/formatting in `toastty-term` `tgp/reply.rs`. Do **not** re-declare `TgpErrorCode` or `wire_code` here — reuse the P1-T16 enum verbatim. The closed-set round-trip assertion is still **co-owned** with P1-T16 (G12.3: P1-T16/P5-T1 both cover all 26) and re-asserted here as a regression guard against the routing layer drifting from the enum.

**Deliverable:** Reusing P1-T16's `TgpErrorCode` + `wire_code()` + `encode_error` (in `toastty-graphics` `tgp/error.rs`), add — in `toastty-graphics` — the correlator types `struct TgpError { code: TgpErrorCode, detail: Option<String>, corr: ErrorCorr }` and `enum ErrorCorr { Txn { txn: u64, op: Option<u32> }, Vp(ViewportId), Asset(u32), None }` (G4). In `toastty-term`, add `tgp/reply.rs::emit_error(&mut self, tok: &TokenId, err: &TgpError)` that formats `ESC _ tgp;x;tok=<T>;[txn=|vp=|asset=]…;[op=…;]code=<c>;[detail=<d>] ESC \` into `queue_reply` (single PTY FIFO). The reachable/reserved partition is fixed by G12.3 (exactly **24 REACHABLE + 2 RESERVED ({msg_too_large, denied})**); `unknown_op` is REACHABLE — its sole ingress trigger is the P4 closed-anim-verb locus (G12.3 row 8), not a P5 path. This task asserts the partition only through the closed-set encode round-trip (still over all 26 codes) — neither of the 2 reserved codes, and no `unknown_op`, is emitted by any P5 ingress test.

**Design refs:** §15.3, §8.4; G3, G4; G12.2/G12.3 (reachability partition).

**Depends on:** P1-T16 (`TgpErrorCode` enum + `wire_code` + `encode_error`, **consumed not redefined**), P1 handshake/token plumbing, P1 `queue_reply`/`pty_replies`.

**Tests:**
- `error_code_wire_strings_closed_set` / ingress / construct each of the 26 `TgpErrorCode` variants in-test (the P1-T16 enum) / assert `wire_code()` returns the exact 26 strings from the G3 list and that the set has no duplicates (`HashSet::len == 26`). This re-asserts the P1-T16 closed set as a regression guard (co-owned per G12.3); it does not re-define the enum.
- `reserved_codes_round_trip_only` / ingress / construct the 2 RESERVED variants `MsgTooLarge`, `Denied` and round-trip `encode_error → wire_code` for each / assert each maps to its exact string (`msg_too_large`, `denied`) AND is in the closed set, documenting (per G12.2) that these have no reachable ingress emitter anywhere. Also round-trip `UnknownOp` and assert it maps to `unknown_op` and is in the closed set — but note it is **REACHABLE** (its sole ingress trigger is the P4 closed-anim-verb locus, G12.3 row 8), so it is carried here as a no-P5-emitter code, NOT a reserved one. (The all-26 closed-set round-trip in `error_code_wire_strings_closed_set` covers every variant regardless.)
- `emit_error_txn_correlator` / ingress / `emit_error` with `ErrorCorr::Txn{txn:42, op:Some(3)}`, code `CapExceeded`, detail `max_verts_per_asset` / assert `pty_replies` contains `tgp;x;tok=…;txn=42;op=3;code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_verts_per_asset`.
- `emit_error_vp_correlator` / ingress / `emit_error` with `ErrorCorr::Vp(7)`, code `BadRef` / assert reply bytes contain `vp=7` and `code=bad_ref` and contain no `txn=`/`op=`.
- `emit_error_no_correlator_code_only` / ingress / `emit_error` with `ErrorCorr::None`, code `Unsupported` / assert reply is `tgp;x;tok=…;code=unsupported` with no `txn=`/`vp=`/`asset=`/`op=`.
- `emit_error_op_omitted_when_unknown` / ingress / `ErrorCorr::Txn{txn:51, op:None}`, code `CapExceeded`, detail `max_msg` (the canonical over-msg-cap shape per G12.1; the historical `msg_too_large` example is reserved) / assert reply has `txn=51`, `code=cap_exceeded`, `detail=max_msg`, and contains no `op=` token. (Pins the `op=`-omission rule for a pre-decode cap breach, per s15.3 line 120 read through G12.1.)

## P5-T2

**Title:** Handshake-gated error emission (G5) + per-token routing/tagging (G1)

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` `tgp/reply.rs`, route every `emit_error`/`encode_ack`/`emit_event` through `fn should_emit(&self, tok: &TokenId) -> bool` that returns true only when the token has a completed `tgp;q`/`tgp;r` handshake (G5) and the terminal is focused with the owning fg-pgrp (G1 gating). Every emitted frame carries `tok=` (tag, never routed). Add a special-case: a `tgp;`-prefixed frame that fails to parse, *from a handshaked token*, still emits `code=parse_error`; an unparseable frame whose token is unknown/absent is dropped silently. Wire the demux so `emit_*` is a no-op (returns without queueing) when `should_emit` is false.

**Design refs:** §15.3, §7.1 ("Handshake = error-reporting opt-in"); G1, G5.

**Depends on:** P1 handshake state machine, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `unhandshaked_writer_silent_on_malformed` / ingress / feed a malformed `ESC _ tgp;p;tok=ZZ;txn=1;enc=b64 …garbage… ESC \` for token `ZZ` that never sent `tgp;q` / assert `pty_replies` is empty (no `x`, no `a`).
- `handshaked_token_gets_parse_error` / ingress / send `tgp;q;v=1;tok=A1` (consume the `r`), then a malformed `tgp;p;tok=A1;…garbage…` / assert `pty_replies` (after the `r`) contains `tgp;x;tok=A1;code=parse_error`.
- `error_tagged_with_owning_token` / ingress / handshake tokens `A1` and `B2`; emit a `bad_ref` for a frame bearing `A1` / assert the queued `x` carries `tok=A1` and not `tok=B2`.
- `emission_gated_off_when_unfocused` / lifecycle / handshake `A1`; set terminal unfocused (`Term::set_focused(false)`); trigger an error for `A1` / assert no `x` queued; refocus and trigger again / assert `x` now queued.
- `gating_off_cancels_on_pgrp_change` / lifecycle / handshake `A1`; signal fg-pgrp change (`Term::on_fg_pgrp_change()`); trigger error for `A1` / assert no `x` queued and `A1` subs/explore/anim cancelled (accessor `Term::token_emission_live("A1") == false`).

## P5-T3

**Title:** Per-token namespace isolation — PRIVATE per-token id maps (`denied` RESERVED)

**Deliverable (rewritten per G11).** In `toastty-graphics` scene tables, implement the ratified **private per-token id map** model (G11): each handshaked token `T` owns a *physically disjoint* `T → (id → object)` map covering node/asset/material/clip/viewport ids. A reference resolves as `(bearing_token, id)`, **never** as `id` alone, and a token's lookup path touches **only its own storage** — no global id table, no shared bucket a cross-token key could land in, no cross-token lookup primitive anywhere (G11(b′), the no-side-channel rule: a single global `(id→object)` table is **non-conforming**). Add `fn resolve_owned(&self, tok: &TokenId, id: &Id) -> Option<&Obj>` that indexes only `tok`'s sub-map and returns `None` on a miss — it is the ordinary in-namespace resolver, **not** a cross-token gate.

**`denied` is RESERVED — no reachable trigger (G11(a), G12.2).** Because a frame can only ever name ids inside its own map, cross-token addressing is **unrepresentable by construction**; there is no ingress event that produces "you named someone else's object." `denied` therefore stays a member of the closed G3 enum (carried by P5-T1's `error_code_wire_strings_closed_set` round-trip) but has **no reachable ingress emitter** in v1 — exactly the reserved-by-precedent treatment `msg_too_large` gets (the other genuinely RESERVED code; `unknown_op`, by contrast, is REACHABLE via the P4 closed-anim-verb locus, G12.3 row 8). The confused-deputy attack is defeated by the private map (B2 *cannot* address A1's `"car"`); it is not backstopped by a runtime `denied` check, which would be dead code. The only observable cross-token outcomes are: **coexistence** (each token's own id), **`bad_ref`** (a handle/patch ref absent from the bearing token's map — no existence oracle, G11(b)), **late-bind default** (viewport `camera`/`root` absent from the map, G11(c)/G2), or **`bad_token`** (no valid handshake, G11(d)/P5-T4).

**Resolver routing (resolves review I-1/I-2/I-3).** Narrow the resolver behaviors per kind:
- **String node-id `upsert`/`remove`** under token `T` always index `(T,id)`: a "collision" with another token's `(A,id)` is **not representable** — the lookup simply hits or misses `T`'s own entry, so `B2.upsert("car")` creates B2's own `"car"` (coexists with `A1`'s), never `denied` (resolves I-6).
- **Handle refs** (`mesh`/`asset`/`material`/`tex`, all `u32`) resolve through the ordinary txn-final resolver against `T`'s committed map; a miss → `code=bad_ref;detail=<kind>:<id>`, **byte-for-byte indistinguishable** from "never created anywhere" (no existence oracle, G11(b)) — never `denied` (resolves I-2).
- **Viewport `camera`/`root`** are **late-bound** (not patch refs, G2/G11(c)): a miss in `T`'s map → render with the implicit auto-framing default camera / whole-scene root, never reject, never `denied` (resolves I-1; the create-before-camera and remove-fallback tests live in P5-T16).

**Note:** numeric handle refs resolve purely within the requester's namespace and a miss is `bad_ref`; string-id `upsert`/`remove`/`parent` index only the requester's own map (a miss creates, for `upsert`, or no-ops, for `remove`). The terminal MUST NOT distinguish "exists under another token" from "never existed" for any resolver (G11(b)).

**Design refs:** §15.2 (addressing isolation, superseded by G11), §8.4 (denied prose superseded by G11), §7.1 (per-app token); **G11 (private per-token map, authoritative), G12.2 (denied RESERVED), G2 (late-bind).**

**Depends on:** P1 scene tables, P1 patch apply/staging, P5-T1, P5-T2.

**Tests:**
- `same_id_two_tokens_coexist` / ingress / handshake `A1` and `B2`; `A1` upserts node `"cam"`, `B2` upserts node `"cam"` / assert `tgp_scene().node("A1","cam")` and `node("B2","cam")` are **two distinct, coexisting** nodes (independently mutable), both committed, **no error** queued (not a collision — distinct map keys, G11(a); cf. P2-T8 cross-token viewport coexistence).
- `cross_token_upsert_creates_own_node_not_denied` / ingress / `A1` creates node `"car"`; then `B2` sends `node.upsert{id:"car", trs:{t:[1,0,0]}}` / assert **no** `denied` queued, `node("B2","car")` is created (B2's own, `trs.t == [1,0,0]`), and `node("A1","car")` TRS is **unchanged** (the two are independent map entries; B2 never reached A1's object). (Rewrites the old `cross_token_upsert_denied` per G11(a)/I-6: `denied` is RESERVED, the cross-token attempt is unrepresentable, so the same op shape that `same_id_two_tokens_coexist` exercises cannot also be a denial.)
- `cross_token_remove_is_local_noop` / ingress / `A1` creates `"car"`; `B2` sends `node.remove{id:"car"}` / assert **no** `denied`, no error, no revision bump for `A1` (removing an id absent from B2's own map is the documented no-op, §8.6), and `node("A1","car")` still present (B2's remove never touched A1's map). (Rewrites the old `cross_token_remove_denied` per G11(a)/I-6.)
- `cross_token_mesh_ref_bad_ref_no_leak` / ingress / `A1` commits asset id `7`; `B2` sends `node.upsert{id:"m", mesh:7}` / assert `code=bad_ref;detail=mesh:7` queued to `B2` (asset 7 absent from B2's map), whole txn rejected, and the reply is **byte-for-byte identical** to the `mesh:7`-never-created case (no existence oracle — assert against the `inflight_id_not_yet_committed`/never-created `bad_ref` bytes). (Rewrites the old `cross_token_mesh_ref_denied` to `bad_ref` per G11(b)/I-2; consistent with P5-T10 `inflight_id_not_yet_committed`.)
- `cross_token_viewport_camera_late_binds_default` / ingress / `A1` creates camera node `"cam"`; `B2` sends `tgp;vp;camera="cam"` / assert the viewport **is created** under B2 with `viewport_uses_default_camera(vp)==true` and **no** `denied`/`bad_ref` queued (indistinguishable from create-before-camera; the name is simply absent from B2's map, G11(c)/G2). (Rewrites the old `cross_token_viewport_camera_ref_denied` per G11(c)/I-1.)
- `tokenless_frame_dropped_silently` / ingress / no handshake; raw `tgp;p` with absent `tok=` attempts an upsert / assert scene unchanged and (per G5/G11(d)) **no** reply queued (a tokenless writer is dropped silently — not `denied`, not `bad_token` for a well-formed `tgp;p`). (Renamed from `tokenless_frame_denied_silently`: there is no `denied` here, only a silent drop.)
- `private_maps_no_existence_oracle` / ingress / handshake `A1` and `B2`; `A1` commits asset `7` and node `"secret"`; `B2` probes `node.upsert{id:"m1", mesh:7}` (→ `bad_ref;detail=mesh:7`) and `node.upsert{id:"m2", mesh:8}` (asset 8 never created anywhere, → `bad_ref;detail=mesh:8`) / assert both rejects are the **same code** (`bad_ref`) and that the only difference in bytes is the literal id in `detail=` (`7` vs `8`), proving `bad_ref` for "absent in my map" and "absent everywhere" are indistinguishable — B2 cannot enumerate A1's asset-id space (G11(b)).

## P5-T4

**Title:** `bad_token` — malformed/unacceptable handshake token

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` handshake handler, validate the proposed `tok=` in `tgp;q` against the token charset/length rule (≤16 bytes, printable, no control/ESC bytes). A malformed or unacceptable token rejects the handshake with `code=bad_token` (no negotiated session established for it). A still-well-formed `tgp;q` body with a bad token is *not* silently dropped (the query parsed): emit the `bad_token` error keyed only by `code` (no usable `tok=` to scope it; reply carries the raw proposed token verbatim-escaped or `code` only). Note synonym map: `token_denied → bad_token`.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (bad_token), §8.4 (token ≤16 bytes), §7.1; G1, G3.

**Depends on:** P1 handshake parse, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `bad_token_too_long` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;tok=` with a 17-byte token / assert `code=bad_token` queued and no session created (`Term::token_handshaked(<that tok>) == false`).
- `bad_token_control_byte` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;tok=ab\x07cd` (BEL inside token) / assert `code=bad_token` and no session.
- `bad_token_esc_byte` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;tok=a\x1bb` / assert `code=bad_token`, no session, and stream re-syncs (next valid `tgp;q` succeeds).
- `good_token_accepted` / ingress / `tgp;q;v=1;tok=app-7` / assert no `bad_token`, `tgp;r` carries `tok=app-7`, and `token_handshaked("app-7") == true`.

## P5-T5

**Title:** Node-id hardening — charset/length guard before reply (defense-in-depth)

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-graphics` id validation, enforce node-id rule at op-decode time: valid UTF-8, ≤64 bytes (`max_id_bytes`), no C0 (`0x00`–`0x1F`), no DEL (`0x7F`), no C1 (`0x80`–`0x9F`), no ESC (`0x1B`). Violation → `code=bad_param;detail=charset` or `detail=too_long`, op rejected, **whole txn** atomically rejected. The check runs before any id is queued into a reply or event (ids re-enter stdin via `tgp;e`, §15.5). Numeric asset/material/clip handles bypass this (u32, not echoed as bytes).

**Design refs:** §8.4 (id charset), §15.5 (node-id hardening); G8 (id hardening retained).

**Depends on:** P1 patch decode/apply, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `node_id_control_byte_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"a\x01b"}` / assert `code=bad_param;detail=charset`, txn rejected, node not created.
- `node_id_esc_byte_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"x\x1by"}` / assert `code=bad_param;detail=charset` and scene unchanged.
- `node_id_too_long_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:<65 ASCII bytes>}` / assert `code=bad_param;detail=too_long`.
- `node_id_64_bytes_ok` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:<exactly 64 ASCII bytes>}` / assert committed, no error, `tgp_scene().node` present.
- `node_id_c1_byte_rejected` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"a\u{0085}b"}` (C1 NEL) / assert `code=bad_param;detail=charset`.
- `hardened_id_safe_in_event` / interaction / create node with a max-length printable id, subscribe + pick it / assert the queued `tgp;e` `node=` field contains exactly those bytes with no escaping and no control bytes.

## P5-T6

**Title:** `max_msg_mb` enforced at text-header parse + drain-then-error (`enc=bin`)

**Deliverable:** **Cross-crate apc_end change.** In `toastty-parser`, the APC-end hook returns `ApcEnd::BinaryFollows { len, enc }`; in `toastty-term`'s header parse, validate `len` against `max_msg_mb` **before** the pre-scanner enters consume-N. The drain-then-error shape applies to **`enc=bin` only** — only `enc=bin` engages the raw-byte-count binary-read state (§8.2/§8.3). On over-cap for `enc=bin` the term still signals `BinaryFollows` so the pre-scanner drains exactly `len` bytes (escape-scan disabled), discards them (no buffering), returns to Ground, and term emits **`code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`** citing the header `txn` (no `op=`, pre-decode). The drain path lives in the parser's binary-read state with a `drain_only: bool` so no accumulation buffer is allocated when over-cap. Add `Term::pending_bytes() -> usize` accessor (returns 0 after a drain completes).

**Canonical code is `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (G12.1, supersedes `msg_too_large`).** The base spine and s15.2/s15.3 historically named a separate `msg_too_large` code for this breach; G12.1 funnels **both** over-`max_msg_mb` loci (the `enc=bin` drain path here and the b64/already-buffered decode-size reject below) through the single `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`, and **`msg_too_large` is RESERVED** (closed-set round-trip only, no reachable ingress trigger — P5-T1 `reserved_codes_round_trip_only`). Read the s15.2 message-size locus (`s15.md:54`) and the s15.3 example (`s15.md:112`) as emitting `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` — the drain-then-error/correlation shape is unchanged, only the code. `detail=` is advisory/non-load-bearing (G3); tests branch on `code=cap_exceeded` and MAY additionally check `detail=max_msg` as documentation.

**b64 over-cap (no drain).** `enc=b64` **never** enters binary-read / consume-N: it stays inside the ST-terminated APC. `max_msg_mb` is the **decoded** size and applies identically to both encodings; for b64 the in-APC encoded length is bounded at `ceil(max_msg_mb×4/3)` (§7.1/§15.2), so an over-cap b64 frame is detected at header parse (encoded char count over the b64 bound) or at decode, **rejected without any drain loop and without entering binary-read state** (the ST still delimits the APC) with the same `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (G12.1, shared decoded-size contract). `pending_bytes` is an `enc=bin`-only accessor and is never nonzero for a b64 frame.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (message size; the `msg_too_large` spelling there is superseded by G12.1), §8.2 (apc_end back-channel, binary-read is bin-only), §8.3 (b64 stays in-APC, decoded-size cap shared), §7.1 (decoded `max_msg_mb`, b64 bound `ceil(max*4/3)`), §8.7 (`cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` on overflow, drain-then-discard); **G12.1 (canonical `cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`), G3, G4.**

**Depends on:** P1 binary framing + apc_end signal, P5-T1, P5-T2.

**Tests:**
- `over_cap_bin_drains_and_errors` / ingress / handshake `A1`; `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=51;enc=bin;len=<max_msg+1>` followed by that many raw bytes / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` with `txn=51` and no `op=`, and `pending_bytes()==0` after the drain. (G12.1: this is the over-msg-cap canonical code; `msg_too_large` is reserved.)
- `over_cap_bin_stream_stays_synced` / ingress / over-cap bin frame (drained), immediately followed by a valid `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=52;enc=bin;len=<ok>` patch / assert txn 52 commits (scene mutated) — proving framer re-synced after the drain.
- `at_cap_bin_accepted` / ingress / bin frame with `len == max_msg` of a valid small patch / assert committed, no `cap_exceeded`.
- `b64_over_cap_decoded_size` / ingress / `enc=b64` frame whose in-APC base64 length exceeds the b64 bound `ceil(max_msg_mb*4/3)` (i.e. decoded size > `max_msg_mb`) / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (G12.1, same code as the bin path — shared decoded-size contract), scene unchanged, and crucially **no binary-read state was ever entered** (`pending_bytes()==0` throughout — never nonzero — and the bytes after the APC's ST are parsed as a fresh frame, not consumed as payload, proving b64 was not wired through the consume-N/drain path).
- `drain_only_allocates_nothing` / ingress / over-cap **bin** frame split across two `advance()` calls / assert `pending_bytes()` stays 0 throughout (no accumulation buffer ever allocated) and final `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg`.
- `control_frame_inside_payload_is_payload` / ingress / a valid in-cap `enc=bin;tok=A1;txn=53;len=N` patch whose raw payload bytes literally spell a complete `ESC _ tgp;q;v=1;tok=A1 ESC \` (escape-scanning is disabled during consume-N, so these are payload by definition, §8.2/§7.2 "Not mid-binary") / assert **no** `tgp;r` is queued for that embedded query (it was consumed as payload, not parsed as a control frame), consume-N exits after exactly N bytes, and the surrounding patch decodes/commits normally (one `tgp;a;txn=53`). This pins the core re-sync invariant: the framer does not scan for control frames inside a binary payload.

## P5-T7

**Title:** `BIN_HARD_CEIL` — refuse to drain a fabricated multi-GB `len`

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` header parse, add internal compile-time constant `BIN_HARD_CEIL` (≥ `max_reasm_mb`). When `len > BIN_HARD_CEIL`, the terminal does **not** enter the drain loop: it emits `code=cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable` and the pre-scanner stays at the header boundary (returns to Ground without consuming any raw bytes). This stops a fabricated gigantic `len` from making the terminal chase bytes that will never arrive.

**Design refs:** §8.7 (BIN_HARD_CEIL); G3.

**Depends on:** P5-T6.

**Tests:**
- `hard_ceil_no_drain` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=99;enc=bin;len=9999999999` (> ceil) / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=unrecoverable` with `txn=99`, and the parser is back in Ground with `pending_bytes()==0` having consumed **no** payload bytes.
- `hard_ceil_then_resync` / ingress / the fabricated-len frame, then bytes that look like raw payload but are actually a fresh `tgp;q;v=1;tok=A1` / assert the `tgp;q` is parsed normally (since no drain happened, those bytes are not eaten) and a `tgp;r` is queued.
- `below_ceil_above_msg_drains` / ingress / `len` between `max_msg_mb` and `BIN_HARD_CEIL` / assert it **drains** (P5-T6 path) and emits `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (G12.1, not the historical `msg_too_large`), distinct from the `detail=unrecoverable` hard-ceil path — both are `cap_exceeded`, separated by `detail=`.

## P5-T8

**Title:** Truncation / inactivity abort of an in-flight binary frame (`truncated`)

**Deliverable:** **Cross-crate apc_end change.** In the parser's binary-read state (`remaining: u64`, accumulation buffer), add a deterministic inactivity bound measured in PTY-read events against the injectable clock (G6), not wall time. `Term::on_pty_idle(now)` (or per-`advance` clock check) aborts an in-flight consume-N frame when the inactivity bound elapses: discard the buffered bytes, free the reassembly budget (decrement `pending_bytes`), and emit `code=truncated` citing the header `txn` captured at frame start. A terminal reset (RIS/DECSTR) also aborts in-flight frames the same way. The consume-N counter still guarantees exit after exactly `len` bytes; a non-aborted frame that completes is handed to decode.

**Taxonomy split (normative, cross-ref P5-T22 / §8.2 / §8.3).** The word "truncated" appears at two distinct loci with two distinct spellings; this task owns the first: **inactivity/stall abort and DECSTR/RIS reset abort of an in-flight binary frame use the top-level `code=truncated` reject path** (s15.2). The *other* spelling — **PTY-EOF mid-binary** and **CBOR-needs-more-than-`len`** — is `code=parse_error;detail=truncated` (s08.2/s08.3), owned by P5-T22 and P5-T23. Both spellings are intended by the design (G3 lists `truncated` as a top-level code; §8.2/§8.3 use it as a `parse_error` detail at the EOF/CBOR loci) — the tests in this task assert top-level `code=truncated`, never `parse_error`.

**Emission is gated per-reset (normative, resolves the T8↔T17 contradiction).** All three abort triggers (inactivity/stall, **DECSTR**, **RIS**) free the buffer and decrement `pending_bytes` identically, but their *observable* output differs because of G1/G5 emission gating: inactivity/stall abort and **DECSTR** abort emit a top-level `code=truncated` to the **still-live handshaked token** (DECSTR leaves the token's handshake and committed scene intact, §10.7, so the abort of the uncommitted in-flight frame is reported); **RIS** abort frees the buffer the same way but **suppresses the emission** because RIS invalidates all session tokens (G10/§10.7), so G1/G5 gating drops the `truncated` frame before it can leak to a shell prompt (this is exactly what P5-T17 `ris_aborts_inflight_binary` asserts — no `truncated` queued). An implementer wires the same teardown for all three resets and routes the `truncated` through `should_emit` (P5-T2), which naturally suppresses it under RIS.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (truncation/stall), §8.2 (cross-read lifecycle), §15.5; G6 (injectable clock), G3 (truncated).

**Depends on:** P1 binary framing, P5-T6, P5-T1, deterministic clock from P1.

**Tests:**
- `inactivity_abort_emits_truncated` / lifecycle / handshake `A1`; `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=60;enc=bin;len=1000` then feed only 400 bytes; advance the injectable clock past the inactivity bound and call `on_pty_idle` / assert `code=truncated` with `txn=60`, `pending_bytes()==0`, scene unchanged.
- `partial_then_resume_no_abort` / lifecycle / feed 400 of 1000 bytes; advance clock *under* the bound; feed the remaining 600 / assert frame completes (decodes), no `truncated` emitted.
- `decstr_aborts_inflight_binary_emits_truncated` / lifecycle / handshake `A1`; feed `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=61;enc=bin;len=1000` then only 300 bytes; feed `ESC [ ! p` (DECSTR) / assert `code=truncated` with `txn=61` queued to `A1` (DECSTR leaves the handshake live so the abort of the *uncommitted* in-flight frame is reported, §10.7), `pending_bytes()==0`, and the committed scene + token survive (`token_handshaked("A1")==true`, `scene_is_empty()` unchanged). This is the DECSTR companion to T17's RIS-abort test (which asserts the RIS path *suppresses* the emission because RIS invalidates the token) — the two pin the per-reset observable difference. (s15.2 + s10.7)
- `truncated_corr_survives_no_payload` / lifecycle / abort an in-flight frame whose payload never decoded / assert the `truncated` error still cites `txn` from the text header (correlation survives un-decoded payload).
- `idle_with_no_inflight_noop` / lifecycle / no in-flight frame; advance clock and call `on_pty_idle` / assert no error queued.

## P5-T9

**Title:** Chunk reassembly cap (`max_reasm_mb`) — check-before-accept, free-on-breach

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` chunk reassembly (keyed by `(token, asset id)`), check cumulative pending size against `max_reasm_mb` (shared session budget with in-flight binary frames) **on every chunk before its bytes are accepted**. A breach rejects the chunk, **immediately frees all buffered bytes for that id**, decrements the session budget, and emits `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_reasm`. No memory is reserved ahead of arrival. Add `Term::reasm_pending(tok, id) -> usize` and `Term::session_pending_bytes() -> usize` accessors.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (chunk reassembly), §8.7 (caps); G3 (cap_exceeded).

**Depends on:** P1 chunking (`more=1|0`), P5-T1, P5-T2.

**Tests:**
- `reasm_over_cap_frees_and_errors` / ingress / handshake `A1`; chunk-stream asset id `5` with `more=1` frames whose cumulative size crosses `max_reasm_mb` / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_reasm` with `asset=5`, `reasm_pending("A1",5)==0` (freed), `session_pending_bytes()` decremented to pre-stream value.
- `reasm_under_cap_commits` / ingress / chunk-stream under cap, final `more=0` / assert asset committed, `asset_revision` bumped once, `reasm_pending==0`.
- `per_frame_msg_cap_within_chunk` / ingress / a single chunk frame whose own `len` > `max_msg_mb` / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_msg` (per-frame cap fires) and the whole id's buffer freed.
- `no_preallocation_before_arrival` / ingress / send `more=1` header declaring a large eventual asset but only a small first chunk / assert `reasm_pending` equals only the bytes actually arrived (no reservation ahead).

## P5-T10

**Title:** `chunk_conflict` — reject-new-keep-prior (interleave + fmt change)

**Deliverable:** In chunk reassembly, enforce single-id non-interleave (G2): a second open `more=1` stream for an id that already has an in-flight buffer, or an `fmt` change mid-stream, → `code=chunk_conflict;detail=chunk_interleave` or `detail=chunk_fmt_mismatch`, **retaining the prior buffer** and dropping the offending new stream. Independent ids within one token interleave freely. A chunk targeting an already-**committed** id → `code=dup_id;detail=<id>`. In-flight ≠ committed: dup-id check and `mesh:`/`asset:` ref resolution see only committed assets.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (chunk reassembly), §8.7 (interleaving); G2 (reject-new-keep-prior), G3.

**Depends on:** P5-T9, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `chunk_interleave_keeps_prior` / ingress / open `more=1` for asset `5` (partial buffer X); open a second `more=1` for asset `5` again / assert `code=chunk_conflict;detail=chunk_interleave` and `reasm_pending("A1",5)` still equals buffer X's size (prior retained, new dropped).
- `chunk_fmt_change_keeps_prior` / ingress / first chunk of asset `5` `fmt=glb`; next chunk `fmt=gltf` / assert `code=chunk_conflict;detail=chunk_fmt_mismatch` and prior buffer retained.
- `independent_ids_interleave_ok` / ingress / interleave `more=1` chunks of asset `5` and asset `6` (5,6,5,6,…), each closed with `more=0` / assert both assets commit, no `chunk_conflict`.
- `chunk_on_committed_id_dup` / ingress / commit asset `7` fully; send a `more=1` chunk for `7` / assert `code=dup_id;detail=7`.
- `inflight_id_not_yet_committed` / ingress / open `more=1` for asset `8`; in another txn reference `mesh:8` before `more=0` / assert `code=bad_ref;detail=mesh:8` (in-flight buffer does not satisfy ref).
- `inflight_first_chunk_no_self_dup` / ingress / first `more=1` chunk for a fresh id `9` / assert no `dup_id` (in-flight buffer is not a registered asset).

## P5-T11

**Title:** Per-asset / per-buffer caps — check-before-allocate on declared sizes

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-graphics` GLB/glTF asset decode, gate each per-asset/per-buffer cap on the **declared** size before allocation: `max_verts_per_asset`, `max_indices_per_asset`, `max_texels_per_texture`, `max_tex_dim`, `max_asset_bytes`. The declared-size check happens *before* the allocation so a tiny blob declaring a billion-element accessor is rejected without attempting it. Each breach → `code=cap_exceeded;detail=<exact advertised field name>`, txn rejected atomically, partial allocation freed immediately. Caps are read from the advertised set (terminal MUST NOT enforce an unadvertised cap). *(`max_instances_per_buffer` is the one per-buffer cap NOT owned here — instance-count enforcement and its retain-last-committed/no-flicker semantics live wholly in P5-T14, which is the single owner of `detail=max_instances`; this keeps the cap→task mapping 1:1. T11 owns only the GLB/glTF asset-decode caps.)*

**Configurable-advertised-set test seam (owned here, resolves review I-4).** P5 names exactly **one** seam for constructing a `Term` whose advertised cap/enc/feat set differs from the default, so the G7 "MUST NOT enforce an unadvertised cap" invariant (and the `enc=bin`-not-advertised reject in P5-T23) are testable: add the constructor `Term::new_with_caps(caps: AdvertisedCaps)` (a builder over the frozen v1 advertised set — caps values, `enc=` list, `feat=` list) **and** the `&self` accessor `Term::advertised_caps() -> &AdvertisedCaps`. Tests that need a non-default advertised set (`unadvertised_cap_not_enforced` here, `enc_bin_without_advertise_rejected` in P5-T23) build the term via `new_with_caps`, assert via `advertised_caps()` that the cap/enc was truly omitted, then feed the breaching bytes. This seam is owned solely by P5-T11; P5-T23 consumes it.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (per-asset caps), §15.5 (bounds-checked asset parsing); G3, G7 (advertised=enforced).

**Depends on:** P1 GLB ingest + default material, P5-T1. *(This task depends only on P1 GLB ingest for the asset-decode caps; the `max_instances_per_buffer` instance-count cap is no longer claimed here — it is owned by P5-T14, which carries the P2-instancing dependency.)*

**Tests:**
- `max_verts_per_asset_predecode` / ingress / GLB whose accessor declares `count` × stride > `max_verts_per_asset` but whose blob is tiny / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_verts_per_asset`, no allocation, txn rejected.
- `max_indices_per_asset_predecode` / ingress / GLB index accessor count > `max_indices_per_asset` / assert `detail=max_indices_per_asset`.
- `max_tex_dim_before_decompress` / ingress / texture declaring dimension > `max_tex_dim` / assert `detail=max_tex_dim`, rejected before decompress.
- `max_texels_per_texture` / ingress / texture whose w×h texels > `max_texels_per_texture` (each within `max_tex_dim`) / assert `detail=max_texels_per_texture`.
- `max_asset_bytes` / ingress / a committed asset blob (post-reassembly) exceeding `max_asset_bytes` / assert `detail=max_asset_bytes`, scene unchanged.
- `at_cap_accepted` / ingress / asset declaring exactly `max_verts_per_asset` verts in a valid blob / assert committed, no error, `tgp_scene` asset table has the entry.
- `unadvertised_cap_not_enforced` / ingress / build a term via `Term::new_with_caps(...)` whose advertised set omits a given cap (e.g. `max_verts_per_asset`), assert `Term::advertised_caps()` confirms the cap is absent, then feed input that would breach it / assert no `cap_exceeded` fires (terminal never enforces an unadvertised cap, G7).

## P5-T12

**Title:** Bounded decode at every expansion point (decompression-bomb guard)

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-graphics` decode paths, enforce expanded-output caps **incrementally during decode**, never only at the end, at the three loci: (1) glTF/GLB accessor expansion (declared `count` × component size vs per-asset cap, plus per-accessor bounds against actual blob length); (2) CBOR decode (cap array/map element counts and nesting depth so a tiny CBOR cannot declare a billion-element array); (3) texture decode (declared dims vs `max_tex_dim` before decompress, abort if running output exceeds budget mid-decompress). Any breach aborts decode, **frees the partial allocation immediately**, rejects the txn, emits `cap_exceeded` (or `parse_error` for malformed structure). Also reject NaN/inf where invalid and overlong strings (§15.5).

**Design refs:** §15.5 (bounded decode), §15.2 (expanded-output); G3.

**Depends on:** P1 CBOR decode + GLB ingest, P5-T11, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `cbor_array_count_bomb` / ingress / a tiny CBOR body declaring a 1e9-element array header / assert decode aborts with `code=cap_exceeded` (or `parse_error`), `pending_bytes()==0`, scene unchanged.
- `cbor_nesting_depth_bomb` / ingress / deeply nested CBOR exceeding the decode nesting cap / assert `code=parse_error`, no unbounded recursion (test completes under the time budget).
- `accessor_oob_offset` / ingress / GLB accessor whose offset+length exceeds the buffer-view length / assert `code=parse_error;detail=accessor_oob`, scene unchanged.
- `texture_running_output_aborts_midstream` / ingress / texture whose declared dims pass but whose decompressed output exceeds budget partway / assert decode aborts, partial allocation freed (`Term::vram_used()` unchanged), `code=cap_exceeded`.
- `instance_matrix_nan_rejected` / ingress / `node.instances` xforms byte string with a NaN f32 element / assert `code=parse_error;detail=instance_nan`, not clamped, txn rejected.
- `overlong_string_rejected` / ingress / glTF with an overlong name string beyond the decode limit / assert `code=parse_error`, scene unchanged.

## P5-T13

**Title:** Two-phase CPU-side VRAM accounting (`max_vram_mb_session`, `vram_exhausted`)

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term`, account VRAM **two-phase, CPU-side**, as the single source of truth (never query the GPU). For each txn compute net VRAM delta from declared/expanded asset, texture, and instance-buffer footprint (shared assets counted once; freed bytes from same-txn removals netted out) and validate against `max_vram_mb_session` **before any upload**. Over-cap rejects the **whole txn** with `code=vram_exhausted`, committing nothing; upload and the `asset_revision`/`scene_revision` bump happen only after the txn validates. Add `Term::vram_used() -> u64` (bytes) and `Term::vram_cap() -> u64`.

**Design refs:** §15.2 (VRAM); G3 (vram_exhausted), G9 (revision bump only after validate).

**Depends on:** P1 asset upload accounting, P5-T1, P5-T11.

**Tests:**
- `vram_over_cap_rejects_whole_txn` / ingress / a txn whose net asset footprint pushes `vram_used + delta > max_vram_mb_session` / assert `code=vram_exhausted`, `vram_used()` unchanged, `asset_revision`/`scene_revision` unchanged (nothing committed).
- `vram_shared_asset_counted_once` / ingress / a txn that references one asset from two nodes / assert `vram_used()` increases by the asset footprint exactly once.
- `vram_same_txn_removal_netted` / ingress / a txn that removes asset A (freeing F bytes) and adds asset B (F bytes) so net delta ≈ 0, while `vram_used` is near cap / assert the txn commits (net delta validated, not gross), `vram_used()` ≈ unchanged.
- `vram_under_cap_commits_then_bumps` / ingress / a valid txn under cap / assert committed, `vram_used()` increased, `asset_revision` bumped exactly once.
- `vram_never_queries_gpu` / ingress / run a GPU-free `Term` (no wgpu device) / assert all VRAM accounting tests above pass with the device absent (CPU is source of truth).

## P5-T14

**Title:** Instance-churn cap — retain-last-committed buffer, no flicker

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` instance handling, a `node.instances` op over `max_instances_per_buffer` (or over VRAM) rejects the txn atomically and **retains the node's last committed instance buffer** — never cleared, never flickered to empty (G2). Over-instance-count → `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances`. Multiple `node.instances` for the same node within one render frame coalesce to the latest committed buffer (one upload per node per frame). Confirm the revision rule: an instance change bumps `scene_revision` + per-node instance-dirty, **never** `asset_revision` (G2/G9).

**Design refs:** §15.2 (instance churn), §8.6 (instanced buffers, revision accounting); G2, G3, G9.

**Depends on:** P2 instancing (`node.instances`/`node.instance_set`), P5-T13, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `over_instance_count_retains_prior` / ingress / commit `node.instances{id:"atoms",count:100}` (buffer P); send `node.instances{id:"atoms",count:max_instances+1}` / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_instances`, and `tgp_scene().instances("atoms").count == 100` (prior retained, not cleared).
- `over_instance_no_asset_revision_bump` / ingress / capture `asset_revision`; send the over-cap instance op / assert `asset_revision` unchanged (and `scene_revision` unchanged since txn rejected).
- `instance_change_bumps_scene_not_asset` / ingress / a valid `node.instances` change / assert `scene_revision` bumped, per-node instance-dirty set, `asset_revision` **unchanged**.
- `coalesce_multiple_instances_one_frame` / ingress / two `node.instances` for the same node in one parse batch before a render tick / assert the node's committed buffer equals the **latest** op's data and only one upload is staged (accessor `Term::pending_instance_uploads("atoms") == 1`).

## P5-T15

**Title:** Per-session structural caps — nodes, depth, viewports, lights, pending bytes

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-graphics`/`toastty-term`, enforce the remaining per-session **structural** caps at commit/create time, each check-before-allocate: `max_nodes_session` (node count), `max_node_depth` (parent-chain walk → `code=depth_exceeded`), `max_viewports_session` (viewport create), `max_lights_per_viewport` (light count per viewport), `max_pending_bytes_session` (total in-flight reassembly + binary budget). Each over-cap rejects the offending unit/txn atomically with `code=cap_exceeded;detail=<field>` (depth uses `depth_exceeded`). Add accessors `Term::node_count(tok)`, `Term::viewport_count(tok)`. *(The two per-session caps NOT enforced here — `anim_speed_max` and `camera_report_hz` — are behavioral clamps/throttles owned by P4 anim and P2/P4 explore respectively, OOS for P5 per the preamble's Cap-ownership scope note; this task enforces only the structural per-session caps.)*

**Design refs:** §15.2 (per-session caps), §8.6 (acyclic/depth-bounded); G3.

**Depends on:** P1 scene/depth walk, P2 multi-viewport, P3 lights (for `max_lights_per_viewport` — depend on P3 light task), P5-T1, P5-T9 (pending-bytes budget).

**Tests:**
- `max_nodes_session` / ingress / commit nodes up to `max_nodes_session`, then one more in a new txn / assert the over-cap txn → `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_nodes_session`, `node_count` stays at cap.
- `max_node_depth_exceeded` / ingress / build a parent chain of `max_node_depth + 1` nodes in one txn / assert `code=depth_exceeded`, whole txn rejected, no nodes committed.
- `max_viewports_session` / ingress / create viewports up to cap, then one more / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewports_session` with `vp=` correlator, `viewport_count` at cap.
- `max_lights_per_viewport` / ingress / assign `max_lights_per_viewport + 1` light nodes to one viewport / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_lights_per_viewport`.
- `max_pending_bytes_session` / ingress / multiple concurrent in-flight chunk streams whose combined pending crosses `max_pending_bytes_session` / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_pending_bytes_session` and the offending stream freed.

## P5-T16

**Title:** Inline cell caps + per-viewport pixel cap — reject, never clamp

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` viewport create/update, enforce inline + pixel sizing caps. An `tgp;vp;anchor=inline` whose `CellRect` exceeds `max_inline_cols` or `max_inline_rows` is **rejected** (not partially created) → `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_inline_cols` (or `max_inline_rows`). A viewport whose on-screen-clamped pixel **width** exceeds `max_viewport_px_w` → `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px_w`; whose clamped pixel **height** exceeds `max_viewport_px_h` → `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px_h`. The terminal never silently clamps/truncates/partially binds. `CellRect` stays `u16`. Allocation is always clamped to the on-screen intersection (so an arbitrarily large cell rect allocates at most screen-sized) — but the cap rejection still fires on the declared/clamped pixel size.

**`detail=` spelling note (G7 same-field-name, resolves the s10.3↔s07.1/s15.2 ambiguity).** s10.3 writes the detail as the unsuffixed `max_viewport_px`, and the spine's G7 cap list (line 72) likewise abbreviates it `max_viewport_px`; but s07.1 actually advertises the two **axis-suffixed** caps `max_viewport_px_w` and `max_viewport_px_h`, and s15.2 (normative) requires that "the same field name appears in the reply, in enforcement, and in the `detail=`." To keep G7's advertised-name == enforced-name == `detail=` rule uniform, this task emits the **axis-specific advertised name** (`max_viewport_px_w` / `max_viewport_px_h`) in `detail=`, not the unsuffixed abbreviation. `detail=` is non-load-bearing (apps branch only on `code`, G3/s15.3), so this is a robustness/uniformity choice, not a wire-compat break; the unsuffixed `max_viewport_px` in s10.3/G7 is read as shorthand for "the per-viewport-pixel cap family" whose two advertised members are the suffixed names.

Also carry the G2 **late-bind / remove-fallback robustness regression** here (the in-token happy path lives in P1/P2, but "never rejects, never crashes on a missing camera/root" is part of the hardening contract): a `vp` created before its camera node never rejects and renders with the implicit auto-framing default until the named camera appears; `node.remove` of a node used as a viewport `camera`/`root` succeeds and silently falls that viewport back to the implicit default (§10.5, never `denied`, never a crash). Add `Term::viewport_uses_default_camera(vp) -> bool`.

**Design refs:** §10.2 (cell caps), §10.3 (pixel cap, on-screen clamp), §10.5 (implicit default camera, late-bind, remove-fallback); G3, G7, G2.

**Depends on:** P1 single viewport + cell→pixel mapping + default camera, P2 multi-viewport, P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `inline_cols_over_cap_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;vp;anchor=inline` with `cols = max_inline_cols + 1` / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_inline_cols` with `vp=` correlator, viewport not created (`viewport_count` unchanged).
- `inline_rows_over_cap_rejected` / ingress / inline viewport `rows = max_inline_rows + 1` / assert `detail=max_inline_rows`, not created.
- `inline_at_cap_accepted` / ingress / inline viewport at exactly `max_inline_cols × max_inline_rows` / assert created, no error, pixel rect computed.
- `viewport_px_over_cap_rejected` / ingress / pinned viewport whose clamped pixel **width** exceeds `max_viewport_px_w` while height is within cap / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px_w` (the axis-specific advertised name, per the spelling note above) with `vp=` correlator, not created (`viewport_count` unchanged). This pins the width-triggered path.
- `viewport_px_h_over_cap_rejected` / ingress / pinned viewport whose clamped pixel **height** exceeds `max_viewport_px_h` while width is within cap / assert `code=cap_exceeded;detail=max_viewport_px_h` (axis-specific advertised name), not created — exercising the independent height-axis enforcement path with its own `detail=` (G7: each advertised cap enforced and cited by its own advertised field name).
- `oversized_cellrect_allocates_screen_clamped` / ingress / pinned viewport with a huge cell rect that clamps on-screen to within the pixel cap / assert viewport created and `Term::viewport_pixel_rect(vp)` equals the on-screen intersection (no off-screen allocation). (Relabeled `lifecycle`→`ingress` per review I-5: bytes-in, CPU-computed pixel-rect accessor out, no RIS/DECSTR/resize/EOF drive.)
- `zero_size_cellrect_legal_not_rendered` / ingress / viewport with `cols=0` / assert viewport created, not rendered (no offscreen target; `Term::viewport_has_target(vp)==false`), no error.
- `vp_created_before_camera_no_reject` / ingress / `A1` sends `tgp;vp;camera="cam"` **before** any node `"cam"` exists / assert the viewport is created (no `denied`, no `bad_ref` rejection of the vp itself), `viewport_uses_default_camera(vp)==true`; then `A1` upserts the real `Camera` node `"cam"` / assert the viewport silently adopts it (`viewport_uses_default_camera(vp)==false`) with no error (G2 late-bind).
- `camera_removed_falls_back_to_default` / lifecycle / `A1` creates `Camera` node `"cam"` + a viewport using `camera="cam"` (`viewport_uses_default_camera(vp)==false`); then `node.remove{id:"cam"}` / assert the remove succeeds (no `denied`, no crash), `viewport_alive(vp)==true`, and the viewport silently falls back to the implicit default (`viewport_uses_default_camera(vp)==true`) — G2 remove-fallback, never rejects.

## P5-T17

**Title:** RIS full teardown vs DECSTR no-op

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term`, wire `ESC c` (RIS) to tear down the **entire** TGP scene across **all tokens**: assets, materials, nodes, viewports, GPU buffers, pending chunk/binary buffers, and invalidate all session tokens (handshakes). RIS is the guaranteed VRAM-recovery path — `vram_used()` returns to 0, `session_pending_bytes()` to 0. RIS also aborts any in-flight binary frame (ties to P5-T8). `ESC [ ! p` (DECSTR, soft reset) leaves the TGP scene **untouched** (no node/asset/viewport/token change). Add `Term::scene_is_empty()` accessor.

**Design refs:** §10.7 (RIS/DECSTR), §15; G10.

**Depends on:** P1 scene model + handshake, P5-T8 (in-flight abort), P5-T13 (vram_used), P5-T9 (session pending).

**Tests:**
- `ris_tears_down_all_tokens` / lifecycle / handshake `A1` and `B2`, each commits nodes/assets/viewports; feed `ESC c` / assert `scene_is_empty()==true`, `vram_used()==0`, `viewport_count` 0 for both, and `token_handshaked("A1")==false` and `token_handshaked("B2")==false`.
- `ris_frees_pending_buffers` / lifecycle / open an in-flight `more=1` chunk and a partial binary frame; feed `ESC c` / assert `session_pending_bytes()==0`, `pending_bytes()==0`.
- `ris_aborts_inflight_binary` / lifecycle / mid-consume-N binary frame for handshaked `A1`; feed `ESC c` / assert in-flight frame aborted (per P5-T8 path) and (token now invalid) no `truncated` leaks to a shell prompt; parser back in Ground.
- `decstr_leaves_scene_intact` / lifecycle / commit nodes/assets/viewports under `A1`; feed `ESC [ ! p` / assert `scene_is_empty()==false`, node/asset/viewport counts unchanged, `token_handshaked("A1")==true`, `vram_used()` unchanged.

## P5-T18

**Title:** Alt-screen affinity — hide/restore, no eviction while hidden

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term`, on alt-screen enter (DECSET 1049/1047/47) hide every viewport whose `screen_affinity` excludes the now-active buffer and render those whose affinity includes it; on exit restore the prior set exactly. Hiding only gates rendering — no GPU teardown, all viewport+scene state retained; an inline viewport hidden by an alt-screen switch is **not** scrollback-evicted while hidden. Inline viewports are implicitly bound to their create-time buffer; pinned defaults to current buffer with opt-in `Both`. Affinity gates only the owning app's viewports; scene nodes/assets are buffer-independent. Add `Term::viewport_rendered(vp) -> bool` and `Term::viewport_screen_affinity(vp)`.

**Design refs:** §10.6 (screen affinity); G10.

**Depends on:** P1 viewport model + alt-screen switch handling, P5-T16.

**Tests:**
- `inline_hidden_on_alt_enter` / lifecycle / create inline viewport on Primary; DECSET 1049 (enter alt) / assert `viewport_rendered(vp)==false` and viewport still exists (state retained).
- `inline_restored_on_alt_exit` / lifecycle / enter alt then exit (DECRST 1049) / assert `viewport_rendered(vp)==true`, same `viewport_pixel_rect` as before.
- `pinned_both_persists_across_switch` / lifecycle / pinned viewport with `screen_affinity=Both`; enter alt / assert `viewport_rendered(vp)==true` on the alt buffer.
- `hidden_inline_not_evicted` / lifecycle / inline viewport on Primary; enter alt; advance scrollback far enough that its Primary placeholder cells *would* evict if visible / assert viewport still exists (not destroyed while hidden); exit alt / assert still alive.
- `scene_nodes_unaffected_by_switch` / lifecycle / commit nodes/assets; toggle alt-screen / assert `scene_revision` unchanged and node/asset tables unchanged.

## P5-T19

**Title:** ED / EL / CSI 2J erase inline placeholder cells (destroy or recompute)

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` grid erase handling, treat inline viewport placeholder cells exactly like text: ED (CSI J) / EL (CSI K) erasing **all** of an inline viewport's placeholder cells destroys it and frees its offscreen layer (same teardown path as scrollback eviction, `reason=detached`); a partial erase that leaves some placeholder cells leaves the viewport alive with a cell rect recomputed over the survivors. `CSI 2J` erases on-screen placeholder cells of inline viewports → destroys them. Pinned viewports are not cell-bound and **persist** across all ED/EL including `2J`/`3J`/`2K`. `CSI 2J`/`3J` never free scene nodes/assets. No `tgp;x` on erase-driven destruction (it is normal teardown). Add `Term::viewport_alive(vp) -> bool` and `Term::viewport_cell_rect(vp)`.

**Design refs:** §10.7 (erase & reset), §10.2 (detach on overwrite); G10.

**Depends on:** P1 grid erase + inline placeholder binding, P5-T16, P5-T2 (event emission gating).

**Tests:**
- `csi_2j_destroys_inline` / lifecycle / create inline viewport occupying on-screen cells; feed `ESC [ 2 J` / assert `viewport_alive(vp)==false`, its offscreen layer freed (`viewport_has_target(vp)==false`), no `tgp;x` queued.
- `partial_erase_recomputes_rect` / lifecycle / inline viewport spanning 4×2 cells; EL-erase one row of its cells / assert `viewport_alive(vp)==true` and `viewport_cell_rect(vp)` shrunk to the surviving cells.
- `pinned_persists_across_2j` / lifecycle / pinned viewport; feed `ESC [ 2 J` / assert `viewport_alive(vp)==true` (not cell-bound).
- `pinned_persists_across_3j` / lifecycle / pinned viewport plus committed nodes/assets; feed `ESC [ 3 J` (erase scrollback) / assert `viewport_alive(vp)==true` and scene nodes/assets still present (s10.7: `2J`/`3J` never free scene nodes/assets and never remove pinned viewports).
- `2k_erases_inline_row_recomputes_rect` / lifecycle / inline viewport spanning 4×2 cells; position the cursor on one of its placeholder rows and feed `ESC [ 2 K` (erase entire line) / assert that row's placeholder cells are erased, `viewport_alive(vp)==true`, and `viewport_cell_rect(vp)` shrinks to the surviving row (EL `2K` treated like text erase, §10.7).
- `2j_never_frees_scene` / lifecycle / commit nodes/assets + an inline viewport; feed `ESC [ 2 J` / assert nodes/assets still present (only the inline viewport destroyed), `scene_is_empty()==false`.
- `erase_destroy_emits_event_if_subscribed` / lifecycle / handshaked `A1` with a lifecycle subscription on the inline viewport; `CSI 2J` / assert exactly one `tgp;e;vp=<id>;ev=destroyed;reason=detached` queued, tagged `tok=A1`.
- `overwrite_placeholder_detaches_cell` / lifecycle / write a non-placeholder glyph into one placeholder cell / assert that cell reverts to text and is removed from the viewport's live set (`viewport_cell_rect` shrinks); re-emitting the identical placeholder glyph does **not** detach.

## P5-T20

**Title:** Scrollback eviction destroys inline viewport + frees layer

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term`, key inline-viewport lifetime on placeholder-cell liveness in the bounded scrollback ring (never on the stored `{line,col}` hint). When every placeholder cell of an inline viewport has been evicted from scrollback, destroy the viewport: free its offscreen color+depth+pick targets and remove it from the scene. Shared scene nodes/assets and the referenced camera are **never** touched (a viewport owns only its compositing resources). If the owning token has an active lifecycle subscription, emit exactly one `tgp;e;vp=<id>;ev=destroyed;reason=evicted` (routed/tagged to that token only); otherwise silent. No `tgp;x` — eviction is normal teardown.

**Design refs:** §10.2 (lifetime bounded by scrollback depth); G10.

**Depends on:** P1 inline viewport + scrollback ring, P5-T19 (shared teardown path), P5-T2.

**Tests:**
- `eviction_destroys_viewport` / lifecycle / inline viewport at top of scrollback; emit enough lines to evict all its placeholder cells from the ring / assert `viewport_alive(vp)==false`, `viewport_has_target(vp)==false`.
- `eviction_keeps_shared_nodes` / lifecycle / inline viewport whose camera is a shared node also used elsewhere; evict the viewport / assert the camera node and all assets still present (`tgp_scene().node(cam)` exists), only the viewport gone.
- `eviction_partial_keeps_alive` / lifecycle / evict only some of the viewport's placeholder cells (others still in ring) / assert `viewport_alive(vp)==true`, cell rect recomputed over survivors.
- `eviction_event_when_subscribed` / lifecycle / handshaked `A1` subscribes lifecycle on the viewport; fully evict / assert exactly one `tgp;e;vp=…;ev=destroyed;reason=evicted` tagged `tok=A1`, and no second/duplicate event.
- `eviction_silent_when_unsubscribed` / lifecycle / no lifecycle subscription; fully evict / assert no `tgp;e` and no `tgp;x` queued.

## P5-T21

**Title:** GPU device-loss recovery — force re-upload from CPU source of truth

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term`/`toastty-render`, the CPU scene is the single source of truth. On device loss, `Term::on_device_lost()` marks all assets/instances/viewport targets for re-upload; `Term::rebuild_device(new_device)` re-uploads **everything ignoring dirty gating** (force) and recreates all offscreen targets. CPU state is never lost. Also (per G7/§7.2) push an unsolicited `tgp;r` (full current caps + bumped `caps_gen`) to every handshaked token on a material capability transition (device-loss/restore alters the renderable budget). Add `Term::reupload_pending() -> bool` and `Term::caps_gen() -> u64`.

**Design refs:** §10.7, §7.2 (runtime-variable caps), §15; G10, G7, G9 (force ignores dirty gating).

**Depends on:** P1 asset upload + render targets, P5-T13 (vram accounting), P5-T18 (viewport targets).

**Tests:**
- `device_loss_preserves_cpu_state` / lifecycle / commit nodes/assets/viewports; call `on_device_lost()` / assert `scene_is_empty()==false`, node/asset tables intact, `reupload_pending()==true`.
- `rebuild_forces_reupload_ignoring_dirty` / lifecycle / after loss, clear all dirty flags (simulate clean gate); call `rebuild_device(dev)` / assert every asset/instance/viewport target re-uploaded despite no dirty flags (accessor `Term::uploaded_asset_count()` equals the committed asset count, `reupload_pending()==false`).
- `device_loss_pushes_unsolicited_r` / lifecycle / handshake `A1` and `B2`; trigger a capability transition via device loss / assert both tokens receive an unsolicited `tgp;r` with a bumped `caps_gen` (greater than the handshake's), tagged `tok=A1` and `tok=B2` respectively.
- `tokenless_gets_no_unsolicited_r` / lifecycle / no handshaked tokens; trigger device-loss transition / assert no `tgp;r` queued.

## P5-T22

**Title:** PTY EOF cleanup mid-binary / mid-chunk

**Deliverable:** **Cross-crate apc_end change.** In `toastty-parser` + `toastty-term`, handle stream close (EOF) while in the binary-read state with `remaining > 0`: discard the partial accumulation buffer, free the budget (decrement `pending_bytes`), and — for a *handshaked* token — emit `code=parse_error;detail=truncated` citing the header `txn` captured at frame start (per §8.2 cross-read lifecycle). For an in-flight chunk stream at EOF, free the buffer (reclaimable under reasm cap/timeout) without partial registration. EOF also stops emission for tokens whose PTY input side closed (G1 gating). Add `Term::on_pty_eof()`.

**Taxonomy split (normative, cross-ref P5-T8 / §8.2).** PTY-EOF mid-binary emits **`code=parse_error;detail=truncated`** (s08.2), distinct from the inactivity/RIS abort in P5-T8 which emits **top-level `code=truncated`** (s15.2). The same in-flight frame therefore reports a different `code` depending on *why* it failed to complete — EOF/CBOR-short ⇒ `parse_error` (with `detail=truncated`), inactivity/reset ⇒ `truncated`. An app branching on `code` must accept both for "binary frame did not deliver its bytes." This split is intentional (G3 + §8.2/§8.3) and the tests in this task assert `parse_error;detail=truncated`, never bare `truncated`.

**Design refs:** §8.2 (cross-read lifecycle, EOF), §8.7 (in-flight buffer reclaim), §7.1 (gating on PTY-input close); G10, G1, G3.

**Depends on:** P5-T6, P5-T8, P5-T9, P5-T2.

**Tests:**
- `eof_mid_binary_emits_truncated` / lifecycle / handshaked `A1`; `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=70;enc=bin;len=1000` then feed 300 bytes; call `on_pty_eof()` / assert `code=parse_error;detail=truncated` with `txn=70`, partial buffer discarded (`pending_bytes()==0`), scene unchanged.
- `eof_mid_chunk_frees_buffer` / lifecycle / open `more=1` chunk for asset `5` (partial); `on_pty_eof()` / assert `reasm_pending("A1",5)==0`, no partial asset registered (`tgp_scene` has no asset 5).
- `eof_unhandshaked_silent` / lifecycle / tokenless writer mid-binary; `on_pty_eof()` / assert buffer freed and **no** `parse_error` queued (G5/G1: PTY-input close stops/never-armed emission).
- `eof_stops_emission` / lifecycle / handshaked `A1` with live subscription; `on_pty_eof()` / assert `token_emission_live("A1")==false` and any subsequent triggered event for `A1` is not queued.
- `eof_with_no_inflight_clean` / lifecycle / quiescent term (no in-flight frame); `on_pty_eof()` / assert no error, `pending_bytes()==0`.

## P5-T23

**Title:** Framing-robustness errors — field_order, trailing_bytes, base64, trailer, optional-ST

**Deliverable:** In `toastty-term` header/payload handling, surface the framing-level `parse_error` details: `field_order` (a `len=` seen before any `enc=` in the same header → handled control-frame style, **no** binary-read state entered, §8.2); `trailing_bytes` (CBOR self-terminates before buffer end, §8.3); `base64` (b64 decode failure, §8.3); `trailer_corrupt` (mismatched/absent integrity trailer when `trl=1`, for `bintrailer`-negotiated `enc=bin`, §8.2). Two further framing rejects from §7.1/§8.3: an `enc=bin` header from a token whose negotiated reply did **not** advertise `bin` → `code=parse_error` and **no** binary-read state entered (§7.1 "An app MUST NOT send `enc=bin` unless the reply advertised `bin`; doing so is a `parse_error`"); and an `enc=b64` header whose `len=` (the in-APC base64 **character** count) does not match the actual char count present → `code=parse_error` (§8.3). Also implement the optional-ST tolerance: a single ST (`ESC \`, `BEL`, or `0x9C`) immediately after the raw payload is consumed as a no-op; a second consecutive ST is ordinary input (bounded to one token so an ST flood cannot be absorbed). All re-sync to Ground after the error.

**Design refs:** §8.2 (field order, integrity trailer, optional-ST), §8.3 (len vs payload, base64, b64 char-count validation), §7.1 (enc=bin requires advertised `bin`); G3.

**Depends on:** P5-T6, P5-T1; P1 framing.

**Tests:**
- `len_before_enc_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=80;len=100;enc=bin` (len first) / assert `code=parse_error;detail=field_order`, **no** binary-read state entered (next bytes parsed as a fresh frame, not consumed as payload).
- `cbor_trailing_bytes_rejected` / ingress / bin frame whose `len` exceeds the CBOR item (trailing bytes) / assert `code=parse_error;detail=trailing_bytes`, parser consumed exactly `len`, scene unchanged.
- `cbor_truncated_in_buffer` / ingress / bin frame whose CBOR needs more than `len` bytes / assert `code=parse_error;detail=truncated`, scene unchanged.
- `b64_decode_error` / ingress / `enc=b64` frame with invalid base64 chars / assert `code=parse_error;detail=base64`.
- `enc_bin_without_advertise_rejected` / ingress / build a term via `Term::new_with_caps(...)` (the P5-T11-owned seam) whose advertised `enc` omits `bin`, assert `Term::advertised_caps()` confirms `bin` absent, handshake `A1`, then `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=82;enc=bin;len=20` followed by 20 bytes / assert `code=parse_error` and **no** binary-read state entered (`pending_bytes()==0` throughout; the 20 trailing bytes are parsed as a fresh frame, not consumed as payload — same shape as `len_before_enc_rejected`).
- `b64_len_mismatch_rejected` / ingress / `tgp;p;tok=A1;txn=83;enc=b64;len=100` whose actual in-APC base64 char count is 80 / assert `code=parse_error` (the `len` char-count mismatch, distinct from `detail=base64` decode failure), scene unchanged, Ground re-synced (next `tgp;q` parses cleanly).
- `trailer_corrupt_detected` / ingress / `bintrailer`-negotiated `A1`; `enc=bin;trl=1;len=N` with a wrong trailer (bad magic or wrong length echo) / assert `code=parse_error;detail=trailer_corrupt`, scene unchanged, Ground re-synced.
- `trailer_ok_accepted` / ingress / valid `trl=1` trailer (`TGPB` + LE echo of `len`) / assert patch committed, no error.
- `optional_st_consumed_as_noop` / ingress / valid bin frame immediately followed by one `ESC \` / assert patch committed and the ST consumed (next `tgp;q` parses cleanly); a *second* consecutive `0x9C` is treated as ordinary input (assert it does not get absorbed silently).

## P5-T24

**Title:** Remaining semantic reject codes — exhaustive taxonomy coverage

**Deliverable:** No new production surface beyond what P1–P4 implement; this task guarantees the testing contract that **every** REACHABLE G3 code (the 24 of G12.3) has ≥1 concrete test by covering the codes not exercised by P5-T1..T23 with dedicated reject-path tests, asserting each fires its exact `code` with the right correlator. Codes covered here: `unknown_node`, `unknown_clip`, `bad_index`, `bad_layout` (instance buffer length mismatch, §8.6), `kind_conflict` (multiple/changed kind selectors **and the `instanced_node_tint` trigger** — see below, §8.6), `cycle` (self-parent / parent loop, §8.6), `null_not_allowed`, `node_busy`, `camera_busy`, `not_playing`, `no_targets`, `invalid_sub`, `unsupported`. (`unknown_op` is REACHABLE but its sole ingress trigger is the **P4 closed-anim-verb locus** (G12.3 row 8), exercised in P4 — *no P5 ingress path emits it*; `unknown_op_skipped_within_version` below is a *negative* test pinning the additive-patch-op skip path, which is the only place `unknown_op` could be confused for reachable inside P5, and it must NOT fire there.) `bad_param` is **already covered** by P5-T5's `node_id_*_rejected` (charset/too_long) — it is **not** covered by `instanced_node_tint`, which G12.4 reassigns to `kind_conflict`. With this set, all 26 G3 codes have ≥1 concrete test across the plan (the 24 reachable via ingress reject sites — `unknown_op` in P4 — + the 2 reserved via P5-T1's closed-set round-trip).

**`instanced_node_tint` → `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (G12.4, supersedes the `bad_param` synonym).** A node-level tint on an `Instanced` node is a single trigger that MUST emit exactly **one** code. G12.4 rules the canonical code is `kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (the kind-invariant reading of s08.md:118 / s06.md:117-119), and **strikes the base-spine `instanced_node_tint→bad_param` synonym**. This task therefore tests it as `kind_conflict`, not `bad_param`.

If any reject path is found unimplemented, add it to the owning P1–P4 crate module (note in deliverable which one): `kind_conflict`/`cycle` live in P1 patch decode + commit-time parent-chain walk (§8.6); `bad_layout` lives in P2 instance-buffer decode (§8.6).

**Design refs:** §15.3 (closed enum, reject sites), §8.6, §12, §14; G3, G4.

**Depends on:** P2 (sub/explore: invalid_sub, no_targets), P2 (instances: bad_index, bad_layout, **instanced_node_tint → `kind_conflict`** per G12.4), P4 (anim: camera_busy, not_playing, **node_busy** — the played-node TRS-write reject is owned by P4 anim playback, §14.5), P1 (patch: unknown_node, null_not_allowed, kind_conflict, cycle/parent-chain walk; `unknown_op` is REACHABLE in P4 via the closed-anim-verb locus, so it has no P5 emitter — the P5 patch-op-skip path is negative-test only), P5-T1.

**Tests:**
- `unknown_op_skipped_within_version` / ingress / patch op with an unknown `do:` token on a negotiated `v=1` session / assert per §7.3 unknown ops are **skipped** within a version (no error, rest of txn applies) — the txn commits and the unknown op had no effect. **Reachability decision (explicit):** an unknown **patch `do:` op** is **never** an `unknown_op` ingress trigger — §7.3 makes it a silent skip (forward-compat). `unknown_op` IS globally **REACHABLE**, but its sole trigger is the **P4 closed-anim-verb locus** (a verb outside the closed `anim` set {play,pause,seek,stop}, s14.3, is a malformed control frame that draws `unknown_op`, G12.3 row 8) — a P4 surface, not a P5 one. So no P5 ingress path emits `unknown_op`, and this negative test pins that the patch-op-skip path does **not** fire the code; its closed-set membership is carried by P5-T1 `error_code_wire_strings_closed_set`.
- `unknown_node_emitted` / ingress / `node.instance_set{id:"ghost", index:0, tint:[255,0,0,255]}` (or `node.visible{id:"ghost", visible:false}`) addressing a node that does not exist (a non-`upsert`, non-`remove` op whose target id must already exist) / assert `code=unknown_node` (positive reject site, §8.6 — addressing a missing node for an op that mutates an existing one), whole txn rejected. **Reachability decision (explicit):** `unknown_node` fires on ops that address an existing node (`instance_set`, `visible`); it does **not** fire on `node.remove` of a missing id (that is a documented no-op) nor on `parent`/`mesh`/`material` refs (those yield `bad_ref`).
- `unknown_node_remove_noop` / ingress / `node.remove{id:"ghost"}` for a non-existent id / assert no error, no revision bump (removing a missing id is a no-op, §8.6) — confirms `unknown_node` does NOT fire on the remove path.
- `unknown_clip_emitted` / ingress / `anim;play;clip=<nonexistent>` (handshaked, anim feature) / assert `code=unknown_clip`.
- `bad_index_instance_set` / ingress / `node.instance_set{id:"atoms",index:>=count}` / assert `code=bad_index`.
- `instanced_node_tint_kind_conflict` / ingress / `node.upsert` setting a node-level tint on an `Instanced` node (commit a valid `Instanced` node first so the only fault is the node-level tint) / assert `code=kind_conflict;detail=instanced_node_tint` (G12.4: the canonical code, superseding the struck `bad_param` synonym), whole txn rejected. (Renamed from `instanced_node_tint_bad_param` per G12.4.)
- `instances_xforms_len_mismatch` / ingress / `node.instances{id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:10, xforms:<9×64=576 bytes>}` (one record short of `count×64`) / assert `code=bad_layout;detail=xforms_len` (or `count_mismatch`), whole txn rejected, prior committed buffer (if any) retained.
- `instances_tints_len_mismatch` / ingress / `node.instances{id:"atoms", mesh:5, count:10, xforms:<10×64=640 bytes>, tints:<9×4=36 bytes>}` (tints one record short) / assert `code=bad_layout;detail=tints_len`, whole txn rejected.
- `kind_conflict_multiple_selectors` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"n", mesh:7, light:{...}}` (two kind selectors in one op; commit asset `7` first so the only fault is the dual selector) / assert `code=kind_conflict;detail=multiple_selectors`, whole txn rejected, `tgp_scene().node("A1","n")` not created.
- `kind_conflict_change_kind` / ingress / first txn `node.upsert{id:"m", mesh:7}` (creates a `Mesh` node); second txn `node.upsert{id:"m", camera:{fov:60}}` (selector implies `Camera`) / assert `code=kind_conflict` (no `detail` required beyond the code), node `"m"` stays kind `Mesh` (unchanged).
- `cycle_self_parent` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"a", parent:"a"}` / assert `code=cycle;detail=a`, whole txn rejected, no nodes committed (`node_count("A1")` unchanged).
- `cycle_two_node_loop` / ingress / one txn with `node.upsert{id:"a", parent:"b"}` and `node.upsert{id:"b", parent:"a"}` (cycle resolved against the txn's final staged state) / assert `code=cycle;detail=a` or `detail=b` (the cited offending id), whole txn rejected, neither node committed.
- `null_not_allowed_on_id` / ingress / `node.upsert` with CBOR null on `id` / assert `code=null_not_allowed;detail=id`.
- `null_not_allowed_on_kind_selector` / ingress / `node.upsert{id:"n", mesh:<CBOR null>}` (a kind selector present-but-null — the §8.6 second non-nullable locus: "a CBOR null on a non-nullable field — `id`, or the kind selector when present") / assert `code=null_not_allowed;detail=mesh`, whole txn rejected, `tgp_scene().node("A1","n")` not created. Pins the kind-selector locus distinct from the `id` locus (both fire `null_not_allowed`, §8.6).
- `camera_busy_explore_vs_anim` / interaction / enable explore on a viewport's camera, then `anim;play` on that camera node / assert `code=camera_busy`.
- `node_busy_upsert_trs_on_played_node` / ingress / handshake `A1` (anim feat); one txn commits `node.upsert{id:"arm", trs:{t:[0,0,0]}}` + a GLB `asset.add` carrying a clip (the commit ack maps the glTF-animation-index → `ClipId` `c`, §14.1); then `anim;play;clip=c;node="arm"` (state = Playing, the clip now exclusively owns `"arm"`'s animated TRS channels, §14.5); then a **new** txn `node.upsert{id:"arm", trs:{t:[1,0,0]}}` (writes a TRS field of a node owned by an active playback) / assert `code=node_busy`, whole txn rejected, `tgp_scene().node("A1","arm")` local TRS unchanged (still `t:[0,0,0]`, the last-evaluated pose, not the rejected `[1,0,0]`). **Boundary (second assertion, pins s14.5):** in a follow-up txn `node.upsert{id:"arm", visible:false}` (a non-TRS field on the same played node) / assert **no** `node_busy` — the visibility change commits — because only TRS-channel writes on a played node fire `node_busy`. This is an **ingress** reject (the trigger is a `node.upsert` patch, not a pointer event), so the test feeds bytes via `advance` and asserts the queued `tgp;x`; the `anim;play` step is also an ingress control frame.
- `not_playing_stop` / interaction / `anim;stop` on a node with no active playback / assert `code=not_playing`.
- `no_targets_sub` / ingress / `sub` whose target set resolves to zero nodes / assert `code=no_targets`.
- `invalid_sub_emitted` / ingress / malformed `sub` (bad event mask / unknown sub kind) / assert `code=invalid_sub`.
- `unsupported_feature_gated_verb` / ingress / a verb requiring a feature the token did not negotiate / assert `code=unsupported`.

## P5-T25

**Title:** `max_backchannel_bytes` enforcement on the ack/error/event queue — EXTENDS P1-T17

**DEDUP note (binding).** This task **REUSES P1-T17's `encode_ack`** (the `tgp;a;tok=…;[txn=|vp=|asset=]…` formatter + its txn-commit/`enc=bin`-probe ack semantics + correlator routing). Do **not** re-define `encode_ack`, its signature, or its ack-emission sites here — those ship in P1-T17. **P5-T25 adds ONLY the new `max_backchannel_bytes` enforcement** in the shared queueing path that `emit_error`/`encode_ack`/`emit_event` all funnel through, plus the `Term::backchannel_pending(tok)` accessor. The ack-correlation behavior is exercised by P1-T17; the few ack tests retained below are regression guards confirming the backchannel cap does not break the P1-T17 ack path (and pinning the v1 no-`vp`-ack site so the cap path sees the right frame set).

**Reused from P1-T17 (not re-specified):** `encode_ack(tok, corr)` → `ESC _ tgp;a;tok=<T>;[txn=|vp=|asset=]… ESC \`; acks available to **any handshaked token** (not gated on `sub`, G4); route through `should_emit` (P5-T2); cite the acked frame's correlator (`txn=` patch-commit §8.6, `asset=` `more=0` chunk-commit §8.7, `txn=` `enc=bin` probe §7.1); v1 does **not** ack `vp` frames (the `encode_ack` signature accepts a `vp=` correlator for G4 completeness but has no v1 positive `vp` ack site); a binary frame that fails to decode draws `parse_error`, never an ack.

**New in P5-T25 — `max_backchannel_bytes` enforcement (G7 advertised=enforced).** All three terminal→app reply types (`tgp;x`/`tgp;a`/`tgp;e`) share the single PTY writeback FIFO, so the advertised per-session `max_backchannel_bytes` cap (s07.1, default **4096**) bounds the queued-but-undrained reply volume per token. Enforce it in the shared queueing path used by `emit_error`/`encode_ack`/`emit_event` (the P1-T16/T17 emit primitives consumed by P5-T1/T2 + §12 events): before queueing a frame, if the token's currently-queued (not-yet-flushed-to-PTY) reply bytes plus the new frame would exceed `max_backchannel_bytes`, **drop the new frame rather than queue it** (a flood of events/errors cannot itself wedge the PTY writeback). The cap is per-token and measured against bytes still pending in `pty_replies` for that token; bytes drained to the PTY no longer count. Add `Term::backchannel_pending(tok) -> usize`. (Dropped acks/errors are dropped silently — the app already lost the channel to backpressure; the cap is a safety bound, not a delivery guarantee.)

**Design refs:** §8.6 (acks — P1-T17), §7.1 (enc=bin probe — P1-T17; `max_backchannel_bytes` — this task), §8.3; G4, G5, G7.

**Depends on:** P1-T17 (`encode_ack` + ack sites, **reused not redefined**), P1 patch commit, P5-T1, P5-T2.

**Tests:**
- `ack_on_commit` / ingress / handshaked `A1` commits a valid `tgp;p;txn=90` / assert `tgp;a;tok=A1;txn=90` queued.
